# Volume II Canonical Implementation Matrix

## Status

- Status: Pass A skeleton. Not frozen.
- Last Updated: 2026-07-17
- Owner: Chief Architect
- Product-behaviour baseline: `v1.5-volume-i-frozen` (frozen Volume I)
- Engineering-practice baseline: `v1.7-engineering-manual-accepted` (accepted Engineering Manual)

## Authority

This matrix is the controlling source for Pass B. It carries no product authority of
its own: every row is derived from an accepted Volume I acceptance criterion and cites
the governing source it came from. Where this matrix and Volume I disagree, Volume I
prevails and this matrix is defective. Under PM-REQ-003 authority is resolved by scope
before rank: Volume I owns product behaviour, the Engineering Manual owns engineering
practice, and Volume II owns product-specific implementation contracts only.

## Derivation

This file is generated by `scripts/build_volume_ii_matrix.py` from the accepted Volume I
corpus. Coverage is therefore a property of the source rather than of authoring
diligence: every acceptance criterion in `ACCEPTANCE_AND_TEST_MAPPING.md` becomes exactly
one row, and every row names the criterion it was derived from. Re-running the generator
against an unchanged Volume I MUST reproduce this file byte for byte.

Pass B fills the technical contract fields per row. It MUST NOT add a row that no Volume I
source governs, and MUST NOT remove a row without a controlled Volume I change.

## Contract Fields

A row is `Complete` when its contract is supplied in `specification/volume-ii/contracts/`
and rendered under Completed Implementation Contracts below. A row is `Pass B required`
until then. Fields carry exact ownership and behaviour; a field that genuinely does not
apply carries `Not applicable - <reason>` rather than being left ambiguous.

- contract owner
- interface type
- route
- request schema
- response schema
- controller
- command
- command input
- command output
- actor
- organization scope
- aggregate
- aggregate boundary
- value objects
- domain service
- repository
- persistence model
- migration
- transaction boundary
- concurrency
- idempotency
- background job
- queue
- retry policy
- terminal failure
- reconciliation
- domain events
- event payload
- event producer
- event consumers
- serializer
- authorization entry point
- permission checks
- tenant boundary
- error contract
- audit record
- observability
- retention
- test contracts
- rollout

## Column Meanings

- `Row`: stable matrix row identifier. Rows are never renumbered.
- `AC`: the governing Volume I acceptance criterion. Exactly one per row.
- `Source`: the Volume I artefact that owns the criterion.
- `PR-REQ`: product requirements reaching this row, via the Volume I traceability matrix.
- `CAP` / `WF` / `PRULE`: governing capability, workflow and product rules.
- `OD`: owner decisions the row depends on, resolved or pending.
- `EM`: Engineering Manual volumes governing how the row is built, never what it does.
- `Slice`: implementation slice. `ALL` marks a cross-cutting rule enforced in every slice.
- `Tests`: planned verification types from Volume I.
- `Status`: `Pass B required` until its contract fields are supplied.
- `Blocker`: a live upstream blocker, or `None`.

### MTX-001 - AC-CAP-001 (CAP: CAP-001)

- Slice: S-01
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-001-onboard-organization-or-invited-account
- interface type: Capability-level obligation satisfied entirely by the WF-001 contract. CAP-001 names WF-001 and WF-013 as its dependencies and defines no interface of its own.
- route: Not applicable - CAP-001 defines no interface independent of WF-001; see MTX-026 for the reason no route is asserted.
- command: As MTX-026. CAP-001 adds no command beyond the WF-001 branches.
- actor: Validated self-service identity principal, invitation-bound identity principal, or existing Account identity principal; Organization Administrator after bootstrap.
- organization scope: As MTX-026. The first OrganizationAdmin Assignment is created inside the self-service atomic commit with null expiry, under the bootstrap exception.
- aggregate: As MTX-026.
- aggregate boundary: As MTX-026 - one atomic multi-root product commit.
- persistence model: As MTX-026.
- transaction boundary: As MTX-026.
- concurrency: As MTX-026.
- idempotency: As MTX-026 - exact replay per branch.
- domain events: As MTX-026.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf001`.
- authorization entry point: The approved identity/bootstrap service. CAP-001 Security Implications require compliance with SEC-REQ-001 through SEC-REQ-024.
- permission checks: As MTX-026. CAP-001 Preconditions additionally require that no pre-Organization Account is required or allowed for bootstrap, and that existing-account sign-in names one Organization and never creates or selects an Account implicitly.
- tenant boundary: As MTX-026 - tenant boundary established before Project creation.
- error contract: As MTX-026. CAP-001 Failure Condition additionally requires that pending Account, Organization and BillingEntity states are never externally visible after a failed atomic bootstrap or invited provisioning.
- audit record: As MTX-026.
- observability: Branch-specific identity/grant/invitation/sign-in, Account, Session, Role, policy, BillingEntity, Organization and Project events and audited no-state outcomes under one correlation ID.
- retention: Account lifecycle events and audit records are persisted; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-E2E: every receipt purpose/field/normalization and the 10-minute freshness boundary (before, at, after per AC assertion rule 2). TYP-E2E: one-principal Bootstrap Grant lifecycle and derived Membership. TYP-SEC: protected and nonprotected Invitation lifecycle and race. TYP-DATA: pending Account/Organization/BillingEntity are never externally visible after a failed atomic branch. TYP-INT: sign-in never creates or selects an Account implicitly. The full assertion set is conjunctive per AC assertion rule 6.
- rollout: As MTX-026 - first product slice, no predecessor behaviour.

### MTX-002 - AC-CAP-002 (CAP: CAP-002)

- Slice: S-02
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#cap-002-organization-setup
- interface type: No interface of its own. CAP-002 Product Behavior states the Organization is established 'In the self-service WF-001 transaction', so creation has no separate command, entry point or transaction. This row contracts the Organization-specific obligations carried by that transaction, and the Organization lifecycle limb (suspend, reactivate, closure) is owned by WF-013 in S-23.
- route: Not applicable - CAP-002 defines no interface. Its creation limb executes inside the WF-001 self-service transaction, whose authority WF-001 assigns to the approved identity/bootstrap service, and whose lack of a Volume I route is recorded on MTX-026. Its lifecycle limb is reached through the WF-013 commands owned by S-23.
- request schema: Not applicable - no independent request exists. The inputs arrive on the WF-001 self-service envelope: `organization-profile-v1`, the immutable `access-policy-v1`, `entitlement-interim-v1` and `interim-baseline-plan-v1` approval content hashes, the first accountable administrator Assignment, the Bootstrap Grant, and the idempotency envelope.
- response schema: Contributes to the WF-001 self-service result: Organization record, active baseline BillingEntity, active Access and Entitlement policy versions, linked Plan Assignment and administrator Role Assignment, each with state version.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter; see route.
- command: `Workflows::Wf001::BootstrapOrganization` (creation limb, S-01). Lifecycle limb: `Workflows::Wf013::SuspendOrganization`, `ReactivateOrganization`, `RequestOrganizationClosure`, `DecideOrganizationClosure`, `ExpireOrganizationClosure`, `ExecuteOrganizationClosure`, owned by S-23. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - carried by the WF-001 self-service command input; see MTX-026.
- command output: Not applicable - carried by the WF-001 self-service command output; see MTX-026.
- actor: CAP-002 names the Organization Administrator as actor, but that Assignment is created by this same commit, so no administrator exists to authorize it. AC-CAP-002 resolves this with the `bootstrap-service-only exception`: the approved bootstrap service is the only actor that may create an Organization, and the first OrganizationAdmin Assignment is an output of the commit rather than its authority. The Organization Administrator is the actor for the WF-013 lifecycle limb only.
- organization scope: The Organization is established by this commit, so no pre-existing tenant scope exists to check. Tenant isolation applies from the moment the Organization row exists, and WF-001 Security Notes require the tenant boundary to be established before Project creation, which the ordered commit satisfies by activating Organization before `ProjectCreated`.
- aggregate: Organization, with BillingEntity, AccessPolicy, EntitlementPolicy, PlanAssignment, RoleAssignment and Project created in the same WF-001 multi-root commit.
- aggregate boundary: Organization is the tenant root. It does not own BillingEntity, AccessPolicy or RoleAssignment as child entities; each is an independent root referencing `organization_id`. They are co-created in one transaction because WF-001 requires atomicity, not because they share an aggregate. Cross-root references are by identity per EM-III-011 section 9.
- value objects: OrganizationId, OrganizationProfile (`organization-profile-v1`), ContentHash for `access-policy-v1`, `entitlement-interim-v1` and `interim-baseline-plan-v1`, BillingEntityId, PlanAssignmentId, AuthorizationEpoch.
- domain service: Not applicable - CAP-002 assigns no domain service; the tenant invariant checks run inside the WF-001 application transaction before Organization activation.
- repository: `OrganizationRepository`, `BillingEntityRepository`, `AccessPolicyRepository`, `EntitlementPolicyRepository`, `PlanAssignmentRepository`, `RoleAssignmentRepository`.
- persistence model: Canonical tables per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. Organization carries its lifecycle state (`pending`, `active`, `suspended`, `closed`) and its authorization epoch. BillingEntity carries its own state; the reserved `past_due` and `suspended` values have no baseline transition and MUST be unreachable.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - every table CAP-002 names already exists in the canonical schema. This row binds obligations to existing owners and adds no schema object.
- transaction boundary: The WF-001 self-service transaction; this row adds no boundary of its own. Ordering inside it is normative: Organization pending, BillingEntity pending, policies and Plan Assignment activated, BillingEntity activated, then Organization activated only when every tenant invariant passes. No provider call occurs inside or outside the transaction.
- concurrency: Serialized by the WF-001 branch uniqueness key and the current-grant uniqueness rule, so two concurrent bootstraps for one principal produce one Organization. Duplicate display name is a validated first-match failure fixture, not a uniqueness violation surfaced from the database. Conflicting active policy is rejected: `access-policy-v1` and `entitlement-interim-v1` activate from a null prior version exactly once, and a second active version of either is a conflict.
- idempotency: Idempotent by the WF-001 self-service idempotency envelope. Exact replay returns the stored Organization result and creates no second Organization, BillingEntity, policy version or Plan Assignment, and emits no event.
- background job: Not applicable - AC-CAP-002 forbids it: no provider, callback, lazy, background, first-use or Invitation path may create the BillingEntity. Creation is synchronous inside the transaction or it does not happen.
- queue: Not applicable - no asynchronous work; see background_job.
- retry policy: Inherits the WF-001 bootstrap branch rule: initial attempt plus exactly two service retries at 1 second and 5 seconds for transient transaction or dependency failure only, each rechecking receipt and grant expiry.
- terminal failure: Bootstrap validation failure rolls back new Organization creation entirely and leaves no tenant record. Retry exhaustion returns `F1-DEPENDENCY-503 / onboarding_transaction_unavailable`. A later lifecycle failure retains the current canonical Organization state with an enumerated reason and MUST NOT invent a failure state.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - creation is atomic and leaves no partial tenant record, so no divergent state exists to reconcile.
- domain events: `OrganizationCreated`, then two ordered `BillingStateChanged` events (`to_state=pending`, then `from_state=pending,to_state=active`), then `OrganizationActivated`, all under the same bootstrap correlation and in their fixed positions within the WF-001 thirteen-event order. Lifecycle events (`OrganizationSuspended`, `OrganizationReactivated`, `OrganizationClosureRequested`, `OrganizationClosureApproved`, `OrganizationClosureRejected`, `OrganizationClosureExpired`, `OrganizationClosed`) belong to WF-013 and are contracted in S-23.
- event payload: The canonical envelope carrying the created Organization's real `organization_id`. The OD-013 bootstrap substitution is confined to `BootstrapGrantIssued` and `BootstrapGrantExpired` and MUST NOT appear on any event in this row, because the Organization exists by the time each is emitted.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf001` for the creation events. `Workflows::Wf013` for the lifecycle events, owned by S-23.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - CAP-002 names no consumer. Consumers are contracted by the slices that own them.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined for CAP-002 by Volume I.
- authorization entry point: The approved bootstrap service inside the WF-001 transaction, before any record is created. There is no route and no controller, so no transport-layer check exists to bypass.
- permission checks: Creation: the bootstrap-service-only exception; no Role permission authorizes Organization creation, and the first administrator Assignment is an output rather than the authority. An unauthorized fixture is mandatory per AC-CAP-002 and must produce no tenant record.
- tenant boundary: Tenant isolation applies from the instant the Organization row exists, including while it is `pending`. AC-CAP-002 requires that `pending` is never returned as current state, so no reader may observe a pending Organization even though its immutable transition event is retained.
- error contract: First-match enumerated reasons over: every `organization-profile-v1` field boundary; duplicate display name; first-policy null-version activation; mismatched `access-policy-v1`, `entitlement-interim-v1` or `interim-baseline-plan-v1` content hash; conflicting active policy; first-administrator invariant violation; unauthorized actor; timeout; rollback; replay. Each carries error class/code, one declared reason, severity, retryability, recovery action and correlation reference. `F1-DEPENDENCY-503 / onboarding_transaction_unavailable` on retry exhaustion.
- audit record: Organization metadata and policy history are auditable per CAP-002 Data Implications. The commit records profile normalization and hashes, the Access/Entitlement/Plan approval and BillingEntity linkage hashes, the first-administrator invariant outcome, and the bootstrap-service-only authorization decision, under the bootstrap correlation ID.
- observability: `OrganizationCreated`, the two ordered `BillingStateChanged` transitions and `OrganizationActivated` under the same bootstrap correlation, per CAP-002 Observability Requirements.

TYP-DATA: first-policy null-version activation succeeds exactly once; a conflicting active policy is rejected. TYP-DATA: byte-equivalent hashes activate; each mismatched `access-policy-v1`/`entitlement-interim-v1`/`interim-baseline-plan-v1` hash is rejected. TYP-SEC: the first-administrator invariant holds. TYP-SEC: the bootstrap-service-only exception - an unauthorized actor creates no tenant record. TYP-DATA: exactly one active baseline BillingEntity exists per Organization, linked to the same-Organization active Plan Assignment. TYP-DATA: no provider, callback, lazy, background, first-use or Invitation path creates the BillingEntity, and none enters reserved `past_due` or `suspended`. ### MTX-004 - AC-CAP-004 (CAP: CAP-004)

- Slice: S-04
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#cap-004-website-or-property-onboarding
- interface type: One application command registering exactly one proposed Source under the `source-registration-v1` contract. Reached by a principal holding `source.register`, so a transport exposure is expected at the Volume II baseline.
- route: Not applicable - Volume I defines the command, its permission and its complete envelope but no HTTP path, method or transport. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline; asserting a path here would fix a product contract Volume I has not defined.
- request schema: `source-registration-v1`: one `submitted_root_uri` with grammar `https://<host>` or `https://<host>/`, target Project identity, and the global command envelope including the idempotency key. An explicit `:443` is accepted and removed. Registration provenance is derived from the command and the authorization decision rather than supplied by the caller.
- response schema: Exactly one proposed Source with its Source ID, canonical root URI, canonical host, lifecycle status `proposed` and state version; or one enumerated rejection reason with no Source. No Onboarding Request and no plural candidate entity is ever returned.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I; see route.
- command: `Workflows::Wf004::RegisterSource`. Registration never verifies, activates, crawls or creates Evidence; each of those is a separate command in a later slice.
- command input: Normalized `submitted_root_uri`, Project identity, expected state version, and the command envelope. Host normalization is `ascii-host-v1`: lowercase A-Z, remove one terminal dot, require 1-253 remaining bytes and at least two dot-separated labels, and require every label to be 1-63 ASCII letters, digits or hyphens without a leading or trailing hyphen. A pre-encoded `xn--` label is allowed. Raw Unicode is `source_host_non_ascii` and MUST NOT be normalized by the implementation, because Volume I fixes that reason precisely so implementations cannot diverge on IDNA handling.
- command output: Exactly one proposed Source with immutable provenance and one `SourceRegistered` event; or no Source and one audited command outcome.
- actor: Organization Administrator, Marketing Operator or Technical Implementer holding `source.register` over the target Project.
- organization scope: An active Organization, and a same-Organization Project in `draft`, `active` or `paused`. Project activation is expressly not a prerequisite.
- aggregate: Source.
- aggregate boundary: Source is its own root, referencing Organization and Project by identity. It is not a child of Project. Volume I states that no separate Onboarding Request or Source-candidate entity is created, so no intermediate aggregate may be introduced to stage a registration.
- value objects: SourceId, SubmittedRootUri, CanonicalRootUri (`https://<lowercase_host>/`), CanonicalHost, RegistrationProvenance, SourceStateVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - the URI grammar and host normalization are pure functions applied in the application command; Volume I names no domain service.
- repository: `SourceRepository`; `ProjectRepository` read-only for the Project state precondition.
- persistence model: Canonical `sources` table per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. A Source carries Source ID, Organization and Project IDs, submitted and canonical root URIs, canonical host, `registration_schema_version=source-registration-v1`, `host_normalization_version=ascii-host-v1`, immutable registration provenance, lifecycle status initially `proposed`, state version, and registered and updated times.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - the canonical `sources` table already exists. The uniqueness key `(project_id, canonical_host)` over every Source not `removed` is a partial unique index in the canonical schema; this row adds no schema object.
- transaction boundary: One transaction: validate, check uniqueness, insert exactly one proposed Source with its immutable provenance, emit `SourceRegistered`. The uniqueness check and the insert serialize, so the check cannot pass and then be raced.
- concurrency: The uniqueness check and insert serialize on `(project_id, canonical_host)` across every Source not `removed`. Two concurrent commands for the same key create exactly one Source; the loser receives `F1-DOMAIN-409 / source_host_already_registered`. A committed removed Source does not reopen: a later command creates a new Source with a new ID and new provenance, and never attaches to the removed lineage.
- idempotency: Idempotent by the command idempotency key. Exact replay returns the same Source and emits no second `SourceRegistered`. Changed content under the same key is `idempotency_conflict`. The idempotency key is also recorded inside the immutable registration provenance, so the replay identity is auditable from the Source itself.
- background job: Not applicable - registration is synchronous. Volume I states registration never verifies, activates, crawls or creates Evidence, so there is no deferred work to enqueue.
- queue: Not applicable - no asynchronous work.
- retry policy: Two service retries at exactly 1 and 5 seconds for transient transaction or dependency failure, per AC-CAP-004. No validation, host, state, tenant, permission, duplicate or replay reason is retried.
- terminal failure: Retry exhaustion creates no Source and emits one audited command outcome. Every rejection is terminal for the attempt and leaves no Source, no partial record and no candidate entity.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - the command is atomic and single-root, and a rejection creates nothing, so no divergent state exists to reconcile.
- domain events: `SourceRegistered` on success, exactly once. Nothing on rejection: every rejection emits one audited command outcome rather than an event.
- event payload: Event envelope plus Source identity, Project identity, canonical host and canonical root URI, registration and normalization versions, provenance actor and command, and state version. It MUST NOT carry a submitted email, identity subject, credential or raw authorization token.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf004` is the sole producer of `SourceRegistered`.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - Volume I names no consumer of `SourceRegistered`. Verification (S-05) is triggered by its own command, not by this event.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined by Volume I; see route.
- authorization entry point: The application command, before validation of the URI and before any insert. The authorization decision ID is captured into the immutable registration provenance, so every Source carries proof of the decision that created it.
- permission checks: `source.register` over the target Project, held by an Organization Administrator, Marketing Operator or Technical Implementer. Absence is `source_register_unauthorized`. The Organization MUST be active and the Project MUST be in `draft`, `active` or `paused`; any other Project state is `project_not_registerable`.
- tenant boundary: The Project MUST be same-Organization; otherwise `tenant_mismatch`. The uniqueness key is Project-scoped, so the same canonical host may exist in different Projects and different Organizations without collision.
- error contract: First-match order, normative: `source_request_schema_unsupported`, `source_uri_malformed`, `unsupported_source_scheme`, `source_userinfo_prohibited`, `source_port_unsupported`, `source_path_not_root`, `source_query_prohibited`, `source_fragment_prohibited`, `source_host_non_ascii`, `source_host_invalid`, `organization_inactive`, `project_not_registerable`, `tenant_mismatch`, `source_register_unauthorized`, `stale_state_version`, `source_host_already_registered`, `idempotency_conflict`. Class mapping is fixed: schema, URI and host reasons to `F1-VALIDATION-400`; inactive, state, duplicate and replay reasons to `F1-DOMAIN-409`; tenant and permission reasons to `F1-AUTH-403`. Every rejection creates no Source and emits one audited command outcome.
- audit record: The immutable registration provenance is itself the audit anchor: `origin=human_command`, registering Account ID, command ID, idempotency key, Identity/Session authorization-decision ID, registered time and correlation ID. Every rejection emits one audited command outcome carrying its first-match reason and correlation ID.
- observability: `SourceRegistered` plus onboarding telemetry per CAP-004 Observability Requirements. Telemetry MUST NOT carry a submitted email, identity subject, credential or raw authorization token, matching the event-payload prohibition.
- retention: Source registration metadata and provenance records are stored per CAP-004 Data Implications. Provenance is immutable. The retention window is owned by specification/015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states none.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: grammar - `https://host` and `https://host/` accepted; `https://host:443` and `https://host:443/` accepted with the port removed. TYP-DATA: each first-match reason in order - a nondefault port is `source_port_unsupported` and not `source_uri_malformed`, proving the order is applied rather than approximated. TYP-DATA: user information, query, fragment, nonroot path, wildcard, IP literal, bracketed literal, non-HTTPS scheme and ASCII control/space each produce their exact reason and no Source. TYP-DATA: host normalization - uppercase lowercased; one terminal dot removed; 253/254-byte boundary; single-label host rejected; a 63/64-character label boundary; leading and trailing hyphen rejected. TYP-DATA: a pre-encoded `xn--` label is accepted; raw Unicode is `source_host_non_ascii` and is never normalized to punycode by the implementation. TYP-DATA: canonical root URI is `https://<lowercase_host>/` for every accepted input. TYP-SEC: `organization_inactive`, `project_not_registerable`, `tenant_mismatch` and `source_register_unauthorized` each create no Source. TYP-INT: a Project in `draft`, `active` and `paused` each permit registration - the paused fixture asserts that reading the paused state as a guard is permitted while no path effects the transition. TYP-DATA: uniqueness on `(project_id, canonical_host)` over non-removed Sources; the same host in a different Project succeeds. TYP-DATA: concurrent same-key registration creates exactly one Source and the loser receives `F1-DOMAIN-409 / source_host_already_registered`. TYP-DATA: post-removal re-registration creates a new Source with a new ID and new provenance and never reopens the removed record. TYP-DATA: exact replay returns the same Source and emits one event only; changed content under the same key is `idempotency_conflict`. TYP-OBS: the 10-second timeout and the exact 1/5-second retry schedule. TYP-OBS: provenance is exactly the seven named fields and is immutable after commit. TYP-SEC: the `SourceRegistered` payload carries no submitted email, identity subject, credential or raw authorization token. TYP-DATA: registration creates no Onboarding Request and no candidate entity, and never verifies, activates, crawls or creates Evidence.
- rollout: No predecessor behaviour exists. S-04 depends on S-03 for a registerable Project and is consumed by S-05 verification and S-06 scope control.
