- audit record: Every Decision with decision ID, Evidence ID, prior and resulting effective status, reason code, authority, policy version, `decided_at_utc`, state version and correlation ID. Immutable provenance and access history per CAP-013 Outputs. Under OD-030, exactly one immutable `security_audit` deletion audit record proves retention-expiry destruction of an already-`invalid` payload; the LifecycleDeletionJob and its Deletion Evidence are asserted ABSENT from that path and remain the Account-deletion and Organization-closure mechanism only.
- observability: Evidence capture success and citation validity rates, per CAP-013 Observability Requirements. Every Decision emits `EvidenceValidationChanged`; every 30-day payload boundary emits exactly one `EvidenceRetentionExpiring`; every retention-expiry destruction writes exactly one `security_audit` record. A destruction with no audit record and a boundary with no event are both detectable.
- retention: `retention-interim-v1` under the resolved OD-011. For every producer-enabled Evidence type in Volume I, `payload_retention_class` is exactly `product_evidence_payload` and NO caller may choose another value; an unknown class rejects Evidence creation. That field classifies ONLY the referenced Evidence Payload. The Evidence envelope, digest, provenance, Validation Decisions and downstream lineage are always `product_history`, INCLUDING AFTER PAYLOAD DESTRUCTION. Staging bytes before Evidence creation use `temporary_processing`; Verification challenge plaintext remains `ephemeral_secret` and is never part of Evidence. Payload expiry is exactly 24 months on the capture cursor; the 30-day warning precedes it. A legal hold suspends destruction and the payload is destroyed at the first eligible instant after release. The window values are owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row restates only what CAP-013 and its resolved decisions make behavioural.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: each of the five producer-enabled `evidence_type` literals -- `source_document`, `crawl_observation`, `parsed_content`, `external_measurement`, `verification_observation` -- succeeds under its exact valid producer, payload and provenance contract. TYP-DATA: the six recognized literals are exhaustive and CASE-SENSITIVE; `Source_Document` is rejected. TYP-DATA: reserved `operator_attestation` returns `F1-DOMAIN-409 / evidence_type_unavailable` on every attempted creation and creates no Evidence -- Volume I defines no creation command, actor authority, applicability, payload schema, provenance profile, validation rule or downstream eligibility for it, and none is invented. TYP-DATA: an unknown or legacy alias, including `operator_submission`, returns `F1-VALIDATION-400 / evidence_type_invalid`; no implementation normalizes an alias into a recognized type -- asserted as a distinct fixture from the reserved-type case, because the two produce different error classes. TYP-DATA: every required Evidence attribute is present and exact; `source_id` is nullable only for project-level external measurements and `evaluation_id` only for Verification Evidence captured before an Evaluation exists. TYP-DATA: `content_sha256` is a lowercase 64-character hex digest and MUST be recomputed and matched before Evidence becomes `valid`. TYP-DATA: the retained bytes, content digest, tenant references, provenance fields and classification are IMMUTABLE after creation; a corrected observation creates a NEW Evidence record -- an update attempt is asserted to have no effect. TYP-DATA: Evidence Source is a conceptual origin view over `source_system` and applicable `source_id` and is asserted NEVER separately persisted as a field, entity or type literal. TYP-SEC: Validation Rule 1 -- referenced Organization, Project, Source and Evaluation records belong to the same Organization; a cross-Organization reference rejects the downstream write with a data-integrity error AND an audit event, with the audit event asserted present. TYP-SEC: missing referenced bytes, digest mismatch and unknown schema version each reject with a data-integrity error and an audit event -- three further fixtures. TYP-DATA: `validation_status` is the IMMUTABLE creation-time decision made by the tenant-scoped integrity-validation service; effective status derives from the append-only Decision history. TYP-SEC: the transition and authority table is exhaustive -- `valid -> quarantined` for `security_restriction`, `consent_review`, `retention_review`, `digest_recheck_required` or `schema_recheck_required`, with the integrity-validation service permitted ANY listed reason and a SecurityOperator in authorized scope permitted only the FIRST THREE; `quarantined -> valid` only by the integrity-validation service with reason `revalidation_passed` and only after retained bytes, digest, schema, tenant references, consent and retention state, and authority all pass again; `valid -> invalid` and `quarantined -> invalid` only by the integrity-validation service for `immutable_bytes_missing`, `digest_mismatch`, `schema_unsupported` or `legal_deletion_completed` after the condition is conclusive. A SecurityOperator attempting `digest_recheck_required` is denied -- asserted separately, because it is the boundary a permissive implementation crosses. TYP-SEC: neither actor may mutate Evidence bytes or an earlier Decision; no other actor may change Evidence validation state at all. TYP-DATA: no same-status Decision is created; `invalid` is terminal and corrected content is new Evidence. TYP-DATA: a stale expected validation-state version, a same-status request and an unlisted transition or reason are each rejected WITHOUT a Decision -- three fixtures. TYP-DATA: every accepted transition emits `EvidenceValidationChanged` IN THE SAME TRANSACTION. TYP-DATA: a transition away from `valid` atomically performs EVERY applicable current effect BEFORE the Decision commits -- mark the Current Score Projection unavailable, add `invalid_evidence` to its de-duplicated fixed-precedence reason set, suppress every published Recommendation Artifact whose origin Issue is affected, and INDEPENDENTLY suppress every published Artifact whose rationale or Citation references that Evidence. The two suppression limbs are asserted with a fixture where only one applies, proving neither subsumes the other. TYP-DATA: no current read returns the stale pre-transition numeric score between the transition and recalculation. TYP-DATA: the event triggers IDEMPOTENT score and Recommendation recalculation, which replaces the COMPLETE reason set with all and only reasons applicable to the recalculated inputs. TYP-DATA: a later `quarantined -> valid` triggers recalculation, removes `invalid_evidence` ONLY when no selected score input has invalid Evidence, and may restore a promoted snapshot and eligible Artifacts; no automatic republication occurs until every publication predicate passes again. TYP-DATA: Check Results and historical snapshots retain the Validation Decision IDs and statuses frozen when they were created and NEVER mutate. TYP-DATA: exact replay of a Decision command returns the Decision without repeating suppression, the event or recalculation. TYP-SEC: classification order is `public < internal < confidential < restricted`; a derived field inherits the STRONGEST classification of any source value or Evidence used to derive it; unknown classification is treated as `restricted`. TYP-SEC: declassification is PROHIBITED -- Volume I defines no declassification permission or workflow, so no actor or service may lower a persisted or derived classification; an attempt has no effect and is asserted for each of the three downward steps. TYP-DATA: `payload_retention_class` is exactly `product_evidence_payload` for every producer-enabled type and no caller may choose another value; an unknown class rejects Evidence creation. TYP-DATA: the retention SPLIT -- the envelope, digest, provenance, Validation Decisions and downstream lineage are `product_history` and REMAIN so after payload destruction, while only the referenced Payload uses `product_evidence_payload`; staging bytes before Evidence creation use `temporary_processing`; Verification challenge plaintext remains `ephemeral_secret` and is asserted never part of Evidence. TYP-OBS (OD-029 Option 2): exactly one `EvidenceRetentionExpiring` per Evidence Payload, emitted by WF-007 under the integrity-validation service authority at 30 days before its current `product_evidence_payload` retention maximum, carrying the Evidence identity, its Organization, the capture cursor, the maximum instant and the correlation ID -- with a boundary fixture immediately before, exactly at, and immediately after the 30-day instant. It requests reassessment when the Project remains active, and creates no new workflow, capability, decision record, Payload state or notification route variant. TYP-DATA (OD-030 Option 3): destruction of an already-`invalid` payload occurs at the 24-month ELAPSED maximum on the capture cursor, is NOT accelerated to the accrued 30-day minimum, appends NO Evidence Validation Decision, and is proved by exactly one immutable `security_audit` deletion audit record; the LifecycleDeletionJob and its Deletion Evidence are asserted ABSENT from that path. TYP-DATA: a legal hold suspends destruction and the payload is destroyed at the FIRST ELIGIBLE INSTANT after release. TYP-SEC: `evidence.validation.manage` is a protected grant requiring approval within 24 hours by a SecurityOperator other than the requester and an active expiry; `evidence.restricted.read` raises only the granted OrganizationAdmin's payload access to `restricted` and broadens no resource scope or other field permission, and authorizes no validation transition. WITHHELD under OD-032 and asserted ABSENT: any test, schema object, log label or telemetry label asserting a canonical identifier namespace or lifecycle owner for LegalHold or Check Result, or a bounded-context assignment for LegalHold. Evidence's own namespace `evd_id` and owner Evidence Context are NOT withheld: 011 DOMAIN_MODEL.md already admits them and OD-032 cites that admission as its precedent.
- rollout: S-11 depends on S-08 for the producer contracts and on S-09 for the Check Results whose frozen Decision tuples it must never rewrite; it gates S-12 (Issue Evidence integrity), S-13 (score availability) and S-14 (Artifact suppression and republication). The OD-032 limb stays withheld: behaviour is permitted under the interim, so the slice reaches its outcome, and completing the limb requires ratification plus a PM-REQ-009 controlled change to 011 DOMAIN_MODEL.md rather than an implementation choice.

### MTX-021 - AC-CAP-021 (CAP: CAP-021)

- Slice: S-19
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: UPSTREAM-V1-CREDENTIAL-ROTATION-TOKEN-009 (OD-023); OD-032 -- OD-023: credential rotation begin/complete withheld; other Credential lifecycle permitted; OD-032: canonical namespace for an unassigned record withheld; behaviour permitted under the interim
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-014-deliver-notifications
- interface type: Capability-level obligation discharged by the WF-014 contract; CAP-021 defines no interface of its own. Its Purpose is to inform users about significant lifecycle outcomes and required actions.
- route: Not applicable - CAP-021 defines no interface independent of WF-014; see MTX-039.
- request schema: Per CAP-021 Inputs: a versioned logical event, the active route and template policy, the exact Account selector recipients or the direct Invitation target binding, redacted payload fields, and `integration-interim-v1` Mailgun Integration and Credential context.
- response schema: Per CAP-021 Outputs: immutable per-channel attempts, dispatch checkpoints, local deduplication and correlation evidence, provider and reconciliation results, known or uncertain aggregate status, user-visible may-still-arrive status, and exactly one escalation for each uncertain or terminally failed required Delivery and each mandatory empty selector union.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: As MTX-039 - `ConsumeNotificationEvent`, `ExecuteDeliveryAttempt`, `ReconcileDelivery`, `ConsumeProviderEvent`, `ActivateNotificationPolicy`, `RequestNotificationReplay`, `ReadOwnInbox`. CAP-021 adds no command.
- command input: As MTX-039.
- command output: As MTX-039.
- actor: Notification service identity; Organization Administrator or SecurityOperator for allowed policy changes; approved SecurityOperator support session for terminal recovery. Per CAPABILITY_MODEL.md the Actor line never grants authority; the Permission Baseline does.
- organization scope: The event Organization. CAP-021 Preconditions require only that event-generating workflows execute, so scope is inherited from the routed event rather than from a caller.
- aggregate: As MTX-039 - Notification, Delivery, DeliveryAttempt, DeliveryReconciliation, NotificationPolicy, escalation and replay acknowledgement.
- aggregate boundary: As MTX-039.
- value objects: As MTX-039. WITHHELD under OD-032: no canonical identifier namespace or lifecycle owner is asserted for Notification or Delivery, both of which DM-REQ-001's core-entity catalogue omits.
- domain service: Not applicable - CAP-021 names no domain service.
- repository: As MTX-039.
- persistence model: Delivery status and notification history are retained per CAP-021 Data Implications, on the canonical tables named in MTX-039. WITHHELD under OD-032: no namespace or lifecycle owner is inferred for Notification or Delivery, and no implementation may invent either; Volume I's existing logical field names and behavioural contracts for both records are unchanged and remain authoritative for behaviour.
- migration: Not applicable - no product schema object is added at this row; the canonical schema owns the CAP-021 tables. Any artifact requiring a canonical namespace for Notification or Delivery remains WITHHELD under OD-032 rather than choosing one.
- transaction boundary: As MTX-039 - the durable local attempt claim precedes platform-managed `mailgun_email` Integration and Credential validation, per CAP-021 Product Behavior.
- concurrency: As MTX-039 - local replay and concurrency cannot repeat one claimed attempt, per CAP-021 Success Condition.
- idempotency: As MTX-039 - application-level at-least-once attempt processing with an exactly-once first submission per claim, and duplicate-tolerant product-action idempotency preserved across acknowledged replay. No provider exactly-once claim is permitted, which CAP-021 Product Behavior states in those words.
- background job: As MTX-039 - `event_consume`, `delivery_execute`, `delivery_reconcile`, `provider_event_consume`.
- queue: As MTX-039 - the `delivery` queue.
- retry policy: As MTX-039 - only exhausted retryable initialization, proven no-network-submission, or definitive provider nonacceptance may join the bounded Delivery retry schedule, per CAP-021 Product Behavior; implicit provider-client and transport retry is disabled.
- terminal failure: Per CAP-021 Failure Condition, each of these is a failure of the capability: unauthorized send; secret or raw Evidence leakage; duplicate application submission for one claimed attempt; automatic resend while acceptance is unknown; a false exactly-once or false terminal-failure claim; retry beyond the definitive-nonacceptance bound; an unclassified state; a duplicate product action from a duplicate email; or a missing audit record or escalation. Provider-visible duplicate delivery after acknowledged replay or provider behavior is tolerated and is expressly not a specification failure.
- reconciliation: As MTX-039 - read-only from exact authenticated provider evidence, and reconciliation never sends. Per CAP-021 Success Condition every failure or uncertainty escalates within 5 minutes and acknowledged replay preserves old history.
- domain events: As MTX-039 - the seventeen WF-014 events plus the applicable reachable `integration-interim-v1` Integration and Credential events. WITHHELD under OD-023: no `CredentialRotationStarted` or `CredentialRotated` emission exists.
- event payload: As MTX-039 - never secret material, per CAP-021 Observability Requirements.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf014`.
- event consumers: The outbox consumer `notification_route_v1`. CAP-021 names no other consumer of its own events.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: As MTX-039 - the command or event-consumption handler before mutation, and again before every attempt. CAP-021 Product Behavior states the obligation directly: reauthorize each attempt.
- permission checks: As MTX-039. CAP-021 Security Implications require recipient and provider-checkpoint authorization to be enforced and prohibit sensitive payload or credential leakage.
- tenant boundary: As MTX-039 - the event Organization, the immutable recipient snapshot, and the template and recipient classification ceiling.
- error contract: As MTX-039. CAP-021 requires that unusable addresses be classified without an attempt or provider call, that every prepared-stage outcome map exactly, that nonretryable Integration and Credential outcomes terminalize, and that unprovable acceptance move to `acceptance_unknown` with automatic resend suppressed.
- audit record: As MTX-039 - per CAP-021 Failure Condition a missing audit record or escalation is itself a capability failure, so the audit write is not incidental to the behaviour but part of it.
- observability: Per CAP-021 Observability Requirements: Integration and Credential versions, local deduplication hits, dispatch checkpoints, definitive retries, acceptance-unknown count and age, reconciliation outcomes, acknowledged replays, and provider and aggregate success, failure and uncertainty telemetry, never secret material.
- retention: Delivery status and notification history retained per CAP-021 Data Implications, in the `product_history` class at 7 years; the window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: the Success Condition is exhaustive - each eligible nonsuppressed Delivery receives its initial durable attempt and then durably succeeds, reaches an authorized governed suppression, reaches a classified terminal failure, or remains explicitly `acceptance_unknown`; no fifth outcome and no unclassified state exists. TYP-INT: local replay and concurrency cannot repeat one claimed attempt. TYP-OBS: every failure and every uncertainty escalates within 5 minutes. TYP-DATA: reconciliation never sends. TYP-DATA: acknowledged replay preserves old history and duplicate-tolerant product-action idempotency. TYP-SEC: each Failure Condition is asserted negative - no unauthorized send; no secret or raw Evidence leakage; no duplicate application submission for one claimed attempt; no automatic resend while acceptance is unknown; no false exactly-once claim; no false terminal-failure claim; no retry beyond the definitive-nonacceptance bound; no unclassified state; no duplicate product action from a duplicate email; no missing audit record or escalation. TYP-DATA: provider-visible duplicate delivery after acknowledged replay or provider behavior is tolerated and the fixture asserts it is NOT reported as a specification failure. TYP-DATA: unusable addresses are classified without an attempt or provider call. TYP-DATA: one durable local email attempt is claimed BEFORE platform-managed `mailgun_email` Integration and Credential validation. TYP-INT: every prepared-stage outcome maps exactly and none is left unmapped. TYP-DATA: only exhausted retryable initialization or proven no-network-submission may join definitive provider nonacceptance on the bounded retry schedule; nothing else may. TYP-INT: implicit provider-client and transport retry is disabled. TYP-DATA: unprovable acceptance moves to `acceptance_unknown` with automatic resend suppressed. TYP-SEC: each attempt is reauthorized. TYP-SEC: acknowledged support replay of an uncertain Delivery targets only that same logical recipient and channel under current policy and authorization. TYP-DATA: Outputs are complete - immutable per-channel attempts, dispatch checkpoints, local deduplication and correlation evidence, provider and reconciliation results, known or uncertain aggregate status, user-visible may-still-arrive status, and exactly one escalation per uncertain or terminally failed required Delivery and per mandatory empty selector union. TYP-DATA: the non-goal holds - no marketing campaign automation surface exists. TYP-OBS: Integration and Credential versions, deduplication hits, checkpoints, definitive retries, acceptance-unknown count and age, reconciliation outcomes and acknowledged replays are all observable and secret material never is. The complete AC-CAP-021 fixture set is asserted in MTX-039. WITHHELD under OD-032 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting a canonical identifier namespace or a bounded-context lifecycle owner for Notification or Delivery, and any artifact that requires one. WITHHELD under OD-023 and asserted ABSENT: the rotation limb enumerated in MTX-039.
- rollout: As MTX-039. CAP-021 is Baseline Core and its Dependencies are WF-006, WF-014 and WF-017.

### MTX-023 - AC-CAP-023 (CAP: CAP-023)

- Slice: S-21
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-018-investigate-and-audit-security-or-compliance-events
- interface type: Capability-level obligation discharged by the WF-018 contract in MTX-043 plus the Emergency Access Grant break-glass commands this row owns. CAP-023 defines no interface of its own; its WF-017 limb - severity, playbook, checkpoint, restoration and the single-use high-risk step-approval contract - is discharged by S-24's MTX-042 and is not redefined here.
- route: Not applicable - Volume I defines the commands, their permissions and their envelopes but no HTTP path, method or transport. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline. For the Grant specifically there is nothing yet to expose or defer: API_CONTRACTS.md carries no emergency-access route at all, and this row mints none. Under OD-020 the Emergency Access Grant is a security object that remains deny-by-default, so no read surface exists either.
- request schema: Grant request: the emergency predicate as a reference to an open severity-critical Incident naming the affected Organization, the recorded reason, the explicit enumerated resource set within exactly one named Organization, the explicit enumerated action set, and the correlation ID. Approval, revocation and request each additionally carry the expected Grant state version. The bounded lifetime is not a request field: it resolves server-side from the active `emergency-access-v1` policy version. Otherwise as CAP-023 Inputs - versioned Incident or Investigation request, exact scope and permissions, required investigation input set, state version, reason and correlation identifiers.
- response schema: The Grant with its immutable predicate, Incident reference, one Organization, enumerated resource scope, enumerated action scope, lifetime, reason, requesting and approving identities and state version. Otherwise as CAP-023 Outputs - the versioned Incident timeline and recovery records from S-24, or the complete, partial or insufficient Investigation report with immutable Investigation Audit Evidence Item custody and gap records from MTX-043.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I; see route.
- command: The Investigation commands as MTX-043. For the Grant, Volume I fixes the actors, permissions, transitions and events but names no command identifiers and no owning workflow, so this row contracts the transitions the contract defines - request, approve, reject, revoke and timed expiry - and does NOT mint a `Workflows::WfNNN::` command name for them. Volume I places the request, approval, rejection and revocation commits with the Security Context and the expiry commit with the emergency-access lifecycle service holding `emergency_access.expire`; the APPLICATION_LAYER.md command registry carries no `Workflows::Wf018` emergency-access entry, and inventing one would assign the Grant to a workflow Volume I does not.
- command input: The Grant transition inside the Logical Command Envelope, carrying the nonnull `organization_id` of the one named Organization, the expected Grant state version, the reason, the idempotency key and the correlation ID. Expiry is not a human command: it is enforced server-side from the persisted lifetime instant under `emergency_access.expire` service authority.
- command output: The committed Grant state and version, or a denial with no side effect. An approved Grant is a capability to proceed with a cross-Organization Support Session within its enumerated scope; it is never itself an access to data.
- actor: Security Operator, and a time-bounded approved SecurityOperator support session, per CAP-023. A SecurityOperator holding `emergency_access.request` requests a Grant; a DISTINCT SecurityOperator holding protected `emergency_access.approve` approves it, mirroring the accepted Support Session and Legal Hold two-person rule; a self-approved request has no effect and commits no transition. A SecurityOperator holding `emergency_access.revoke` may revoke an active Grant before expiry. Only the emergency-access lifecycle service holding `emergency_access.expire` commits expiry - the Permission Baseline denies `emergency_access.expire` to every role including SecurityOperator.
- organization scope: A Grant names exactly ONE Organization and no Grant spans Organizations; every Grant event and its Audit Evidence carry that `organization_id` in the canonical event envelope. The contract states expressly that this creates no dependency on the OD-013 event tenant identity and MUST NOT be used to infer one - a caution written while OD-013 was open and now moot, since OD-013 is resolved to Option 1 and agrees: canonical ownership is always singular and always an Organization. For Investigation scope see MTX-043; CAP-023's Preconditions require an Incident, failure or customer support trigger to exist.
- aggregate: Emergency Access Grant is a root of its own, deliberately held OUTSIDE the Incident: OD-012 Option 3 authorizes emergency access by a dedicated break-glass artifact rather than by a field on the Incident. Investigation is the other root, owned by MTX-043; Incident is S-24's.
- aggregate boundary: The Grant references the Incident; the Incident carries NO reference to the Grant and no break-glass semantics. That direction is the whole architecture: it is why the Incident field list stays exhaustive and unchanged, why no `emergency_customer_access` field exists, and why no actor or service may set, default, infer or synthesize one. The Grant confers no authority beyond its enumerated resource and action scope and never exceeds the acting identity's existing classification ceilings; Evidence classification limits and field-level redaction are unchanged by it. It substitutes for exactly one thing - the affected tenant's OrganizationAdmin `support.session.customer_approve` - and for nothing else: the mandatory distinct SecurityOperator Support Session approval remains required alongside it.
- value objects: EmergencyAccessGrantId, GrantState covering the requested, approved, rejected, revoked and expired transitions the contract enumerates, EmergencyPredicate as the open severity-critical Incident reference plus recorded reason, ResourceScope as an explicit enumerated set within one Organization, ActionScope as an explicit enumerated set, GrantLifetime resolved from the active `emergency-access-v1` policy version, EmergencyAccessPolicyVersion, ExpectedStateVersion, CorrelationId. Investigation value objects as MTX-043.
- domain service: The emergency-access lifecycle service is named by Volume I and is a real collaborator with its own authority: it holds `emergency_access.expire`, no role holds it, and it alone commits `EmergencyAccessExpired` from the persisted lifetime instant. That is the only domain service either capability limb names; the predicate, scope and least-privilege checks are pure functions over the Grant and the acting identity's ceilings.
- repository: `EmergencyAccessGrantRepository`, and `IncidentRepository` read-only for the severity-critical predicate. Investigation repositories as MTX-043.
- persistence model: The Grant's fields, states, immutability and audit obligations are fixed by Volume I, but schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md carries NO `emergency_access_grants` table: the canonical schema predates the OD-012 ratification and was not extended by it. This row contracts the ratified logical shape - one Organization, immutable predicate, Incident reference, enumerated resource and action scope, lifetime, reason, requesting and approving identities, state and state version, retained as `security_audit` - and records the missing table as a gap for the schema's own controlled change. It does not invent a table name, column set or state CHECK to close it, and the Grant is not implementable until that controlled change lands. Investigation persistence as MTX-043.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - adding the Emergency Access Grant table and its constraints is a controlled change to schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md consequent on the OD-012 ratification, not a product migration this row may issue. Adding `emergency_access_grant` to the Volume II `EventEntityType` enum and the five `EmergencyAccess*` names to the `EventType` enum are likewise API_CONTRACTS.md's controlled changes.
- transaction boundary: Audit Evidence is written in the SAME transaction as the transition it records - the contract says so for every request, approval, denial, exercise, revocation and expiry, which is why a Grant transition can never exist without its audit trail. Request, approval, rejection and revocation each commit in one transaction under their expected state version. Expiry commits in the lifecycle service's own transaction from the persisted lifetime instant. A denied exercise commits its denial audit event and nothing else: no Support Session, no provider call, no other side effect.
- concurrency: Concurrent approvals of the same Grant commit exactly ONE transition; the loser observes the terminal or active state and returns the stored result. A revocation racing expiry commits exactly one terminal transition and the Grant is terminal either way - which is the point: both paths end the authority, so the race cannot leave it live. Request, approval and revocation each carry the expected Grant state version, so a stale transition commits nothing. Investigation concurrency as MTX-043.
- idempotency: Exact replay of a request, approval or revocation returns the stored result and commits NO second transition, keyed by the expected Grant state version inside the Logical Command Envelope's idempotency contract. An approved Grant's predicate, Incident reference, resource scope, action scope, lifetime and reason are immutable; any change requires a NEW Grant under a new approval, so there is no in-place amendment for a replay to race. Investigation idempotency as MTX-043.
- background job: The emergency-access lifecycle service enforces expiry server-side at the bound resolved from the active `emergency-access-v1` policy version. Volume I names the service and its `emergency_access.expire` authority but no job identifier, cadence or queue, and BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md registers no emergency-access action; this row names none rather than mint one. `investigation_collect` as MTX-043.
- queue: Not applicable for the Grant - Volume I registers no background action for it and this row invents no queue placement; see background_job. Investigation collection uses the `control` queue class as MTX-043.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the Grant defines no retry. A denied, rejected, revoked or expired Grant is terminal for that authority and is never auto-retried; a further attempt requires a NEW Grant under a new distinct approval. Investigation retry as MTX-043.
- terminal failure: Per CAP-023's Failure Condition, unauthorized or unnamed remediation, synthetic reconstruction, missing custody, unapproved closure or export, or a hidden evidence gap constitutes failure. For the Grant: a request asserting emergency access with NO active approved Grant, a Grant outside its resource or action scope, and an expired or revoked Grant each return `F1-AUTH-403`, create no Support Session, perform no provider call or other side effect, and emit the standard denial audit event. No new error code is minted. A self-approved request has no effect and commits no transition.
- reconciliation: Every Grant reaches exactly one terminal state - rejected, revoked or expired - and each terminal transition persists its immutable Audit Evidence in the same transaction, so the complete break-glass history reconciles from `security_audit` alone. Because the Grant is immutable once approved and every amendment is a new Grant under a new approval, the audit record is a true sequence of distinct authorities rather than a mutated one. Investigation reconciliation as MTX-043.
- domain events: Exactly five, all named by the Emergency Cross-Organization Access Contract: `EmergencyAccessRequested` on the persisted request, `EmergencyAccessApproved` on the distinct approver's approval, `EmergencyAccessRejected` on denial, `EmergencyAccessRevoked` on an authorized revocation, and `EmergencyAccessExpired` on timed expiry. Each carries affected entity `emergency_access_grant` under the profile matching its transition. The Volume II `EventType` enum carries none of the five and the `EventEntityType` enum carries no `emergency_access_grant`; that gap is recorded, not closed here. Volume I names no `workflow_id` for these five events while the Logical Event Envelope requires one, so this row states no workflow ID rather than assign the Grant to a workflow Volume I does not name. The Investigation events are the MTX-043 set; the Incident events are S-24's.
- event payload: The canonical Logical Event Envelope with the nonnull `organization_id` of the Grant's one named Organization, affected entity `emergency_access_grant`, plus the correlation ID, the requesting and approving identities, the Incident reference, and the exact resource and action scope - the same fields the Audit Evidence carries, because the audit record and the event describe one transition. The referenced Incident's own scope does not travel: the Grant and its events are always Organization-scoped. Under ratified OD-013 Option 1 there is no platform-scoped Incident record for the predicate to reference in any case, which makes the contract's caution that 'the referenced Incident may be platform-scope' pre-ratification prose; the predicate resolves against the Organization-scoped Incident naming that Organization. Investigation payloads as MTX-043.
- event producer: Each of the five has EXACTLY ONE producer and no other actor or service emits any of them: the Security Context commits the request, approval, rejection and revocation transitions, and the emergency-access lifecycle service holding `emergency_access.expire` commits expiry. S-24 emits none of them and the Incident carries no reference to a Grant. `Workflows::Wf018` produces the five Investigation events as MTX-043.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - the Emergency Cross-Organization Access Contract names no consumer for its five events, and the `notification-policy-v1` baseline route table lists none of them. A consumer that notified the customer would pre-empt the withheld OD-012 notification limb.
- serializer: Not applicable - Volume I defines no transport exposure for the Grant or the Investigation, and under OD-020 both remain deny-by-default security objects with no authorized read projection to serialize.
- authorization entry point: Each command, before mutation. For the Grant, authority is checked at three distinct points and they are not interchangeable: at request against `emergency_access.request` and the emergency predicate; at approval against protected `emergency_access.approve` held by a DIFFERENT active SecurityOperator; and at every exercise against the Grant's current active state and its enumerated resource and action scope. An approved Grant is not a standing authority - each exercise revalidates state and scope, which is why an expired or revoked Grant fails at exercise even though it was validly approved. Investigation entry points as MTX-043.
- permission checks: `emergency_access.request` and `emergency_access.revoke`: SecurityOperator allow, every other role and the Service Identity deny. `emergency_access.approve`: protected allow with the approver differing from the requester; every other role deny. `emergency_access.expire`: denied to EVERY role including SecurityOperator, and permitted only to the emergency-access lifecycle service - a human cannot expire or extend a Grant. Cross-Organization support access is itself a protected grant. The Grant substitutes only for the affected tenant's `support.session.customer_approve` and never for the mandatory distinct SecurityOperator `support.session.approve`, nor for any target-workflow permission: least privilege confers no authority beyond the enumerated scope and never exceeds the acting identity's existing classification ceilings. Investigation permissions as MTX-043; the WF-017 permission surface is S-24's.
- tenant boundary: There is NO standing cross-tenant support access and normal support operations retain no tenant access - a permanent baseline, not an interim. A Grant names exactly one Organization, so it can never authorize a Support Session in another; any action not enumerated is denied; any resource outside the enumerated set within that one Organization is denied. The only authority that substitutes for the affected tenant's `support.session.customer_approve` is an active approved Grant; every other cross-Organization Support Session continues to require it. Investigation tenant boundary as MTX-043.
- error contract: `F1-AUTH-403` for a request asserting emergency access with no active approved Grant, for a Grant outside its enumerated resource or action scope, and for an expired or revoked Grant - each creating no Support Session, performing no provider call or other side effect, and emitting the standard denial audit event. NO new error code is minted for this path; the contract says so expressly. A self-approved request commits no transition. A stale expected Grant state version is `F1-DOMAIN-409`. Investigation errors as MTX-043.
- audit record: Per CAP-023's Data Implications, investigation artifacts and operator actions are immutable in audit logs. For the Grant, EVERY request, approval, denial, exercise, revocation and expiry persists immutable Audit Evidence retained under `security_audit`, carrying the correlation ID, the requesting and approving identities, the Incident reference, and the exact resource and action scope, written in the same transaction as the transition it records. The denial audit event is mandatory on every fail-closed path, so a refused break-glass attempt is as auditable as a granted one. Investigation audit as MTX-043; the WF-017 audit record is S-24's.
- observability: Incident investigation traceability and gap reports, per CAP-023's Observability Requirements. The five `EmergencyAccess*` events with their correlation ID, identities, Incident reference and exact scope are the break-glass observability signal, and the denial audit event makes a refused emergency attempt observable even though it emits none of the five. Investigation observability as MTX-043.
- retention: Investigation artifacts and operator actions are immutable in audit logs per CAP-023 Data Implications; Grant Audit Evidence and the emergency access trail follow `security_audit`, which OD-012's own Data Implications state expressly. That is 7 years on a cursor of 7 elapsed years after terminal transition with irreversible destruction only under two-person security approval, per 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md and fixed by ratified OD-011. Emergency access reaches tenant Evidence and payload data under the EXISTING classification ceilings, which the Grant never raises. The window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states no new one.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: two-person control - a Grant requires a requesting SecurityOperator holding `emergency_access.request` and a DISTINCT approving SecurityOperator holding protected `emergency_access.approve`; a self-approved request commits NO transition and is asserted to leave no Grant. TYP-SEC: `emergency_access.approve` absent, held by a non-SecurityOperator, or held as an unprotected grant each authorize nothing. TYP-SEC: `emergency_access.expire` is denied to every role including SecurityOperator and permitted only to the emergency-access lifecycle service; a human attempt to expire or extend a Grant performs no side effect. TYP-SEC: scope enforcement - an approved Grant is exercisable ONLY within its enumerated resource and action scope and ONLY for its single named Organization; an action outside the enumerated action set, a resource outside the enumerated resource set, and a target in another Organization are each denied. TYP-SEC: an expired Grant, a revoked Grant, a Grant outside scope, and an ABSENT Grant each return `F1-AUTH-403`, create NO Support Session and perform no side effect, each emitting the standard denial audit event; asserted at the exact expiry instant and one either side. TYP-DATA: no new error code is minted for any emergency-access denial - `F1-AUTH-403` is asserted as the exact code. TYP-DATA: the bounded lifetime resolves from the ACTIVE `emergency-access-v1` policy version and is enforced server-side; the fixture drives the policy version rather than a hard-coded constant, and a lifetime change is asserted to be a policy-version change requiring no Volume I revision. TYP-SEC: the emergency predicate is mandatory - a Grant without an open severity-critical Incident naming the affected Organization, or without the recorded reason, commits nothing. TYP-DATA: the Grant references the Incident and the Incident carries NO reference to the Grant and no break-glass semantics. TYP-SEC: the Incident carries no `emergency_customer_access` field and no path sets, defaults, infers or synthesizes one; a command, payload or service attempting it is refused and commits nothing. TYP-SEC: substitution is exactly one thing - an active approved Grant substitutes for the affected tenant's OrganizationAdmin `support.session.customer_approve` and for nothing else; the mandatory distinct SecurityOperator Support Session approval is asserted STILL required alongside it, and a cross-Organization Support Session without an active approved Grant still requires `support.session.customer_approve`. TYP-SEC: least privilege - the Grant confers no authority beyond its enumerated scope and never exceeds the acting identity's existing classification ceilings; Evidence classification limits and field-level redaction are asserted unchanged under an active Grant. TYP-DATA: immutability - an approved Grant's predicate, Incident reference, resource scope, action scope, lifetime and reason cannot be changed; any change requires a NEW Grant under a new approval. TYP-DATA: idempotency and concurrency - exact replay of a request, approval or revocation returns the stored result and commits no second transition; concurrent approvals of one Grant commit EXACTLY ONE transition and the loser returns the stored result; a revocation racing expiry commits exactly one terminal transition and the Grant is terminal either way. TYP-OBS: every Grant transition emits EXACTLY ONE of `EmergencyAccessRequested`, `EmergencyAccessApproved`, `EmergencyAccessRejected`, `EmergencyAccessRevoked` or `EmergencyAccessExpired` from its SINGLE defined producer - the Security Context for request, approval, rejection and revocation, the emergency-access lifecycle service for expiry - each carrying the Grant's one `organization_id` and affected entity `emergency_access_grant`; no other actor or service emits any of them. TYP-SEC: every request, approval, denial, exercise, revocation and expiry persists immutable Audit Evidence under `security_audit` in the SAME transaction as its transition, carrying correlation ID, requesting and approving identities, Incident reference and exact resource and action scope; a transition without its audit record is asserted impossible. TYP-SEC: NO customer-notification behaviour on any emergency-access path is asserted or implemented, because that limb of OD-012 remains outstanding pending qualified legal review; the fixture asserts no Notification, no route and no consumer exists for the five `EmergencyAccess*` events. TYP-DATA: there is no standing cross-tenant access and normal support operations retain no tenant access, asserted with no Grant present. TYP-E2E: reconstruction - given retained correlated records, an authorized investigation reconstructs the ordered events and the access chain, asserted end to end against the AC-CAP-023 fixture. TYP-DATA: each missing, expired, denied or integrity-invalid required investigation input produces ONE explicit gap and the exact `partial` or `insufficient` completeness; see MTX-043. TYP-SEC: the one- and cross-Organization complete, missing, expired and mismatched Support Session sets produce their exact side effect or denial; see MTX-043. TYP-SEC: self-approved closure and unscoped export are denied AND audited; see MTX-043. TYP-SEC: every high-risk step approval hash, actor, one-hour, expiry, single-use and retry fixture holds; the WF-017 step-approval limb is exercised by S-24's MTX-042 and the WF-018 export-approval limb by MTX-089. TYP-E2E: break-glass end to end - a severity-critical Incident naming Organization B, a Grant requested and distinctly approved, a cross-Organization Support Session proceeding without B's customer approval within the enumerated scope only, an investigation query under that session, then revocation, asserting the exact Grant states, denials, audit records and events at each commit and asserting no customer notification at any step.
- rollout: As MTX-043. This row additionally carries the entire Emergency Access Grant contract and is the reason S-24 emits no `EmergencyAccess*` event and references the Grant only as the authority it never confers. The Grant is not implementable until the recorded schema and event-enum gaps are closed by their owning documents' controlled changes; that is a Volume II completeness gap, not a withheld limb, because OD-012's architecture is ratified and nothing about it awaits an owner. The customer-notification limb IS withheld and stays withheld until qualified legal review completes.

### MTX-024 - AC-CAP-024 (CAP: CAP-024)

- Slice: S-22
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-015-enforce-entitlements
- interface type: Capability-level obligation discharged by the WF-015 contract at MTX-040; CAP-024 defines no interface of its own. CAP-024's Dependencies are WF-015 and WF-013, so the Organization and Account lifecycle states it reads as Decision predicates are owned by WF-013 and consumed here.
- route: Not applicable - CAP-024 defines no interface independent of WF-015; see MTX-040, where the policy limb carries the registered `POST /api/v1/organizations/:organization_id/entitlement-policies` route and the enforcement limb has none.
- request schema: Versioned Entitlement and plan policy, operation class and units, UTC counter window, atomic counter snapshot, idempotency key, and actor/resource scope, per CAP-024 Inputs.
- response schema: Immutable Allow, AllowWithWarning or Block Decision; the reservation lifecycle; the exact low-cost usage record; the exact actor, unit, window, counters, cache, retry, reason and recovery fields; and the usage and policy snapshot, per CAP-024 Outputs.
- controller: As MTX-040 - a controller exists only for policy activation and MUST NOT call an entitlement checkpoint directly.
- command: As MTX-040 - the seven registered `Workflows::Wf015` operations plus the `LowCostRead` coordinator. CAP-024 adds no command.
- command input: As MTX-040. CAP-024 Inputs name the atomic counter snapshot explicitly: the snapshot is taken at the checkpoint under the counter-window lock, which is what makes it atomic rather than merely recent.
- command output: As MTX-040.
- actor: System automation - specifically the tenant-scoped entitlement service identity - and the Billing Operator for policy activation, per CAP-024 Actor.
- organization scope: The Organization and its WF-001-created active baseline BillingEntity linked to the current Plan Assignment MUST exist, per CAP-024 Preconditions. That linkage is MTX-053's invariant, owned by S-02.
- aggregate: BillingEntity as the canonical write entry; EntitlementPolicy as an immutable versioned Policy Artifact; Decision, usage record, fallback snapshot and heartbeat as immutable appends; counter window, reservation and commit intent as mutable children.
- aggregate boundary: As MTX-040 - the counter window row is the serialization guard and the gated operation's aggregate is never joined.
- value objects: As MTX-040.
- domain service: Not applicable - CAP-024 names no domain service; the decision functions are pure and applied under the counter-window lock.
- repository: As MTX-040.
- persistence model: Usage counters and entitlement states are retained per CAP-024 Data Implications, in the canonical tables listed at MTX-040. CAP-024's Failure Condition forbids a stale or missing high-cost policy or counter from failing open, so the counter row is authoritative and its absence is a Block rather than a default.
- migration: Not applicable - no product schema object is added; see MTX-040.
- transaction boundary: As MTX-040 - reserve, commit and release are atomic at execution, and the low-cost record commits at the durable response checkpoint. CAP-024's Product Behavior requires no queue-time Decision or reservation, so no transaction opens at enqueue.
- concurrency: As MTX-040 - CAP-024's Success Condition is the assertion under test: concurrent AND replayed high-cost requests never reserve or commit above hard limits, and warning-only low-cost records may pass and increment beyond their hard threshold under the ratified policy but never double-count.
- idempotency: As MTX-040 - CAP-024 states the two rules this row inherits: never reopen a terminal replay, and require a linked NEW identity for retry. A low-cost record is deduplicated by its Decision ID under OD-019's server-minted identity.
- background job: As MTX-040 - `entitlement_reconcile` on `control`, plus the 15-second `entitlement_invariant_sweep_due` on `maintenance`.
- queue: As MTX-040.
- retry policy: Not applicable as a workflow retry - CAP-024 defines none; a new attempt is a new linked identity, not a retry. See MTX-040.
- terminal failure: CAP-024 Failure Condition, asserted rather than described: a client-side decision, a stale or missing high-cost policy or counter failing open, a high-cost side effect above the hard limit, duplicate high- or low-cost consumption, or an unexplained legitimate block each constitute failure of this capability.
- reconciliation: As MTX-040 - the lease handler's strictly-before test, `entitlement_commit_intents` as the persisted output evidence, and forward-only low-cost counter resolution that includes each record exactly once even while authoritative reconciliation is delayed.
- domain events: The ten WF-015 events; CAP-024 adds none. Every denial or warning is auditable, per CAP-024 Success Condition.
- event payload: As MTX-040.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf015`.
- event consumers: As MTX-040 - `notification_route_v1` for the two notice events; no gated workflow consumes an event.
- serializer: As MTX-040 - only the policy limb serializes.
- authorization entry point: As MTX-040. CAP-024 Security Implications: entitlement checks are server-side and auditable, and a client-side decision is an enumerated Failure Condition rather than a quality concern.
- permission checks: As MTX-040 - `policy.entitlement.manage` BillingOperator-only for activation, `entitlement.notice.read` for notices, and no tenant-actor permission on the enforcement checkpoints.
- tenant boundary: As MTX-040.
- error contract: As MTX-040 - the rejection-versus-Decision split, the thirteen reason codes, the nine recovery actions, `F1-CAPACITY-429` for the bound, and `F1-DOMAIN-409 / reservation_expired`.
- audit record: As MTX-040 - every Decision field, counter before and after, recovery action, cached-snapshot ID and age, each usage record and reconciliation state, idempotency and retry lineage, every heartbeat and reservation state, commit or release, actor and correlation ID.
- observability: Entitlement enforcement metrics and failure telemetry, per CAP-024 Observability Requirements. AI Implications: none directly - `ai.generate` is metered as an ordinary high-cost operation and no model call participates in a Decision.
- retention: Usage counters and entitlement states retained per CAP-024 Data Implications; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-INT: the full AC-CAP-024 fixture set - byte-exact bootstrap `interim-baseline-plan-v1`, approval and policy materialization against the WF-001-created same-Organization active BillingEntity. TYP-SEC: a missing, cross-Organization, closed or lazily created BillingEntity each fails the bootstrap materialization, proving the entitlement subject cannot be conjured later. TYP-DATA: each UTC-day soft and hard value and the shared-read counter, asserted at the boundary. TYP-DATA: immediate policy activation with no client-supplied time. TYP-DATA: queue-with-no-Decision, then pre-execution reserve. TYP-DATA: every negative reason, every null-field rule and every cached-fallback reason and age. TYP-DATA: low-cost commit and replay. TYP-SEC: human and service actor forms. TYP-DATA: concurrent, lease and maximum-execution, nested, terminal replay and conflict, unapproved upgrade, and new-key retry fixtures each yield exact Decisions, reservations, counters, records and events. TYP-DATA: no provider-dependent bootstrap - the whole set passes with no billing provider reachable. TYP-DATA: no reopen of a terminal reservation. TYP-DATA: no double count on any path. TYP-SEC: the four Failure Conditions asserted as absent - no client-side decision is honoured; a stale or missing high-cost policy or counter fails CLOSED and never open; no high-cost side effect occurs above the hard limit; no duplicate high- or low-cost consumption exists. TYP-OBS: no unexplained legitimate block - every Block is reconstructible from its own persisted Decision fields without recomputation. TYP-DATA: CAP-024 Non-goal asserted absent - no dynamic commercial experimentation path exists outside an approved pricing model; the only way to change a limit is an approved Plan Approval artifact plus a `policy.entitlement.manage` activation.
- rollout: As MTX-040.

### MTX-025 - AC-CAP-025 (CAP: CAP-025)

- Slice: S-23
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: OD-031; OD-032 -- OD-031: routine retention-expiry destruction withheld under retention-destruction-trigger-interim-v1; OD-032: canonical namespace for an unassigned record withheld; behaviour permitted under the interim
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-013-manage-tenant-lifecycle
- interface type: Capability-level obligation discharged by the WF-013 contract in MTX-038, plus the one behaviour AC-CAP-025 attaches to CAP-025 alone: the SecurityOperator single-Session revocation under OD-016. CAP-025 defines no interface of its own.
- route: Not applicable - CAP-025 defines no interface independent of WF-013; see MTX-038 and `routes_position`.
- request schema: Per CAP-025 Inputs: an Invitation create, approve, respond, revoke or reissue action; an Account or Organization lifecycle action; a Closure Request; a Legal Hold request or decision; a LifecycleDeletionJob replay; and the retention and hold snapshots plus the resource and class manifest. `RevokeSession` additionally takes exactly one Session identifier supplied by the command.
- response schema: Per CAP-025 Outputs: the exact Account or Organization state, the Closure Request, the Legal Hold, the LifecycleDeletionJob, the Deletion Evidence, the retained-grant result, and the audited deadlines and events. `RevokeSession` returns the revoked Session with revoke reason `security_revocation`.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I for CAP-025; see `routes_position`.
- command: As MTX-038 for every lifecycle limb. This row additionally owns `RevokeSession`, named verbatim by Volume I's `session-termination-v1` and assigned to CAP-025 by AC-CAP-025. Its Volume II namespace is NOT invented here; see `session_command_namespace_gap`. `TerminateCurrentSession` is WF-001 work owned by S-01 and is referenced, not contracted.
- command input: As MTX-038. `RevokeSession`: exactly one Session identifier, from a SecurityOperator holding `session.revoke`.
- command output: As MTX-038. `RevokeSession`: the `active -> revoked` transition with revoke reason `security_revocation`, or an indistinguishable denial.
- actor: Organization Administrator or Security Operator, per CAP-025's Actor clause, plus the named lifecycle services in MTX-038. `RevokeSession` is a SecurityOperator action only.
- organization scope: The target record's Organization, with the WF-013 suspended and closed admissions in MTX-038.
- aggregate: As MTX-038, plus Session as the `RevokeSession` root.
- aggregate boundary: As MTX-038. `RevokeSession` never widens beyond the single Session it names: revoking every Session of an Account remains reachable only through the existing `account.suspend` and `account.revoke` paths, whose behaviour is unchanged.
- value objects: As MTX-038, plus SessionState (`active`, `revoked`, `expired`) and RevokeReason `security_revocation`.
- domain service: Not applicable - CAP-025 names no domain service; see MTX-038.
- repository: As MTX-038, plus `SessionRepository` for the single-Session revocation.
- persistence model: As MTX-038. WITHHELD under OD-032: no canonical DM-REQ-001 identifier namespace and no DM-REQ-001 lifecycle owner is asserted for Session, LegalHold or LifecycleDeletionJob, which DM-REQ-001's core-entity catalogue omits. This row uses Volume I's logical field names and the canonical physical table names and asserts no `ses_`-style, `lh_`-style or `ldj_`-style prefix. The lifecycle owners 016 STATE_MODEL.md already names -- Data Lifecycle Context for LifecycleDeletionJob, Security Context for LegalHold -- stand and are not reopened, exactly as OD-032's interim directs.
- migration: Not applicable - no product schema object is added by this row. The OD-033 and OD-031 DDL prohibitions in MTX-038 apply unchanged.
- transaction boundary: As MTX-038. `RevokeSession` commits the existing `active -> revoked` edge in one transaction and cascades to nothing.
- concurrency: As MTX-038 - the Organization authorization epoch serializes every effective-access mutation. `RevokeSession`: concurrent revocations of the same Session commit exactly one transition and the loser observes the terminal state and returns the same success; concurrent Sessions of the same Account are unaffected.
- idempotency: As MTX-038 - every command is keyed by its idempotency key and expected state version, and exact replay returns the stored result. `RevokeSession` is idempotent on the target Session identity: replay against an already `revoked` or `expired` Session returns the same success and changes no state.
- background job: As MTX-038. `RevokeSession` is synchronous and has no job. Session expiry is the separate `session_expire` ScheduledAction and is not this command.
- queue: As MTX-038 - `control`, `lifecycle` and `maintenance`, owned by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md.
- retry policy: As MTX-038 - the bounded `retention-interim-v1` deletion schedule and no other. A denied `RevokeSession` is terminal and is never auto-retried.
- terminal failure: CAP-025 Failure Condition, asserted rather than described: access remains active, last-admin or dual-control is bypassed, held bytes are destroyed, deadline failure claims completion, or a lifecycle outcome is missing or unclassified. Each is a test obligation in test_contracts, not a narrative.
- reconciliation: As MTX-038 - blocked deletion resumes on exactly one hold release, failed deletion resumes only through an authorized new replay generation, and the OD-026 guard re-evaluates on each authorization-epoch advance.
- domain events: The WF-013 closed list in MTX-038. This row additionally emits `SessionRevoked` with revoke reason `security_revocation` for `RevokeSession`, which AC-CAP-025 requires expressly. Note the frozen inconsistency this row records rather than resolves: WF-013's Domain Events line does not list `SessionRevoked`, yet its Account and Organization subflows revoke Sessions and `session-termination-v1` states that revocations driven by Account or Organization suspension, revocation or closure retain their existing reasons. `SessionRevoked` is canonical Volume I vocabulary under OD-016 and ADR-019; it is emitted here on the authority of `session-termination-v1` and AC-CAP-025, and no new event, state or edge is minted.
- event payload: As MTX-038. `SessionRevoked` carries its revoke reason, plus the SM-REQ-004 transition audit event with `actor_id`, `entity_id`, `from_state`, `to_state`, `outcome` and `timestamp_utc`.
- event producer: As MTX-038 per limb. `SessionRevoked` for `security_revocation` is produced by the SecurityOperator's command path.
- event consumers: As MTX-038 - S-19's WF-014 routes the security and lifecycle notification classes. CAP-025 names no consumer of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined by Volume I for CAP-025; see `routes_position`.
- authorization entry point: The application command, before mutation, and again at each durable checkpoint of a running privileged operation. `RevokeSession` authorizes before the revocation and discloses nothing on failure.
- permission checks: As MTX-038 for every lifecycle limb. `session.revoke` for one identified Session for security action, held by a SecurityOperator only; the Permission Baseline denies it to every other role including OrganizationAdmin. No `session.*` permission grants any read, and no Session is readable through these commands.
- tenant boundary: As MTX-038 - one Organization per record, forced RLS, Support Session and Emergency Access Grant each naming exactly one Organization. `RevokeSession` is scoped to the actor's authorized scope, and a Session outside it is denied indistinguishably from one that does not exist.
- error contract: As MTX-038. `RevokeSession` adds the deliberate indistinguishability rule and mints no new code: an unauthorized request, a Session outside the actor's authorized scope, and a nonexistent Session identifier ALL return `F1-AUTH-403` with no state change and the standard denial audit event, so Session existence is not disclosed.
- audit record: As MTX-038's enumerated WF-013 audit set. Immediate access revocation and Audit Evidence are mandatory per CAP-025 Security Implications. Every `RevokeSession` success or denial writes its audited outcome; a denial records the reason without disclosing whether the named Session exists.
- observability: Per CAP-025 Observability Requirements: Account, Organization, Closure, Hold and Deletion state, deadline, retry, block, failure, completion-evidence and access-revocation telemetry. The OD-031 over-retention telemetry and single critical compliance escalation in MTX-038 apply here unchanged.
- retention: Data retention and deletion controls follow 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md per CAP-025 Data Implications, under `retention-interim-v1` as the fixed baseline OD-011 approved. Class assignment is as MTX-038. WITHHELD under OD-031: the routine retention-expiry destruction trigger for `product_history`, `identity_commercial` and `security_audit`.
- test contracts: TYP-E2E: CAP-025's Success Condition end to end -- the next protected request is DENIED after suspension or closure, caches converge within 60 seconds, a hold blocks destruction but not revocation or closure, and eligible jobs complete only with exact primary and backup evidence. TYP-E2E: CAP-025's Failure Condition is asserted unreachable -- access does not remain active, last-admin and dual control are not bypassed, held bytes are not destroyed, deadline failure does not claim completion, and no lifecycle outcome is missing or unclassified. TYP-SEC: OD-016 single-Session revocation -- a SecurityOperator holding `session.revoke` revokes exactly ONE identified Session to `revoked` with reason `security_revocation`, emitting one `SessionRevoked` and the SM-REQ-004 audit event. TYP-SEC: concurrent Sessions of the same Account are UNAFFECTED by that revocation; sign-out-everywhere is asserted absent from baseline behaviour. TYP-SEC: an unauthorized request, a Session outside the actor's authorized scope, and a nonexistent Session identifier return an INDISTINGUISHABLE `F1-AUTH-403` with no state change, proven by asserting the responses are byte-identical so Session existence is not disclosed. TYP-SEC: no `session.*` permission returns any Session content. TYP-DATA: `RevokeSession` replay against a terminal Session returns the same success and changes no state. TYP-SEC: the Permission Baseline denies `session.revoke` to OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator, Read-Only Executive Buyer and every service identity. TYP-DATA: the exact Invitation duplicate, existing-member, wrong-account, direct-delivery and terminal behaviours; the authorization-epoch-safe suspend, reactivate, revoke and close paths; immediate Session and work revocation; two-person hold; and the asynchronous primary/backup deletion, tombstone and evidence contract, each per CAP-025 Product Behavior. TYP-OBS: no duplicate grant, transition, Delivery generation or lifecycle job survives replay. WITHHELD under OD-032 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting a DM-REQ-001 identifier namespace or DM-REQ-001 lifecycle owner for Session, LegalHold or LifecycleDeletionJob, including any `ses_`-style, `lh_`-style or `ldj_`-style prefix. The behaviour of all three records is contracted in full; only the namespace assignment is withheld, and the 016 lifecycle-owner assignments stand. WITHHELD under OD-031 and asserted ABSENT: every routine retention-expiry destruction test enumerated in MTX-038.
- rollout: As MTX-038. The OD-032 limb requires owner ratification and a controlled 011 DOMAIN_MODEL.md change; behaviour ships without it because the interim permits behaviour and withholds only the namespace.

### MTX-026 - AC-WF-001 (WF: WF-001)

- Slice: S-01
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-001-onboard-organization-or-invited-account
- interface type: Four application commands behind the approved identity/bootstrap service. WF-001 authorization states that grant issuance and self-service authority belong only to the approved identity/bootstrap service and are not Role permissions held by the registrant, so no registrant-facing product route effects these transitions.
- route: Not applicable - WF-001 names four logical commands (request_bootstrap_grant, self_service_bootstrap, accept_invitation, sign_in_existing_account) executed by the approved identity service. Volume I defines no HTTP exposure for them, and transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline; inventing a route here would assert a product contract Volume I does not define.
- request schema: Per branch, exactly the WF-001 trigger envelope: versioned Identity Validation Receipt (purpose-bound, fresh, 10-minute boundary per AC-CAP-001); plus grant-request principal identity, or Bootstrap Grant ID/state version with complete `organization-profile-v1` and the WF-002 first-Project body, or Invitation ID/expected state version, or exact Organization identifier and optional logical return target; plus the logical command and idempotency envelope.
- response schema: Grant branch returns Grant ID, state version and expiry only. Self-service returns Organization, Account, BillingEntity, Role Assignment, Access Policy, Entitlement Policy, Plan Assignment, draft Project and Session identifiers each with state version. Invitation acceptance returns existing Organization plus Account, Role Assignment, Invitation and Session identifiers. Decline returns only the terminal Invitation result. Sign-in returns Account and Session identifiers/state versions plus the destination decision. All responses are the authorized redacted result.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I for this workflow. The approved identity/bootstrap service is the only entry point.
- command: `Workflows::Wf001::RequestBootstrapGrant`, `Workflows::Wf001::BootstrapOrganization`, `Workflows::Wf001::AcceptInvitation`, `Workflows::Wf001::DeclineInvitation`, `Workflows::Wf001::SignInExistingAccount`. Reconciled from the existing APPLICATION_LAYER inventory, which already names these five. Service-only lifecycle operations `ExpireBootstrapGrant`, `ExpireInvitation` and `ExpireSession` are ScheduledAction transitions, not principal commands.
- command input: The branch envelope above, normalized. Normalized input hashes are recorded for audit per WF-001 Audit and Observability.
- command output: The branch result above plus the ordered durable event result. Exact replay returns the stored authorized result and emits no event.
- actor: Validated self-service identity principal, invitation-bound identity principal, or existing Account identity principal, always executed through the approved identity/bootstrap service. WF-001 forbids the registrant holding this authority as a Role permission.
- organization scope: Grant issuance has no tenant scope and creates no tenant record. Self-service establishes the Organization within its atomic commit. Invitation acceptance and sign-in are bound to exactly one named Organization; sign-in MUST never search another Organization.
- aggregate: Multi-root atomic commit. Roots: BootstrapGrant (grant branch); Organization, Account, BillingEntity, RoleAssignment, AccessPolicy, EntitlementPolicy, PlanAssignment, Project, Session (self-service); Invitation, Account, RoleAssignment, Session (invitation); Session only (sign-in).
- aggregate boundary: WF-001 requires one atomic product commit across multiple roots, which the single-aggregate default in EM-III-011 does not cover. This is a product-specific specialisation: EM-II-010 permits a multi-root transaction where higher authority requires atomicity, and WF-001 requires that no partial onboarding branch is ever visible. Orchestration is in the Application Layer per EM-III-011 section 7.
- value objects: IdentityValidationReceipt (purpose, freshness boundary, nonce), BootstrapGrantId, OrganizationId, AccountId, SessionId, ProjectId, ContentHash for `organization-profile-v1`, `access-policy-v1`, `entitlement-interim-v1`, `interim-baseline-plan-v1`.
- domain service: Not applicable - WF-001 assigns validation and orchestration to the approved identity/bootstrap service at the application layer; no domain service is named by Volume I.
- repository: `BootstrapGrantRepository`, `OrganizationRepository`, `AccountRepository`, `BillingEntityRepository`, `RoleAssignmentRepository`, `AccessPolicyRepository`, `EntitlementPolicyRepository`, `PlanAssignmentRepository`, `ProjectRepository`, `SessionRepository`, `InvitationRepository`. Persistence-adapter shape per EM-V-013.
- persistence model: Canonical tables per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. The grant branch writes only the BootstrapGrant record and no tenant row. Pass B does not redefine the physical schema; it binds each command to the existing canonical owner.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - the canonical schema already carries every table WF-001 names. Migration ordering is owned by schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md and S-00 supplies the migration harness only.
- transaction boundary: One transaction per branch, covering exactly the records that branch materializes. Grant issuance commits the BootstrapGrant and receipt-nonce consumption only. Self-service commits the full thirteen-event ordered result. Sign-in commits receipt-nonce consumption and one Session and changes no other entity state. No billing provider is called inside any transaction; WF-001 states no billing provider is called at all in this workflow.
- concurrency: Serialization on the branch uniqueness key with a race outcome recorded for audit. Self-service is guarded by current grant uniqueness and the consumed-self-service index, so a second concurrent bootstrap for the same principal loses and returns its first-match failure reason without partial write. Invitation acceptance is version-checked on Invitation state version; a stale version returns its first-match reason and changes nothing. At grant or invitation expiry equality the lifecycle transition wins, per WF-001 Failure Path.
- idempotency: Idempotent by the logical command idempotency envelope, per branch. Grant issuance: exact replay returns the same grant and emits no event. Self-service, invitation and sign-in: exact replay returns the stored authorized result and emits no event, per WF-001 Recovery Path and Domain Events. Receipt nonce is single-use and consumed inside the atomic commit, so a replayed command never re-consumes a nonce. Altered payload against the same idempotency key is a mandatory failure fixture per AC assertion rule 4.
- background job: Not applicable for the principal commands - all four branches commit synchronously. `ExpireBootstrapGrant`, `ExpireInvitation` and `ExpireSession` are service-only ScheduledAction transitions and are contracted with their owning lifecycle rows, not here.
- queue: Not applicable - no principal branch of WF-001 enqueues work.
- retry policy: Grant, bootstrap and Invitation branches only: initial attempt plus exactly two service retries at 1 second and 5 seconds after the preceding failure, for transient transaction or dependency failure only. Each retry rechecks receipt and grant/invitation expiry. Sign-in receives no automatic retry. There is no retry for validation, authentication, authorization, state, uniqueness or expiry outcomes.
- terminal failure: Retry exhaustion on grant/bootstrap/invitation returns `F1-DEPENDENCY-503 / onboarding_transaction_unavailable` and exposes no partial write. Sign-in timeout returns `F1-TIMEOUT-504 / sign_in_timeout`, retryable=false, recovery `reauthenticate_and_submit_new_command`. Each transaction attempt has a 10-second elapsed deadline; at equality the timeout wins and rolls back the attempt.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - every branch is atomic and exposes no partial state, so there is no divergent state to reconcile. Recovery is a new command against current state per WF-001 Recovery Path.
- domain events: Grant: `BootstrapGrantIssued`. Self-service, in exactly this order: `AccountProvisionRequested`, `OrganizationCreated`, `BillingStateChanged(to_state=pending)`, `RoleGranted`, `AccessPolicyActivated`, `PlanAssigned`, `EntitlementPolicyActivated`, `BillingStateChanged(from_state=pending,to_state=active)`, `AccountActivated`, `OrganizationActivated`, `ProjectCreated`, `BootstrapGrantConsumed`, `SessionCreated`. New-Account invitation: `AccountProvisionRequested`, `AccountActivated`, `RoleGranted`, `InvitationAccepted`, `SessionCreated`. Existing-Account invitation: `RoleGranted` only when a new Assignment is created, then `InvitationAccepted`, `SessionCreated`. Sign-in: `SessionCreated` with its creation reason. Decline: no event beyond the terminal Invitation result. Exact replay emits none.
- event payload: The canonical envelope per DM-REQ-013. Under OD-013 Option 1, `BootstrapGrantIssued` and `BootstrapGrantExpired` alone substitute the immutable `bootstrap_principal_id` into `organization_id`, because no Organization exists at grant issuance or expiry. The substitution is confined to those two types, is never available once an Organization exists, and `BootstrapGrantConsumed` and every later event carry the created Organization's real `organization_id`. No event carries null, a sentinel, an invented platform tenant, an omitted tenant field, or an `event_scope` field.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf001` is the sole producer of every event listed above. `BootstrapGrantExpired` is produced by the service-only `ExpireBootstrapGrant` ScheduledAction.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - WF-001 defines no consumer. Consumers are contracted by the slices that own them (S-19 Notifications, S-22 Entitlements) and must not be inferred here.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined for WF-001 by Volume I. Response serialization is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline.
- authorization entry point: The approved identity/bootstrap service, before any mutation. Authorization precedes validation of the branch body and precedes the atomic commit. Because no route reaches these commands, there is no controller-only check to bypass; the service is the only entry point and enforces authority per branch.
- permission checks: Grant issuance and self-service: the exact current receipt/eligibility predicate or the `organization.bootstrap` Grant. These are expressly not Role permissions held by the registrant. Invitation acceptance/decline: the single-use invitation-bound capability, recipient-bound, executed by the identity service for the matching principal. Sign-in: receipt-bound principal and exact Organization only. The resulting Session grants only the separately resolved current effective permissions and no path grants authority beyond its exact commit.
- tenant boundary: Enforced before Project creation per WF-001 Security Notes. Sign-in MUST NOT search another Organization. Invitation acceptance reuses or creates only a same-Organization Account. The grant branch creates no tenant record at all, so no tenant boundary exists to cross.
- error contract: Exhaustive `onboarding-interim-v1` first-match reasons. Named: `F1-DEPENDENCY-503 / onboarding_transaction_unavailable` (transient exhaustion, retryable=false at exhaustion, no partial write); `F1-TIMEOUT-504 / sign_in_timeout` (retryable=false, recovery reauthenticate_and_submit_new_command); `access_unavailable` returned by sign-in when the resolved effective-permission set contains no allowed protected action after explicit-deny precedence. Every failure carries error class/code, one declared reason, severity, retryability, recovery action and correlation/support reference per AC assertion rule 7. Any failure before commit exposes none of that branch's records, state changes, events, BillingEntity or Session.
- audit record: Per WF-001 Audit and Observability: receipt/grant/invitation schema and state versions, normalized input hashes, Access/Entitlement/Plan approval and BillingEntity linkage hashes, serialization/race outcome, branch, exact event IDs and order, commit attempts and latency, Session and authorization context, sign-in destination decision, all under one correlation ID. A rejected no-state command emits only the standard audited command outcome.
