Authorized dead-letter replay increments the replay generation and queues attempt one; exact replay returns one generation.
- background job: `ParsingJobWorker` and `IndexingJobWorker` per queued job; `EvaluationInputSealJob` fired by the manifest-terminal plus intake-close predicate; `ParsingRetryTimer` and `IndexingRetryTimer` on the fixed schedule.
- queue: The parsing queue and the indexing queue, per the Volume I Background Processing action registry. Queue identity is owned by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md; this row does not rename it.
- retry policy: Parsing and indexing internal retry each follows its fixed 30/120-second schedule: one initial attempt plus two retries at exactly 30 and 120 seconds after the prior failure. Indexing retries ONLY `index_timeout` and `index_dependency_unavailable`; every other reason moves `running -> failed -> dead_letter` at one checkpoint, as does an exhausted third retry. Each indexing attempt has a 30-second elapsed timeout; equality belongs to the timeout and late output is discarded.
- terminal failure: Exhausted or nonretryable ParsingJob moves `Failed -> DeadLetter`; authorized `DeadLetter -> Queued`. Any exact blocked predicate produces `evaluation_inputs_unavailable`, no Check or provider side effect, and the specified initial or reassessment Evaluation failure transaction. Failure and retry never regress Document state or alter a prior Index Receipt.
- reconciliation: Later parsing success is visible ONLY to a new Evaluation Input Snapshot and Evaluation; later indexing success advances ONLY the Retrieval projection. Neither reaches back into a sealed snapshot. A successful replay completion event carries `recovery_of_id` and the replay generation and is the ERR-REQ-020 recovery-complete signal.
- domain events: `IngestionSucceeded`, `ParsingQueued`, `ParsingStarted`, `DocumentParsed`, `ParsingSucceeded`, `ParsingFailed`, `ParsingDeadLettered`, `ParsingReplayRequested`, `IndexingQueued`, `IndexingStarted`, `DocumentIndexed`, `IndexingSucceeded`, `IndexingFailed`, `IndexingDeadLettered`, `IndexingReplayRequested`, `ExternalMeasurementAccepted`, `EvaluationInputsReady`, `EvaluationInputsBlocked`, `EvaluationStarted`, `EvaluationFailed`.
- event payload: The canonical envelope with the real `organization_id`, plus the job, Artifact, Receipt or snapshot identity, digests, versions, attempt and replay generation, reason code and correlation ID. A replay completion event carries `recovery_of_id` and the replay generation.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf006` is the sole producer of every event above. `IngestionSucceeded` originates in WF-005 (S-07) and is the trigger this workflow consumes.
- event consumers: WF-007 (S-12) consumes the sealed Evaluation Input Snapshot, not these events. The Retrieval projection consumes Index Receipts. AC-WF-006 states the initial Evaluation starts only in WF-007, so this workflow never starts it.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined by Volume I; see route.
- authorization entry point: The service identity for automated execution; the replay command for human-requested replay, before mutation. Only the corresponding recovery service performs a replay, so requesting and performing are separate authorities.
- permission checks: `parsing.recover` and `indexing.recover` for dead-letter replay, held by an OrganizationAdmin or MarketingOperator, or reached by a SecurityOperator only through an active Support Session naming the job and action. `external_measurement.submit` for a tenant-scoped adapter. `measurement_set.activate` for the owner-approval release service. Tenant-scoped service identities for execution and retry.
- tenant boundary: Every input and output reference is same-Organization. Quarantined or invalid Evidence cannot produce a Parsed Artifact. Cross-tenant external content is `F1-DATA-409 / tenant_mismatch`. Input sanitation and untrusted-content isolation are mandatory: the parser consumes attacker-controlled bytes, so JSON-LD remote contexts are never fetched and DTDs and external entities are disabled.
- error contract: Manifest: `input_manifest_invalid` for a missing tuple, duplicate Document, cross-Organization reference, manifest/content-digest mismatch, or a manifest omitting a succeeded IngestionJob -- it blocks readiness and an implementation cannot silently drop it. Indexing first-match before execution: `tenant_mismatch`, `parsed_artifact_missing`, `parsed_artifact_quarantined`, `parsed_artifact_digest_mismatch`, `index_schema_unsupported`, `index_target_policy_unavailable`. During execution: `index_timeout`, `index_dependency_unavailable`, `index_output_invalid`; unknown diagnostics map to `index_output_invalid` and remain restricted. Readiness: `evaluation_inputs_unavailable` on any blocked predicate. External intake: `F1-DATA-409 / tenant_mismatch`, `F1-DOMAIN-409 / measurement_set_unavailable`, `F1-VALIDATION-400 / external_measurement_invalid`, `idempotency_conflict`.
- audit record: Every contract field, the parse and index manifest and snapshot hash, platform-derived Evidence and Measurement Evidence or the missing reason, the intake deadline, attempt and replay generation, scheduled and actual times, reason, failed subset, readiness derivation, non-gating indexing outcome, Evaluation transition, authorization, event ID and correlation ID, per WF-006 Audit and Observability.
- observability: Parsing and indexing attempt and replay telemetry, failed-subset identities and reasons, readiness derivation, the non-gating indexing outcome and Evaluation transitions under one correlation ID. A missing or dead-letter index never disappears from telemetry.
- retention: Parsed Artifacts, Index Receipts and the Evaluation Input Snapshot are immutable and retained under their own contracts. `payload_retention_class=product_evidence_payload` on accepted external observations. The retention window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states none.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: the parse manifest is the complete set of distinct Documents whose IngestionJobs reached `succeeded` for the selected Crawl, ordered by Source ID, then canonical Document URL UTF-8 bytes, then Document ID. TYP-DATA: a missing tuple, duplicate Document, cross-Organization reference, manifest/content-digest mismatch, or a manifest omitting a succeeded IngestionJob is `input_manifest_invalid` and blocks readiness -- asserted as five separate fixtures, each proving the member is not silently dropped. TYP-DATA: exactly one ParsingJob per `(document_id, content_digest, parser_definition_version)`; an exact duplicate ingestion signal returns the existing job; a changed digest or parser version creates a DISTINCT job and cannot overwrite an earlier result. TYP-DATA: `ParsingSucceeded` and `DocumentParsed` commit together; neither exists without the validated Artifact and successful job -- asserted absent when the transaction fails. TYP-DATA: an exact replay against a Document already parsed or indexed returns the Artifact and emits no second transition. TYP-DATA: Parsed Artifact content never mutates. TYP-DATA: `parsed-observation-v1` top-level object contains exactly `schema_version`, `canonical_document_url`, `document_kind` (`html` or `xhtml`), nullable lower-case BCP-47 `document_language`, `title_nodes`, `link_edges`, `organization_nodes`; an unknown top-level field is rejected. TYP-DATA: strings decoded, NFC-normalized, internal whitespace retained unless a field rule says otherwise. TYP-DATA: `title_nodes` ordered by document position with zero-based `position`, character-reference-decoded `text`, stable `locator` of `head/title[n]`; blank text is RETAINED. TYP-DATA: `link_edges` ordered by position with decoded `href`, stable locator, sorted distinct lower-case ASCII `relation_tokens`, and either `target_canonical_url` or `target_rejection_reason` -- never both and never neither. TYP-DATA: a rejected or non-HTTP(S) target has a null canonical URL and exactly one reason from `empty`, `unsupported_scheme`, `invalid_url`, `outside_source_scope`. TYP-SEC: the target uses the FROZEN Crawl URL canonicalization and scope versions, not current ones. TYP-DATA: `organization_nodes` contains only JSON-LD objects whose `@type` set includes exact `Organization` or a `structured-identity-v1` subtype; ordered by script document position then object traversal path; duplicates remain DISTINCT. TYP-SEC: JSON-LD remote contexts are NEVER fetched; DTDs and external entities are disabled; malformed JSON-LD produces one malformed item with null name and url rather than disappearing. TYP-DATA: the Source-root payload includes the frozen Crawl terminal-outcome map keyed by canonical in-scope URL with exactly one outcome from `document_valid`, `content_absent`, `content_fetch_failed`, `policy_excluded`; non-root payloads OMIT that map. TYP-DATA: the payload is invalid if positions repeat, ordering is wrong, a locator is blank, a target has both or neither, the root map is absent on a Source root, or a referenced outcome contradicts the sealed Crawl snapshot -- six fixtures. TYP-DATA: one `parsed_content/document-title-observation-v1` Evidence per successfully parsed Document. TYP-DATA: one `crawl_observation/internal-link-observation-v1` Evidence per active Source, combining same-Source `link_edges` with the sealed root terminal-outcome map; an admitted target without a covered terminal outcome is `unobserved`; coverage is partial if the Crawl or relevant parser subset is partial. TYP-DATA: one `parsed_content/organization-identity-observation-v1` Evidence per active Source root from a frozen `public-identity-interim-v1` snapshot whose ID is its SHA-256 content hash; expected public name is the Organization's normalized display name and expected public URL is the Source canonical HTTPS root, and those are the ONLY interim identity comparison fields. TYP-DATA: missing root input creates NO identity Evidence and remains an explicit expected Check entry. TYP-DATA: a derivation or schema failure is recorded by Document, Source and reason, creates NO substitute Evidence, and becomes the corresponding handled Check input error rather than silently omitting the expected result key. TYP-DATA: derived Evidence carries source Artifact and Crawl Evidence lineage and the strongest classification, and is ordered and stored BEFORE the snapshot seals. TYP-DATA: `external-measurement-v1` bundles no active Measurement Set while OD-010 is pending -- the three always-applicable external entries deterministically select no Evidence and return handled `input_evidence_missing`; `CHK-LP-001` does so only when local presence is applicable and otherwise returns its catalog-defined not-applicable result without Evidence. TYP-SEC: no implementation invents a query, prompt, provider, listing directory, threshold or adapter. TYP-DATA: missing applicable Evidence makes the numeric score unavailable. TYP-SEC: an external submission validates authority, Organization/Project/Evaluation, exact set keys/order/coverage/freshness, adapter eligibility, payload schema/digest and one-record uniqueness IN THAT ORDER. TYP-DATA: exactly one `external-observation-v1` per `(evaluation_id, measurement_kind, measurement_set_version)`; exact replay returns it; altered replay or a second payload is `idempotency_conflict` and changes nothing. TYP-SEC: cross-tenant content is `F1-DATA-409 / tenant_mismatch`; unknown or inactive set is `F1-DOMAIN-409 / measurement_set_unavailable`; invalid content is `F1-VALIDATION-400 / external_measurement_invalid`. TYP-DATA: exactly one IndexingJob per `(parsed_artifact_id, normalized_payload_sha256, index_target_id=tenant-retrieval, index_schema_version=retrieval-index-interim-v1)`; exact repeated parse success returns the same job; a changed Artifact digest or index version creates a distinct job and never replaces an earlier receipt. TYP-DATA: indexing first-match reasons in order before execution, and the three execution reasons; an unknown diagnostic maps to `index_output_invalid` and remains restricted. TYP-DATA: each indexing attempt has a 30-second elapsed timeout; equality belongs to the timeout and late output is DISCARDED. TYP-OBS: only `index_timeout` and `index_dependency_unavailable` retry, at exactly 30 and 120 seconds; every other reason and an exhausted third retry move `running -> failed -> dead_letter` at ONE checkpoint. TYP-DATA: a successful indexing attempt changes the same-version Document parsed -> indexed only when it is STILL parsed; a Document already indexed by exact replay does not transition again. TYP-SEC: the Index Receipt contains NO copied Evidence bytes and is NOT lineage authority -- retrieval traverses to the Parsed Artifact and Evidence. TYP-DATA: failure and retry never regress Document state or alter a prior Index Receipt. TYP-SEC: authorized dead-letter replay requires the exact permission, expected job version, a 20-2,000 character reason and a NEW idempotency key; it increments replay generation and queues attempt one; exact replay returns one generation; the 19/20/2,000/2,001 reason boundaries are asserted. TYP-SEC: a SecurityOperator reaches replay only through a Support Session naming the job and action; only the recovery service performs it. TYP-INT: indexing is NOT an Evaluation-input prerequisite -- a missing or dead-letter index never blocks or changes a sealed Evaluation, and an implementation MUST NOT wait for indexing in one code path and bypass it in another. TYP-DATA: the snapshot is created only after every parse-manifest job is `succeeded` or `dead_letter` AND the intake close predicate occurs. TYP-DATA: the snapshot is immutable and its canonical content hash is stable; concurrent completion order never changes the ordered snapshot or hash. TYP-DATA: `ready_partial` continues with exact failed-subset identities and reasons and partial coverage. TYP-DATA: any exact blocked predicate produces `evaluation_inputs_unavailable`, no Check or provider side effect, and the specified Evaluation failure transaction. TYP-DATA: later parsing success is visible ONLY to a new snapshot and Evaluation; later indexing success advances ONLY the Retrieval projection. TYP-DATA: the initial Evaluation starts only in WF-007 and reassessment emits no second start. TYP-SEC: quarantined or invalid Evidence cannot produce a Parsed Artifact. WITHHELD under OD-027 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting a second IndexingJob per ParsingJob, a `has_one` narrowing, or a `unique (parsing_job_id)` constraint.
- rollout: S-08 depends on S-07 for succeeded IngestionJobs with valid `source_document` Evidence and gates S-09. The OD-027 limb stays withheld: `indexing-interim-v1` pins both index keys, and the final multiplicity requires ratification plus controlled Volume I integration rather than an implementation choice.

### MTX-038 - AC-WF-013 (WF: WF-013)

- Slice: S-23
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: OD-031 -- OD-031: routine retention-expiry destruction withheld under retention-destruction-trigger-interim-v1
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-013-manage-tenant-lifecycle
- interface type: Application commands plus service-executed timed transitions across five subflows Volume I keeps distinct and this row does not merge: Invitation, Account, Organization, Legal Hold And Deletion, and Support Session. Human commands are synchronous; every timed transition is executed only by its named lifecycle service from a persisted deadline; irreversible destruction alone is asynchronous.
- route: Not applicable - WF-013 defines commands, permissions, envelopes and persisted deadlines but no HTTP path, method or transport; see `routes_position`. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline.
- request schema: Per command, and always the Logical Command Envelope. Invitation create: Organization, normalized target email, canonical role, `permission_mode`, nullable persona, normalized scope, protected-permission preview, intended Assignment expiry, idempotency key, correlation ID. Invitation decide/revoke/reissue: opaque Invitation ID, expected state version, 20-2,000 character reason where required. Account suspend: Account ID, expected Account state version, nonblank 20-2,000 character reason. Account reactivate under `reactivation-proof-v1`: the ACTOR's current active Session with `mfa_satisfied=true`, `account.reactivate` in the target's Organization, expected Account state version, expected Organization authorization epoch, 20-2,000 character reason, and for a SecurityOperator one active Support Session naming the Account and exact action; it accepts NO Identity Validation Receipt. Account revoke/delete: Account ID, expected state version, reason. Organization suspend/reactivate: expected Organization state version AND expected authorization epoch, 20-2,000 character reason; reactivation additionally requires a fresh `organization_reactivation` receipt bound to the target Organization with `mfa_satisfied=true`. Closure request: expected versions, 20-2,000 character reason. Closure decide: Closure Request ID, expected state version, decision, reason. Legal Hold request/decide/release-request/release-decide: exact resource IDs and/or retention classes, inclusive UTC event-time interval, 20-2,000 character legal-purpose reason, expected state version. Support Session request/decide/revoke: Organization, resource and action allowlists, support-case or Incident ID, 20-2,000 character reason, expected state version. Deletion retry: failed LifecycleDeletionJob ID, expected state version, authorized new replay generation.
- response schema: The mutated record with its new state and incremented state version; for closure execution the closed Organization, its closed BillingEntity, the executed Closure Request and the created Organization LifecycleDeletionJob; for Account deletion the revoked Account and its LifecycleDeletionJob; for an accepted Invitation the Account, Role Assignment, Invitation and Session identifiers; for an approving release decision the released Legal Hold and each requeued blocked-deletion action. A denial returns its exact first-match reason with no record.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I for WF-013; see `routes_position`.
- command: `Workflows::Wf013::CreateInvitation`, `DecideInvitation`, `RevokeInvitation`, `ReissueInvitation`, `RequestRoleAssignment`, `DecideRoleAssignment`, `RevokeRoleAssignment`, `ExpireRoleAssignment`, `ActivateAccessPolicy`, `SuspendAccount`, `ReactivateAccount`, `RevokeAccount`, `DeleteAccount`, `SuspendOrganization`, `ReactivateOrganization`, `RequestOrganizationClosure`, `DecideOrganizationClosure`, `ExpireOrganizationClosure`, `ExecuteOrganizationClosure`, `RequestSupportSession`, `DecideSupportSession`, `RevokeSupportSession`, `ExpireSupportSession`, `RequestLegalHold`, `DecideLegalHold`, `RequestLegalHoldRelease`, `DecideLegalHoldRelease`, `RetryDeletion`, plus `Workflows::Wf001::ExpireInvitation` for timed Invitation expiry and `Workflows::DataLifecycle::StartLifecycleDeletion`, `ExecuteLifecycleDeletionCheckpoint`, `FailLifecycleDeletion` and `VerifyBackupTombstone` for service-only deletion execution. `ReactivateAccount` is REACHABLE under ratified OD-021 `reactivation-proof-v1`; see `stale_prose_corrections`. `RecordEvidenceRetentionWarning` appears in the registry but is NOT contracted here; see `volume_i_contradiction_refused`.
- command input: As request_schema. Two inputs are mandatory rather than optional and are the row's load-bearing guards: every command on an existing record carries its expected record state version, and it additionally carries the expected Organization authorization epoch whenever effective access could change. WF-013's Preconditions state both.
- command output: The accepted transition with its incremented versions and its exact event set, or an auditable denial that changes nothing. Every accepted effective-access mutation increments the Organization authorization epoch exactly once.
- actor: Organization Administrator and Security Operator per subflow, plus five named lifecycle service identities Volume I keeps separate and this row does not merge: the invitation lifecycle service, which alone writes timed Invitation expiry; the role-expiry lifecycle service, the sole producer of a blocked expiry; the support-session lifecycle service holding `support.session.expire`; the tenant lifecycle service, the sole executor of approved closure; and the deletion lifecycle service. WF-013's Authorization clause is explicit that each service executes only its own accepted records and has no broader permission. No actor may approve its own protected request.
- organization scope: The target record's Organization. WF-013 Preconditions require the Organization to be active EXCEPT that Organization reactivation/closure lifecycle and protected Legal Hold/deletion execution are admitted in suspended or closed state exactly as their contracts require -- the same exception MTX-070's gate must not override. A Role Assignment or Account action requires its target Account to belong to the Organization; Invitation, Access Policy, Support Session, Organization, hold and deletion actions use their exact Organization or resource scope.
- aggregate: Invitation, RoleAssignment, AccessPolicy, SupportSession, Account, Organization, BillingEntity, ClosureRequest, LegalHold and LifecycleDeletionJob, each a separate root. `OrganizationMembership` is NOT a root: PRODUCT_DEFINITION.md and `onboarding-interim-v1` both state it is a derived `(organization_id, account_id)` authorization view over current Account state, Organization authorization epoch and Role Assignments, not a separate mutable entity, permission source or expiry clock, so no command here creates, updates or overrides it. `RoleExpiryBlockDecision` is an immutable `decision` record with no lifecycle, written once and never transitioned.
- aggregate boundary: Approved closure execution is a bounded multi-root commit because WF-013 requires it: one transaction atomically changes the Organization's one current active BillingEntity and the Organization to closed, increments their versions and the Organization authorization epoch, revokes all Sessions and uncommitted work, records the Closure Request as executed, and creates the exact Organization LifecycleDeletionJob. Invitation acceptance is likewise multi-root by requirement, atomically creating or reusing the Account and the exact Role Assignment and creating a Session. Every other command commits its own root. This is a specialisation the workflow demands, not a design preference.
- value objects: OrganizationState (`pending`, `active`, `suspended`, `closed`), AccountState (`active`, `suspended`, `revoked`), AuthorizationEpoch, StateVersion, InvitationId (at least 128 bits of entropy), TargetEmailDigest, PermissionMode (`standard`, `read_only`), Persona, NormalizedScope, ProtectedPermissionAllowlist, ClosureRequestStatus (`pending`, `approved`, `rejected`, `expired`, `executed`), ApprovalDueAt, LegalHoldScope, DeletionMode (`logical_deletion`, `physical_deletion`, `archival`, `revocation`, `irreversible_destruction`), ResourceClassManifest, DeletionEvidenceDigest, SupportSessionAllowlist, RevokeReason, LifecycleReason (nonblank 20-2,000 characters).
- domain service: Not applicable - WF-013 names no domain service. The last-administrator predicate, the requester/approver separation predicate, the effective-permission algorithm and the hold scope-intersection predicate are pure functions over current records, applied inside the commands. The Organization-state gate they run behind is PRULE-019, owned by S-02's MTX-070 and consumed without redefinition.
- repository: `InvitationRepository`, `RoleAssignmentRepository`, `AccessPolicyRepository`, `SupportSessionRepository`, `AccountRepository`, `OrganizationRepository`, `BillingEntityRepository`, `ClosureRequestRepository`, `LegalHoldRepository`, `LifecycleDeletionJobRepository`, `SessionRepository`.
- persistence model: Canonical tables per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md, each carrying `organization_id NOT NULL` with `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, plus state and state version. Retention classes are assigned by `retention-interim-v1`'s Core And Auxiliary Record Assignment and are not chosen here: Organization, Account and BillingEntity are `identity_commercial`; Invitation and Session are `identity_commercial`; LegalHold, LifecycleDeletionJob, Deletion Evidence, Role Assignment, Access Policy and authorization decisions, Support Session, protected approval and Closure Request are `security_audit`. WITHHELD under OD-031 and MUST NOT be built: any retention-cursor-driven partition drop for `product_history`, `identity_commercial` or `security_audit`. The physical schema already presumes one; OD-031's Risks clause records that no higher layer authorizes it and that it must be corrected, so this row does not implement it.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - the canonical schema owns every product table WF-013 writes, and no schema object is added by this contract. Two DDL prohibitions are load-bearing rather than incidental: under OD-033 no schema, migration, function or grant may admit a LifecycleDeletionJob `queued -> completed` edge; under OD-031 no retention-expiry destruction function, grant, scheduled sweep or partition-drop migration exists.
- transaction boundary: Account suspension: one transaction that atomically transitions active to suspended AND revokes Sessions before success returns. Account reactivation: one transaction validating the actor's own MFA-satisfied Session, both expected versions and the retained-grant set, restoring only still-active unexpired explicit Assignments. Organization suspension: one transaction changing active to suspended, recording the reason, incrementing both versions, revoking every active human Session and preventing new scheduled work. Closure request: one transaction creating the immutable Closure Request, which confers no state change. Closure execution: the single multi-root transaction in aggregate_boundary, allowed from active or suspended, rechecking expected versions, capturing the current legal-hold and retention snapshot and proving requester/approver separation. Deletion: immediate access revocation and logical deletion commit before success returns; physical and irreversible stages execute asynchronously and re-evaluate holds at EVERY destructive checkpoint. Completion is one transaction that persists the immutable Deletion Evidence and emits the completion event together, so no completion exists without its evidence. No external call sits inside a database transaction.
- concurrency: The Organization authorization epoch is the serialization point for effective access, and it is a product requirement rather than a mechanism choice: Account suspend, revoke and delete and every Role Assignment revoke, reject, expiry and scope change that removes effective OrganizationAdmin authority serialize on it, so competing commands against DIFFERENT Accounts cannot both pass from the same epoch. The mutation is rejected as `last_organization_admin` unless another active Account already holds an active, in-scope, unexpired OrganizationAdmin Assignment or the same atomic transaction activates that replacement before removing the old authority. Closure: pending to approved/rejected/expired and approved to executed are the only transitions, so a decision racing the 24-hour due instant resolves with expiry winning at equality. Support Session and Invitation expiry likewise win at exact equality. Concurrent reactivation commands against one Organization commit exactly one transition; the loser observes the current state version and returns the stored result. A blocked expiry and a concurrent epoch advance serialize on the epoch, and exactly one `RoleExpiryBlockDecision` is written per `(role_assignment_id, authorization_epoch)`.
- idempotency: Every command carries an idempotency key and its expected record state version; exact replay returns the stored result and commits no second transition, while an altered command under an existing idempotency identity returns `F1-DOMAIN-409 / idempotency_conflict`. Invitation creation is keyed by the open-invitation uniqueness preimage -- Organization, target-email digest, target identity when bound, canonical role, permission mode and persona, normalized scope, protected-permission preview and intended Assignment expiry -- with at most one pending-approval or active Invitation per full preimage; exact replay returns it and a different-key duplicate returns `invitation_duplicate_open` with the Invitation ID only while the requester remains authorized. Account deletion and Organization closure each create or replay exactly ONE LifecycleDeletionJob; exact replay duplicates no deletion. `RoleExpiryBlockDecision` is idempotent on `(role_assignment_id, authorization_epoch)`, so re-evaluating within an unchanged epoch writes no second decision and emits no second `RoleExpiryBlocked`. `DecideLegalHoldRelease` creates or replays each exact blocked-deletion due-now action once; there is no second release command.
- background job: Timed transitions run as ScheduledActions per BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md, each executed only by its named lifecycle service from the persisted deadline: `invitation_expire`, `role_assignment_expire`, `closure_request_expire` and `support_session_expire` on `control`; `organization_closure_execute`, `lifecycle_deletion_start` (within 60 seconds of acceptance), `lifecycle_deletion_attempt_due`, `lifecycle_deletion_deadline` and `backup_tombstone_verify` on `lifecycle`; `deletion_tombstone_invariant_sweep_due` on `maintenance`. Queue arrival time never decides a Volume I equality race; the receiving job rechecks the product deadline at transaction time. WITHHELD under OD-031: no retention-expiry sweep, cursor job or scheduled destruction action exists in this catalogue, and none may be added.
- queue: `control` for expiry and authorization-epoch effects, `lifecycle` for closure execution, deletion manifests, key and object destruction and backup tombstones, `maintenance` for the deletion/hold/deadline/tombstone invariant sweep. Queue identity, ownership and per-process concurrency are owned by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md; this row does not rename or re-topologise them.
- retry policy: Bounded and per-stage, exactly as `retention-interim-v1` fixes it: each primary, index, cache and key attempt has a 15-minute timeout and exactly ONE retry after 5 minutes, and only for `deletion_dependency_unavailable`. Schema, scope, digest, policy and authorization failures are nonretryable. A denied lifecycle command is terminal for that command and is never auto-retried. A failed job returns to queued only through an authorized new replay generation, never automatically.
- terminal failure: Closed Organization and BillingEntity, released or rejected Legal Hold, executed, rejected or expired Closure Request, accepted, declined, rejected, revoked or expired Invitation, revoked Account, and completed deletion job are terminal. Closed permits only retention and legal-hold job execution and immutable audit retrieval by authorized security or legal scope, and never reactivates. A missing or nonactive current BillingEntity returns `F1-DOMAIN-409 / billing_entity_state_invalid` and changes nothing; reserved `past_due` and `suspended` never exit through closure in the accepted baseline. Deadline failure or exhausted work emits `LifecycleDeletionFailed` and a critical escalation and NEVER fabricates completion evidence. All other transitions are invalid.
- reconciliation: Blocked deletion remains blocked until exactly one hold release event queues it once against current policy; a changed policy may lengthen but never silently shorten an already accrued mandatory minimum. Failed replay requires a new authorized recovery generation; completed never reopens. Backup tombstones are written BEFORE primary completion and every restore applies them before any product read. The `deletion_tombstone_invariant_sweep_due` sweep detects missing start, primary, backup, hold and tombstone actions and deadlines. Under OD-026 a blocked expiry re-evaluates on each Organization authorization-epoch advance and commits through the existing `active -> expired` edge once a replacement administrator is active: no timed grace window, no terminal ceiling, and no admin-less outcome exists.
- domain events: The forty-three-event closed list in WF-013's Domain Events line, emitted exactly as named: `InvitationApprovalRequested`, `InvitationActivated`, `InvitationAccepted`, `InvitationDeclined`, `InvitationRejected`, `InvitationRevoked`, `InvitationExpired`, `RoleAssignmentRequested`, `RoleGranted`, `RoleRejected`, `RoleRevoked`, `RoleExpired`, `RoleExpiryBlocked`, `AccessPolicyActivated`, `AccessPolicySuperseded`, `AccessPolicyRetired`, `SupportSessionRequested`, `SupportSessionActivated`, `SupportSessionRejected`, `SupportSessionExpired`, `SupportSessionRevoked`, `AccountSuspended`, `AccountReactivated`, `AccountRevoked`, `AccountDeletionRequested`, `AccountDeletionBlocked`, `AccountDeletionCompleted`, `OrganizationSuspended`, `OrganizationReactivated`, `OrganizationClosureRequested`, `OrganizationClosureApproved`, `OrganizationClosureRejected`, `OrganizationClosureExpired`, `BillingStateChanged`, `OrganizationClosed`, `LegalHoldRequested`, `LegalHoldActivated`, `LegalHoldRejected`, `LegalHoldReleased`, `OrganizationDeletionRequested`, `OrganizationDeletionBlocked`, `LifecycleDeletionFailed`, `OrganizationDeletionCompleted`. Closure emits `BillingStateChanged` with `from_state=active` and `to_state=closed` BEFORE `OrganizationClosed`; that order is normative. WITHHELD under OD-031: no event represents routine retention expiry, and none may be minted -- the Logical Event Envelope requires a producing `workflow_id`, an `event_profile` and a nonnull affected-entity triple, and no accepted profile admits a retention-cursor record with no aggregate.
- event payload: The canonical envelope with the real `organization_id`, plus the record identity, from and to state, the record state version and the Organization authorization epoch, requester and approver references, separation result, scope, reason, due, effective and expiry times, and the correlation ID. `RoleExpiryBlocked` carries the decision ID, `organization_id`, `role_assignment_id`, `expires_at_utc`, the authorization epoch and the block reason `expiry_blocked_last_admin`. `LegalHoldActivated` and `LegalHoldReleased` identify exact scope and NEVER include held payload. Deletion Evidence contains no deleted payload.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf013` for the command-driven transitions; the named lifecycle service for each timed transition, which is why the producer is not collapsed to one identity. `RoleExpiryBlocked` has exactly one producer, the role-expiry lifecycle service, and no human actor commands a block: the block is an outcome of an attempted timed expiry, never a request. `Workflows::DataLifecycle` produces the deletion execution outcomes.
- event consumers: S-19's WF-014 routes the Invitation, security and lifecycle notification classes, including the mandatory `RoleExpiryBlocked` security route; it consumes these events and is not redefined here. Cache convergence consumes the authorization-epoch effect through the `control` queue.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined by Volume I for WF-013; see `routes_position`.
- authorization entry point: The application command, before mutation, and again at every durable checkpoint of a running privileged operation. A running privileged operation rechecks at each durable checkpoint and stops before the next protected side effect after revocation; a suspended Organization's running protected work stops at its next authorization or source-policy checkpoint and cannot publish a new current projection after suspension. Authorization established at request time is never trusted at execution time.
- permission checks: Every token is verified present in the Permission Baseline. `invitation.create` and `invitation.revoke` within the actor's grant authority; `invitation.approve` as a protected explicit grant held by a different SecurityOperator. `role.manage` for non-protected tenant grants for an OrganizationAdmin, allow for a SecurityOperator; `policy.access.manage` likewise. `account.suspend`, `account.reactivate`, `account.revoke` and `account.delete` for an OrganizationAdmin for tenant-managed accounts subject to the last-admin rule, and for a SecurityOperator for security action -- a SecurityOperator Account action additionally requires ONE active Support Session naming the Account and exact action, and that session's distinct approval supplies the dual control; an Incident without such a session is insufficient. `organization.suspend` and `organization.reactivate` for an OrganizationAdmin for its own Organization or a SecurityOperator under a Support Session. `organization.close` for an OrganizationAdmin to REQUEST for its own Organization; the SecurityOperator approval cell is `protected approval only` and authorizes only approval or rejection, not a request. `legal_hold.manage` as a protected explicit grant plus Support Session and distinct approval, two different active SecurityOperators, with the release approver differing from the release requester; no OrganizationAdmin or lifecycle service may invent or release a hold. `support.session.request`, `support.session.approve` (approver differs from requester), `support.session.revoke`, `support.session.expire` (support-session lifecycle service only), `support.session.customer_approve` (affected OrganizationAdmin). `deletion.retry` for an OrganizationAdmin's own Organization's failed job or a SecurityOperator under a Support Session. Cross-Organization access collects the affected OrganizationAdmin approval unless an active approved Emergency Access Grant authorizes it under `emergency-access-v1`; there is no standing cross-tenant access, `emergency_customer_access` does not exist, and a request asserting emergency access with no active approved Grant returns `F1-AUTH-403` and creates no Support Session.
- tenant boundary: Every record belongs to exactly one Organization and every tenant table is protected by forced RLS resolving `organization_id = f1_current_organization_id()`; application code cannot make tenant context authoritative by executing `SET`, `SET LOCAL`, `set_config` or a raw SQL wrapper. A Support Session names its affected Organization, so a session for one Organization can never authorize a lifecycle action in another. An Emergency Access Grant names exactly one Organization and spans none. An Account belongs to exactly one Organization, and the `(organization_id, identity_issuer_key, identity_subject)` uniqueness key lets one human identity hold separate tenant-scoped Accounts without any cross-Organization authority. `f1_lifecycle` cannot issue arbitrary `DELETE`: it claims one eligible manifest entry through RLS and calls only `lifecycle_apply_manifest_entry`, which validates the Organization, target, retention class, due instant, entry digest and current active LegalHold intersection.
- error contract: Codes are the canonical five. `F1-VALIDATION-400` for input shape, and for an Identity Validation Receipt supplied to `ReactivateAccount`, which accepts none. `F1-AUTHN-401` with the first-match receipt reason for a failed `organization_reactivation` proof, and `identity_assurance_failed` for insufficient MFA. `F1-AUTH-403` for missing authority, cross-Organization selection, `organization_inactive`, `account_reactivate_forbidden`, and an emergency-access assertion with no active approved Grant. `F1-DOMAIN-409` for `last_organization_admin`, `billing_entity_state_invalid`, `stale_state_version`, `stale_authorization_epoch`, `account_state_invalid`, `idempotency_conflict`, `active_policy_conflict`, `access_policy_invalid`, `invitation_duplicate_open`, `invitation_grant_already_active`, `invitation_account_ineligible`, `invitation_not_active`, `invitation_expired`, `invitation_role_scope_changed` and `invitation_approval_required`. Failed reactivation predicates leave the Organization suspended with exactly one reason by first-match order: `access_policy_unavailable`, `organization_admin_unavailable`, `identity_assurance_failed`, `stale_state_version`, `stale_authorization_epoch`. Deletion blocked by an active hold yields `deletion_blocked_legal_hold` for irreversible destruction ONLY, while access remains revoked. Every unauthorized, self-approved protected, privilege-escalating, cross-Organization, stale-version, conflicting-active-policy, expired or idempotency-conflicting command changes nothing and emits an auditable denial.
- audit record: WF-013's Audit and Observability clause enumerates it and this row does not abbreviate it: canonical prior and proposed content hashes; record, Organization and BillingEntity state versions and the authorization epoch; request and approver; separation result; scope; reason; due, effective and expiry times; every Support Session approval reference and allowlist; event IDs; Account, Organization and BillingEntity from and to state; the last-admin candidate set AND result; the Closure Request, retention and legal-hold decision; deletion outcome; affected sessions and work; cache convergence time; and every denied or stale attempt. Immutable audit logs are mandatory under WF-013 Security Notes. No command silently hard-deletes Audit Evidence.
- observability: Account, Organization, Closure Request, Legal Hold and Deletion state; deadline, retry, block, failure, completion-evidence and access-revocation telemetry; cache convergence time as a measured value rather than an assumed one. WITHHELD under OD-031 and required as telemetry instead of destruction: a `product_history`, `identity_commercial` or `security_audit` record reaching its 7-year maximum with no accepted request is recorded as Audit Evidence and operational telemetry under the existing `security_audit` and `operational_telemetry` classes and raises exactly ONE critical compliance escalation through the existing operational escalation path.
- retention: Assigned entirely by `retention-interim-v1`, which OD-011 approved as the FIXED Volume I retention baseline and which this row therefore does not treat as interim or configurable. Organization, Account, BillingEntity, Invitation and Session records are `identity_commercial`, destroyed 7 elapsed years after Organization closure or Account terminal transition, whichever applies later. LegalHold, LifecycleDeletionJob, Deletion Evidence, Role Assignment, Access Policy and authorization decisions, Support Session, protected approval and Closure Request are `security_audit`, destroyed 7 elapsed years after terminal transition and only with two-person security approval. Legal hold suspends ONLY irreversible destruction and never restores access or permits a record to be used past its product-validity boundary. WITHHELD under OD-031: the destruction TRIGGER at those maxima where no accepted deletion or closure request exists. The windows are recorded; the routine destruction that would act on them is not authorized, so the record is retained in full subject to legal hold.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: each command requires its own named Permission Baseline token; holding `organization.close` as an OrganizationAdmin permits a request and never an approval, and the SecurityOperator `organization.close` grant permits approval or rejection only. TYP-SEC: no actor approves its own protected request -- self-approved protected grant, self-approved closure, self-approved hold and self-approved Support Session each commit nothing. TYP-SEC: a SecurityOperator Account action without one active Support Session naming the Account AND the exact action is denied; a session naming a different action or target is likewise denied; an Incident without such a session is insufficient. TYP-DATA: the last-admin invariant -- a suspend, revoke, delete, or Assignment revoke/reject/expiry/scope change that would remove the last effective OrganizationAdmin returns `last_organization_admin` and changes nothing, unless another active Account already holds an active, in-scope, unexpired OrganizationAdmin Assignment or the same atomic transaction activates the replacement first. TYP-DATA: competing commands against DIFFERENT Accounts from the same authorization epoch cannot both pass; exactly one commits and the other observes a stale epoch. TYP-DATA: every accepted effective-access mutation increments the epoch exactly once. TYP-SEC: Account suspension revokes Sessions BEFORE success returns. TYP-SEC: OD-021 `reactivation-proof-v1` -- `ReactivateAccount` proves only the ACTOR's current MFA-satisfied Session, consults no target identity or identity-service state, accepts no Identity Validation Receipt and returns `F1-VALIDATION-400` if one is supplied; success creates NO Session and the target must complete `existing_account_sign_in` with a fresh MFA-satisfied receipt; the exact first-match failure order holds and each failure leaves the Account suspended with no side effect. TYP-SEC: OD-022 `organization-reactivation-v1` -- `ReactivateOrganization` succeeds only on a fresh `organization_reactivation` receipt bound to the target Organization with `mfa_satisfied=true`, committing `suspended -> active` with one `OrganizationReactivated` and NO Session; a wrong purpose, wrong bound Organization, `mfa_satisfied=false`, expired, consumed or altered receipt each returns its exact first-match reason and commits nothing. TYP-SEC: the two reactivation contracts are asserted DISTINCT and never substituted -- `account.reactivate` consults no target identity, `organization.reactivate` requires the bound receipt. TYP-DATA: reactivation restores only still-active unexpired explicit grants; an expired or revoked Assignment or Session stays terminal. TYP-DATA: reactivation is the sole ordinary mutation admitted while suspended. TYP-DATA: closure -- one immutable Closure Request confers no state change; a different SecurityOperator approves or rejects; at the 24-hour due instant expiry WINS over approval; pending to approved/rejected/expired and approved to executed are the only transitions. TYP-DATA: closure execution is performed only by the tenant lifecycle service, is allowed from active or suspended, rechecks expected versions, proves requester/approver separation, and atomically closes the Organization and its one current active BillingEntity. TYP-OBS: `BillingStateChanged(from_state=active,to_state=closed)` is emitted BEFORE `OrganizationClosed`, asserted on order and not merely on presence. TYP-DATA: a missing or nonactive current BillingEntity returns `billing_entity_state_invalid` and changes nothing; reserved `past_due` and `suspended` never exit through closure. TYP-DATA: an active hold at closure blocks ONLY that job's irreversible-destruction stage with `deletion_blocked_legal_hold` and does not keep customer access, the BillingEntity or the Organization open. TYP-DATA: closed is terminal, permits only retention and legal-hold job execution and immutable audit retrieval by authorized security or legal scope, and never reactivates. TYP-SEC: OD-026 last-administrator expiry block -- a timed expiry that would remove the last effective OrganizationAdmin writes exactly one immutable `RoleExpiryBlockDecision` with block reason `expiry_blocked_last_admin`, emits one `RoleExpiryBlocked` from the role-expiry lifecycle service alone, routes the mandatory security and OrganizationAdmin notification at severity `critical`, and leaves the Assignment `active` and EFFECTIVE past `expires_at_utc`. TYP-DATA: the Assignment status set never admits `expiry_blocked_last_admin`; it is a block reason on a decision record and is asserted absent from every Assignment status. TYP-DATA: re-evaluation on an unchanged authorization epoch writes no second decision and emits no second event; the guard clearing commits `active -> expired` through the existing edge; no tenant is left without an effective administrator. TYP-SEC: Legal Hold requires two different active SecurityOperators, `legal_hold.manage`, a Support Session for customer-Organization scope, and a release approver differing from the release requester; no OrganizationAdmin or lifecycle service may invent or release a hold. TYP-DATA: `LegalHoldActivated` and `LegalHoldReleased` identify exact scope and never include held payload. TYP-DATA: an active hold blocks irreversible destruction only and does not prevent access revocation, Organization closure, logical deletion or package retrieval expiry. TYP-DATA: blocked deletion remains blocked until exactly ONE hold release event queues it once; a changed policy may lengthen but never silently shorten an accrued mandatory minimum. TYP-DATA: OD-033 -- a LifecycleDeletionJob NEVER transitions `queued` directly to `completed`; every job enters `running` first; completion occurs only from `running` with immutable Deletion Evidence; a job whose frozen manifest is EMPTY or whose every member is already provably destroyed at admission STILL enters `running`, records that manifest in its Deletion Evidence, and completes from `running`. TYP-DATA: a `queued to completed` attempt is rejected and logged as an invalid transition under SM-REQ-007, changes no state, and no schema, migration, function or grant admits it. TYP-DATA: the job starts within 60 seconds of acceptance; each attempt has a 15-minute timeout and exactly one 5-minute retry, only for `deletion_dependency_unavailable`; schema, scope, digest, policy and authorization failures are nonretryable. TYP-DATA: eligible primary, index, cache and key removal completes within 30 elapsed days of acceptance; backup tombstones are written BEFORE primary completion; affected bytes age out or are cryptographically erased from backup paths within 35 elapsed days after primary completion; at due-time equality the deadline and escalation checkpoint wins. TYP-DATA: every restore applies tombstones before any product read. TYP-OBS: a missed deadline emits `LifecycleDeletionFailed` and a critical escalation and NEVER claims completion or fabricates completion evidence. TYP-DATA: completion requires the immutable Deletion Evidence in the SAME transaction as the completion event, and that evidence contains no deleted payload. TYP-DATA: `blocked -> queued` after hold release and `failed -> queued` by an authorized new replay generation are the only recovery transitions; completed never reopens. TYP-SEC: `f1_lifecycle` cannot issue arbitrary `DELETE` and reaches only `lifecycle_apply_manifest_entry`, which fails closed on a hold intersection. TYP-DATA: Invitation open-tuple duplicate -- exact creation replay returns the same Invitation; a different-key duplicate returns `invitation_duplicate_open` with the ID only while the requester remains authorized. TYP-DATA: `invitation_grant_already_active` when a same-Organization active Account already has the exact effective Assignment; `invitation_account_ineligible` when the known matching Account is suspended or revoked; neither creates an Invitation. TYP-SEC: a wrong-identity acceptance leaves the Invitation ACTIVE, binds that receipt nonce to the denied command, emits no Invitation lifecycle event, and records restricted Audit Evidence WITHOUT revealing the intended email or identity. TYP-DATA: at active expiry equality, expiry wins over acceptance and decline; declined, rejected, revoked, expired and accepted never reopen; reissue creates a new linked Invitation and repeats current authority, approval and delivery. TYP-DATA: a failed Delivery does not roll back or extend an active Invitation, and acceptance, decline, rejection, revocation and expiry do not change their terminal transition if Notification delivery fails. TYP-SEC: Support Session -- `approval_due_at_utc` is exactly 24 hours after request; the final required approval activates immediately for exactly four hours; at exactly the pending due time expiry wins over an approval; reject, expiry, revoke, stale version or replay can never extend or reactivate it; extension is a new session. TYP-SEC: cross-Organization access requires the affected OrganizationAdmin's `support.session.customer_approve` unless an active approved Emergency Access Grant authorizes it; a request asserting emergency access with no active approved Grant returns `F1-AUTH-403`, creates no Support Session and performs no side effect; `emergency_customer_access` is asserted absent from the Incident record. TYP-DATA: every command carrying a stale expected record state version or stale Organization authorization epoch is rejected with no side effect. TYP-DATA: exact replay returns the stored result and emits no duplicate transition, grant, Delivery generation or lifecycle job; an altered command under an existing idempotency identity returns `idempotency_conflict`. TYP-SEC: `OrganizationMembership` is asserted underivable-by-write -- no command creates, updates or overrides it, and every permission decision derives it from current records. WITHHELD under OD-031 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting routine retention-expiry destruction, a retention-expiry LifecycleDeletionJob, retention-expiry Deletion Evidence, a retention-expiry domain event, a retention-cursor sweep, or a retention-cursor-driven partition drop for `product_history`, `identity_commercial` or `security_audit`. TYP-OBS: the POSITIVE interim assertion that replaces them -- a record of one of those three classes reaching its 7-year maximum with no accepted deletion or closure request performs NO destruction, creates NO job, produces NO Deletion Evidence and emits NO domain event; it is retained in full subject to legal hold, recorded as Audit Evidence and operational telemetry, and raises exactly one critical compliance escalation.
- rollout: S-23 depends on S-02 for an Organization with its baseline Access Policy and BillingEntity, and on S-01 for the Account and Session records its lifecycle mutates. It gates nothing. The PRULE-019 gate it moves state behind is already live from S-02. The OD-031 limb requires owner ratification, a PM-REQ-009 controlled change to 016 STATE_MODEL.md and 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md, and a correction to the physical schema's presumed partition drop before it can be completed; it is not an implementation choice.

### MTX-039 - AC-WF-014 (WF: WF-014)

- Slice: S-19
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: UPSTREAM-V1-CREDENTIAL-ROTATION-TOKEN-009 (OD-023) -- OD-023: credential rotation begin/complete withheld; other Credential lifecycle permitted
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-014-deliver-notifications
- interface type: Event-driven dispatch plus three application commands. The dispatch path is asynchronous: a routed logical event is consumed from the durable outbox, one logical Notification and its Deliveries are created, each Delivery attempt executes under a claimed lease, and uncertainty starts read-only reconciliation. The commands are Notification Policy activation, privileged replay, and the acting principal's own inbox read. Provider event ingress is an authenticated inbound transport surface, not a product command.
- route: Not applicable - Volume I defines the commands, their permissions and their envelopes but no HTTP path, method or transport. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline. This holds for the inbox read too: OD-020 ratifies `notification.inbox.read` scoped to the acting principal's own inbox and binds it to WF-014, but a permission is not a route and Volume I fixes no path, method, pagination or projection for it. The authenticated Mailgun webhook ingress is a provider transport surface rather than a product route: Volume I fixes its authentication obligations - signature and timestamp authentication, resolution exclusively from an authenticated restricted locator carrying the correlation digest, claimed Delivery and replay generation - but names no path or method, it carries no product authorization, and failed signature or timestamp authentication is retained as restricted security telemetry and changes no state.
- request schema: Event consumption: the Logical Event Envelope plus a complete `notification_context_v1` carrying Organization ID; nullable Project ID; affected resource type/ID; initiating Account ID nullable for service work; initiating service identity nullable for human work; current status; reason code; action-reference type/ID; data classification; and the exact policy/definition versions governing visibility and outcome, with exactly one initiator form nonnull, plus the exhaustive trigger-specific required fields for its trigger class. Policy activation: expected active version, complete normalized route/template/selector content, `activation_mode=immediate`, and no client-supplied effective or expiry time. Replay: replay mode (`delivery_replay` or `empty_recipient_replay`), the source Delivery ID for `delivery_replay` or the source Notification ID for `empty_recipient_replay`, a 20-2,000 character reason, and `duplicate_delivery_risk_acknowledged` required true only when the source Delivery is `acceptance_unknown`.
- response schema: Event consumption returns one logical Notification carrying notification ID, triggering event ID and type, Organization and resource scope, template and policy versions, severity, action reference, occurred time, correlation ID, replay generation and aggregate status, with its immutable stored recipient snapshot and per-channel Deliveries. Policy activation returns the new immutable activated version with its commit-time effectiveness. Replay returns the linked new Notification generation and its single new Delivery for `delivery_replay`, or the newly resolved union's Deliveries for `empty_recipient_replay`, leaving every source record unchanged.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I; see route.
- command: `Workflows::Wf014::ConsumeNotificationEvent` (executes `notification_route_v1` from the claimed outbox route and owns the resulting Notification/Delivery writes), `Workflows::Wf014::ExecuteDeliveryAttempt` (worker path, claims the attempt and performs at most one provider submission), `Workflows::Wf014::ReconcileDelivery` (worker path, read-only), `Workflows::Wf014::ConsumeProviderEvent` (authenticated ingress), `Workflows::Wf014::ActivateNotificationPolicy`, `Workflows::Wf014::RequestNotificationReplay`, `Workflows::Wf014::ReadOwnInbox` (nonmutating). WITHHELD under OD-023: `BeginCredentialRotation` remains registered but unreachable and `CompleteCredentialRotation` has no independent entry; neither is invoked from any path here.
- command input: As request_schema. The attempt claim input is the exact tuple `(delivery_id, replay_generation, attempt_number, request_digest)`. Resolved adapter-policy artifact ID/version/content hash and Integration/Credential/material versions are NOT inputs at claim: they are null at claim and are filled monotonically as prepared-stage validation resolves them.
- command output: One logical Notification per `(event_id, policy_version, template_version, generation)`; one stored deduplicated recipient snapshot; one Delivery per `(notification_id, recipient_id, channel, replay_generation)`; one claimed attempt per `(delivery_id, generation, attempt_number)`; one reconciliation per `(delivery_id, generation, sequence)` with sequence in 0/1/2/3; and exactly one escalation per `(delivery_id, generation, kind)` or, for an empty selector union, per `(notification_id, generation, kind)`.
- actor: The Notification service identity dispatches and may invoke only the exact platform-managed integration checkpoint. Policy activation is an OrganizationAdmin holding `policy.notification.manage` for optional informational routes, templates and preferences only, or a SecurityOperator holding the same permission for security templates, support-queue roster and critical selectors only. Replay is a SecurityOperator in an approved time-bounded support session holding `notification.replay`. The inbox read is the acting principal reading only their own inbox. Owner-approval release and Integration/Credential lifecycle services hold only their named service permissions and are not reachable from the dispatch path.
- organization scope: The event Organization, taken from `notification_context_v1` and equal to the routed event's Organization; a `notification_context_v1` whose Organization or resource is inconsistent with the event fails consumption as `notification_event_invalid`. Selector components resolve active Accounts in the event Organization and resource scope only. A SecurityOperator support-queue component additionally requires an active roster entry in the Notification Policy.
- aggregate: Notification as the logical root with its immutable recipient snapshot; Delivery as the per-recipient-per-channel root; DeliveryAttempt and DeliveryReconciliation as immutable checkpoint records under the Delivery; NotificationPolicy as an immutable versioned Policy Artifact; the escalation and the replay acknowledgement as immutable decision records. Integration and Credential are separate roots owned by `integration-interim-v1` and are read and validated at the checkpoint, never mutated by a Delivery.
- aggregate boundary: Event consumption commits Notification, its recipient snapshot and all its Deliveries in one transaction, because the route table, selector union and per-channel Delivery set are all derived from one event consumption and a partially created Notification would let a Delivery exist with no aggregate to recompute. The attempt claim is a separate transaction on the Delivery root and MUST precede Integration/Credential validation - that ordering is the whole point of the claim and is not an implementation preference. Each attempt result, each reconciliation result and each aggregate recomputation commits separately. Escalation is its own immutable decision record so that a failed escalation write can never silently vanish into a Delivery update. This is a bounded multi-root specialisation of the EM-III-011 default, required by WF-014 rather than chosen.
- value objects: NotificationState, DeliveryState, DispatchCheckpoint (`prepared`, `submission_started`, `recorded`), Severity (`informational`, `warning`, `critical`), ReplayGeneration, AttemptNumber, RequestDigest, ApplicationDeduplicationKeyDigest, ProviderCorrelationTokenDigest, AdapterPolicyIdentity fixed to `mailgun-email-v1`, AdapterCodeVersion, IntegrationVersion, CredentialVersion, MaterialVersion, ProviderMessageId, ProviderEventId, TransportCertainty, RetryClassification, ReconciliationSequence (0, 1, 2, 3), ReconciliationOutcome (`matched`, `none`, `ambiguous`, `dependency_error`, `authentication_failed`), ReplayMode (`delivery_replay`, `empty_recipient_replay`), ClassificationCeiling, ReasonCode, NotificationContextV1. WITHHELD under OD-032: no canonical identifier namespace is asserted for Notification or Delivery.
- domain service: Not applicable - WF-014 names no domain service. The selector union, the provider status mapping, the authenticated-event mapping, the `terminal_failed > delivered > accepted` reduction and the aggregate derivation are pure functions applied by the application; the Mailgun adapter and the secret store are infrastructure adapters invoked from the claimed attempt.
- repository: `NotificationRepository`, `NotificationRecipientRepository`, `DeliveryRepository`, `DeliveryAttemptRepository`, `DeliveryReconciliationRepository`, `NotificationEscalationRepository`, `NotificationReplayAcknowledgementRepository`, `NotificationPolicyRepository`, `OutboxMessageRepository`; `IntegrationRepository` and `CredentialRepository` read-and-validate only at the checkpoint; `SupportSessionRepository` read-only; `AccountRepository` and the identity service's current verified delivery address, read-only at each reauthorization.
- persistence model: Canonical `notifications`, `notification_recipients`, `deliveries`, `delivery_attempts`, `delivery_reconciliations`, `notification_escalations`, `notification_replay_acknowledgements` and `outbox_messages` per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. `notifications` carries `state CHECK ('pending','uncertain','sent','partial','failed','suppressed')` and unique `(event_id, policy_version, template_version, generation)`. `deliveries` carries `state CHECK ('pending','attempting','accepted','delivered','retry_scheduled','acceptance_unknown','terminal_failed','suppressed')` and unique `(notification_id, recipient_id, channel, replay_generation)`, with Integration/Credential versions always NULL for in-app and nullable for email until individually resolved by an attempt. `delivery_attempts` carries `dispatch_checkpoint CHECK ('prepared','submission_started','recorded')` whose order permits `prepared -> recorded` only for a proven no-submission outcome and otherwise only `prepared -> submission_started -> recorded`. `credentials` carries the frozen `state CHECK ('pending','active','rotating','revoked','expired')` and conceptual pending-material pointer with no secret plaintext and no rotation-token column: the frozen states preserve the conceptual model, but WITHHELD under OD-023 no baseline DDL function or granted path may set pending material, enter `rotating`, or accept replacement material while `UPSTREAM-V1-CREDENTIAL-ROTATION-TOKEN-009` is unresolved. F1 records never contain provider secret bytes, decrypted material, or a value from which the secret can be recovered; secret bytes exist in process memory only for the provider attempt and are zeroized immediately after use.
- migration: Not applicable - the canonical schema owns the product tables and this row adds no schema object of its own. WITHHELD under OD-023 and MUST NOT be migrated: any rotation-token column on `credentials`, any DDL function or grant that sets pending material or enters `rotating`, and any `credential_rotation_retry` scheduled-action branch, row or grant. Volume I's `integration-interim-v1` names a `rotation token` field on the Credential while schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md states no rotation-token column; OD-023's own Data Implications records that contradiction and requires the two to be reconciled on correction, so this row adds neither and awaits the controlled change.
- transaction boundary: Event consumption: one transaction creating the Notification, its recipient snapshot and every Delivery, after resolving the union at consumption time. Attempt claim: one transaction atomically claiming the unique `(delivery_id, replay_generation, attempt_number, request_digest)`, persisting the application deduplication and correlation evidence, the required logical adapter-policy identity `mailgun-email-v1`, the executing adapter code version and `dispatch_checkpoint=prepared` - this commits BEFORE Integration/Credential validation and before any provider call. Prepared-stage validation runs inside the claimed attempt and fills the resolved identifiers monotonically; `submission_started` requires the complete valid set and is advanced in its own durable commit before any network write. No external call sits inside a database transaction: the Mailgun submission and the reconciliation query are outside every transaction and only their recorded outcomes commit. Policy activation: one transaction that atomically assigns commit-time effectiveness, activates and supersedes under the Policy Artifact contract. Replay: one transaction creating the linked generation and its acknowledgement record, leaving every source record immutable.
- concurrency: The attempt claim is the concurrency control for submission: while the checkpoint is `prepared`, exactly one serialized executor may validate Integration/Credential state, record a proven no-submission outcome, or durably advance to `submission_started` and perform that attempt's first provider submission. Owner or process loss while still `prepared` permits another executor to resume that same not-yet-submitted attempt, which is why resume is safe - nothing has reached the network. At or after `submission_started`, no executor may submit that attempt again, and a crash or incomplete result after that checkpoint is treated as possible provider acceptance rather than as a retry opportunity. Duplicate event consumption, job redelivery, command replay and concurrency reuse that attempt identity rather than create another claim. The outbox route holds a 30-second claim lease. The provider concurrency gate is a separate 4-slot lease acquired immediately before `submission_started` for send or `request_started_at` for reconciliation, with send contenders in exact FIFO `(due_at, delivery_id, attempt_number)` order and a 15-second lease with a five-second heartbeat that stops at the outbound-request checkpoint. A RetryScheduled transition caused by a prior-attempt event atomically cancels its timer, so an authenticated late event and a due retry cannot both fire.
- idempotency: Notification creation is idempotent by unique `(event_id, policy_version, template_version, generation)` - the same event consumed twice yields the same Notification rather than a second one. Delivery creation is idempotent by unique `(notification_id, recipient_id, channel, replay_generation)`. Attempt submission is idempotent at the APPLICATION level only, by the atomic claim of `(delivery_id, replay_generation, attempt_number, request_digest)` plus the `submission_started` checkpoint: one claim receives exactly one first submission and no executor re-enters it after `submission_started`. This is explicitly NOT provider-side idempotency. Mailgun supplies none, so the application deduplication key and the Mailgun user-variable correlation token are evidence lookup and local deduplication mechanisms only and cannot guarantee one external delivery; external delivery may be zero, one, or more than one and no exactly-once claim is permitted. Duplicate tolerance is instead carried by the product action: the template retains one logical action reference whose invitation, expiry, authorization, command replay and underlying product-transition rules remain independently idempotent, so a duplicate email duplicates no authority and no product action. Provider event IDs are deduplicated: for one provider event ID only the first authenticated normalized content applies, later byte-equivalent duplicates are ignored, and different normalized content under that ID makes the reconciliation set `ambiguous`.
- background job: `event_consume` executes `notification_route_v1` and claims its `outbox_messages` route row; there is no separate `notification_route` work type. `delivery_execute` claims its `delivery_attempts` row. `delivery_reconcile` claims its `delivery_reconciliations` row. `provider_event_consume` claims a `work_executions` row for authenticated Mailgun event ingress. WITHHELD under OD-023: no `credential_rotation_retry` scheduled action exists, and no branch, row or grant for it may be added while `UPSTREAM-V1-CREDENTIAL-ROTATION-TOKEN-009` is unresolved.
- queue: The `delivery` queue carries the outbox route, per the `outbox_messages` fixed `queue_name text CHECK (queue_name='delivery')`. Queue identity and worker topology are owned by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md; this row does not rename them.
- retry policy: Only definitive nonacceptance retries, and it retries on a fixed schedule: the initial attempt plus exactly three retries at 1, 5 and 30 minutes after completion of the preceding failed attempt, exhausting at four definitively nonaccepted attempts. Definitive nonacceptance is exhaustively three things: a complete synchronous `429`; Integration initialization dependency or timeout exhaustion after its one-second internal retry; and a transport result that proves no network submission began. `Retry-After` replaces a `429` retry's delay only when its raw value is an unsigned base-10 HTTP delta-seconds integer from `60` through `1800` inclusive, and the delay is then exactly that many elapsed seconds; HTTP-date, signed, fractional, whitespace-containing, malformed and out-of-range values are ignored. Every implicit transport and client retry inside the Mailgun adapter is disabled, so the adapter performs exactly one network submission operation per attempt. `integration-interim-v1` initialization has its own bounded retry - exactly one retry one second later, only for `adapter_initialization_timeout` or `adapter_dependency_unavailable`, under the same attempt generation - and that retry completes INSIDE the claimed attempt and never schedules a second Delivery timer; WF-014 remains the sole owner of Delivery retry timing. Uncertainty always wins over a retry classification: any result for which acceptance cannot be disproved is `acceptance_unknown` and receives no automatic resend at all.
- terminal failure: `terminal_failed` and `suppressed` are absorbing. Terminal on the prepared stage: invalid or missing policy or configuration, a disconnected or retired Integration, or an unavailable, invalid, revoked or expired Credential completes the already-claimed attempt as `terminal_failed` with the exact `integration-interim-v1` reason and makes no Mailgun call. No other prepared-stage failure is retryable. Terminal before any attempt claim: an email Delivery with no current verified delivery address transitions directly to `terminal_failed` with `recipient_address_unavailable`, makes no Mailgun call and claims no attempt. Terminal on the provider result: a complete `400`, `401`, `403`, `404` or other non-`408`/non-`429` `4xx`. Terminal on exhaustion: four definitively nonaccepted attempts give `terminal_failed / provider_retry_exhausted`. Terminal on authenticated evidence: `failed` with permanent severity, `rejected`, `bounced`, `complained` or `unsubscribed`, which has precedence and can change retry-scheduled, acceptance-unknown, accepted or delivered to terminal-failed exactly once. Absence of evidence never proves nonacceptance or terminal failure, and `acceptance_unknown` has no age-based inference: it is neither success nor terminal failure and is never converted to either without exact authenticated provider evidence.
- reconciliation: On entering `acceptance_unknown`, read-only reconciliation starts immediately and repeats at exactly 1, 5 and 30 minutes later, giving sequences 0, 1, 2 and 3. It consumes authenticated Mailgun webhooks and events and queries provider event history by the exact correlation token, recipient and replay generation, and it never submits a message. Before state reduction every token-matched record MUST carry the same exact recipient and replay generation and MUST bind unambiguously to this Delivery; a missing or conflicting tuple, one token bound to another recipient, generation or Delivery, or one provider event ID with different normalized content is exactly `ambiguous`. Otherwise one or more exact decisive events reduce by the fixed precedence `terminal_failed > delivered > accepted`, apply that state, and emit one `NotificationDeliveryReconciled` with the contributing event IDs and the selected precedence. The four nonresolving outcomes are exact: zero decisive exact events is `none`, provider query timeout or dependency failure is `dependency_error`, failed event or query authentication is `authentication_failed`, and the tuple violation above is `ambiguous`; each retains `acceptance_unknown`, records the exact result and sends nothing. After the final scheduled query, later exact authenticated provider evidence remains authoritative. A temporary Mailgun delivery failure maps to `accepted`, because Mailgun owns that message's provider-side retry and F1 MUST NOT resend it. Unknown authenticated event types are retained as `provider_event_ignored` and change no state.
- domain events: The seventeen WF-014 events: `NotificationPolicyActivated`, `NotificationCreated`, `NotificationDeliveryAttempted`, `NotificationDeliveryAccepted`, `NotificationDelivered`, `NotificationRetryScheduled`, `NotificationDeliveryAcceptanceUnknown`, `NotificationDeliveryReconciled`, `NotificationDeliverySuppressed`, `NotificationDeliveryFailed`, `NotificationUncertain`, `NotificationSent`, `NotificationPartial`, `NotificationFailed`, `NotificationSuppressed`, `NotificationEscalated`, `NotificationReplayRequested`. Plus the applicable `integration-interim-v1` Integration/Credential events reachable from the checkpoint: `IntegrationProposed`, `IntegrationConnected`, `IntegrationDegraded`, `IntegrationDisconnected`, `IntegrationRetired`, `CredentialProvisioned`, `CredentialActivated`, `CredentialRevoked`, `CredentialExpired`. `NotificationDeliveryAttempted` is emitted only from the terminal `recorded` checkpoint and its physical event field is exactly `recorded`, never an invented `provider_responded` checkpoint. WITHHELD under OD-023: `CredentialRotationStarted` and `CredentialRotated` are canonical Volume I names but no emission, outbox row or Notification route for either exists while the rotation limb is withheld.
- event payload: The canonical envelope with the real `organization_id` and the routed event's Project and lineage values, plus the Notification, Delivery, recipient, channel, replay generation and attempt identity; the policy, template, adapter, Integration, Credential and material versions; the dispatch checkpoint; the reason code; the aggregate outcome; and the provider message and event IDs when known. Prohibited from every payload: raw Evidence payloads, secrets, verification tokens, provider credentials, unrestricted internal errors, message bodies, the correlation-token plaintext and any value from which the secret can be recovered. Reconciliation events additionally carry the contributing event IDs and the selected precedence.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf014` is the sole producer of all seventeen notification events. The Integration and Credential events are produced by the integration and credential lifecycle services under `integration-interim-v1` and are consumed here rather than produced here. Authenticated Mailgun webhook ingress, provider event IDs, provider status text, transport certainty, `delivery` queue depth, the provider concurrency gate, worker leases, adapter timing and end-to-end latency are technical telemetry and MUST NOT be emitted as domain events.
- event consumers: The outbox route consumer is exactly `notification_route_v1`, which is the required consumer this workflow executes; the event transaction creates that route only when the exact event type and major are active in the effective Notification Policy and complete `notification_context_v1` is present, and otherwise creates no row. WF-014 itself names no downstream consumer of its own seventeen events. The inbox read surface consumes Notification state under `notification.inbox.read`.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined by Volume I; see route.
- authorization entry point: The application command or the event-consumption handler, before mutation, and AGAIN before every single attempt. Reauthorization per attempt is mandatory and is not an optimisation: before each attempt the platform re-evaluates the route permission, Organization and resource scope, Account status and current verified delivery address, and later loss of authorization becomes `suppressed`, not sent.
