An authorization decision made at event consumption is never trusted at attempt time. For the direct Invitation route the equivalent per-attempt recheck is that the Invitation remains active, the target digest is unchanged and server time is strictly before expiry. Replay reaches its entry point only through an active support session; the SecurityOperator role alone grants nothing.
- permission checks: Route Required Permissions, each verified present in the Permission Baseline and applied at event consumption and again at every attempt: `invitation.create` for Invitation lifecycle notices to Accounts; `source.scope.propose` for `SourceVerified`; `source.verify` for `SourceVerificationExpired`; `history.read` for `CrawlCompleted` with partial coverage, `CrawlFailed` and `CrawlLimitReached`; `score.summary.read` for `ScoreSnapshotPromoted` with partial status and `ScoreCalculationUnavailable`; `issue.read` for `IssueAdjudicated` and `IssueDisputeWithdrawn`; `issue.adjudicate` for `IssueAdjudicationOverdue`, `IssueAdjudicationReminder` and `IssueAdjudicationCritical`; `entitlement.notice.read` for `EntitlementWarningIssued` and `EntitlementViolationDetected`; `export.retrieve` for `ExportAvailable` and every transition to `ExportFailed`; `security.notice.read` for `SecurityIncidentCustomerActionRequired`. The `InvitationActivated` route uses the matching invitation-bound email binding rather than a permission, and a same-Organization Account when one already exists. Management: `policy.notification.manage`, allow for an OrganizationAdmin for optional informational routes, templates and preferences only, and for a SecurityOperator for security templates, support-queue roster and critical selectors only; it grants neither the other's scope. `notification.replay` is deny for every role except SecurityOperator support-session only. `notification.inbox.read` is allow for own inbox for every human role and deny for service identities. The Notification service identity may invoke only the exact platform-managed integration checkpoint and holds no policy, replay or lifecycle authority. WITHHELD under OD-023: no `credential.rotation_token.issue` permission exists and every mint request is denied as an undefined permission under the Permission Baseline; `credential.rotate` remains a canonical baseline token (SecurityOperator protected explicit grant plus Support Session) but is unreachable because the mandatory fresh rotation token has no defined issuer, format, lifetime, consumption point, binding or transport.
- tenant boundary: Every Notification belongs to the event Organization and every selector component resolves only active Accounts within that Organization and resource scope. The stored recipient snapshot is immutable, so a later roster or authorization change affects reauthorization and suppression but never adds a recipient. Data classification is bounded by the template and recipient ceiling: a classification above that ceiling fails consumption as `notification_event_invalid` rather than delivering. The Mailgun webhook context derives Organization, Integration, Delivery and attempt exclusively from an authenticated restricted locator and trusts no caller-supplied scope, so a provider event cannot name its own tenant. A support session for one Organization authorizes replay in no other.
- error contract: Consumption: a missing trigger-specific field, inconsistent Organization or resource, unknown major schema, or classification above the template or recipient ceiling is `notification_event_invalid`, creates no Notification and no Delivery, and creates one operational escalation within five minutes. Recipient resolution: an empty final authorized union for a mandatory route makes the Notification `failed` with `no_authorized_recipient`, creates no Delivery and creates exactly one operational escalation within 5 minutes, which exists even if no human is then assigned. Pre-attempt: `recipient_address_unavailable` with no Mailgun call and no attempt claim; `informational_email_opt_out` as `suppressed`, which the direct Invitation email may never use. Prepared stage, terminal with the exact `integration-interim-v1` reason: `integration_policy_unavailable`, `credential_unavailable`, `adapter_configuration_invalid`, `operator_disconnected`, `policy_retired`, `credential_material_invalid`, `credential_revoked`, `credential_expired`. Prepared stage, definitively nonaccepted: `adapter_initialization_timeout` and `adapter_dependency_unavailable` after the one-second internal retry, plus a proven no-network-submission transport result. Provider: `429` is definitive nonacceptance; non-`408`/non-`429` `4xx` is terminal; four definitively nonaccepted attempts give `provider_retry_exhausted`. Uncertainty: `provider_acceptance_unknown` classifies as `F1-DEPENDENCY-503`, severity `error`, `retryable=false` for automatic dispatch, recovery `await_provider_reconciliation_or_submit_acknowledged_replay`, and MUST NOT be converted to success, terminal failure, timeout retry or rate-limit retry. Replay: `F1-DOMAIN-409 / duplicate_delivery_risk_unacknowledged`, severity `warning`, `retryable=false`, recovery `acknowledge_duplicate_delivery_risk_and_submit_new_replay_command`, changing nothing and changing no replay generation; `F1-DOMAIN-409 / notification_replay_ineligible` for an unknown mode, wrong source state or source/target mismatch, changing nothing. Authorization denial returns `F1-AUTH-403`, emits one authorization audit event, and performs no state change and no provider call. Integration/Credential command classification: `F1-DOMAIN-409` for stale state and replay conflict, `F1-DATA-409` for invalid policy, configuration or material, `F1-AUTH-403` for missing authority, `F1-DEPENDENCY-503` for named dependency failure, `F1-TIMEOUT-504` for named deadline failure. A provider-visible duplicate after acknowledged replay or provider behaviour is not an error when the local attempt claim and the underlying product action remain unique.
- audit record: Per WF-014 Audit and Observability: the triggering event; recipient-resolution and authorization decisions; redacted field codes; channel; Delivery generation and attempt; the local attempt claim, deduplication and request digests; the `prepared` and `submission_started` checkpoints; Integration, Credential, material and policy versions; the initialization generation, deadline and result; the exact retry and reconciliation scheduled AND actual times; provider timeout and response certainty; provider and adapter versions; the provider message and event ID when known; the provider-status and reconciliation mapping; uncertainty age; reason code; aggregate outcome; escalation ID; the administrative replay acknowledgment; and end-to-end latency. Never recorded: secret material, message bodies, or the raw correlation token. The escalation and replay acknowledgement are themselves immutable decision records carrying `decision_type`, `decision_value`, `decision_status`, the exact nonempty sorted-unique governing `definition_versions`, `input_sha256` and `output_sha256`; the replay row additionally carries the nonblank human rationale, which remains Audit Evidence and is never copied into the machine or Event reason.
- observability: Per CAP-021 Observability Requirements: Integration and Credential versions, local deduplication hits, dispatch checkpoints, definitive retries, the acceptance-unknown count AND age, reconciliation outcomes, acknowledged replays, and provider and aggregate success, failure and uncertainty telemetry, never secret material. Uncertainty age is the load-bearing signal here precisely because `acceptance_unknown` has no age-based inference in the product: age is observable and escalatable but never resolves the state. Escalation latency is directly observable against the 5-minute bound. Queue depth, the provider concurrency gate, worker leases and adapter timing are technical telemetry and are asserted absent from the domain event stream.
- retention: Notification, Delivery, DeliveryAttempt and reconciliation records fall in the `product_history` retention class at 7 years, computed as 7 elapsed years after record creation or terminal transition, whichever is later, with irreversible destruction after reference-integrity proof. The window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states none of its own. Secret material is never persisted at all, so it has no retention: secret bytes exist in process memory only for the provider attempt and are zeroized immediately after use.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: every baseline route's Required Permission is enforced at event consumption and again at every attempt - `invitation.create`, `source.scope.propose`, `source.verify`, `history.read`, `score.summary.read`, `issue.read`, `issue.adjudicate`, `entitlement.notice.read`, `export.retrieve`, `security.notice.read` each denies a recipient lacking it. TYP-SEC: authorization held at consumption and lost before the attempt yields `suppressed`, not sent, and no Mailgun call. TYP-SEC: `policy.notification.manage` held by an OrganizationAdmin cannot change security templates, the support-queue roster or critical selectors; held by a SecurityOperator it cannot change optional informational routes, templates or preferences. TYP-SEC: `policy.notification.manage` cannot remove a baseline warning or critical route, remove a channel, or broaden field visibility - each changes nothing and emits exactly one denial audit event. TYP-SEC: a stale expected active version, incomplete content, a client-supplied effective or expiry time, `activation_mode` other than `immediate`, or a future-effective or expiring version each changes nothing and emits one denial audit event. TYP-DATA: a valid activation atomically assigns commit-time effectiveness, activates, supersedes and emits exactly one `NotificationPolicyActivated`. TYP-SEC: `notification.replay` is deny for OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator, ExecutiveBuyer and every service identity, and allow for SecurityOperator only inside an active support session. TYP-SEC: `notification.inbox.read` reads only the acting principal's own inbox and is deny for service identities; it confers no write authority and is not implied by any companion mutation permission. TYP-SEC: the Notification service identity may invoke only the exact platform-managed integration checkpoint and holds no policy, replay or lifecycle authority. TYP-DATA: `notification_context_v1` validation - a missing trigger-specific field, inconsistent Organization or resource, unknown major schema, or classification above the template or recipient ceiling is `notification_event_invalid`, creates no Notification and no Delivery, and creates one operational escalation within five minutes. TYP-DATA: exactly one initiator form is nonnull - both null and both nonnull each fail consumption. TYP-DATA: the consumer never guesses a recipient, status, scope or action from a free-text payload. TYP-DATA: the direct Invitation exception - `InvitationActivated` creates one mandatory Mailgun email Delivery to the exact normalized target email, and one in-app Delivery only when the Invitation is already bound to an active same-Organization Account. TYP-SEC: the Invitation email template contains only the opaque acceptance reference, Organization display name, inviter display name, offered role/mode/persona and normalized scope summary, and exact expiry - no protected-permission internals, approval identities, security diagnostics or other tenant data. TYP-DATA: the direct Invitation email cannot be opted out, while informational email to Accounts can and then becomes `suppressed` with `informational_email_opt_out`. TYP-DATA: before each Invitation attempt the service rechecks that the Invitation remains active, the target digest is unchanged and server time is strictly before expiry; a terminal or expired Invitation suppresses an unstarted attempt without Mailgun. TYP-DATA: a terminal Invitation email failure does not roll back or extend the Invitation and escalates under the normal five-minute rule. TYP-DATA: selector union - semicolon-separated components resolve active Accounts holding the route's Required Permission at consumption, union and deduplicate by Account ID into one stored snapshot, and a zero-result component is not independently a failure when another component supplies an authorized recipient. TYP-SEC: a SecurityOperator support-queue component additionally requires an active roster entry; later roster changes affect only reauthorization and never add a recipient. TYP-DATA: each Account recipient receives one Delivery per required channel. TYP-DATA: an empty final authorized union for a mandatory route makes the Notification `failed` with `no_authorized_recipient`, creates no Delivery, and creates exactly one escalation within 5 minutes that exists even with no human assigned. TYP-DATA: an email Delivery with no current verified delivery address goes directly to `terminal_failed` with `recipient_address_unavailable`, makes no Mailgun call, claims no attempt, and follows the required-delivery escalation rule. TYP-DATA: the attempt claim precedes Integration/Credential validation - assert that no Credential read occurs before a durable `prepared` row exists. TYP-DATA: the claim is atomic on `(delivery_id, replay_generation, attempt_number, request_digest)` and persists the application deduplication key, provider-correlation-token digest, the required logical adapter-policy identity `mailgun-email-v1`, the executing adapter code version and `dispatch_checkpoint=prepared`. TYP-DATA: resolved adapter-policy artifact ID/version/content hash and Integration/Credential/material versions are null at claim, fill monotonically, and are ALL nonnull before `submission_started`; failed validation retains every safely resolved identifier and digest and leaves each unresolved field null. TYP-INT: duplicate event consumption, job redelivery, command replay and concurrency each reuse the attempt identity rather than create a second claim. TYP-INT: one prepared claim receives exactly one first submission under N concurrent executors. TYP-INT: owner or process loss while still `prepared` permits another executor to resume that same not-yet-submitted attempt; the resumed attempt still submits exactly once. TYP-INT: at or after `submission_started` no executor may submit that attempt again - a redelivered job observing `submission_started` performs no second application submission. TYP-DATA: `NotificationDeliveryAttempted` is emitted only from the terminal `recorded` checkpoint and its physical checkpoint field is exactly `recorded`; no `provider_responded` checkpoint exists. TYP-INT: synchronous provider mapping is exhaustive - complete `2xx` to `accepted`; complete `429` to `retry_scheduled` as definitive nonacceptance; complete `400`, `401`, `403`, `404` and every other non-`408`/non-`429` `4xx` to `terminal_failed`; timeout, `408`, every `5xx`, malformed or partial response, connection loss and process failure after `submission_started` to `acceptance_unknown`. TYP-INT: the provider attempt timeout is exactly 10 seconds. TYP-INT: uncertainty always wins over a retry classification - a `5xx` after `submission_started` is `acceptance_unknown`, never a retry. TYP-INT: the `integration-interim-v1` initialization deadline is 10 seconds with equality to timeout, only `adapter_initialization_timeout` or `adapter_dependency_unavailable` receives one retry exactly one second later under the same attempt generation, and that retry completes inside the claimed attempt and schedules no second Delivery timer. TYP-DATA: a connected or degraded Integration with an active unexpired Credential may reach a provider checkpoint; disconnected or retired may not. TYP-DATA: at `now_utc >= expires_at_utc` Credential expiry wins BEFORE a provider checkpoint and no new call uses that Credential. TYP-DATA: retry timing is exact - the initial attempt plus three retries at 1, 5 and 30 minutes after completion of the preceding failed attempt, and four definitively nonaccepted attempts exhaust to `terminal_failed / provider_retry_exhausted`. TYP-DATA: `Retry-After` replaces a `429` delay only for an unsigned base-10 delta-seconds integer from `60` through `1800` inclusive, asserted at `59`, `60`, `1800` and `1801`; HTTP-date, signed, fractional, whitespace-containing and malformed values are ignored. TYP-DATA: `acceptance_unknown` suppresses every automatic resend for that Delivery generation and has no age-based inference - a Delivery unknown for 24 hours is still neither success nor terminal failure. TYP-DATA: authenticated event mapping is exhaustive - `accepted` or `stored` to `accepted`; `delivered` to `delivered`; `failed` with permanent severity, `rejected`, `bounced`, `complained` and `unsubscribed` to `terminal_failed`; a TEMPORARY delivery failure to `accepted` because Mailgun owns that message's provider-side retry and F1 MUST NOT resend it; unknown authenticated types retained as `provider_event_ignored` changing no state. TYP-SEC: failed signature or timestamp authentication is retained as restricted security telemetry and changes no state. TYP-DATA: for one provider event ID only the first authenticated normalized content applies; later byte-equivalent duplicates are ignored; different normalized content under that ID makes the set `ambiguous`. TYP-DATA: reduction precedence `terminal_failed > delivered > accepted` - accepted-then-delivered resolves `delivered`; accepted or delivered then permanent failure resolves `terminal_failed`; multiple lifecycle events are NOT ambiguous merely because their mapped states differ. TYP-DATA: duplicate or out-of-order acceptance or delivery events cannot regress a state; permanent failure can change retry-scheduled, acceptance-unknown, accepted or delivered to terminal-failed exactly once. TYP-DATA: an authenticated event for any prior provider attempt is authoritative while the Delivery is `retry_scheduled` or `acceptance_unknown`, and a RetryScheduled transition caused by a prior-attempt event atomically cancels its timer. TYP-DATA: reconciliation runs immediately and at exactly 1, 5 and 30 minutes, sequences 0/1/2/3, and NEVER submits a message. TYP-DATA: tuple binding - a missing or conflicting recipient/generation tuple, one token bound to another recipient, generation or Delivery, or one event ID with different normalized content each yields exactly `ambiguous`. TYP-DATA: a matched reduction emits one `NotificationDeliveryReconciled` carrying the contributing event IDs and the selected precedence. TYP-DATA: after the final scheduled query, later exact authenticated evidence remains authoritative; absence of evidence never proves nonacceptance or terminal failure. TYP-DATA: `accepted` and `delivered` are success-stable - no callback deadline and no F1 resend applies, and accepted may remain accepted indefinitely. TYP-DATA: in-app becomes `delivered` when the authorized recipient record is durably created. TYP-DATA: suppressed Deliveries count as neither success nor failure. TYP-DATA: Notification transitions - Pending to Uncertain, Sent, Partial, Failed or Suppressed; Uncertain to Sent, Partial or Failed only when exact provider evidence resolves EVERY unknown required Delivery; Pending to Failed with `no_authorized_recipient` valid for a mandatory empty union; a later permanent Mailgun result may move Sent to Partial or Failed, or Partial to Failed; no other aggregate regression is valid. TYP-DATA: the alternate path - one channel succeeds while another retries, becomes acceptance-unknown or terminally fails; the generation is `uncertain` while any required Delivery is unknown and becomes `partial` only after every uncertainty resolves and at least one required Delivery succeeds while another terminally fails. TYP-DATA: no unapproved third or delayed channel is used. TYP-OBS: every terminally failed required Delivery and every Delivery entering `acceptance_unknown` creates exactly ONE escalation within 5 minutes in the SecurityOperator support queue carrying the Notification, recipient, channel, reason, attempts, uncertainty status when applicable and correlation ID. TYP-OBS: a later permanent Mailgun failure recomputes a known aggregate and escalates exactly once - not twice. TYP-DATA: user-visible uncertain status says provider acceptance could not be confirmed and the email may still arrive. TYP-DATA: `delivery_replay` re-resolves current route inclusion, recipient authorization, verified address or Invitation binding and state, policy and template for only that target; ineligibility suppresses the new Delivery without Mailgun. TYP-DATA: a known terminal-failure replay needs no extra acknowledgment. TYP-DATA: `empty_recipient_replay` is permitted ONLY for a Notification failed with `no_authorized_recipient`, targets no source Delivery, resolves the complete then-current selector union, and needs no duplicate-risk acknowledgment because the source generation made no provider call; repaired roster or authorization recovers the source empty-union failure and removed or ineligible recipients receive nothing. TYP-DATA: an unknown mode, wrong source state or source/target mismatch returns `F1-DOMAIN-409 / notification_replay_ineligible` and changes nothing. TYP-DATA: every replay leaves source recipients, attempts, state and aggregate immutable, creates a linked NEW generation rather than reopening the old one, and emits `NotificationReplayRequested` with mode and source identities. TYP-SEC: NO EXACTLY-ONCE CLAIM - assert that no code path, event, status, audit field or user-visible string asserts exactly-once external delivery; external delivery may be zero, one or more than one. TYP-DATA: duplicate tolerance - a duplicate external email duplicates no authority and no product action, asserted by replaying the template's logical action reference twice and observing one product transition. TYP-SEC: no secret material, message body or raw correlation token appears in any event, error, audit record, Evidence or export; only Credential and material version identifiers appear. TYP-SEC: secret bytes exist in process memory only for the provider attempt and are zeroized immediately after use. TYP-SEC: the Mailgun webhook context derives Organization, Integration, Delivery and attempt exclusively from an authenticated restricted locator and trusts no caller-supplied scope. TYP-SEC: a support session for one Organization authorizes replay in no other. TYP-OBS: uncertainty age and the acceptance-unknown count are observable, and age never resolves the state. TYP-OBS: queue depth, the provider concurrency gate, worker leases and adapter timing are technical telemetry and are asserted ABSENT from the domain event stream; an authenticated Mailgun webhook is asserted never to appear as a domain event by passthrough. TYP-E2E: a `CrawlFailed` event routes at severity `warning` to the run initiator, Project MarketingOperator accounts and OrganizationAdmin accounts holding `history.read`, creating one in-app and one email Delivery per recipient, and reaches `sent` on complete `2xx` responses. TYP-E2E: `SecurityIncidentCustomerActionRequired` routes at severity `critical` within the event's disclosure scope and cannot be opted out. WITHHELD under OD-023 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting rotation initiation or completion, a rotation token issuer, permission, format, TTL, consumption point or binding, a `credential.rotation_token.issue` permission, a reachable `BeginCredentialRotation` or any `CompleteCredentialRotation` entry, a path that sets pending material or enters `rotating` or accepts replacement material, a `CredentialRotationStarted` or `CredentialRotated` emission or outbox or Notification route, a `credential_rotation_retry` scheduled action, or a rotation DDL function or grant. Positively asserted instead: a mint request is denied as an undefined permission; `rotating` and the pending-material pointer are reachable by no granted path; and Credential revocation, expiry and Integration isolation each remain fully available and each correctly terminalize a claimed prepared attempt with the exact `integration-interim-v1` reason and no Mailgun call.
- rollout: S-19 consumes routed events produced by every event-generating slice and depends on S-00 for the outbox and Sidekiq Job Catalogue and on `integration-interim-v1` for the platform-managed Mailgun Integration and Credential. CAP-021 Dependencies are WF-006, WF-014 and WF-017. The direct Invitation limb is invoked from WF-013, owned by S-01/S-23, which calls this contract's delivery path rather than reimplementing it. Delivery behaviour ships complete under `credential-rotation-blocked-interim-v1`; production storage of a real Mailgun provider secret is blocked by OD-023's Blocking Impact because SEC-REQ-016 requires a defined per-credential-class rotation and maximum age policy that the interim does not supply, so prelaunch operation uses synthetic secrets. Completing the rotation limb requires OD-023 ratification and a controlled Volume I change reconciling `integration-interim-v1`'s `rotation token` field with schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md's no-rotation-token-column statement; it is not an implementation choice.

### MTX-040 - AC-WF-015 (WF: WF-015)

- Slice: S-22
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-015-enforce-entitlements
- interface type: Two unrelated surfaces under one workflow. (1) Enforcement: server-side decision checkpoints with no actor-facing interface -- a low-cost read about to reach its durable response checkpoint, or an immediate or queued high-cost operation about to execute its first protected side effect. (2) Policy management: a synchronous authenticated command activating an immutable Entitlement Policy version. The two share no permission and no actor; WF-015's Authorization is explicit that only the server-side entitlement service identity makes decisions.
- route: Split by limb. Policy management HAS a route: API_CONTRACTS.md registers `POST /api/v1/organizations/:organization_id/entitlement-policies` -> `ActivateEntitlementPolicy`, permission `policy.entitlement.manage`, policy-conditional `If-Match`, `201`, attributes `ATTR-ActivateEntitlementPolicy` (`candidate: object<EntitlementPolicyCandidate>`, `plan_approval_id: uuid`). Enforcement has none and must not acquire one - `DecideEntitlement`, `ReserveEntitlement`, `HeartbeatEntitlement`, `CommitEntitlement`, `ReleaseEntitlement` and `RecordLowCostUsage` are internal checkpoints of the operation being gated, and an inbound endpoint that let a client assert a Decision would be exactly the client-side trust WF-015's Security Notes prohibit. The five low-cost read GET paths remain listed in API_CONTRACTS.md as blocked mappings returning transport `404`: their metering semantics are ratified under OD-019 and contracted here, while their transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline.
- request schema: Enforcement request envelope: Organization, exactly one human Account ID or tenant-scoped service identity ID (both forms or neither is a schema rejection), operation string, requested units, idempotency key. Policy command: complete immutable `EntitlementPolicyCandidate` (`policy_schema_version=entitlement-policy-v1`, `semantic_version`, `activation_mode=immediate`, `rules`), expected current policy version, `plan_approval_id`, and no client-supplied effective or expiry time. `EntitlementPolicyRules.operation_rules` has exactly the nine keys `crawl.start`, `reassessment.start`, `ai.generate`, `export.generate`, `report.view`, `history.view`, `issue.read`, `recommendation.read`, `score.read`; a missing or duplicate class makes the policy invalid.
- response schema: The immutable Entitlement Decision with every field in the WF-015 Decision contract; for a high-cost Allow or AllowWithWarning, its one Reservation and current state; for an allowed low-cost read, the authorized response plus its one LowCostUsageRecord. Policy activation returns the new active policy version with commit-time effectiveness assigned and the prior version superseded.
- controller: Only for the policy limb: the registered JSON command controller and its browser twin for `POST /api/v1/organizations/:organization_id/entitlement-policies`, which constructs the logical command and invokes exactly one `Workflows::Wf015::Handlers::ActivateEntitlementPolicy`. Enforcement has no controller - a controller MUST NOT call an entitlement checkpoint directly; the low-cost path reaches it only through the `Workflows::Wf015::LowCostRead` coordinator.
- command: `Workflows::Wf015::DecideEntitlement`, `ReserveEntitlement`, `HeartbeatEntitlement`, `CommitEntitlement`, `ReleaseEntitlement`, `RecordLowCostUsage`, `ActivateEntitlementPolicy`, exactly as the APPLICATION_LAYER.md state-changing operation registry declares for `Workflows::Wf015`. The low-cost read coordinator `Workflows::Wf015::LowCostRead` authenticates and authorizes, obtains the immutable Decision, materializes the AuthorizedDTO through the context query handler, then atomically appends the one LowCostUsageRecord, `EntitlementCommitted`, the required query event intent, authorization/audit evidence and Session activity at the durable response checkpoint.
- command input: Enforcement: the resolved snapshot of plan, entitlement, operation class, counter group and window, soft and hard limits, requested units and actor scope, taken at the checkpoint and not earlier. Policy: the candidate, expected current version and plan approval reference. Low-cost read: no client idempotency key is accepted - OD-019 prohibits client-supplied keys on GET and mints the Decision ID server-side from Organization, human Account or service identity, declared operation, resolved target identity and state version, and counter window.
- command output: One immutable Decision (`allow`, `allow_with_warning` or `block`); for high-cost allowance one Reservation in `reserved`; for an allowed low-cost read one LowCostUsageRecord keyed by Decision ID; for policy activation one active version and one superseded prior version. Queueing a high-cost action outputs nothing: it creates no Decision and no reservation.
- actor: The tenant-scoped entitlement service identity is the only actor that makes an entitlement Decision; the human Account or service identity named in the envelope is the SUBJECT of the Decision, not its author. A BillingOperator holding `policy.entitlement.manage`, or the authorized billing adapter acting for an already approved plan version, activates policy. An OrganizationAdmin is not an actor here at all: the Permission Baseline denies `policy.entitlement.manage` to every role except BillingOperator, and an OrganizationAdmin may only read allowed notices through `entitlement.notice.read`.
- organization scope: The Organization named in the envelope, which must have its WF-001-created active baseline BillingEntity linked to the current Plan Assignment, per CAP-024 Preconditions. Exactly one Plan Assignment is active per Organization and its BillingEntity MUST be that Organization's one nonclosed BillingEntity; that invariant is MTX-053's, owned by S-02, and is consumed here as a precondition rather than re-enforced. An inactive Organization is not a precondition failure - it is evaluated into a Block Decision with `organization_inactive`.
- aggregate: BillingEntity is the canonical write entry for entitlement state, per the APPLICATION_LAYER.md canonical write-entry registry row 'Billing and approved commercial linkage'. EntitlementPolicy is an immutable versioned Policy Artifact. EntitlementDecision, LowCostUsageRecord, LowCostFallbackSnapshot and EntitlementLeaseHeartbeat are immutable appends; EntitlementCounterWindow, EntitlementReservation and EntitlementCommitIntent are mutable children of that root.
- aggregate boundary: The counter window row is the serialization guard, exactly as the `Workflows::Wf015` registry row states ('BillingEntity/counter-window serialization; each decision/usage identity commits once'). A Decision, its reservation delta and the counter window advance commit in one transaction against that guard, so no reservation can exist without the counter row that admitted it. The gated operation's own aggregate is never joined: WF-015 hands back a Decision and a reservation identity, and the Crawl, Evaluation, AIResponse or Export commits its own root under its own workflow carrying the root Decision/reservation ID.
- value objects: EntitlementDecisionId, OperationClass, OperationString, UsageUnit, CounterGroupId, CounterWindow, RequestedUnits, SoftLimit, HardLimit, GraceBehavior, ReasonCode, RecoveryAction, ReservationId, LeaseGeneration, HeartbeatGeneration, PrestartLifetime, MaximumExecutionDuration, DurableCommitPoint, CachedSnapshotId, CachedSnapshotAge, RetryOfDecisionId, PolicyVersion, PlanVersion, PlanApprovalId.
- domain service: Not applicable - WF-015 names no domain service. The short-circuit precedence, the allow predicate, the nullability derivation, the recovery mapping and the cached-snapshot age test are pure total functions over the resolved snapshot, applied inside the `Commercial` context handler under the counter-window lock.
- repository: `BillingEntityRepository` is the canonical write path and loads the entitlement child graph; the counter window, reservation and commit-intent rows are checkpoints of that root and expose no independent `save`. Read-only: `EntitlementPolicyRepository`, `PlanAssignmentRepository`, `LowCostFallbackSnapshotRepository`, and the immutable append paths for `entitlement_decisions`, `entitlement_lease_heartbeats` and `low_cost_usage_records`.
- persistence model: Canonical tables per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md, all `organization_id`-scoped: `entitlement_counter_windows` (`T-MUT`; unique `(organization_id,counter_group,window_start,window_end)`, carrying reserved/committed/low-cost units and `reconciliation_state`), `entitlement_decisions` (`T-IMM`, `LINEAGE`; actor/service XOR, cached snapshot ID/age nullable, retry-of ID nullable), `entitlement_reservations` (`T-MUT`, `LINEAGE`; `state CHECK ('reserved','executing','committed','released','expired')`, one reservation per Decision), `entitlement_lease_heartbeats` (`T-IMM`; unique `(entitlement_reservation_id,heartbeat_generation)`), `low_cost_fallback_snapshots` (`T-IMM`; whole-tuple payload/hash, unique tuple hash), `low_cost_usage_records` (`T-IMM`; unique Decision), `entitlement_commit_intents` (`T-MUT`; unique reservation/output), `plan_assignments` (`T-MUT`; partial unique active per Organization). The Decision, the fallback snapshot and the usage record are append-only by table class: a Decision is never edited to reflect a later reservation outcome, which is why reservation state is read from its own row rather than from the Decision.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md already declares all nine entitlement and commercial tables with their constraints, including the `(organization_id,counter_group,window_start,window_end)` uniqueness that makes the counter window addressable and the unique Decision that makes the usage record replay-safe. No product schema object is added by S-22.
- transaction boundary: Enforcement: one `Platform::UnitOfWork` per checkpoint, opened at the checkpoint and never at queue time. It acquires the Organization and counter-window rows in the declared Rails total order, resolves the snapshot under those locks, appends the immutable Decision, and for a high-cost allowance creates the Reservation and advances `active_reserved_units` in the same commit. Commit or release advances `committed_units` and terminates the reservation in one transaction against the same counter window. The low-cost record, `EntitlementCommitted`, the query event intent, authorization/audit evidence and Session activity commit together at the durable response checkpoint that makes the response returnable; if response materialization or that commit fails, no response is returnable and no usage record commits. Policy: one transaction assigning commit-time effectiveness, activating the new version, superseding the prior and emitting `EntitlementPolicyActivated`. No provider or network call sits in any of these transactions - CAP-024's Success Condition and OD-008 both require entitlement to work without a billing-provider call.
- concurrency: The counter window row is the serialization point and the reason the hard bound is true rather than approximately true: concurrent high-cost decisions for one `(organization_id, counter_group, window_start, window_end)` serialize against that row, so `committed_units + active_reserved_units + requested_units` is evaluated against a value no other in-flight decision can invalidate, and N concurrent requests at the boundary admit only those that fit. Reservation lifecycle races are decided by fixed winners, not by arrival: at exactly the prestart expiry instant, execution-start loses to expiry; at exactly 15 minutes since the last accepted heartbeat, or at the maximum execution instant, the lease-expiry handler wins over both a new heartbeat and a protected side effect. A heartbeat renews inside a parent-row lock transaction that reads and stores `prior_lease_expires_at`, verifies the exact reservation generation, `executing` state and winning deadline, inserts the immutable heartbeat row and advances the parent to the stored strictly later expiry; the prior expiry is never reconstructed after parent mutation. Nesting cannot double meter: `reassessment.start` subsumes its internally invoked WF-005 through WF-008 core and those stages MUST NOT also reserve or commit `crawl.start`, and every stage records the root Decision/reservation ID.
- idempotency: Three distinct identities, none of them interchangeable. (1) Enforcement replay: exact replay with the same idempotency key returns the immutable original Decision plus the CURRENT linked Reservation state and consumes nothing further. (2) Terminal-key exhaustion: after a Block, or once a Reservation reaches `released` or `expired`, a new attempt MUST use a NEW idempotency key and set `retry_of_decision_id` to the preceding Decision; reuse of the terminal key never creates or reopens a reservation. Executing with an expired linked Reservation returns the original Allow Decision plus current Reservation state and domain conflict `reservation_expired`, creating no new Decision and no side effect - only the new-key retry creates a fresh Decision. (3) Low-cost read identity: no client key exists, because OD-019 prohibits client-supplied idempotency keys on GET; the Decision ID is server-minted deterministically from Organization, human Account or service identity, declared operation, resolved target identity and state version, and counter window, and a repeated tuple within the window replays the stored record without another increment or event. Decision ID is the LowCostUsageRecord's uniqueness key, so a transport retry of an identical read cannot be counted twice.
- background job: `entitlement_reconcile` (queue class `control`) claims one `work_executions` row on the reservation/generation identity and invokes exactly one of `HeartbeatEntitlement`, `CommitEntitlement` or `ReleaseEntitlement`, selected solely from persisted Reservation state and deadline - the job chooses no product outcome of its own. It is dispatched by the `entitlement_lease_expire` scheduled action. `entitlement_invariant_sweep_due` runs `invariant_sweep` on the `maintenance` queue every 15 seconds and ensures or invokes the exact reservation expiry/commit/release action identity; it detects a missing action, it does not decide one.
- queue: `control` (Sidekiq queue `worker_control`, concurrency 5, 30-second claim) for `entitlement_reconcile`; `maintenance` for `entitlement_invariant_sweep_due`. Queue identity and topology are owned by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md; this row renames nothing.
- retry policy: Not applicable as a workflow retry - WF-015 defines no retry of an entitlement Decision, and inventing one would be a second consumption. A denied Decision is terminal for its key; the only forward path is the Recovery Path (upgrade or correct the plan, wait for a new usage window, release an executing reservation when the operation terminates, or restore the authoritative counter) followed by a NEW-key attempt carrying `retry_of_decision_id`. Physical deadlock or serialization retry on the counter-window lock reuses the same command/idempotency claim and never becomes a new product attempt.
- terminal failure: High-cost hard threshold, inactive Organization, inactive actor, unauthorized service, inactive entitlement, unknown operation, missing or invalid policy or counter, stale reservation or counter conflict blocks BEFORE the first protected side effect with its exact reason. A low-cost read whose LowCostFallbackSnapshot has expired returns a Block Decision with `policy_unavailable` or `counter_unavailable` plus outward reason `entitlement_unavailable`. Reservation terminals are `committed`, `released` and `expired`, and all three are terminal. Cancellation or any terminal failure before the listed durable commit point RELEASES exactly once even when intermediate Documents or other partial artifacts exist: those artifacts remain governed by their own workflow but are not a usage commitment.
- reconciliation: The lease-expiry handler is the reconciler and its rule is exact: it commits exactly once ONLY if the durable commit point committed strictly before the winning instant, and otherwise releases exactly once. `entitlement_commit_intents` records the durable output type, ID and hash with `state CHECK ('pending','committed','released')` and unique reservation/output, so the commit decision is read from persisted evidence of the output rather than inferred from timing. Low-cost accounting reconciles forward, never backward: while authoritative reconciliation is delayed, counter resolution still includes every LowCostUsageRecord exactly once by record ID, and the cached counter view additionally includes every unreconciled record for the same Organization and window committed strictly after snapshot capture, once by record ID. `entitlement_counter_windows.reconciliation_state` carries that status.
- domain events: Exactly the ten WF-015 events: `EntitlementPolicyActivated`, `EntitlementChecked`, `EntitlementWarningIssued`, `EntitlementViolationDetected`, `EntitlementReserved`, `EntitlementExecutionStarted`, `EntitlementLeaseRenewed`, `EntitlementCommitted`, `EntitlementReleased`, `EntitlementReservationExpired`. `EntitlementCommitted` carries a null reservation ID for a low-cost usage record, which is what distinguishes low-cost accounting from a reservation commit on the same event type. Exact replay emits none. A denied policy command emits no `EntitlementPolicyActivated` and exactly one denial audit event.
- event payload: The canonical logical event envelope with the real `organization_id` (an Organization always exists at an entitlement checkpoint, so the OD-013 bootstrap substitution is unavailable here), plus the Decision ID, the human Account or service identity, operation, usage unit, counter group and window bounds, policy and plan versions, soft and hard limits, before and after counters, requested units, reservation ID where one exists, cached snapshot ID and age where the fallback was used, retry lineage, reason code, recovery action and correlation ID. `EntitlementLeaseRenewed` copies its generation, prior and renewed expiry, worker service identity and content hashes from the immutable heartbeat row and names that row ID as the created affected entity with `to_state=renewed`.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf015` is the sole producer of all ten. Counter-window lock waits, Sidekiq claim latency and sweep cadence are technical telemetry and MUST NOT be emitted as domain events.
- event consumers: `notification_route_v1` consumes `EntitlementWarningIssued` and `EntitlementViolationDetected` through the outbox and routes them to the human initiating Account when present, otherwise Project MarketingOperator accounts, plus OrganizationAdmin and BillingOperator accounts, requiring `entitlement.notice.read` at severity `warning` on both baseline channels. The gated workflows do not consume an event: they hold the Decision and reservation ID returned at their own checkpoint.
- serializer: Only the policy limb serializes: `ATTR-ActivateEntitlementPolicy` on the way in and the standard terminal command result on the way out. Decisions, reservations and usage records have no transport representation of their own; the actor-visible surface is the separately defined notice field set returned by `QRY-018 EntitlementNoticeCollection` under `entitlement.notice.read`, which is owned elsewhere and is not broadened here.
- authorization entry point: Two entry points that must not be confused. Enforcement: the server-side entitlement service identity at the checkpoint, inside the unit of work, under the counter-window lock. WF-015's Security Notes are absolute - no client-side trust for entitlement decisions - so no request field, header or cached client assertion may supply or influence a Decision. Policy: the `ActivateEntitlementPolicy` handler, before mutation. Entitlement is not authorization and never substitutes for it: a read is authorized by its own named permission through the sole authorization facade and metered separately by its declared operation, and a Block is a capacity outcome, not a denial of authority.
- permission checks: `policy.entitlement.manage` for policy activation - allowed to BillingOperator alone and denied to OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, SecurityOperator and the read-only Executive Buyer by the Permission Baseline; the billing adapter service identity may synchronize only the already approved plan version and cannot invent a plan or alter system-governed operation classes. `entitlement.notice.read` for reading allowed notices - allowed to OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer and BillingOperator, denied to SecurityOperator, the Executive Buyer and every service identity. The enforcement checkpoints carry no tenant-actor permission at all: they are reachable only by the entitlement service identity, and the operation being gated has already been authorized by its own permission (`crawl.trigger` for `crawl.start`, `export.create` for `export.generate`, and the named read permission for each low-cost read).
- tenant boundary: Every counter window, Decision, reservation and usage record is keyed by `organization_id` and resolved under proved RLS context. The counter window's uniqueness key includes `organization_id`, so one Organization's usage can never be compared with another's limit or consume another's budget. A nonnull cross-Organization reference in the envelope is `F1-AUTH-403 / tenant_mismatch` with no side effect and creates no Decision. The Plan Assignment's BillingEntity MUST be same-Organization by composite foreign key, so a plan cannot be borrowed across tenants. The `plan-approval-interim-baseline-v1` artifact is authority only for the exact interim bytes and no tenant actor can forge or edit it.
- error contract: The distinction that matters is rejection versus Decision, and Volume I draws it precisely. REJECTIONS creating NO Decision: invalid tenant references, both actor forms or neither, malformed units, or a malformed command envelope - `F1-VALIDATION-400`; cross-Organization reference - `F1-AUTH-403 / tenant_mismatch`. EVALUATED INTO an immutable Block Decision rather than rejected: inactive Organization (`organization_inactive`), inactive actor (`actor_inactive`), unauthorized service (`service_unauthorized`), inactive entitlement (`entitlement_inactive`), unknown operation (`operation_unknown`), missing policy (`policy_unavailable`), unavailable counter (`counter_unavailable`), reservation conflict (`reservation_conflict`), high-cost hard threshold (`hard_limit_exceeded`). A capacity or entitlement bound denying work selects `F1-CAPACITY-429`, severity `warning`, retryable false unless the result names a new eligible window, recovery `wait_for_next_window_or_change_approved_entitlement`; the Decision's own `recovery_action` for hard is `wait_for_window` and the next-window instant is the exclusive end of the current UTC-day window. Execution against an expired linked Reservation is `F1-DOMAIN-409 / reservation_expired` with the original Allow Decision and current Reservation state and no side effect. Low-cost fallback expiry surfaces outward reason `entitlement_unavailable`. `upgrade_plan` without a distinct valid owner-approved Plan Approval artifact deterministically returns `upgrade_plan_unavailable`. Policy denial (stale, future-effective, expiring, incomplete, commercially unapproved, broadened beyond global safety, or unauthorized) changes nothing and emits one denial audit event.
- audit record: Per WF-015 Audit and Observability, persisted in full: every Entitlement Decision field, counter before and after values, recovery action, cached-snapshot ID and age, each LowCostUsageRecord and its reconciliation state, idempotency and retry lineage, every reservation heartbeat and state, the commit or release event, actor and correlation ID. CAP-024's Failure Condition includes an unexplained legitimate block, so a Block that cannot be reconstructed from its own persisted fields is itself the defect: the reason code, the counters that produced it and the snapshot identity are all stored on the immutable Decision rather than recomputed.
- observability: Entitlement enforcement metrics and failure telemetry per CAP-024 Observability Requirements. Saturation is observable before denial through `EntitlementWarningIssued` at the soft threshold; denial through `EntitlementViolationDetected`; the reservation lifecycle through `EntitlementReserved`, `EntitlementExecutionStarted`, `EntitlementLeaseRenewed`, `EntitlementCommitted`, `EntitlementReleased` and `EntitlementReservationExpired`. A cached-fallback Decision is distinguishable in telemetry by its `cached_snapshot_id` and `cached_snapshot_age`, so degraded low-cost operation is visible rather than silent. Entitlement counters and reservations MUST NOT be cached in Redis; SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md forbids it by name and a cache outage cannot consume entitlement or change a decision winner.
- retention: Usage counters and entitlement states are retained per CAP-024 Data Implications. The LowCostFallbackSnapshot has a bounded usable age of 24 hours from `snapshot_captured_at`, which is an eligibility bound rather than a retention window. The retention window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states none.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: the OD-006 high-cost predicate at the boundary - `committed + active_reserved + requested == hard_limit` is ALLOWED and `> hard_limit` is blocked, proving equality is admitted rather than rejected. TYP-DATA: `>= soft_limit` returns allow_with_warning, proving soft is inclusive. TYP-DATA: the OD-006 low-cost rule - a read AT and ABOVE the 1,000 hard limit is still allowed with an over-limit warning and still creates exactly one usage record while the Organization is active and the human Account or tenant service identity remains authorized, proving warning-only is implemented as ratified rather than withheld as interim. TYP-DATA: each `entitlement-interim-v1` value - `crawl.start` 3/4, `reassessment.start` 1/2, `ai.generate` 20/25, `export.generate` 8/10, and the five low-cost operations sharing `baseline_reads` at 800/1,000 - asserted at the boundary and one either side. TYP-DATA: the five low-cost operations share ONE counter, proving a `score.read` consumes the same budget as an `issue.read`; each high-cost operation uses its own named counter group, proving they do not. TYP-DATA: the counter window is the half-open UTC day `[00:00:00Z, next 00:00:00Z)` containing the SERVER decision time - a decision at `23:59:59.999Z` and one at `00:00:00.000Z` fall in different windows, and the next-window recovery instant is the exclusive end. TYP-DATA: queueing a high-cost action creates NO Decision and NO reservation, proving the gate is at the protected execution checkpoint and not at enqueue. TYP-DATA: a queued high-cost action re-resolves CURRENT policy at execution and has no earlier authoritative Decision to reuse. TYP-SEC: the short-circuit precedence is exact and total - `organization_inactive` beats `actor_inactive`/`service_unauthorized` beats `entitlement_inactive` beats `operation_unknown` beats `policy_unavailable` beats `counter_unavailable` beats `reservation_conflict` beats `hard_limit_exceeded` beats `soft_limit_reached` beats `within_limit`; a fixture failing two predicates returns only the higher one. TYP-DATA: the recovery mapping is fixed - within/soft->`none`, hard->`wait_for_window`, Organization->`reactivate_organization`, actor->`reactivate_actor`, service or unknown operation->`contact_support`, inactive entitlement->`upgrade_plan`, policy/cached-policy->`restore_policy`, counter/cached-counter->`restore_counter`, reservation conflict->`submit_new_attempt`. TYP-DATA: an inactive Organization, inactive actor, unauthorized service, unknown operation, inactive entitlement, missing policy or unavailable counter each produce an immutable BLOCK DECISION, not a workflow-precondition rejection - the Decision exists and is auditable. TYP-VALIDATION contrast, asserted as TYP-DATA: an invalid tenant reference, both actor forms, neither actor form, malformed units or a malformed envelope create NO Decision at all. TYP-SEC: no client-side trust - a request header, body field or cached client assertion claiming an allowance changes no Decision. TYP-SEC: `policy.entitlement.manage` is BillingOperator-only; an OrganizationAdmin holding every other tenant permission cannot activate, amend or supersede an Entitlement Policy, and holds only `entitlement.notice.read`. TYP-SEC: the billing adapter may synchronize only the already approved plan version; a candidate naming an unapproved plan, an invented plan or an altered system-governed operation class changes nothing. TYP-DATA: policy activation is immediate with commit-time effectiveness - a client-supplied effective or expiry time, a future-effective candidate, an expiring candidate, a stale expected version, an incomplete operation map (a missing or duplicate class among the nine keys), a commercially unapproved candidate, or a candidate broadened beyond global safety each change nothing and emit exactly one denial audit event. TYP-DATA: a valid activation atomically activates the new version, supersedes the prior and emits `EntitlementPolicyActivated` at commit. TYP-DATA: soft limit MUST be lower than hard limit; an omitted soft limit in an approved replacement derives as 80 percent of hard rounded DOWN to the usage unit. TYP-DATA: prestart lifetime is exactly 15 minutes under the interim; an Organization policy may shorten it to a whole 1..15 minutes and cannot lengthen it; a missing, zero, fractional or greater value makes the policy INVALID and blocks the high-cost action. TYP-DATA: an approved additional metered resource MUST name its unit, reservation-amount derivation, window and commit/release point; absence of any one field makes that policy invalid and blocks the high-cost action. TYP-DATA: at exactly the prestart expiry instant, execution-start LOSES to expiry. TYP-DATA: at exactly 15 minutes since the last accepted heartbeat, and at the maximum execution instant, the lease-expiry handler WINS over a new heartbeat and over a protected side effect. TYP-DATA: the lease handler commits exactly once only when the durable commit point committed STRICTLY BEFORE that instant, and otherwise releases exactly once - a commit point reached at exactly that instant releases. TYP-DATA: cancellation or terminal failure before the durable commit point releases exactly once even when partial Documents or artifacts exist, proving a partial artifact is not a usage commitment. TYP-DATA: each durable commit point is the exact Volume I one - `crawl.start` at Crawl `completed` with at least one valid Document; `reassessment.start` at replacement full-Project Issue set AND ScoreSnapshot promoted; `ai.generate` at validated AIResponse persisted; `export.generate` at Export available. TYP-DATA: maximum execution is 65/120/2/15 minutes respectively. TYP-DATA: heartbeat renewal is atomic under the parent lock - it stores `prior_lease_expires_at` before mutating the parent, verifies the exact generation, `executing` state and winning deadline, and `(entitlement_reservation_id,heartbeat_generation)` is unique so a replayed heartbeat inserts nothing. TYP-DATA: nesting cannot double meter - a `reassessment.start` run executing WF-005 through WF-008 reserves and commits `reassessment.start` ONLY and never `crawl.start`; an optional AI generation inside it still takes its own `ai.generate`; every stage records the root Decision/reservation ID. TYP-DATA: reservation transitions - `reserved -> executing|released|expired`, `executing -> committed|released`; `committed`, `released` and `expired` are terminal and no path reopens them. TYP-DATA: exact replay with the same key returns the immutable original Decision plus CURRENT reservation state and consumes nothing. TYP-DATA: after a Block, or after `released` or `expired`, reuse of the terminal key never creates or reopens a reservation; only a new key with `retry_of_decision_id` set to the preceding Decision creates a fresh Decision. TYP-DATA: executing against an expired linked Reservation returns the original Allow plus current reservation state and `F1-DOMAIN-409 / reservation_expired`, creating no new Decision and no side effect. TYP-DATA: N concurrent high-cost requests serialize against one counter window and never reserve or commit above the hard limit; the window's `(organization_id,counter_group,window_start,window_end)` uniqueness is what makes that true. TYP-DATA: OD-019 root subsumption - one accepted top-level document read appends exactly ONE record; its Turbo Frames and partials carry the root Decision ID and append NONE, so a page plus four Frames is one unit and not five. TYP-DATA: a Frame reached by DIRECT navigation is itself a root and appends its own record. TYP-SEC: a metered read route with no static operation declaration resolves `operation_unknown`, returns Block with `contact_support` and emits the standard denial audit event - an undeclared metered route is unreachable, never silently unmetered. TYP-DATA: a read route declares exactly ONE of the five operations; a dashboard combining score, Issue and Recommendation regions charges its single declared operation once, not three. TYP-SEC: client-supplied idempotency keys are prohibited on GET; the Decision ID is server-minted from Organization, human Account or service identity, declared operation, resolved target identity and state version, and counter window, and a repeated tuple within the window replays the stored record with no second increment and no second event. TYP-DATA: a transport retry of an identical read produces no second record, because Decision ID is the record's uniqueness key. TYP-DATA: a request that never reaches the durable authorized-response checkpoint creates NO record, including a conditional response returning no representation. TYP-DATA: a denied read creates no record. TYP-DATA: if response materialization or the durable commit fails, no response is returnable and no usage record commits. TYP-SEC: Evidence and entitlement-notice reads are not among the five operations - they are unmetered standalone and subsumed when nested. TYP-DATA: the LowCostFallbackSnapshot is captured ONLY when the active policy and the authoritative counter are simultaneously valid. TYP-DATA: fallback uses the WHOLE tuple and never mixes a current component with a cached one. TYP-DATA: `cached_snapshot_age = decision_time - snapshot_captured_at`; exactly 24 hours is ALLOWED and any greater age is expired. TYP-DATA: an expired fallback returns Block with `policy_unavailable` or `counter_unavailable` plus outward `entitlement_unavailable`. TYP-DATA: when BOTH dependencies are unavailable the reason is `cached_policy_snapshot_used` - policy wins - and the one snapshot ID and age govern every cached field. TYP-DATA: the cached counter view includes every unreconciled LowCostUsageRecord for the same Organization and window committed strictly after capture, once by record ID. TYP-SEC: high-cost operations FAIL CLOSED on a missing, stale or invalid policy or counter and never use the fallback; only low-cost reads may. TYP-OBS: `EntitlementCommitted` for a low-cost record carries a NULL reservation ID, distinguishing it from a reservation commit on the same event type. TYP-OBS: exactly the ten WF-015 events exist and exact replay emits none. TYP-OBS: `EntitlementWarningIssued` and `EntitlementViolationDetected` route to the human initiating Account when present, otherwise Project MarketingOperators, plus OrganizationAdmins and BillingOperators, requiring `entitlement.notice.read` at severity `warning`. TYP-OBS: counter-window lock waits, Sidekiq claim latency and sweep cadence are asserted ABSENT from the domain event stream. TYP-SEC: entitlement counters and reservations are asserted absent from Redis; a cache outage grants no allowance and changes no decision winner. TYP-INT: `entitlement_reconcile` invokes exactly one of `HeartbeatEntitlement`, `CommitEntitlement` or `ReleaseEntitlement` chosen solely from persisted reservation state and deadline. TYP-INT: the 15-second `entitlement_invariant_sweep_due` ensures the exact expiry/commit/release action identity exists and decides no product outcome itself. TYP-E2E: bootstrap - a WF-001 self-service commit materializes the byte-equivalent `entitlement-interim-v1` policy and BillingEntity-linked `interim-baseline-plan-v1` Plan Assignment with no prior active version, and the first high-cost operation for that Organization decides against exactly those bytes with no billing-provider call. TYP-DATA: `upgrade_plan` without a distinct valid owner-approved Plan Approval artifact returns `upgrade_plan_unavailable` deterministically and the customer may wait for the next window.
- rollout: S-22 depends on S-02 for the active Organization, its baseline BillingEntity and the byte-equivalent baseline policy and Plan Assignment (MTX-053, complete). Nothing depends on S-22 as a slice, but every high-cost workflow consumes its checkpoint: S-07 for `crawl.start` (contracted at MTX-058), and the reassessment, AI-generation and export slices for their own root operations. The low-cost enforcement path is fully specified here; its GET transport activates with the API_CONTRACTS.md Volume II transport baseline and requires no further owner decision.

### MTX-042 - AC-WF-017 (WF: WF-017)

- Slice: S-24
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-017-handle-incident-and-recovery
- interface type: Application commands plus one control-plane timed observation: declare or correlate an Incident, run a named diagnostic playbook step, observe restoration, and resolve. Every step of the only active playbook is diagnostic; actual remediation is a separately authorized command in its own named workflow and only its immutable result may be linked. The workflow reads and seals telemetry, never mutates a customer resource.
- route: Not applicable - Volume I defines the commands, their permissions and their envelopes but no HTTP path, method or transport. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline, which already carries `POST /api/v1/incidents`, `POST /api/v1/incidents/:id/step-attempts` and `POST /api/v1/incidents/:id/resolution` against `incident.respond`; this row neither renames nor re-derives them. No Incident read route exists: under OD-020 Incident is a security and internal operational object that remains deny-by-default pending a separate owner decision, so `QRY-030 IncidentCollection` and `QRY-031 IncidentDetail` stay deferred and `incident.respond` is not inferred as collection or detail read authority. No high-risk step-approval route exists either, because `incident-diagnostic-interim-v1` declares no high-risk mutating step and a dormant approval route would create baseline behaviour.
- request schema: Declaration: correlation ID, observed condition, affected workflow and resource scope, first-observed time, and source telemetry reference, per WF-017 Preconditions; a manual declaration by a SecurityOperator holding `incident.respond` adds a nonblank 20-2,000 character reason. Severity and playbook are never client-supplied: severity is computed from the frozen triggering Evidence set and the playbook is resolved and hashed server-side. Step attempt: Incident ID, expected state version, idempotency key, and one step name from the closed set `capture_diagnostic_snapshot`, `link_named_remediation`, `verify_trigger_cleared`; `link_named_remediation` alone carries the nullable command or result reference of an already committed WF-005, WF-006, WF-013, WF-014 or WF-016 action, and a baseline step accepts no mutation target or action. Restoration observation: the persisted playbook and observation identity for a registered sequence-one or sequence-two check. Resolution: Incident ID and expected state version; the distinct-approver requirement for critical and high is checked server-side against the Incident and no client approval object is accepted.
- response schema: The Incident with its exhaustive contract fields: ID, its one Organization scope, affected resources and workflows, severity, severity reason code, detected and declared times, declaring identity, commander, active playbook ID and version, status, state version, mitigation and restoration-check records, closure actor and approver, and correlation ID. A step attempt returns its sequence, generation, input and output hashes, terminal outcome and exact machine reason. A restoration observation returns its sequence, outcome `passed` or `failed`, and its observation hashes. Resolution returns the committed Incident state and version. The field list is exhaustive and contains no `emergency_customer_access` field.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I; see route. The Volume II physical adapter is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md.
- command: `Workflows::Wf017::DeclareIncident`, `Workflows::Wf017::RunIncidentStep`, `Workflows::Wf017::ObserveRestoration`, `Workflows::Wf017::ResolveIncident`, per the APPLICATION_LAYER.md command registry. The route-fixed `RunIncidentStep(step=link_named_remediation)` branch atomically links an already committed separately authorized remediation Command Result and does not invoke a second Incident command. No command in this registry mutates a credential, Account, policy, dataset, Export or customer resource.
- command input: As request_schema, inside the Logical Command Envelope: `command_id`, `idempotency_key`, `command_type`, `schema_version`, exactly one of `actor_id` or `service_identity_id`, the nonnull `organization_id` of the one owning Organization, target type and ID, requested action, `expected_state_version` for every existing target, every policy version used to authorize or constrain the command, `requested_at_utc`, `correlation_id`, `causation_id`, and one schema-valid `command_payload`. Deadlines and race decisions use the server-assigned `authorization_check_at_utc`, never the client instant.
- command output: One Logical Result: `outcome=success` with the Incident, attempt or check identifiers and committed state version, or `outcome=failure` with `error_class`, `error_code`, one contract-defined `reason_code`, `severity`, `retryable` and one `recovery_action`. Incident severity is a separate field on the Incident and never alters the result severity, which stays exactly `warning` or `error` as the error table lists.
- actor: A SecurityOperator holding `incident.respond` declares an Incident with a 20-2,000 character reason, or a versioned alert emits an incident candidate. The Permission Baseline gives `incident.respond` to SecurityOperator only, denies it to OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator and Read-Only Executive Buyer alike, and admits the Service Identity as 'incident service only'. The five-minute second restoration observation is made by the lifecycle service, not by a human. Critical or high resolution additionally requires a DIFFERENT active SecurityOperator holding the protected `security.investigation.approve`. A Support Session is permitted only within approved incident scope and confers no additional action.
- organization scope: Exactly one Organization owns each Incident record, per OD-013 Option 1 as integrated by ADR-020. An incident affecting N Organizations is N Organization-scoped Incident records, each emitting its own Organization-scoped events, all linked by one shared `correlation_id` that expresses coordination and confers no ownership. There is no platform-owned Incident record and no platform-scoped event to create. The ability to detect, respond to and report on an incident spanning many Organizations is preserved in full and is exercised as orchestration over those Organization-owned records.
- aggregate: Incident is the root; the step attempt, the severity and customer-action decision, the high-risk approval and the restoration check are its children. Incident is not a DM-REQ-001 core entity -- that list is a MUST-include floor and never names Incident -- but a lifecycle-bearing auxiliary domain record whose contract, field list, states and events are defined in full by WF-017, in the same way Evidence is classified as auxiliary at 011 DOMAIN_MODEL.md line 138.
- aggregate boundary: The Incident root owns declaration, severity, mitigation and resolution. Step start commits its own `incident_step_attempts` row and, for a hypothetical future high-risk step, atomically consumes the approval in the same transaction; the restoration check commits its own immutable row. The boundary that matters is what the aggregate may NOT reach: cross-Organization read, restricted-Evidence export, credential revoke or rotation, Account security lifecycle action, destructive data action, and security-policy change cannot be performed by the baseline playbook at all; each must enter its existing named workflow with THAT workflow's permission, scope, support or approval, state-version and idempotency contract. Incident authority never crosses into another aggregate.
- value objects: IncidentId, IncidentSeverity restricted to `critical`, `high`, `medium`, `low`, SeverityReasonCode, IncidentState restricted to `open`, `mitigated`, `resolved`, PlaybookArtifactId, PlaybookVersion, PlaybookContentSha256, StepName restricted to `capture_diagnostic_snapshot`, `link_named_remediation`, `verify_trigger_cleared`, StepAttemptState restricted to `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, ObservationSequence restricted to 1 and 2, ObservationOutcome restricted to `passed` and `failed`, TriggerPredicateSha256, InputSha256, OutputSha256, ApprovalId, ApprovalState, CanonicalCommandRequestHash, ExpectedStateVersion, CorrelationId, RecoveryOfId, RecoveryGeneration, FailedStepGeneration, DisclosureScopeVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - WF-017 names no domain service. The severity predicate is a pure function over the frozen triggering Evidence set with a fixed strongest-wins tie-break; playbook resolution is a hash comparison against the immutable signed artifact; the frozen trigger predicate re-evaluation is a pure recomputation. None of these is a collaborator with its own lifecycle.
- repository: `IncidentRepository`, `IncidentOrganizationRepository`, `IncidentDecisionRepository`, `IncidentStepAttemptRepository`, `IncidentRestorationCheckRepository`, `HighRiskApprovalRepository`; `ReleaseArtifactRepository` read-only for the signed playbook bytes; `SupportSessionRepository` read-only for the scope check. Every one is Organization-scoped.
- persistence model: Canonical `incidents`, `incident_organizations`, `incident_decisions`, `incident_step_attempts`, `high_risk_approvals` and `incident_restoration_checks` per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md, with the signed playbook bytes in `release_artifacts`. `incidents` carries `state CHECK ('open','mitigated','resolved')` and no `restoration_verified` state or substitute lifecycle flag. `incident_step_attempts` carries `state CHECK ('running','succeeded','failed')`; step start creates the row directly in `running` and the only edges are `running -> succeeded` and `running -> failed`, so no pending row and no pending-to-running event exists. `high_risk_approvals` carries `state CHECK ('pending','approved','rejected','used','expired','revoked')` with distinct-actor and one-use constraints. `incident_restoration_checks` carries `observation_sequence CHECK (observation_sequence IN (1,2))` and the five-minute observation hash null for sequence one and nonnull for sequence two. The canonical schema's `incidents.platform_wide` boolean and its nullable primary-Organization and per-child Organization columns are pre-OD-013 artifacts that Volume I now overrides: under OD-013 Option 1 every Incident row and every child row resolves to exactly one nonnull Organization, and no platform-wide row exists. This row contracts the Option 1 shape and records the divergence; correcting the DDL is the schema's own controlled change and not S-24's to make.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - the canonical schema owns the six Incident tables and their CHECK constraints. Bringing `incidents.platform_wide` and the nullable Organization columns into line with the ratified OD-013 Option 1 shape is a controlled change to schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md, not a product migration this row may issue.
- transaction boundary: Declaration: one transaction that idempotently creates or correlates exactly one Incident, freezes the triggering Evidence set, computes severity and assigns an authorized commander. Playbook resolution: the artifact is resolved and hashed before any step; an unknown, altered or missing artifact permits only Incident creation plus critical operational escalation, never a step or a mutation. Step: one transaction per attempt recording input and output hashes and rechecking session and permission; for a hypothetical high-risk step the complete step command and the atomic step-start plus approval-consumption commit as one transaction that must land strictly before `expires_at_utc`. Restoration: each observation commits its own immutable check row; the sequence-one pass commits `Open -> Mitigated`; two passing observations with identical predicate and version permit `Mitigated -> Resolved`. `capture_diagnostic_snapshot` reads and seals only the Incident's already-authorized telemetry and Evidence references within a 60-second attempt; the read is bounded outside the commit and only its sealed IDs and digests are committed.
- concurrency: Three named races, each resolved by Volume I rather than by an implementation choice. (1) At exactly five elapsed minutes the lifecycle service's second observation WINS over a concurrent closure command, so a resolution racing the timer loses and the Incident's mitigated-to-resolved transition derives from two committed passing observations rather than from whoever arrived first. (2) At the exact expiry instant of a high-risk approval the approval-expiry check wins, so a step command landing on the boundary performs no side effect. (3) Step start atomically sets `used_by_step_attempt_id` on the approval, so two concurrent attempts cannot both consume one approval; the constraint is the one-use column, not an application guard. Every command carries `expected_state_version`, so a stale declaration, step, observation or resolution commits nothing. `incident_restoration_checks` is unique on `(incident_id, trigger_predicate_sha256, observation_sequence)` and `incident_decisions` on `(incident_id, decision_type, input_sha256)`, so a concurrent duplicate observation or decision cannot persist twice.
- idempotency: WF-017 Primary Path step 1 is a single obligation: idempotently create OR correlate exactly one Incident. The key is the Logical Command Envelope's create scope -- Organization, command type and idempotency key -- so a replayed declaration returns the stored Incident and correlation rather than creating a second record for the same condition. For an existing target the scope is Organization, command type, target resource type and ID, and idempotency key. Exact replay of a step attempt returns the ORIGINAL step attempt; it cannot consume the approval and cannot execute the step twice, which is the contract's own wording and is the reason the attempt row rather than the delivery is the unit of protection. Each retry or different high-risk action requires a NEW approval; the old one is spent. Reuse with a different canonical request hash is `idempotency_conflict`. Restoration observations are keyed by `(incident_id, trigger_predicate_sha256, observation_sequence)`, so a redelivered timer produces the same check row and no second observation. Before returning retained identifiers an exact replay reauthorizes the current actor and applies current field redaction; denial returns `F1-AUTH-403` with no retained payload while the stored outcome is unchanged.
- background job: `incident_restoration_observe`, a `control`-class timer that performs the exact second five-minute observation, executing work type `incident_observe` and dispatching exactly `ObserveRestoration` for the persisted playbook and observation identity. It claims one `work_executions` row whose immutable owner reference is the Incident, step and generation identity; no handler may choose a different claim table, use a domain row lacking `WORK-CLAIM` as a lease, or hold two transport leases for one invocation. The timer is defined by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md and this row does not redefine it. There is no incident job that mutates a customer resource.
- queue: The `control` queue class, per the Volume I Background Processing action registry as realised in BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md. Queue identity, worker topology and lease mechanics are owned by that document; this row does not rename them.
- retry policy: WF-017 defines no automatic retry schedule and no hidden retry for any diagnostic step. Its Recovery Path is exhaustive and is entirely operator-driven: correct the authorization or input, restore the signed baseline playbook, run a separately authorized named remediation workflow when needed, or submit a NEW idempotent diagnostic step attempt. A `capture_diagnostic_snapshot` attempt is bounded at 60 seconds and its expiry is a terminal attempt outcome rather than an instruction to retry. A denied or expired high-risk approval is never auto-retried; the retry requires a fresh distinct approval.
- terminal failure: A denied, stale, failed or unverifiable recovery step leaves the Incident Open or Mitigated -- never a new failure state -- records the exact failed step, reason and rollback result, emits `IncidentRecoveryFailed`, and NEVER marks restoration complete. An unknown, altered or missing `incident-diagnostic-interim-v1` artifact is `recovery_playbook_unavailable`, which permits only Incident creation plus critical operational escalation and permits no step and no mutation.
