For a hypothetical high-risk step, a changed hash, version or scope, a self-approval, a reuse, or an expired approval performs NO side effect. Critical or high failure creates exactly one operational escalation record for the current failed-step generation.
- reconciliation: The Alternate Path is the reconciliation contract: diagnostic collection CONTINUES while remediation is pending or unavailable, the Incident remains Open, the exact link and observation gap is recorded, and privileged mutation is never inferred from incident authority. This is the WF-017 limb of PRULE-009 -- the successful diagnostic subset is preserved and the gap is retained as an addressable record rather than collapsed into a single failure; the PRULE-009 row MTX-060 is owned by S-07 for its WF-005 limb and S-08 for WF-006, and this row consumes the rule without redefining it. Escalation is exactly one record per current failed-step generation for a critical or high failure: the generation is the uniqueness key, so repeated failures within one generation do not multiply escalations and a new generation earns its own. Incident-step recovery uses the single ERR-REQ-020 recovery-complete rule: a success event is recovery-complete only when it carries a nonnull `recovery_of_id`, a positive `recovery_generation`, the authorized recovery command ID and the earlier terminal reason; no uncorrelated second 'recovered' event may be emitted and recovery may not be claimed from a retry telemetry record alone.
- domain events: Exactly eight, all named by WF-017: `IncidentRaised`, `IncidentSeverityAssigned`, `IncidentRecoveryStepStarted`, `IncidentRecoveryStepCompleted`, `IncidentRecoveryFailed`, `IncidentMitigated`, `IncidentRestorationVerified`, `IncidentResolved`. `IncidentRestorationVerified` is a created event whose affected identity is the sequence-two restoration check row and only when its outcome is `passed`; it carries `to_state=passed` and both observation hashes copied from that row, and it neither mutates nor implies an Incident `restoration_verified` state -- the separate accepted Incident transition remains mitigated-to-resolved. `SecurityIncidentCustomerActionRequired` is a canonical Volume I event, but it appears only in the `notification-policy-v1` baseline route table and not in WF-017's Domain Events enumeration, and WF-017 names no command, step, actor or state rule that emits it; S-24 therefore does not contract its production, and its route -- recipients within the event's disclosure scope holding `security.notice.read`, severity `critical` -- is WF-014 consumption owned by S-19. Alert queue depth, worker leases, observation timing and step latency are technical telemetry and MUST NOT be emitted as domain events.
- event payload: The canonical Logical Event Envelope with a nonnull `organization_id` resolving to the one owning Organization -- the bootstrap substitution is reserved to WF-001 and is unavailable here -- plus `correlation_id` linking the coordinated per-Organization records, the frozen trigger and telemetry hashes, the severity predicate hash, the playbook artifact ID, version and content SHA-256, the step and observation identities, and `input_hash` and `output_hash`. `event_profile` is selected by the deterministic first-match order and never chosen for convenience: `recovery` whenever `recovery_of_id` is nonnull, then `created`, `state_transition`, `attempt`, `decision`. A producer may not emit with null, a sentinel, an invented platform tenant, an arbitrarily selected Organization or an omitted tenant field; an event whose semantics match none or more than one profile fails before publication as `F1-DATA-409 / event_profile_unmapped` and emits no malformed substitute. Raw Evidence payloads, secrets and unrestricted internal diagnostics never appear.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf017` is the sole producer of all eight events; the sequence-two `IncidentRestorationVerified` is committed by the restoration-observation service identity through `ObserveRestoration` within that workflow. No other workflow or service emits any of them. The five `EmergencyAccessRequested`, `EmergencyAccessApproved`, `EmergencyAccessRejected`, `EmergencyAccessRevoked` and `EmergencyAccessExpired` events each have exactly one defined producer in the Emergency Cross-Organization Access Contract and are NOT WF-017 events; S-24 emits none of them and the Incident carries no reference to a Grant.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - WF-017 names no consumer for its eight events. The `notification-policy-v1` route table lists no WF-017 event among its baseline triggers except `SecurityIncidentCustomerActionRequired`, which this row does not produce.
- serializer: Not applicable - Volume I defines no transport exposure for the Incident aggregate and, under OD-020, Incident read remains deny-by-default with `QRY-030` and `QRY-031` deferred, so there is no authorized read projection to serialize. Physical event serialization is owned by the Volume II baseline.
- authorization entry point: Each application command, before mutation, and AGAIN inside every step: WF-017 Primary Path step 3 requires the workflow to execute the diagnostic steps in order 'recording input/output hashes and rechecking session/permission', so a session or permission valid at declaration is never trusted at step time. Resolution reauthorizes the distinct-approver requirement against the Incident at the resolution commit. A Support Session is checked against the approved incident scope at each step rather than once at session start.
- permission checks: `incident.respond`, held by a SecurityOperator; the Permission Baseline denies it to OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator and the Read-Only Executive Buyer, and admits only the incident Service Identity. The baseline playbook requires `incident.respond` ONLY for its diagnostic records and declares no credential, Account, policy, data, export or customer-resource mutation, so it consumes no High-Risk Remediation Approval. Critical or high resolution requires a different active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve`, which the Permission Baseline marks as a protected explicit grant and which is a protected grant under the Role Assignment artifact. `incident.respond` and a High-Risk Approval NEVER substitute for an underlying target-workflow permission: cross-Organization read, restricted-Evidence export, credential revoke or rotation, Account security lifecycle action, destructive data action and security-policy change must each enter their named workflow with that workflow's own permission. The Volume I Permission Baseline enumerates no read action for the Incident object class at all, and none may be inferred: under OD-020 Incident is named among the security, administrative and internal operational objects that remain deny-by-default pending a separate owner decision. The SecurityOperator reads that DO exist -- `project.read`, `source.read`, `crawl.read`, `evaluation.read`, `evidence.metadata.read`, `evidence.payload.read`, `evidence.restricted.read` at 'authorized incident/adjudication scope only', and `security.notice.read` 'allow in incident scope' -- are the target objects' permissions, not the Incident's, and reach only what `capture_diagnostic_snapshot` is already authorized to seal.
- tenant boundary: One Incident record, one Organization, one nonnull `organization_id` on every event and audit record. The shared `correlation_id` across N coordinated records expresses coordination and grants no access to another Organization's data. Cross-Organization read is outside the baseline playbook entirely and must enter its named workflow. Incident authority confers no cross-tenant support access: there is no standing cross-tenant access, normal support operations retain no tenant access, and under OD-012 Option 3 an active approved Emergency Access Grant -- an artifact held OUTSIDE the Incident, bound to one Organization and an enumerated resource and action scope -- is the only authority that substitutes for the affected tenant's OrganizationAdmin `support.session.customer_approve`. The Incident carries no `emergency_customer_access` field and no break-glass semantics, and no actor or service may set, default, infer or synthesize one.
- error contract: Playbook: `recovery_playbook_unavailable` for an unknown, altered or missing artifact, permitting only Incident creation plus critical operational escalation. Authorization: `F1-AUTH-403` for a missing `incident.respond`, a step outside the approved Support Session scope, a self-approved critical or high resolution, or an attempted mutation outside the playbook, emitting one authorization audit event and performing no state change or provider call; a Project-scoped command with null or cross-Organization `project_id` is `F1-AUTH-403 / tenant_mismatch`. Domain: `F1-DOMAIN-409` for a stale `expected_state_version`, a race loss at the five-minute boundary, or a duplicate semantic target; `F1-DOMAIN-409 / idempotency_conflict` for reuse under a different canonical request hash. Validation: `F1-VALIDATION-400 / command_schema_unsupported` for an unsupported major, missing required field, changed required-field type or prohibited field, creating only the audited command outcome; a reason outside 20-2,000 characters is `F1-VALIDATION-400`. Timeout: the 60-second `capture_diagnostic_snapshot` bound is a persisted attempt deadline and uses `F1-TIMEOUT-504`. Envelope derivation follows the fixed precedence -- authentication, authorization or scope denial, a named dependency reason, a named attempt or deadline timeout reason, capacity, rate-limit, subsystem reasons, request-shape validation, domain state and concurrency, then data-integrity -- and if no row selects unambiguously the workflow returns `F1-DATA-409 / contract_invariant_unmapped` and commits no side effect. No invented reason code is exposed outward.
- audit record: Per WF-017 Audit and Observability, exhaustively: every Incident contract field, the trigger and telemetry hash, the severity predicate, the playbook, step and version, the authorization and approval, the expected and actual state, the rollback, BOTH restoration observations, the timeline, the event, and every denied attempt. This is the WF-017 limb of PRULE-022: every recovery action carries its named permission, scoped support or incident authority, expected state version, reason, idempotency key and audit envelope, and an unauthorized, stale or altered replay has no side effect; the PRULE-022 row MTX-073 is owned by S-07 for its WF-005 limb and S-21 for the CAP-023 breadth, and this row consumes the rule for WF-017 rather than redefining it. Audit Evidence is a `security_audit` record and is not Evidence.
- observability: The eight Incident events with their exact playbook, step, predicate and observation hashes carry the incident investigation traceability that CAP-023 requires. Severity is observable at declaration through `IncidentSeverityAssigned` carrying its severity predicate hash and machine reason code; failed-step progress through `IncidentRecoveryFailed` carrying the exact failed step, reason and rollback result; restoration through the sequence-one and sequence-two check rows and `IncidentRestorationVerified` on the sequence-two pass only. The critical or high operational escalation record for the current failed-step generation is the human-attention signal and exists even when no human is then assigned. Alert queue depth, worker leases, observation timing and step latency are technical telemetry, are retained under restricted telemetry, and are asserted absent from the domain event stream.
- retention: Incident records, their step attempts, approvals, restoration checks, custody and access logs and every denied attempt are `security_audit` records retained for 7 years from 7 elapsed years after the record's terminal transition, destroyed irreversibly only with two-person security approval, per 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md. PRULE-037 requires the `security_audit` retention contract for every Incident. The canonical request hash and terminal result are retained for the resulting resource's retained lifetime plus 30 days, or 30 days for a rejected command, subject to longer legal retention. The window itself is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states no new one.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: `incident.respond` absent denies declaration; an OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator and Read-Only Executive Buyer are each denied it, and only the incident Service Identity may act as a service. TYP-SEC: every severity predicate and tie - confirmed or suspected cross-Organization disclosure, credential compromise, irreversible integrity loss and complete platform-wide protected-workflow outage each yield `critical`; one or more Organizations unable to complete a protected workflow, or integrity at risk without confirmed loss, yields `high`; a partial or degraded protected workflow with a safe workaround yields `medium`; no current protected-workflow or customer-data impact yields `low`; when multiple predicates match, strongest severity wins. TYP-DATA: severity is server-derived from the frozen trigger and is never accepted from the client. TYP-DATA: alert-emitted and manually declared candidates both produce exactly one Incident; a manual declaration below 20 or above 2,000 reason characters is rejected. TYP-DATA: declaration idempotently CREATES OR CORRELATES one Incident - a replayed declaration returns the stored Incident and correlation, not a second record. TYP-DATA: an exact `incident-diagnostic-interim-v1` hash permits its three steps in order; an unknown, altered or missing artifact is `recovery_playbook_unavailable` and permits ONLY Incident creation plus critical operational escalation - asserted with no step attempt row, no mutation and no step event. TYP-DATA: `capture_diagnostic_snapshot` reads only the Incident's already-authorized telemetry and Evidence references, seals their IDs and digests, and completes within 60 seconds; the 60-second bound is asserted at the boundary and one either side. TYP-SEC: `capture_diagnostic_snapshot` reaches no reference the Incident was not already authorized to read. TYP-DATA: `link_named_remediation` records a NULLABLE command or result reference from an existing WF-005, WF-006, WF-013, WF-014 or WF-016 action and performs no mutation itself; a null link leaves the Incident Open with the exact link gap recorded. TYP-SEC: prohibited direct mutation - a cross-Organization read, a restricted-Evidence export, a credential revoke or rotation, an Account security lifecycle action, a destructive data action and a security-policy change are each REFUSED to the baseline playbook and each requires entry into its named workflow with that workflow's permission, scope, support or approval, state-version and idempotency contract; asserted as no side effect plus a denial audit event. TYP-SEC: `incident.respond` grants none of those permissions, and no High-Risk Approval substitutes for the underlying target-workflow permission. TYP-DATA: `verify_trigger_cleared` changes Open to Mitigated ONLY when the immediate observation proves the frozen predicate false; a passing predicate leaves the Incident Open. TYP-DATA: the immediate and exact-five-minute restoration race - at exactly five elapsed minutes the lifecycle service's second observation WINS over a concurrent closure command; asserted at the exact boundary and one either side. TYP-DATA: two passing observations with identical predicate and version permit Mitigated to Resolved; a version or predicate mismatch between the two observations permits nothing. TYP-SEC: distinct critical and high closure - resolution of a `critical` or `high` Incident by the same SecurityOperator is refused; a different active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve` is required, and a holder whose grant is not the protected explicit grant is refused. TYP-DATA: `medium` and `low` resolution needs no second approver. TYP-DATA: `IncidentRestorationVerified` is created for the sequence-two row ONLY when its outcome is `passed`, carries `to_state=passed` and both observation hashes, and creates no Incident `restoration_verified` state or substitute lifecycle flag. TYP-DATA: `incident_restoration_checks` is unique on `(incident_id, trigger_predicate_sha256, observation_sequence)`; a redelivered timer produces no second row. TYP-DATA: the failure path - a denied, stale, failed or unverifiable step leaves the Incident Open or Mitigated, records the exact failed step, reason and rollback result, emits `IncidentRecoveryFailed`, and never marks restoration complete. TYP-OBS: failed-step escalation - a critical or high failure creates EXACTLY ONE operational escalation record for the current failed-step generation; repeated failures within that generation create no second record and a new generation creates its own. TYP-DATA: a `medium` or `low` failure creates no escalation record. TYP-SEC: stale and replay - a step, observation or resolution with a stale `expected_state_version` performs no side effect; exact replay of a step returns the ORIGINAL step attempt, executes the step no second time, and consumes no approval; reuse under a different canonical request hash is `idempotency_conflict`. TYP-SEC: the hypothetical high-risk fixture, exercised even though `incident-diagnostic-interim-v1` declares no high-risk step - the approval carries `approval_id`, Incident and Organization, playbook and step versions, target type and ID, requested action, expected target state version, canonical command request hash, requester and approver Accounts, `approved_at_utc`, `expires_at_utc=approved_at_utc+1 hour`, nullable `used_by_step_attempt_id` and approval state version; the approver is a DIFFERENT active SecurityOperator holding protected `security.investigation.approve`; the complete step command and the atomic step-start plus approval-consumption transaction commit strictly before expiry; at EXACT expiry the approval-expiry check wins; step start atomically sets `used_by_step_attempt_id`; a changed hash, version or scope, a self-approval, a reuse and an expired approval each perform NO side effect; each retry or different high-risk action requires a NEW approval; exact replay returns the original step attempt and neither consumes the approval nor executes the step twice; and the approval replaces no required Support Session, affected-Organization customer approval or emergency predicate. TYP-SEC: a high-risk approval never substitutes for the underlying target-workflow permission - the fixture asserts the underlying permission is checked independently. TYP-DATA: `incident_step_attempts` starts directly in `running`; the only edges are `running -> succeeded` and `running -> failed`; no pending row and no pending-to-running event exists. TYP-SEC: OD-013 Option 1 - every Incident record is owned by exactly one Organization and every Incident event carries that Organization's nonnull `organization_id`. TYP-DATA: an incident affecting N Organizations produces EXACTLY N Organization-scoped Incident records and N sets of Organization-scoped events sharing one `correlation_id`; no platform-owned Incident record and no platform-scoped event is produced. TYP-SEC: the shared `correlation_id` grants no access to another Organization's data; a read attempted across the correlation is denied. TYP-DATA: no producer emits with null, a sentinel, an invented platform tenant, an arbitrarily selected Organization or an omitted tenant field; each is asserted rejected rather than defaulted. TYP-SEC: the Incident contract field list is exhaustive and contains NO `emergency_customer_access` field; a command, payload or service attempting to set, default, infer or synthesize one is refused and commits nothing. TYP-SEC: incident authority confers no cross-tenant access - a cross-Organization Support Session without the affected tenant's `support.session.customer_approve` and without an active approved Emergency Access Grant returns `F1-AUTH-403`, creates no Support Session and performs no side effect; the Grant is exercised outside the Incident and S-24 emits no `EmergencyAccess*` event. TYP-SEC: no customer-notification behaviour on any emergency path is asserted, because that limb of OD-012 remains outstanding pending qualified legal review. TYP-SEC: no Incident read surface exists - `incident.respond` is not accepted as collection or detail read authority and `QRY-030` and `QRY-031` return nothing, per OD-020's deny-by-default for security, administrative and internal operational objects. TYP-OBS: no hidden action - every step, approval, denial, rollback, both restoration observations and every denied attempt appear in the audit record with the trigger and telemetry hash, severity predicate, playbook and step version, expected and actual state, and timeline. TYP-OBS: the eight WF-017 events are exactly `IncidentRaised`, `IncidentSeverityAssigned`, `IncidentRecoveryStepStarted`, `IncidentRecoveryStepCompleted`, `IncidentRecoveryFailed`, `IncidentMitigated`, `IncidentRestorationVerified` and `IncidentResolved`; alert queue depth, worker leases, observation timing and step latency are asserted ABSENT from the domain event stream. TYP-OBS: incident-step recovery is recovery-complete only with a nonnull `recovery_of_id`, a positive `recovery_generation`, the authorized recovery command ID and the earlier terminal reason; an uncorrelated second 'recovered' event and a recovery claim from retry telemetry alone are each asserted absent. TYP-E2E: alert candidate to declaration to three ordered diagnostic steps to immediate pass to five-minute pass to critical resolution by a distinct approver, asserting the exact Incident state, records and events at each commit. TYP-E2E: declaration under a missing playbook artifact yields Incident plus escalation and no step, end to end. TYP-E2E: an N-Organization incident yields N independent Incident timelines correlated by one `correlation_id` with no cross-tenant read at any step. TYP-DATA: `incident_decisions` is unique on `(incident_id, decision_type, input_sha256)`. TYP-SEC: `SecurityIncidentCustomerActionRequired` is asserted NOT produced by any S-24 command or step, because WF-017's Domain Events enumeration omits it and names no producer for it.
- rollout: S-24 depends on S-07 for the Crawl recovery surface that an incident links to as a named remediation, and gates nothing. It discharges only the WF-017 limb: CAP-023's WF-018 limb and its capability row MTX-023, the PRULE-037 and PRULE-038 rows MTX-088 and MTX-089, the Support Session lifecycle and the entire Emergency Access Grant break-glass contract are owned by S-21; the PRULE-009 and PRULE-022 rows MTX-060 and MTX-073 are owned by S-07 and S-21 and are consumed here for their WF-017 limbs only. No withheld limb exists on this row: OD-012, OD-013 and OD-020 are each resolved and are contracted as ratified. Two follow-on corrections are outside S-24's authority and are recorded rather than made - schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md still carries the pre-OD-013 `incidents.platform_wide` boolean and nullable Organization columns, and APPLICATION_LAYER.md and API_CONTRACTS.md still defer platform-wide Incident emission under the retired `UPSTREAM-V1-EVENT-SCOPE-001`. No predecessor behaviour exists.

### MTX-043 - AC-WF-018 (WF: WF-018)

- Slice: S-21
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#wf-018-investigate-and-audit-security-or-compliance-events
- interface type: Application commands plus one service-only bounded collection loop: open an Investigation against a frozen query plan and required-input set, collect each required input page under that Organization's frozen Support Session, publish an immutable versioned report, and close it under a distinct approver. The collector is service-only and has no HTTP route. The workflow reads, orders and seals records; it executes no remediation, because WF-018's Recovery Path routes remediation through WF-017 or another named workflow and never out of the report.
- route: Not applicable - Volume I defines the commands, their permissions and their envelopes but no HTTP path, method or transport. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline, which already carries `POST /api/v1/investigations` against `security.investigate`, `POST /api/v1/investigations/:id/reports` against `security.investigate` and `POST /api/v1/investigations/:id/closure` against `security.investigation.approve`; this row neither renames nor re-derives them. Those three rows are still marked 'deferred under `UPSTREAM-V1-EVENT-SCOPE-001`', a blocker the same document records as 'Resolved by ADR-020'; the deferral is stale prose and correcting it is API_CONTRACTS.md's own controlled change, not this row's. The collector has no route at all: `CollectInvestigationInput` enters from the `investigation_collect` control-queue job with a verified service identity and a persisted ScheduledAction cause, and an HTTP collector would be a surface with no actor behind it. No Investigation read route exists under OD-020.
- request schema: Open: `request_kind` one of `security_anomaly`, `compliance_inquiry`, `formal_audit`; the sorted-unique approved `organization_ids`; `required_inputs` as one or more `InvestigationInputSpec` each naming its Organization, `input_type`, sorted-unique `resource_ids` and `required` flag; `interval_start_utc` strictly before `interval_end_utc`; `legal_purpose` of 20-2,000 characters; `classification_ceiling` one of `public`, `internal`, `confidential`, `restricted`; and, when the request spans Organizations, one active Support Session ID per affected Organization. Each nested Organization occurs exactly once in the top-level set. Collect: the registered checkpoint identity only - Investigation, Organization, required input, query hash and page sequence; the collector selects no scope of its own. Publish: `completeness`, the committed `investigation_audit_evidence_item_ids` and `gap_ids` from the frozen plan, `conclusions` and `proposed_actions`; report version and digest are server-derived from canonical content and are never client-supplied. Close: the `report_digest` and the expected Investigation state version.
- response schema: The Investigation with its contract fields: ID, its one owning Organization, the frozen Support Session IDs when cross-Organization, request, actor and legal purpose, the frozen time interval and required investigation input set per Organization, status and state version, collected Investigation Audit Evidence Item IDs, gap records, completeness `complete`, `partial` or `insufficient`, report version and digest, proposed actions, closure approver, and correlation ID. Each Investigation Audit Evidence Item returns its immutable input reference, collection query hash, collected-by and time, content digest, classification, custody transfers and access log. A report returns its version, scope, completeness, ordered timeline, item list and digests, explicit gaps, conclusions separated from facts, and proposed actions.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I; see route. The Volume II physical adapter is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md.
- command: `Workflows::Wf018::OpenInvestigation`, `Workflows::Wf018::CollectInvestigationInput`, `Workflows::Wf018::PublishInvestigationReport`, `Workflows::Wf018::CloseInvestigation`, per the APPLICATION_LAYER.md command registry. `CollectInvestigationInput` is service-only. No command in this registry mutates a customer resource, executes a remediation, or reaches an Organization outside the page's frozen Support Session.
- command input: As request_schema, inside the Logical Command Envelope: `command_id`, `idempotency_key`, `command_type`, `schema_version`, exactly one of `actor_id` or `service_identity_id`, the nonnull `organization_id` of the one owning Organization, target type and ID, requested action, `expected_state_version` for every existing target, every policy version used to authorize or constrain the command, `requested_at_utc`, `correlation_id`, `causation_id`, and one schema-valid `command_payload`. The nested Organization inside an `InvestigationInputSpec` is semantic investigation scope and is never the envelope tenant. Interval, expiry and race decisions use the server-assigned `authorization_check_at_utc`, never the client instant.
- command output: One Logical Result: `outcome=success` with the Investigation, item, gap or report identifiers and committed state version, or `outcome=failure` with `error_class`, `error_code`, one contract-defined `reason_code`, `severity`, `retryable` and one `recovery_action`. Completeness is a field on the Investigation and never alters the result severity.
- actor: A SecurityOperator holding Organization-scoped `security.investigate` submits the request with a 20-2,000 character reason. The Permission Baseline gives `security.investigate` to SecurityOperator only, denies it to OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator and the Read-Only Executive Buyer alike, and admits the Service Identity as 'incident service only'. The investigation collector service identity executes each page. Closure requires a DIFFERENT SecurityOperator holding protected `security.investigation.approve`; per the WORKFLOW_SPECIFICATIONS.md preamble, a Chief Security action in WF-018 means exactly that permission holder. Every investigation Export additionally requires a different active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve` as its single-use approver.
- organization scope: Exactly one Organization owns each Investigation record, per OD-013 Option 1 as integrated by ADR-020. An investigation spanning N Organizations is N Organization-scoped Investigation records, each with its own frozen Support Session, required-input set, items, gaps and report, all linked by one shared `correlation_id` that expresses coordination and confers no ownership and no access. There is no platform-owned or cross-Organization-owned Investigation record. The ability to conduct an investigation spanning many Organizations is preserved in full and is exercised as orchestration over those Organization-owned records.
- aggregate: Investigation is the root; the frozen Organization scope, the ordered required-input plan, the collection checkpoint, the Investigation Audit Evidence Item with its custody and access log, the gap and the versioned report with its items are its children. An Investigation Audit Evidence Item is Audit Evidence, not an Evidence record: WF-018 says so expressly, and 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md classes Audit Evidence as `security_audit` while Evidence metadata is `product_history`. Investigation is not a DM-REQ-001 core entity - that list is a MUST-include floor - but a lifecycle-bearing auxiliary domain record whose contract, fields, states and events WF-018 defines in full.
- aggregate boundary: The Investigation root owns the frozen plan, collection, ordering, gaps, report versions and closure. The boundary that matters is what it may NOT reach: no query leaves the one Organization the page's frozen Support Session names, no report executes an action, and no Export is packaged here. Remediation enters WF-017 or another named workflow with THAT workflow's permission, scope, approval, state-version and idempotency contract, and only its immutable result may be referenced. The Export enters WF-016 with `export.create`, its own entitlement reservation and its own single-use approval; the Export lifecycle row is owned by S-20. The Support Session lifecycle transitions belong to WF-013 and land with S-23; this row freezes an already-active session and effects no transition on it.
- value objects: InvestigationId, InvestigationState restricted to `open`, `reported`, `closed`, RequestKind restricted to `security_anomaly`, `compliance_inquiry`, `formal_audit`, Completeness restricted to `complete`, `partial`, `insufficient`, ClassificationCeiling restricted to `public`, `internal`, `confidential`, `restricted`, LegalPurpose of 20-2,000 characters, FrozenInterval, InputPlanSha256, QuerySha256, PageSequence, PageCursor as the exclusive `(occurred_at, event_id)` tuple, ContentSha256, CustodySha256, KmsMetadataSha256, GapReasonCode, ReportVersion, ReportDigest, SupportSessionId, CorrelationId, ExpectedStateVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - WF-018 names no domain service. Completeness is a pure function over the terminal state of the frozen required-input set: `complete` when every required input yielded validated records, `partial` when at least one but not all did, `insufficient` when none did. Record ordering is the total order `(occurred_at, event_id)`. The report digest is a hash over canonical report content. None is a collaborator with its own lifecycle.
- repository: `InvestigationRepository`, `InvestigationOrganizationRepository`, `InvestigationRequiredInputRepository`, `InvestigationCollectionCheckpointRepository`, `InvestigationAuditEvidenceItemRepository`, `InvestigationAccessLogRepository`, `InvestigationGapRepository`, `InvestigationReportRepository`, `InvestigationReportItemRepository`; `SupportSessionRepository` read-only for the frozen session, scope and current expiry or revocation. Every read resolves within the one Organization the page is authorized for.
- persistence model: Canonical `investigations`, `investigation_organizations`, `investigation_required_inputs`, `investigation_collection_checkpoints`, `investigation_audit_evidence_items`, `investigation_access_logs`, `investigation_gaps`, `investigation_reports` and `investigation_report_items` per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. `investigations` carries `state CHECK ('open','reported','closed')`. `investigation_collection_checkpoints` is the `WORK-CLAIM` table for `investigation_collect`, carrying the immutable `query_sha256`, a positive `page_sequence`, an exclusive cursor whose `cursor_occurred_at` and `cursor_event_id` are both NULL exactly for page one and both nonnull otherwise, page record count 0..1000 and canonical byte count 0..8388608 NULL until terminal, staged object and KMS digests NULL until staged, mutually exclusive committed item and gap IDs, and `state CHECK ('pending','claimed','item_committed','gap_committed')`. `investigation_gaps` is unique on required input and report version; `investigation_reports` is unique on version and digest. The canonical schema's nullable primary-Organization column and cross-org boolean on `investigations` are pre-OD-013 artifacts that Volume I now overrides: under ratified Option 1 every Investigation row resolves to exactly one nonnull Organization and no cross-org-owned row exists. That schema's `investigation_collection_checkpoints` note gating row creation on `UPSTREAM-V1-EVENT-SCOPE-001` cites a retired blocker. This row contracts the Option 1 shape and records both divergences; correcting the DDL is the schema's own controlled change and not S-21's to make.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - the canonical schema owns the nine Investigation tables and their CHECK constraints. Bringing the nullable primary-Organization column and the cross-org boolean into line with the ratified OD-013 Option 1 shape, and removing the retired-blocker gate on checkpoint creation, are controlled changes to schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md, not a product migration this row may issue.
- transaction boundary: Open: one transaction that creates exactly one Organization-owned Investigation, freezes the authorized query plan, the interval, the classification ceiling, the ordered required-input set and the per-Organization Support Session ID, and creates one `investigation_collection_checkpoints` row plus one due-now `investigation_input_collect` action per required input. Page: three phases in a fixed order. The authorized read transaction revalidates the frozen Support Session, `security.investigate`, the Organization and resource set, the interval, the classification ceiling, the query hash and current expiry or revocation at the read transaction's PostgreSQL timestamp, materializes a deeply immutable page DTO with its query and result digests, records the access decision and closes. The encrypted staging object is then written OUTSIDE any transaction and its size, digest and KMS metadata are validated - the object write never sits inside a database transaction. A final restricted transaction appends either the immutable Investigation Audit Evidence Item with its custody record or the exact gap, terminalizes the page, and either creates exactly one due-now action for the next sequence or marks that required input complete. Publish: one transaction writing an immutable report version referencing only committed item and gap IDs from the frozen plan. Close: one transaction that verifies report digest, gaps, proposed actions and access trail under the distinct approver and commits `Reported -> Closed`.
- concurrency: The checkpoint identity `(investigation_id, organization_id, required_input_id, query_sha256, page_sequence)` is unique, so a redelivered or concurrent page cannot commit twice and cannot fork the cursor. The query, scope, Support Session, authorization inputs and cursor are immutable once written; item and gap identity, counts, object proofs and terminal state commit exactly once. Expired preterminal ownership may return `claimed -> pending` ONLY through claim recovery, reusing the same page and object identity and incrementing no page sequence; a committed `item_committed` or `gap_committed` state never reopens. Every command carries `expected_state_version`, so a stale open, publish or close commits nothing. The decisive race is the Support Session against the collector: a session expiring or being revoked before a query denies THAT query at the read transaction's server timestamp and creates its explicit gap, and it never permits another Organization's session to substitute, so concurrent expiry degrades completeness rather than leaking an out-of-session read.
- idempotency: Open is keyed by the Logical Command Envelope's create scope - Organization, command type and idempotency key - so a replayed open returns the stored Investigation rather than creating a second record for the same request; for an existing target the scope adds target resource type and ID. Collection is idempotent on the checkpoint identity rather than on the delivery: process loss before terminalization reuses the same page and object identity, and a committed item or gap is a duplicate no-op. Report publication is append-only - a new version is a NEW report row, prior versions and gaps never mutate, and a replayed publish returns the stored version rather than minting a second one. Close is idempotent on the Investigation and its expected state version; a replayed close returns the stored closure. Reuse with a different canonical request hash is `idempotency_conflict`. Before returning retained identifiers an exact replay reauthorizes the current actor and applies current field redaction; denial returns `F1-AUTH-403` with no retained payload while the stored outcome is unchanged.
- background job: `investigation_collect`, a `control`-class job that collects one bounded page for one frozen required input under its exact Organization Support Session, dispatched from the `investigation_input_collect` ScheduledAction that `OpenInvestigation` persists per required input. It claims one `investigation_collection_checkpoints` row keyed by Investigation, required input, query hash and page-cursor generation; no handler may claim a different table or hold two transport leases for one invocation. A nonterminal page returns at most 1,000 records and at most 8 MiB of canonical bytes ordered by `(occurred_at, event_id)`, persists the last tuple as the exclusive next-page cursor and creates exactly one due-now action for the next sequence. The bounds segment one logical required input and NEVER turn omitted records into a complete result. The job is defined by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md and this row does not redefine it; that document's remark that creation and execution 'remains blocked by event-scope ambiguity' cites the retired `UPSTREAM-V1-EVENT-SCOPE-001` and is stale against its own Investigation collection registry, which already carries the Option 1 shape.
- queue: The `control` queue class, per the Volume I Background Processing action registry as realised in BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md. Queue identity, worker topology and lease mechanics are owned by that document; this row does not rename them. Collection has no public or service HTTP route and no direct Sidekiq enqueue.
- retry policy: WF-018 defines no automatic retry schedule and no hidden retry. Its Recovery Path is exhaustive and operator-driven: correct the request, restore an authorized investigation input, or append newly collected Investigation Audit Evidence Items in a NEW report version. Collection has no implicit retry with a new query identity - process loss before terminalization reclaims the SAME page and object identity under the lease rules rather than reissuing under a new one. A denied, expired or revoked Support Session, a missing input, an object failure or an integrity failure creates the Volume I gap; a gap is a terminal outcome for that input, not an instruction to retry, and the collector does not read another Organization or silently skip a page in response.
- terminal failure: Per the Failure Path: invalid scope, a missing or mismatched cross-Organization Support Session, an unauthorized query, a digest or custody failure, stale state, or a missing required report field changes NO Investigation state and records a denial or integrity outcome. A session that expires after Investigation creation denies subsequent affected-Organization queries and produces explicit gaps, so a report may become `partial` or `insufficient` but no out-of-session read occurs. Per the Alternate Path, partial or insufficient collection still produces a Reported outcome with the exact completeness value and gap reasons and CANNOT claim a complete reconstruction. Missing data is never synthesized: a gap is created without fabricating an event.
- reconciliation: Every required input in the frozen plan reaches exactly one terminal outcome - a validated custody-linked Investigation Audit Evidence Item, or an explicit gap. Completeness is then derived exactly as WF-018 defines and the Investigation remains Open until the separately authorized report command. That total function over the frozen plan is what makes an incomplete investigation auditable rather than approximated: there is no third state in which an input is quietly absent. Newly collected Items append in a NEW report version; prior report versions and gaps never mutate, so the history of what was known when is reconstructible. Report publication can reference only committed item and gap IDs from the frozen plan.
- domain events: Exactly five, all named by WF-018: `SecurityInvestigationOpened`, `InvestigationAuditEvidenceCollected`, `InvestigationGapRecorded`, `SecurityInvestigationReported`, `SecurityInvestigationClosed`. Under OD-013 Option 1 every one carries its Organization's nonnull `organization_id`, and an investigation spanning N Organizations emits N Organization-scoped sets linked by one shared `correlation_id`. The five `SupportSession*` events are enumerated in WF-013's Domain Events and are NOT WF-018 events; this row emits none of them. The five `EmergencyAccess*` events belong to the Emergency Cross-Organization Access Contract and are contracted on MTX-023, not here. Collector queue depth, worker leases, page latency and staging-object timing are technical telemetry and MUST NOT be emitted as domain events.
- event payload: The canonical Logical Event Envelope with `workflow_id=WF-018` and a nonnull `organization_id` resolving to the one owning Organization - the bootstrap substitution is reserved to WF-001 and is unavailable here - plus `correlation_id` linking the coordinated per-Organization records. Affected entity is `investigation` for `SecurityInvestigationOpened`, `SecurityInvestigationReported` and `SecurityInvestigationClosed`, and `investigation_input` for `InvestigationAuditEvidenceCollected` and `InvestigationGapRecorded`. The `investigation` extra schema carries sorted-unique `approved_organization_ids`, sorted-unique `support_session_ids` and a nullable `report_id`; `approved_organization_ids` is investigation SCOPE and is never record ownership. The `investigation_input` extra schema carries `investigation_id`, `input_type`, `coverage_status` of `collected` or `gap`, a nullable `content_sha256` and a nullable `gap_reason_code`, with exactly one of the digest or the gap reason nonnull - which is the envelope-level expression of the same rule that forbids synthesizing a missing record. `event_profile` follows the deterministic first-match order: `created` for the opened and per-input events with no prior aggregate, `state_transition` for reported and closed. Root `reason_code` is an exact machine code copied byte-for-byte from its named source or null; human `legal_purpose`, reason, conclusion and rationale text stay in the restricted retained and audit records and never populate it. Raw collected record payloads, secrets and restricted fields above the frozen classification ceiling never appear.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf018` is the sole producer of all five events; `InvestigationAuditEvidenceCollected` and `InvestigationGapRecorded` are committed by the investigation collector service identity through `CollectInvestigationInput` within that workflow. No other workflow or service emits any of them, and this workflow emits no `SupportSession*` or `EmergencyAccess*` event.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - WF-018 names no consumer for its five events, and the `notification-policy-v1` baseline route table lists none of them among its triggers. The report is read by the closure approver through the command result, not by reacting to `SecurityInvestigationReported`.
- serializer: Not applicable - Volume I defines no transport exposure for the Investigation aggregate and, under OD-020, Investigation read remains deny-by-default with `QRY-032 InvestigationDetail` deferred, so there is no authorized read projection to serialize. Physical event serialization is owned by the Volume II baseline.
- authorization entry point: Each application command, before mutation, and AGAIN on EVERY page: WF-018 Primary Path step 1 requires that every query recheck Organization, interval, classification, incident scope and the affected Organization's frozen Support Session, and BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md restates that each page separately validates the frozen session, `security.investigate`, the Organization and resource set, the interval, the classification ceiling, the query hash and current expiry or revocation at the read transaction's PostgreSQL timestamp. An authority valid at Investigation creation is never trusted at collection time - that is precisely why a session expiring mid-investigation yields gaps rather than a completed read. Closure reauthorizes the distinct approver against the Investigation at the closure commit.
- permission checks: `security.investigate`, Organization-scoped, held by a SecurityOperator; the Permission Baseline denies it to every other role and admits only the incident Service Identity. It is a protected grant, so it requires approval within 24 hours by a SecurityOperator other than the requester and carries an active expiry; revocation takes effect on the next protected request, authorization caches and active sessions reflect it within 60 seconds, and a running collection rechecks at each page and stops before the next protected read. A one-Organization request is confined to that permission scope. A cross-Organization request additionally requires one active Support Session per affected Organization, each naming the requester, `security.investigate`, every required input and resource, and the affected Organization, and carrying the exact customer approval or emergency-access bypass plus the distinct SecurityOperator approval the Support Session contract requires; no standing role and no single session grants access to another Organization. Closure requires a DIFFERENT holder of protected `security.investigation.approve`; self-approved closure is denied and audited. Every SecurityOperator investigation Export is high-risk and additionally requires the WF-016 single-use approval plus `export.create`; `security.investigate` substitutes for neither. The Permission Baseline enumerates no read action for the Investigation, Support Session or Emergency Access Grant object class, and none may be inferred: under OD-020 those are the security and administrative objects that remain deny-by-default pending a separate owner decision.
- tenant boundary: One Investigation record, one Organization, one nonnull `organization_id` on every event and audit record. Cross-Organization support and investigation process ONE Organization per transaction with its exact Support Session, and the control-plane bridge rows are global restricted metadata that grant no query seeing several Organizations at once; the restricted work-dispatch binding establishes only the one Organization context for that page. `f1_enter_scoped_security_context` proves the actor or worker-claimed dispatch binding and then validates the exact active Support Session and Organization scope before RLS; it accepts no caller-supplied actor, service identity or scope. The shared `correlation_id` across N coordinated Investigations expresses coordination and grants no access to another Organization's data. A session for one Organization can never authorize a query in another, and an expired session never falls back to another Organization's.
- error contract: Authorization: `F1-AUTH-403` for absent `security.investigate`, a missing, expired, revoked or mismatched cross-Organization Support Session, a query outside the frozen resource set or above the frozen classification ceiling, a self-approved closure, or an unscoped or combined multi-Organization Export - each emitting one authorization audit event and performing no state change; a Project-scoped command with null or cross-Organization `project_id` is `F1-AUTH-403 / tenant_mismatch`. Domain: `F1-DOMAIN-409` for a stale `expected_state_version`, a duplicate semantic target or a race loss, and `F1-DOMAIN-409 / idempotency_conflict` for reuse under a different canonical request hash. Validation: `F1-VALIDATION-400 / command_schema_unsupported` for an unsupported major, a missing required field, a changed required-field type or a prohibited field, creating only the audited command outcome; a `legal_purpose` outside 20-2,000 characters, an interval whose start is not before its end, a duplicate nested Organization, or a report missing a required field is `F1-VALIDATION-400` and changes no Investigation state. Data integrity: `F1-DATA-409` for a digest, custody or immutable-data failure on a collected page or a report reference to an uncommitted item. Envelope derivation follows the fixed precedence - authentication, authorization or scope denial, a named dependency reason, a named attempt or deadline timeout reason, capacity, rate-limit, subsystem reasons, request-shape validation, domain state and concurrency, then data-integrity - and if no row selects unambiguously the workflow returns `F1-DATA-409 / contract_invariant_unmapped` and commits no side effect. No invented reason code is exposed outward, and no new error code is minted for the emergency-access denial path.
- audit record: Per WF-018 Audit and Observability, exhaustively: the request and query hashes, the input-by-input outcomes, every Investigation Audit Evidence Item with its custody and access record, the ordering inputs, every gap, the completeness derivation, every report version and digest, the distinct approval, the linked remediation, and the Export IDs. Every denied query, denied closure and denied Export is audited with its exact reason. The access log records actor or service, Support Session ID, action, `accessed_at` and outcome per item, which is what makes the access trail the closure approver verifies a real record rather than a reconstruction. Audit Evidence is a `security_audit` record and is not Evidence.
- observability: The five WF-018 events with their exact query hashes, item digests, gap reason codes, completeness value and report digests carry the incident investigation traceability and gap reporting CAP-023 requires. Collection progress is observable per input through `InvestigationAuditEvidenceCollected` and `InvestigationGapRecorded`, each carrying `coverage_status` of `collected` or `gap`; completeness is observable at `SecurityInvestigationReported`; the distinct approval at `SecurityInvestigationClosed`. A `partial` or `insufficient` outcome is a first-class observable signal rather than an absence: the gap records name which required inputs failed and why. Collector queue depth, worker leases, page latency and staging-object timing are technical telemetry, are retained under `operational_telemetry`, and are asserted absent from the domain event stream.
- retention: Investigations, required inputs, collection checkpoints, Investigation Audit Evidence Items, custody and access logs, gaps, report versions and every denied attempt are `security_audit` records retained 7 years, on a cursor of 7 elapsed years after the record's terminal transition, destroyed irreversibly only with two-person security approval, per 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md. Under OD-011 that window is the fixed approved baseline and no customer-configurable retention exists or may be inferred. The canonical request hash and terminal result are retained for the resulting resource's retained lifetime plus 30 days, or 30 days for a rejected command, subject to longer legal retention. The window itself is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states no new one. Routine retention-expiry destruction at the 7-year maximum is OD-031's withheld limb and is owned by S-23, not by this row; see MTX-088.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: `security.investigate` absent denies the request; an OrganizationAdmin, MarketingOperator, TechnicalImplementer, BillingOperator and Read-Only Executive Buyer are each denied it, and only the incident Service Identity may act as a service. TYP-SEC: `security.investigate` is a protected grant - an unapproved or expired grant authorizes nothing, and a revocation stops a running collection at the next page rather than after it. TYP-DATA: a one-Organization request is confined to that permission scope and needs no Support Session. TYP-SEC: the cross-Organization Support Session set - a COMPLETE set with one active session per affected Organization proceeds; a MISSING session for any affected Organization, an EXPIRED session, and a MISMATCHED session naming a different requester, permission, input, resource or Organization each make the request invalid with no Investigation state change. TYP-SEC: one Organization's session never substitutes for another's; a session for Organization A authorizes no query against Organization B, asserted as denial plus gap rather than fallback. TYP-DATA: session expiry or revocation BEFORE a query denies that query and creates its explicit gap; a session expiring AFTER Investigation creation denies subsequent affected-Organization queries and produces gaps, so the report becomes `partial` or `insufficient` and NO out-of-session read occurs - asserted at the exact expiry instant and one either side. TYP-DATA: every query rechecks Organization, interval, classification, incident scope and the frozen Support Session, at the read transaction's server timestamp and not at Investigation creation. TYP-DATA: completeness is exhaustive and exact - `complete` only when EVERY required input yielded validated records; `partial` when at least one but not all did; `insufficient` when none did; each boundary asserted with one input either side. TYP-DATA: a `partial` or `insufficient` outcome still produces a Reported outcome with the exact completeness value and gap reasons and cannot claim a complete reconstruction. TYP-DATA: no synthesis - a missing, expired, denied or integrity-invalid required input produces exactly ONE explicit gap and no fabricated event; the timeline contains no record that no input yielded. TYP-DATA: every required input in the frozen plan reaches exactly one terminal outcome, an item or a gap; no input is quietly absent. TYP-DATA: ordering is `(occurred_at, event_id)` with correlation and causation linked; equal occurred times break by event ID deterministically. TYP-DATA: page bounds - at most 1,000 records and at most 8 MiB of canonical bytes per nonterminal page; page one has null cursor fields and every later page has both nonnull; the last tuple persists as the exclusive next-page cursor; bounds segment one logical input and never turn omitted records into a complete result, asserted by a multi-page input yielding `complete`. TYP-DATA: checkpoint identity `(investigation_id, organization_id, required_input_id, query_sha256, page_sequence)` is unique - a redelivered or concurrent page commits no second row and does not fork the cursor. TYP-DATA: claim recovery returns `claimed -> pending` reusing the SAME page and object identity and incrementing no page sequence; `item_committed` and `gap_committed` never reopen. TYP-DATA: process loss before terminalization reuses the same page and object identity; a committed item or gap is a duplicate no-op. TYP-DATA: the object write happens OUTSIDE any transaction and its size, digest and KMS metadata are validated before the item commits; an object failure creates the gap rather than a partial item. TYP-SEC: the collected page never exceeds the frozen classification ceiling; a record above it is excluded under the ceiling rather than redacted into the timeline. TYP-DATA: each Investigation Audit Evidence Item stores its immutable input reference, collection query hash, collected-by and time, content digest, classification, custody transfers and access log; a digest or custody mismatch is an integrity outcome with no state change. TYP-SEC: an Investigation Audit Evidence Item is Audit Evidence retained as `security_audit` and is asserted NOT to be an Evidence record - it appears in no Evidence projection and consumes no `product_history` or `product_evidence_payload` contract. TYP-DATA: the report is immutable and versioned, containing scope, completeness, ordered timeline, item list and digests, explicit gaps, conclusions separated from facts, and proposed actions; a report missing a required field changes no state. TYP-DATA: report publication references ONLY committed item and gap IDs from the frozen plan; a reference to an uncommitted or foreign item is rejected. TYP-DATA: a later Investigation Audit Evidence Item appends a NEW report version; prior report versions and gaps are asserted unmutated. TYP-SEC: distinct closure - `Reported -> Closed` requires a DIFFERENT SecurityOperator holding protected `security.investigation.approve` who verifies report digest, gaps, proposed actions and access trail; self-approved closure is denied AND audited; a holder whose grant is not the protected explicit grant is refused. TYP-DATA: state transitions are exactly `Open -> Reported -> Closed`; no other edge exists and no closure occurs from Open. TYP-SEC: every SecurityOperator investigation Export is high-risk - it requires its own single-use approval carrying `approval_id`, Organization, requester, approver, logical format, exact object and field allowlists, request hash, `approved_at_utc`, `expires_at_utc` exactly 1 hour later, nullable `used_by_export_id` and state version, with a different active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve` as approver; reuse, altered content, self-approval and a retry without a new approval are each rejected before reservation or generation; an unscoped export is denied and audited. TYP-DATA: an approved cross-Organization Investigation creates one separately approved Organization-scoped Export PER Organization; a combined multi-Organization package is always rejected. TYP-SEC: remediation never executes from the report - a proposed action is inert, and the remediation runs through WF-017 or another named workflow with that workflow's own permission and contract; asserted as no side effect from publish or close. TYP-SEC: OD-013 Option 1 - every Investigation record is owned by exactly one Organization and every Investigation event carries that Organization's nonnull `organization_id`. TYP-DATA: an investigation spanning N Organizations produces EXACTLY N Organization-scoped Investigation records and N sets of Organization-scoped events sharing one `correlation_id`, each separately authorized and tenant-scoped; no cross-Organization-owned record is produced. TYP-SEC: no record reaches another Organization's data by virtue of the shared `correlation_id`; a read attempted across the correlation is denied. TYP-DATA: no producer emits with null, a sentinel, an invented platform tenant, an arbitrarily selected Organization or an omitted tenant field; each is asserted rejected rather than defaulted. TYP-DATA: `investigation_input` events carry exactly one of `content_sha256` or `gap_reason_code` nonnull; both-null and both-nonnull are each rejected. TYP-SEC: no Investigation read surface exists - `security.investigate` is not accepted as collection or detail read authority and `QRY-032` returns nothing, per OD-020's deny-by-default for security and administrative objects. TYP-SEC: cross-Organization work processes ONE Organization per transaction with its exact Support Session; a query returning rows from two Organizations at once is asserted impossible, and `f1_enter_scoped_security_context` rejects a caller-supplied actor, service identity or scope. TYP-SEC: no customer-notification behaviour is asserted on any emergency-access path reached from an investigation, because that limb of OD-012 remains outstanding pending qualified legal review. TYP-OBS: the five WF-018 events are exactly `SecurityInvestigationOpened`, `InvestigationAuditEvidenceCollected`, `InvestigationGapRecorded`, `SecurityInvestigationReported` and `SecurityInvestigationClosed`; the five `SupportSession*` events and the five `EmergencyAccess*` events are asserted ABSENT from this workflow's stream, as are collector queue depth, worker leases, page latency and staging-object timing. TYP-OBS: the access trail records actor or service, Support Session ID, action, `accessed_at` and outcome for every item access, including denied ones. TYP-SEC: stale and replay - an open, publish or close with a stale `expected_state_version` performs no side effect; exact replay returns the stored result and mints no second Investigation, report version or closure; reuse under a different canonical request hash is `idempotency_conflict`; replay reauthorizes the current actor and applies current redaction before returning retained identifiers. TYP-E2E: a one-Organization formal audit from open through multi-page collection to a `complete` report to distinct closure, asserting exact state, records and events at each commit. TYP-E2E: a cross-Organization security anomaly over three Organizations where one session expires mid-collection yields three Organization-scoped Investigations under one `correlation_id`, the affected one `partial` with explicit gaps and no out-of-session read, end to end. TYP-E2E: a cross-Organization investigation export yields one separately approved Organization-scoped Export per Organization and rejects the combined package, end to end.
- rollout: S-21 depends on S-02 for the Organization and its baseline access policy, and gates nothing. It owns the WF-018 limb of CAP-023, PRULE-037 and PRULE-038 plus the entire Emergency Access Grant contract on MTX-023; the WF-017 limb of all three is owned by S-24 on MTX-042, the Support Session lifecycle transitions and their five events land with WF-013 on MTX-038 in S-23, the Export lifecycle with S-20, and PRULE-022 row MTX-073 is already complete under S-07 and is consumed here. The only withheld limb is OD-012's customer notification on emergency access, blocked pending qualified legal review. Six Volume II divergences are recorded rather than corrected; see `recorded_divergences`. No predecessor behaviour exists.

### MTX-051 - AC-SM-008 (SM: Verification Evidence)

- Slice: S-05
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#source-verification-state-transition
- interface type: State-model obligation. AC-SM-008 governs the Source verification transition and its evidence, not an interface.
- route: Not applicable - a state transition is not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the transition has no request of its own.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: The transition is effected only inside the `Workflows::Wf003` observation-completion transaction on a matched observation. No other command, job, service or administrative path may transition a Source to `verified`.
- command input: Not applicable - effected by the completion transaction.
- command output: Not applicable - effected by the completion transaction.
- actor: The observation completion path. No human actor writes the transition directly; a principal triggers an observation, and the matched predicate effects the transition.
- organization scope: The Source Organization.
- aggregate: Source, transitioned within the multi-root success commit.
- aggregate boundary: Source is a separate root from VerificationRequest; both, plus the scope policy and Evidence, commit atomically because Volume I forbids any of the success work items appearing without the others.
- value objects: SourceState (`proposed`, `verified`, `active`, `disabled`, `removed`), SourceStateVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - the transition is a guarded aggregate mutation inside the completion transaction.
- repository: `SourceRepository`.
- persistence model: The `sources` lifecycle status column. `proposed` is the state before verification; `verified` is the state after. No intermediate `verifying` or Boolean `is_verified` flag exists: Volume I defines a state machine, and collapsing it into a flag would lose the canonical state and its transition event.
- migration: Not applicable - the canonical `sources` table already carries the lifecycle status.
- transaction boundary: The observation-completion transaction. `Source.Proposed -> Source.Verified` commits with Request `verified`/`matched`, redelivery disablement, `SourceVerified` and `source-scope-interim-v1` materialization, or none of them commit.
- concurrency: Guarded by the Source state version and by the at-most-one-pending-Request-per-Source constraint. A concurrent second matched completion finds the Source no longer `proposed` and cannot transition it twice; the transition happens exactly once. A stale Source state version rejects the completion without side effects.
- idempotency: The transition is naturally idempotent through its state guard: it fires only from `proposed`. A repeated matched observation after success does not re-transition, does not re-emit `SourceVerified` and does not re-materialize the scope policy.
- background job: Not applicable - the transition is not a job; it occurs inside the completion transaction, which may be reached from an automated slot or an on-demand attempt.
- queue: Not applicable - not queued independently.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the transition itself is not retried. Its enclosing completion transaction is retried under the same reserved attempt identity.
- terminal failure: Mismatch or dependency failure MUST NOT change the Source lifecycle state. Expiry, cancellation and integrity failure all leave the Source `proposed`. There is no failed or rejected Source state reachable from verification.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - the transition is atomic with its Request decision, so Source state and Request status can never diverge.
- domain events: `SourceVerified`, exactly once, only inside the success commit. The transition never occurs without the event and the event never occurs without the transition.
- event payload: The canonical envelope with the Source identity and its resulting state version; no challenge material.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf003`, sole producer.
- event consumers: Not applicable at this row - the scope-policy materialization is part of the same transaction, not a consumer.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The observation-completion path, which is reachable only from an authorized Request; the transition itself exposes no independent entry point.
- permission checks: No permission transitions a Source directly. Authority is established at Request creation and at on-demand acceptance; the transition is a consequence of a matched predicate, not of an actor's permission.
- tenant boundary: The Source and its Request share an Organization by construction.
- error contract: A stale Source state version rejects the completion without side effects. A Source not in `proposed` cannot transition; a Request for such a Source is `source_not_proposed` at creation.
- audit record: The matched `verification_observation` Evidence is the audit anchor for the transition, and its Audit Evidence is retained separately.
- observability: `SourceVerified` and the matched observation telemetry with its reason code and latency.
- retention: The transition event and its Evidence are retained under their contracts; the window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: the Source is `proposed` before verification and `verified` after; no intermediate state and no Boolean flag substitutes for the canonical state. TYP-DATA: the transition fires exactly once - a repeated matched observation after success does not re-transition or re-emit. TYP-DATA: concurrent matched completions transition once; the loser finds the Source no longer proposed. TYP-DATA: a stale Source state version rejects the completion with no side effect. TYP-DATA: mismatch, dependency failure, expiry, cancellation and integrity failure each leave the Source `proposed`. TYP-DATA: the transition, Request `verified`/`matched`, redelivery disablement, `SourceVerified` and `source-scope-interim-v1` materialization commit together or not at all - each is asserted absent when the transaction fails. TYP-OBS: challenge redelivery and deletion, slot, skip, on-demand concurrency, count and cursor boundaries, the DNS and HTTP 4,096/4,097-byte hash and count rules, network and content reasons, match, expiry, cancel and integrity authority, transaction failure, second request, replay and authorization fixtures each produce exact Request, Source, scope-policy, Evidence and event behaviour. TYP-SEC: no plaintext token and no raw observation is retained at rest.
- rollout: As MTX-028.

### MTX-052 - AC-PRULE-001 (PRULE: PRULE-001)

- Slice: S-01
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-001-onboarding-interim-v1-enforcement
- interface type: Cross-branch product rule enforced inside the WF-001 commands and the WF-013 lifecycle commands. PRULE-001 governs CAP-001 and applies to WF-001 and WF-013.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-001 is a rule, not an interface.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf001::*` (this slice) and `Workflows::Wf013::*` (S-23). This row owns the WF-001 limb; the WF-013 limb is contracted in S-23.
- actor: As MTX-026 - the approved identity service for each branch's bound principal.
- organization scope: Exact Organization selection is mandatory; PRULE-001 forbids implicit Organization selection.
- persistence model: Not applicable - PRULE-001 introduces no persistent concept of its own; it constrains the records MTX-026 writes.
- transaction boundary: No active access or tenant record before the atomic authorized branch commits, per the rule.
- concurrency: Concurrent-Session behaviour: concurrent Sessions are permitted and independent. Under OD-016, self-service sign-out terminates only the acting Session and never cascades to concurrent Sessions; sign-out-everywhere is absent from baseline behaviour and MUST NOT be implemented.
- idempotency: Exact replay is required by the rule itself, per branch, returning the stored authorized result.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-001 emits no event of its own; it constrains the WF-001 event orders in MTX-026.
- authorization entry point: The approved identity/bootstrap service, before any mutation, per SEC-REQ-001.
- permission checks: Fresh purpose-bound identity proof; one-principal bootstrap eligibility; recipient-bound Invitation response; exact Organization selection; Account/Organization/assurance gating; current authorization-context initialization. Each is a distinct enforced predicate, not a single check.
- tenant boundary: No active access or tenant record exists before the atomic authorized branch commits. This is the rule's core assertion and is enforced by the transaction boundary in MTX-026.
- error contract: Deterministic logical destination: the authorized requested logical destination when supplied and allowed, `organization_home` otherwise, and `access_unavailable` when the resolved effective-permission set contains no allowed protected action after explicit-deny precedence.
- audit record: As MTX-026 - the authorization-context initialization and destination decision are audited.
- observability: As MTX-026, without raw identity assertion, email, token, credential or authentication factor.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-001 defines no retention obligation; Account lifecycle retention is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: no-refresh behaviour - a Session is never silently refreshed. TYP-SEC: Session concurrency and expiry fixtures. TYP-SEC: OD-016 sign-out terminates only the acting Session; sign-out-everywhere asserted absent. TYP-SEC: destination decision matrix - allowed requested destination, disallowed requested destination, none supplied, and all-denied yielding `access_unavailable`. TYP-DATA: no active access or tenant record is observable before commit, asserted against each failure fixture.
- rollout: As MTX-026.

### MTX-053 - AC-PRULE-002 (PRULE: PRULE-002)

- Slice: S-02,S-22
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#prule-002-organization-creation-invariants
- interface type: Invariant enforced inside the WF-001 self-service transaction. PRULE-002 names WF-001 as its only workflow, so it has no interface and no lifecycle limb.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-002 is an invariant, not an interface.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf001::BootstrapOrganization`. This row adds no command. PRULE-002 lists CAP-024 among its capabilities because the BillingEntity it creates is the entitlement subject; the CAP-024 consumption limb is owned by S-22 and is not contracted here.
- actor: The approved bootstrap service, under the bootstrap-service-only exception; see MTX-002.
- organization scope: Every record the rule governs is same-Organization by construction: the Plan Assignment MUST be same-Organization and linked to that Organization's BillingEntity.
- aggregate: Organization, BillingEntity, PlanAssignment, RoleAssignment, Membership.
- aggregate boundary: Membership is derived, not stored as an independent mutable grant. The rule forbids a separate mutable membership record from granting access, so Membership MUST be a projection of the active Account plus the active Role Assignment, and MUST NOT be a writable row that can drift from them.
- value objects: OrganizationId, AccountId, BillingEntityId, PlanAssignmentId, RoleAssignmentId.
- domain service: Not applicable - the invariant is enforced by the transaction, not by a named domain service.
- repository: `OrganizationRepository`, `BillingEntityRepository`, `PlanAssignmentRepository`, `RoleAssignmentRepository`, `AccountRepository`.
- persistence model: Canonical tables per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. No membership table may exist as an independent grant source; Membership is derived from Account and RoleAssignment at resolution time.
- migration: Not applicable - the rule introduces no schema object; it constrains records MTX-002 writes.
- transaction boundary: The WF-001 self-service transaction. The administrator Assignment, the BillingEntity and the Plan Assignment link are atomic with Organization creation: the rule says Organization creation MUST atomically assign an accountable administrator.
- concurrency: Serialized with the WF-001 branch; the invariant is checked inside the transaction, so no concurrent path can produce an Organization with zero accountable administrators or a second baseline BillingEntity.
- idempotency: Inherits the WF-001 self-service idempotency envelope; replay creates no second administrator Assignment, BillingEntity or Plan Assignment.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule forbids lazy creation, so no deferred path may satisfy it.
- queue: Not applicable - no asynchronous work.
- retry policy: Inherits the WF-001 bootstrap retry rule; each retry re-checks the invariant.
- terminal failure: A first-administrator invariant violation, a missing or duplicate baseline BillingEntity, or an unlinked or cross-Organization Plan Assignment fails the transaction with its enumerated reason and leaves no tenant record.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - atomic; no partial state.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-002 emits no event of its own. It constrains `RoleGranted`, `BillingStateChanged` and `PlanAssigned` in the MTX-002 and MTX-026 orders.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The WF-001 transaction, before commit. The rule's final clause is itself an authorization statement: Account existence or a separate mutable membership record MUST NOT grant access, so access resolution reads the derived Membership only.
- permission checks: Access is granted only by an active Account plus an active Role Assignment resolving to a derived Membership. Account existence alone grants nothing. A mutable membership record grants nothing and MUST NOT exist as a grant source.
- tenant boundary: The Plan Assignment MUST be same-Organization and linked to that Organization's BillingEntity; a cross-Organization link is a violation.
- error contract: First-match enumerated reasons: first-administrator invariant violation; absent, duplicate or unlinked baseline BillingEntity; cross-Organization or inactive Plan Assignment link. Each fails the transaction with no partial write.
- audit record: The first-administrator invariant outcome and the BillingEntity/Plan Assignment linkage hash are recorded under the bootstrap correlation.
- observability: Covered by the MTX-002 event set; the rule adds no signal of its own beyond the linkage evidence in the audit record.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-002 defines no retention obligation.
