- test contracts: TYP-SEC: an Organization can never exist without an accountable administrator, asserted against every failure fixture. TYP-DATA: exactly one active baseline BillingEntity per Organization, linked to the active same-Organization Plan Assignment. TYP-DATA: no provider call and no lazy creation - the BillingEntity exists at commit or the commit fails. TYP-SEC: an active Account with no active Role Assignment resolves to no Membership and is denied. TYP-SEC: a fabricated or mutated membership record grants no access, because Membership is derived rather than stored. TYP-SEC: a cross-Organization Plan Assignment link is rejected.
- rollout: Ships with S-01/S-02; no predecessor behaviour.

- error contract: The rule's five failure classes map exactly: mismatch to the observation reason codes; dependency failure to the network outcomes; denial to `on_demand_limit_reached`, `on_demand_observation_in_progress`, `on_demand_rate_limited` and the authorization reasons; expiry to `challenge_expired`. None of them verifies, and none of them disables the Source.

TYP-SEC: pending challenge delivery is recoverably protected - the plaintext exists only in the authorized creation response and the two authorized redelivery paths, and decryption failure yields `challenge_redelivery_unavailable` rather than a lost Request. TYP-DATA: on-demand cursor and count reservations serialize - concurrent commands cannot reserve the same slot and cannot double-count. TYP-DATA: exactly one restricted decision Evidence per started observation, for matched, not-matched and indeterminate alike. TYP-INT: verification completes before Source activation - an unverified Source can never satisfy an activation prerequisite. TYP-DATA: mismatch, dependency failure, denial and expiry each leave the Source proposed, asserted as four separate fixtures.

### MTX-057 - AC-PRULE-006 (PRULE: PRULE-006)

- Slice: S-06
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#prule-006-source-lifecycle-transitions
- interface type: State-path rule enforced on every Source lifecycle transition. PRULE-006 governs CAP-006 and applies to WF-004 only.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-006 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-029 commands.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf004::ActivateSource`, `DisableSource`, `ReactivateSource` and `RemoveSource`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-029 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-029 output.
- actor: OrganizationAdmin or MarketingOperator holding `source.lifecycle.manage`.
- organization scope: The Source's Organization.
- aggregate: Source.
- aggregate boundary: The rule constrains the Source root only; the pinned policy version is a reference, not a child.
- value objects: SourceState (`proposed`, `verified`, `active`, `disabled`, `removed`), SourceStateVersion, PinnedPolicyVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - a guarded aggregate transition.
- repository: `SourceRepository`.
- persistence model: The `sources` lifecycle status column with a CHECK constraint admitting only the canonical states, and the pinned policy version persisted on transition. No Boolean substitutes for a canonical state.
- migration: Not applicable - the canonical column already exists.
- transaction boundary: One transaction per transition, persisting the pinned policy version with the new state so the two can never disagree.
- concurrency: Guarded by the expected Source state version. A concurrent second transition finds the state moved and is denied as an invalid transition rather than applying twice.
- idempotency: Naturally idempotent through the state guard: each allowed transition succeeds exactly once, and a repeat from the new state is an unlisted transition and is denied.
- background job: Not applicable - lifecycle transitions are synchronous commands. The running-Crawl restriction they trigger is applied by S-07 at its next checkpoint.
- queue: Not applicable - not queued.
- retry policy: As MTX-029 - dependency failure only, at 1 and 5 seconds, each retry rechecking state.
- terminal failure: Removed is terminal. An unlisted or stale transition is denied and audited, and changes nothing.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - atomic; no partial transition.
- domain events: `SourceActivated`, `SourceDisabled`, `SourceRemoved`, each exactly once with its transition.
- event payload: The canonical envelope with the Source identity, resulting state version and pinned policy version.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf004`.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no consumer is named; S-07 reads the pinned policy rather than reacting to the event.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The application command, before the transition.
- permission checks: `source.lifecycle.manage`, held by an OrganizationAdmin or MarketingOperator only. A TechnicalImplementer cannot mutate lifecycle regardless of other permissions.
- tenant boundary: The Source belongs to the actor's Project and Organization.
- error contract: An unlisted transition is denied with its invalid-transition reason and audited. Direct `Active -> Removed` and `Active -> Verified` are explicitly invalid. A stale Source state version rejects without side effects.
- audit record: Every denied transition is audited with its reason, per the rule's own requirement that unlisted and stale transitions are denied AND audited.
- observability: The full Source lifecycle events plus the audited denial reason for every rejected transition.
- retention: Source lineage and state transitions are persisted; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: each allowed transition succeeds exactly once - `Verified -> Active`, `Active -> Disabled`, `Disabled -> Active`, `Disabled -> Removed`. TYP-DATA: every unlisted transition is denied and audited - direct `Active -> Removed`, `Active -> Verified`, `Proposed -> Active`, `Removed -> Active`, and any transition from `Removed`. TYP-DATA: a stale Source state version is denied and audited. TYP-INT: disable and remove immediately apply the exact running-Crawl restriction and discard behaviour - no new URL scheduled, in-flight content discarded from Evaluation input, immutable history retained. TYP-DATA: removal is allowed only from disabled; re-registration never attaches to the removed lineage. TYP-SEC: a TechnicalImplementer cannot mutate lifecycle.
- rollout: As MTX-029.

### MTX-059 - AC-PRULE-008 (PRULE: PRULE-008)

- Slice: S-07
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-008-crawl-policy-bounds
- interface type: Numeric bound rule enforced throughout crawl execution. PRULE-008 governs CAP-007 and CAP-008 and applies to WF-005. Its decision dependency OD-005 is resolved.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-008 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains execution, not a request.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced by the crawl scheduler and worker within `Workflows::Wf005`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains execution.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains execution.
- actor: Not applicable - the bounds are actor-independent; they constrain the run, not a principal.
- organization scope: The Crawl's Organization; bounds resolve from the Organization or Project policy version pinned on the Crawl.
- aggregate: Crawl.
- aggregate boundary: The bounds constrain the Crawl root and its per-URL attempts; they cross no boundary.
- value objects: AccountedResponseBytes, LimitProbeBytes, ByteReservation, SoftLimit, HardLimit, DimensionName.
- domain service: Not applicable - the byte formula and limit precedence are pure functions.
- repository: `CrawlPolicyRepository` read-only; the byte reservation is an implementation-owned technical record.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a product concept - the byte reservation and limit records are implementation-owned technical infrastructure. The limit observations recorded on the Crawl are product data.
- migration: Not applicable - no product schema object.
- transaction boundary: Reservation is taken before body reading and released in canonical order; accounting commits with the attempt result.
- concurrency: Reservation-safe concurrent accounting is the rule's own term: concurrent reservations MUST NOT sum above the run-wide maximum, and an attempt cannot add accounted bytes beyond its reservation. This is what makes the run-wide byte bound true under concurrency rather than approximately true.
- idempotency: A retried attempt takes a new reservation; accounting applies to every content, robots and sitemap attempt including retries and bodies later rejected, so a retry consumes budget rather than being free.
- background job: Not applicable - enforced inside the worker, not a separate job. The 60-minute wall-clock ceiling is enforced by `CrawlWallClockJob` in MTX-030.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule constrains retries rather than defining them; every retry is accounted.
- terminal failure: At a capacity hard limit the exceeding page, URL or accounted bytes are not accepted; at any other hard limit, stop scheduling affected work, preserve successful artifacts, record the dimension, configured value, observed value and affected Source and URL counts, set `coverage_status=partial` and `completion_reason=limit_reached`, and emit `CrawlLimitReached` exactly once per dimension and run.
- reconciliation: Unused reserved bytes are released in the same canonical order, so an abandoned attempt returns its budget rather than stranding it.
- domain events: `CrawlSoftLimitApproaching` once per dimension and run; `CrawlLimitReached` exactly once per dimension and run.
- event payload: Dimension, configured value, observed value, affected Source and URL counts.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf005`.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no consumer is named.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Not applicable - the bounds are not an authorization check. Policy activation authority is MTX-030's `policy.crawl.manage`.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-008 adds no permission. Narrowing the bounds requires `policy.crawl.manage` and may only be more restrictive.
- tenant boundary: Bounds resolve per Organization or Project policy version; one tenant's run cannot consume another's budget because accounting is per run.
- error contract: A URL deeper than 10, a response with a sentinel beyond 10 MiB on either byte path, a request exceeding 15 seconds, a fourth-level sitemap index, a fifty-first sitemap, or an eleventh redirect fails that URL without retaining its body as valid Evidence.
- audit record: Every limit observation with its dimension, configured value, observed value and affected counts; `limit_probe_bytes` recorded separately as detection telemetry.
- observability: Soft-limit crossing emits `CrawlSoftLimitApproaching` once per dimension and run, which is the saturation signal.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-008 defines no retention obligation.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: every numeric bound in `crawl-policy-v1` at its soft and hard values, asserted at the boundary and one either side. TYP-DATA: the per-attempt byte formula uses `max(received_after_transfer_coding, expanded_after_content_decoding)` - a highly compressed body is accounted at its expanded size. TYP-DATA: run-wide bytes are the sum in canonical dequeue and attempt order. TYP-DATA: the one-byte limit probe - EOF at the maximum is allowed; one sentinel byte fails the URL as over-limit; `limit_probe_bytes` never enter either accounted counter and are never parsed or retained. TYP-DATA: reservation-safe concurrent accounting - N concurrent attempts never sum above the run-wide maximum; an attempt cannot exceed its reservation; unused bytes release in canonical order. TYP-DATA: accounting applies to content, robots and sitemap attempts including retries and bodies later rejected. TYP-DATA: canonical retained-work order - root, sitemap, breadth-first. TYP-OBS: a soft event fires when the observed OR RESERVED value first equals the soft limit, once per dimension and run. TYP-OBS: a capacity hard-limit event fires BEFORE the action would exceed the maximum and the exceeding item is not accepted. TYP-DATA: at any other hard limit, scheduling stops for affected work, successful artifacts are preserved, and `coverage_status=partial` with `completion_reason=limit_reached`. TYP-OBS: `CrawlLimitReached` fires exactly once per dimension and run. TYP-DATA: limits are inclusive maxima; `limit_probe_bytes` are not accepted or accounted capacity.
- rollout: As MTX-030.

The WITHHELD limb: no `unique (parsing_job_id)` constraint and no `has_one` narrowing may be added to the ParsingJob-to-IndexingJob relation while OD-027 is pending; `indexing-interim-v1` pins both index keys.
- migration: The `unique (parsing_job_id)` constraint is WITHHELD under OD-027 and MUST NOT be migrated. No other schema object is added by this row.
- transaction boundary: The terminal checkpoint is serialized; coverage and completion derive there. The Document advance commits with its job success.
- concurrency: Transition successful same-version Documents ingested-to-parsed and parsed-to-indexed exactly once. Concurrent same-version jobs cannot advance the Document twice.
- idempotency: The exactly-once advance is the rule's idempotency guarantee, enforced on the Document by its version rather than by delivery deduplication.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains job outcomes, not a job of its own.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule constrains retries rather than defining them.
- terminal failure: Partial outcomes preserve successful subsets. Blocked or partial input MUST NOT be presented as full, and Indexing MUST NOT gate Evaluation.
- reconciliation: Coverage and completion derive exactly from the retained manifest, so a partial run is auditable rather than approximated.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-009 emits no event of its own; it constrains the coverage, completion and readiness values carried by the MTX-030 events.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Not applicable - the rule is not an authorization check.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-009 adds no permission.
- tenant boundary: As MTX-030.
- error contract: Every failed Source, URL, Document, ParsingJob, count and reason is retained rather than collapsed into a single failure.
- audit record: The complete failed subset with counts and reasons; the derived coverage and completion; the readiness state.
- observability: Failed-subset, readiness and Evaluation-transition telemetry.
- retention: Failure lineage retained; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: partial Crawl outcomes preserve successful subsets - a run with some failed roots still yields its valid Documents. TYP-DATA: every failed Source, URL, Document, ParsingJob, count and reason is retained and individually addressable. TYP-DATA: a successful same-version job advances the Document ingested-to-parsed exactly once; a duplicate delivery advances nothing further. TYP-DATA: coverage and completion derive exactly from the retained manifest. TYP-DATA: partial or blocked input is never presented as full. TYP-DATA: Indexing does not gate Evaluation - a Document with no IndexingJob outcome does not block Evaluation readiness. WITHHELD under OD-027 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting a second IndexingJob per ParsingJob, a `has_one` narrowing, or a `unique (parsing_job_id)` constraint. The parsed-to-indexed exactly-once clause is contracted for the interim keys that `indexing-interim-v1` pins; the final multiplicity is not implemented and MUST NOT be inferred.
- rollout: The WF-006 parsing limb lands with S-08 and the WF-017 incident limb with S-24. The OD-027 limb requires ratification and controlled Volume I integration before it can be completed.

### MTX-061 - AC-PRULE-010 (PRULE: PRULE-010)

- Slice: S-09
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#prule-010-check-materialization-canonical-order-and-determinism
- interface type: Materialization, ordering, determinism and identity rule over every expected `check-catalog-v1` result key. PRULE-010 governs CAP-009, CAP-010 and CAP-011 and applies to WF-007. This is the core of S-09: CAP-009, CAP-010 and CAP-011 are the per-Definition obligations discharged inside it.
- route: Not applicable - Volume I defines the commands, their permissions and their envelopes but no HTTP path, method or transport. Transport exposure is owned by API_CONTRACTS.md at the Volume II baseline. Check execution is service automation triggered by a ready Evaluation Input Snapshot, not by an inbound request.
- request schema: The sealed Evaluation Input Snapshot ID and content hash with its `readiness_status` and `coverage_status` from S-08; the active Check Catalog version and content hash; every Definition ID/version/content digest; the frozen Project profile version with its `local_presence_applicable` decision and nonblank reason when false; the active Source IDs sorted ascending; Crawl coverage and parsing-readiness identities; and per expected entry the subject scope/type/key, nullable Source and Document IDs, ordered selected Evidence ID/digest/Validation-Decision tuples, and the frozen absence-selector instance.
- response schema: One immutable Check Applicability Snapshot with its content hash and exhaustive ordered expected entries; one materialized Check Result identity per retained uniqueness preimage; and one immutable terminal Check Result per expected entry carrying `deterministic_input_hash`, `deterministic_output_hash`, `check_result_key_sha256` and its retained canonical preimage.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter is defined by Volume I; see route.
- command: `Workflows::Wf007::ValidateActiveCheckCatalog`, `Workflows::Wf007::SealCheckApplicabilitySnapshot`, `Workflows::Wf007::MaterializeCheckResultKey`, `Workflows::Wf007::ExecuteCheckAttempt`, `Workflows::Wf007::PersistCheckResult`. All are tenant-scoped evaluation-service commands and none is principal-reachable. The Evaluation lifecycle transitions they run inside are WF-007's and are owned by S-12.
- command input: As request_schema. Each `ExecuteCheckAttempt` receives ONLY its frozen deterministic input tuple: Organization, Project, Evaluation input-snapshot identifier, Check Catalog version and content hash, Check Applicability Snapshot identifier and content hash, Check Definition identifier and version, pillar, subject scope and canonical subject identity, frozen absence-coverage-selector snapshot, sorted Evidence identifiers and digests, their effective Validation Decision identifiers and statuses, policy versions including impact and effort rules, `rule_or_model_version`, locale `en-AU` and time-zone assumption UTC unless the Definition explicitly versions another value.
- command output: Exactly one immutable Check Result per expected entry, or -- at the Catalog, applicability, tenant-integrity or result-identity boundary -- no Check Result at all and a failed Evaluation.
- actor: Tenant-scoped evaluation service identity. CAP-009, CAP-010 and CAP-011 all name Actor `System automation`; no human principal executes a Check.
- organization scope: The Evaluation's Organization. Every Snapshot reference, expected entry and selected Evidence record resolves inside it.
- aggregate: CheckCatalog and CheckDefinition as immutable versioned artifacts; CheckApplicabilitySnapshot as an immutable root sealed once per Evaluation; CheckResult as a separate immutable root; Evaluation as the lifecycle subject owned by S-12.
- aggregate boundary: The applicability seal and the Check Results are separate commits: the Snapshot is sealed once before any execution and its content hash is the input authority every Result freezes. Result materialization commits the identity before execution, and execution commits the semantic output onto that identity, so a crashed attempt leaves a materialized key rather than a phantom Result. A Check Result never spans into Issue creation; that boundary is CAP-014's in S-12.
- value objects: CheckCatalogVersion, CheckDefinitionId, CheckDefinitionVersion, CheckApplicabilitySnapshotId, SnapshotContentHash, CheckResultKeySha256, CheckResultKeyPreimage, DeterministicInputHash, DeterministicOutputHash, PillarId, SubjectScope, CanonicalSubjectType, CanonicalSubjectKey, AbsenceCoverageSelectorSnapshot, SubjectSetComplete, ExecutionStatus, OutcomeCode, ErrorReasonCode, NormalizedObservation, EvidenceSetHash, ExecutionAttemptCount.
- domain service: Not applicable - Volume I names the tenant-scoped evaluation service identity rather than a domain service. Canonical ordering, the canonical-JSON hash function and each Definition's outcome rule are pure functions applied by the executor over frozen inputs.
- repository: `CheckCatalogRepository` read-only, `CheckDefinitionRepository` read-only, `CheckApplicabilitySnapshotRepository`, `CheckResultRepository`, `EvaluationInputSnapshotRepository` read-only, `EvidenceRepository` read-only, `EvidenceValidationDecisionRepository` read-only, `ProjectProfileRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: `check_applicability_snapshots` immutable, unique on `evaluation_id`, carrying the content hash and its exhaustive ordered expected entries. `check_results` immutable, unique on the retained preimage tuple `(evaluation_id, evaluation_input_snapshot_id, check_applicability_snapshot_id, check_catalog_version, check_definition_id, check_definition_version, canonical_subject_type, canonical_subject_key, pillar_id)` -- the complete retained preimage, not `check_result_key_sha256`, is the uniqueness authority, so the hash alone MUST NOT be the unique index. A separate `check_result_key_collisions` record holds each distinct same-hash/different-preimage record. Catalog and Definition rows are immutable and version-keyed; reuse of a Catalog or Definition version for changed content is prohibited. Per-attempt telemetry is implementation-owned technical infrastructure and is not a product entity.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - the canonical schema owns `check_applicability_snapshots`, `check_results` and the collision record per schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md. No product schema object is added here. OD-032 names a pending namespace question for Check Result, but its Affected Acceptance Criteria are AC-CAP-013, AC-CAP-015, AC-CAP-021, AC-CAP-025, AC-PRULE-018, AC-PRULE-046 and AC-SM-002 -- not AC-PRULE-010 -- so no limb of this row is withheld; the interim identifier behaviour is permitted and S-11 owns that limb.
- transaction boundary: Catalog validation is a read-only precondition committing nothing. Snapshot seal: one transaction creating the single immutable Check Applicability Snapshot with its content hash, after which no expected entry may be added, removed or reordered. Key materialization: one transaction per preimage that atomically creates or returns one Check Result identity BEFORE execution. Execution: the attempt runs outside any transaction and only its semantic output is committed onto the materialized identity, in expected-key order. No external call sits inside a database transaction, because no Check makes an external call at all.
- concurrency: Materialization atomically creates or returns one Check Result identity per retained preimage before execution, so two concurrent executors cannot produce two Results for one key. Concurrent completion is committed in expected-key order. Exact or concurrent replay with the same deterministic input returns that identity and its terminal semantic output and emits no second `CheckResultCreated`. Because the Snapshot is sealed before any execution, completion order never changes the expected-entry set, its order or its content hash. At exactly five elapsed seconds `check_internal_timeout` wins the race against completion; a later completion is discarded and cannot create or replace a Check Result.
- idempotency: The retained canonical uniqueness preimage -- Evaluation ID, Evaluation Input Snapshot ID, Check Applicability Snapshot ID, Check Catalog version, Check Definition ID and version, canonical subject type and key, and pillar ID in that order -- is uniqueness authority within the Evaluation; `check_result_key_sha256` is its canonical-JSON SHA-256 and is an index, never the authority. Exact replay returns the stored identity and terminal semantic output. A DIFFERENT deterministic input for the same preimage fails the Evaluation as `F1-DATA-409 / check_result_input_conflict` and changes neither Result. A same hash with a different retained preimage never merges: it creates distinct internal collision records, emits restricted `CheckResultFingerprintCollision` telemetry, and fails the affected Evaluation as `check_result_key_collision` before Check execution. Altered input under the same result identity is `idempotency_conflict` and fails the Evaluation.
- background job: `CheckExecutionWorker` per materialized expected key, and `CheckRetryTimer` for the single permitted retry exactly one second after a retryable attempt terminalizes. The five-second attempt ceiling is enforced inside the attempt, not by a separate job.
- queue: The evaluation queue, per the Volume I Background Processing action registry. Queue identity and worker topology are owned by BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md; this row does not rename them.
- retry policy: `check-executor-interim-v1` exactly: each attempt has five elapsed seconds and at exactly five seconds `check_internal_timeout` wins over completion. Only `check_dependency_unavailable` and `check_internal_timeout` are retryable, receiving one initial attempt and exactly one retry starting one second after the failed attempt completes, reusing the identical applicability entry, Evidence tuple, input hash and attempt identity lineage. No other reason retries. Passed, failed and not-applicable results use one attempt. This bounded per-result retry is the ONLY retry inside an Evaluation.
- terminal failure: Retry exhaustion persists one `error` Check Result with outcome and `error_reason_code` both equal to `check_dependency_unavailable` or `check_internal_timeout`, `execution_attempt_count=2`, null `confidence_value`, `confidence_status=missing`, display band `low`, null `impact_band`, no Issue, and `subject_set_complete=false`. A semantic input problem persists a one-attempt `error`. Handled `error` Results are terminal and do NOT fail WF-007; after every expected entry is terminal the Evaluation may complete, the affected pillar is insufficient and the overall score becomes unavailable. Catalog, Definition, applicability-snapshot, tenant-integrity or result-idempotency failure instead transitions the Evaluation to failed and publishes no Issue Set.
- reconciliation: Each expected entry ultimately yields exactly one Check Result or the whole Evaluation fails under the Catalog, applicability and integrity boundary; there is no third outcome and no expected entry is silently omitted. A missing selected input permitted by an otherwise valid selector remains an entry and produces the Definition's handled `error`. A retry or replay whose computed output differs for the same deterministic input fails as `check_nondeterministic_output`, publishes no replacement Result or Issue, and preserves the original attempt evidence for restricted diagnosis.
- domain events: `CheckResultCreated`, emitted only with the one persisted Result; `CheckExecutionRetried` on the single permitted retry; restricted `CheckResultFingerprintCollision` on a same-hash/different-preimage collision. `EvaluationStarted`, `EvaluationCompleted` and `EvaluationFailed` are WF-007 lifecycle events owned by S-12 and are not emitted here.
- event payload: The canonical envelope with the real `organization_id`, plus Catalog and Definition versions, the applicability and result key, attempt number, `deterministic_input_hash`, execution status and outcome, elapsed time, retry-of attempt, and correlation ID, per WF-007 Domain Events. Collision telemetry is restricted and carries the distinct retained preimages.
- event producer: `Workflows::Wf007` is the sole producer. The executor's per-attempt telemetry record -- Definition, subject, attempt number, queued, started and completed times, terminal reason, input hash and correlation ID -- is technical telemetry and MUST NOT be emitted as a domain event. Retrying or completing late cannot duplicate `CheckResultCreated`.
- event consumers: S-12 consumes the terminal Check Results at the WF-007 Issue-derivation and seal steps, by reading them rather than by reacting to `CheckResultCreated`. S-13 consumes them as score-capable pillar coverage. Neither consumer may re-execute a Check.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure is defined by Volume I; see route.
- authorization entry point: Each evaluation-service command, before mutation. WF-007 Authorization names the evaluation service identity for checks and creation, and states that no other actor may change Issue or Evidence validation state.
- permission checks: Tenant-scoped evaluation service identity only. Volume I defines no principal permission for catalog validation, applicability sealing, key materialization or Check execution, and none may be invented. `issue.dispute`, `issue.adjudicate` and `evidence.validation.manage` are WF-007 subflow permissions owned by S-12 and S-11 and grant nothing on this path.
- tenant boundary: Every Snapshot reference, expected entry, subject and selected Evidence record MUST belong to the Evaluation's Organization. A cross-Organization reference is tenant-integrity failure and fails the Evaluation through the applicability boundary rather than producing a handled Result. Checks perform no network request, provider call, mutable read, clock-dependent query or tenant-policy mutation, so no execution path can reach outside the frozen tuple it was given.
- error contract: Catalog: `check_catalog_unavailable` for a missing, altered, incomplete or unrecognized active Catalog; `check_catalog_integrity_failure` for digest mismatch, duplicate Definition identity, cross-version semantic reuse, an accepted-but-nonexhaustive mapping, corruption or an unmapped failed outcome discovered after activation, or an attempted not-applicable result from `CHK-TI-001`, `CHK-CQ-001`, `CHK-TR-001`, `CHK-SP-001`, `CHK-AIP-001` or `CHK-AS-001`. Applicability: `check_applicability_snapshot_invalid` for a missing or duplicate expected entry, an entry outside the active Source set, a stale profile or input version, tenant mismatch, altered order, or content-digest mismatch. Identity: `F1-DATA-409 / check_result_input_conflict`, `check_result_key_collision`, `check_nondeterministic_output`, `idempotency_conflict`. All of these fail the Evaluation before any Check or Issue write and no partially validated Catalog executes. Handled Check errors, which do NOT fail the Evaluation, are the first-match semantic order `input_evidence_missing`, `input_evidence_stale`, `input_evidence_indeterminate`, `input_evidence_invalid`, `normalized_input_invalid`, `output_schema_invalid`, plus retryable `check_dependency_unavailable` and `check_internal_timeout`; an unknown runtime failure maps to nonretryable `output_schema_invalid` with restricted diagnostics.
- audit record: Frozen input and output hashes; Catalog version and content hash; every Definition ID and version; the applicability Snapshot ID and content hash; the retained result-key preimage and its hash; Evidence references with their effective Validation Decision IDs and statuses; execution status, outcome and error reason; attempt count and retry-of attempt; elapsed time; replay and collision outcome; and the exact failure reason, per WF-007 Audit and Observability.
- observability: Evaluation and check-result telemetry per CAP-009; `EvaluationCompleted` plus content-check metrics per CAP-010; parsing and validation success or failure metrics per CAP-011. One attempt telemetry record per attempt with Definition, subject, attempt number, queued, started and completed times, terminal reason, input hash and correlation ID. Completion and failure events carry expected and actual result counts and the ordered error-result keys, so an omitted expected key is observable rather than inferred.
- retention: Check Results, their frozen Evidence references and their Validation Decision IDs and statuses are immutable and retain the values frozen at creation; a later Validation Decision never changes an existing Check Result or its `evidence_set_hash`. The retention window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md; this row states none.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: `check-catalog-v1` membership is exactly the ordered seven `CHK-TI-001@1.0.0`, `CHK-CQ-001@1.0.0`, `CHK-TR-001@1.0.0`, `CHK-SP-001@1.0.0`, `CHK-AIP-001@1.0.0`, `CHK-AS-001@1.0.0`, `CHK-LP-001@1.0.0`, with owner Chief Product, release `2026-07-16T00:00:00Z`, executor `check-executor-interim-v1`, external-measurement policy `external-measurement-v1` and effort policy `effort-interim-v1` -- proving the catalogue is neither narrowed nor broadened by OD-010's ratification. TYP-DATA: activation validation recomputes the Catalog and Definition digests, proves exactly one score-capable Definition per Pillar, proves every outcome, impact, effort, template, subject, error and fixture mapping exhaustive, and rejects unknown or duplicate membership; a partially validated Catalog executes nothing. TYP-DATA: a missing, altered, incomplete or unrecognized Catalog is `check_catalog_unavailable`; digest mismatch, duplicate Definition identity, cross-version semantic reuse and an accepted-but-nonexhaustive mapping are each `check_catalog_integrity_failure` -- four separate fixtures, each failing the Evaluation before any Check or Issue write. TYP-DATA: expected entries are exhaustive and ordered by Catalog position, canonical subject key UTF-8 bytes, Source ID, then Document ID; an array permutation of the input produces an identical Snapshot content hash. TYP-DATA: expected-entry cardinality is exact -- one `CHK-TI-001` per active Source; one `CHK-CQ-001` per successfully parsed HTML or XHTML Document in the Evaluation Input Snapshot; one `CHK-TR-001` per active Source INCLUDING a Source whose root input is missing, which produces an explicit handled error rather than disappearing; exactly one Project entry each for `CHK-SP-001`, `CHK-AIP-001`, `CHK-AS-001` and `CHK-LP-001`. TYP-DATA: a missing or duplicate expected entry, an entry outside the active Source set, a stale profile or input version, tenant mismatch, altered order and a content-digest mismatch are each `check_applicability_snapshot_invalid` -- six fixtures, each failing the Evaluation before Check execution. TYP-DATA: actual Source IDs are bound only in the Snapshot and its absence-selector instances and are asserted ABSENT from every global Definition; an instance contains the Definition's subject namespace plus exactly the entry's Organization, Project, nullable Source, subject type and canonical subject key. TYP-DATA: a missing selected input permitted by an otherwise valid selector REMAINS an entry and produces the Definition's handled `error`; it is asserted not silently omitted. TYP-DATA: the retained preimage is exactly Evaluation ID, Evaluation Input Snapshot ID, Check Applicability Snapshot ID, Check Catalog version, Check Definition ID and version, canonical subject type and key, and pillar ID in that order; `check_result_key_sha256` is its canonical-JSON SHA-256. TYP-DATA: materialization atomically creates or returns one Check Result identity for that preimage BEFORE execution. TYP-DATA (FX-CHECK-COM-001): exact replay returns one Result and one event. TYP-DATA: concurrent replay with the same deterministic input returns that identity and terminal semantic output and emits no second `CheckResultCreated`. TYP-DATA: a different deterministic input for the same preimage is `F1-DATA-409 / check_result_input_conflict`, fails the Evaluation and changes NEITHER Result. TYP-DATA: the same hash with a different retained preimage never merges, creates distinct collision records, emits restricted `CheckResultFingerprintCollision`, and fails the Evaluation as `check_result_key_collision` BEFORE Check execution -- proving the hash is an index and the preimage is the authority. TYP-DATA (FX-CHECK-COM-002): a changed Evidence digest changes `deterministic_input_hash`. TYP-DATA: the canonical input tuple is exactly the ordered tuple named in the Check Result Contract, hashed as SHA-256 over UTF-8 canonical JSON with keys sorted lexicographically, no insignificant whitespace, decimals without exponent notation, arrays in declared order and strings NFC-normalized. TYP-DATA: locale is `en-AU` and the time-zone assumption is UTC unless the Definition explicitly versions another value. TYP-DATA: two executions with the same canonical input tuple produce the same semantic output tuple -- execution status, outcome code, normalized observation, impact band, confidence value or status, confidence band, pillar, subject identity, absence selector and subject-set-complete flag -- while generated identifiers, attempt timestamps, correlation identifiers and `produced_at_utc` are asserted EXCLUDED from semantic equality. TYP-DATA: a retry or replay whose computed output differs for the same deterministic input is `check_nondeterministic_output`, publishes no replacement Result or Issue, and preserves the original attempt evidence. TYP-OBS (FX-CHECK-COM-006): completion at 4.999 seconds commits. TYP-OBS (FX-CHECK-COM-007): completion at exactly five seconds loses to `check_internal_timeout` and the retry starts at exactly six seconds. TYP-OBS (FX-CHECK-COM-008): completion after five seconds is DISCARDED and creates or replaces no Check Result. TYP-OBS (FX-CHECK-COM-009): a dependency failure receives exactly one retry, one second later, reusing the identical applicability entry, Evidence tuple, input hash and attempt lineage. TYP-OBS (FX-CHECK-COM-010): a second timeout persists the two-attempt terminal error with `execution_attempt_count=2`, null confidence value, `confidence_status=missing`, band `low`, null impact, no Issue and `subject_set_complete=false`. TYP-DATA (FX-CHECK-COM-011): invalid output schema is a one-attempt error. TYP-DATA: no reason other than `check_dependency_unavailable` and `check_internal_timeout` retries; passed, failed and not-applicable use one attempt. TYP-DATA: the semantic input reason first-match order is `input_evidence_missing`, `input_evidence_stale`, `input_evidence_indeterminate`, `input_evidence_invalid`, `normalized_input_invalid`, `output_schema_invalid`, with the outcome equal to that reason unless the Definition expressly names a narrower domain outcome; an unknown runtime failure maps to nonretryable `output_schema_invalid` with restricted diagnostics. TYP-SEC: a Check performs NO network request, provider call, mutable read, clock-dependent query or tenant-policy mutation -- asserted by observing zero outbound sockets and zero mutable reads across all seven Definitions. TYP-SEC (FX-CHECK-COM-003): a cross-Organization, cross-Project or cross-Evaluation input fails the Evaluation BEFORE a Result, rather than producing a handled Result. TYP-DATA: `subject_set_complete` is true only when the selected evidence proves complete coverage for that exact selector instance, and is false for every `error`, for partial relevant Crawl, parser or external coverage, and when a required subject could not be observed; a `not_applicable` Local Presence result has TRUE subject-set completeness for its inapplicable Project selector. TYP-DATA: a passed or failed Result references at least one effectively valid Evidence record; a `not_applicable` Result may have none because the frozen applicability entry is its authority; an `error` caused by missing Evidence may have none, and every other error retains the Evidence that proves it. TYP-DATA: handled `error` Results are terminal and do NOT fail WF-007; after every expected entry is terminal the Evaluation may complete, the affected pillar is insufficient and the overall score is unavailable. TYP-OBS: exactly one attempt telemetry record per attempt; queue depth and worker scheduling are asserted absent from the domain event stream; completion and failure events carry expected and actual result counts and ordered error-result keys.
- rollout: S-09 depends on S-08 for the sealed immutable Evaluation Input Snapshot and its derived Check-facing Evidence, and gates S-12 (Issue derivation) and S-13 (score coverage). Nothing here is withheld: OD-010 is ratified and its approved bundle-nothing consequence -- handled `input_evidence_missing` on the external entries and an unavailable numeric score -- is the baseline this slice implements.

### MTX-062 - AC-PRULE-011 (PRULE: PRULE-011)

- Slice: S-09,S-11,S-12
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-011-issue-origination-and-evidence-integrity
- interface type: Origination and referential-integrity rule at the Check-to-Issue boundary. PRULE-011 governs CAP-009, CAP-013 and CAP-014 and applies to WF-007. Its decision dependency OD-010 is RATIFIED. S-09 owns this rule for all three slices that cite it; S-11 (Evidence capture) and S-12 (Issue creation) CONSUME it and MUST NOT redefine it.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-011 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the transformation from a Check Result to an Issue; see MTX-061 and S-12's MTX-014.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced as a precondition inside S-12's `Workflows::Wf007::DeriveIssuesFromCheckResults`, and as an invariant on every Issue write. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the S-12 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the S-12 output: an Issue exists only where the rule permits one.
- actor: Tenant-scoped evaluation service identity for creation; the rule binds every writer, including the adjudication and reassessment services, because it is an invariant on the record rather than a check in one command.
- organization scope: The Issue's Organization, which MUST equal the Check Result's, the Evaluation's and every referenced Evidence record's.
- aggregate: Issue as the constrained root; CheckResult and Evidence as its required antecedents.
- aggregate boundary: The rule is what makes the Issue root non-free-standing: an Issue may not exist without exactly one originating failed catalog Check Result and at least one effectively valid same-Organization Evidence reference. There is no path that creates an Issue from Evidence directly -- `Evidence bypassing Check Result and Issue directly into score` is a prohibited transformation.
- value objects: CheckResultId, CheckDefinitionId, CheckDefinitionVersion, IssueType, PillarId, EvidenceReference, ValidationStatus, EffectiveValidationDecision, PublicationStatus, AdjudicationStatus.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-011 names no domain service; the origination predicate is evaluated in the derivation command and enforced as a database-level referential invariant.
- repository: `IssueRepository`, `CheckResultRepository` read-only, `EvidenceRepository` read-only, `EvidenceValidationDecisionRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-011 introduces no record. It constrains `issues`: `check_result_id` NOT NULL referencing a `failed` catalog Check Result; at least one `evidence_references` member; `organization_id` equal across Issue, Check Result, Evaluation and every referenced Evidence record. The at-least-one-Evidence obligation applies to a WITHHELD review candidate exactly as it does to a published Issue, so a `candidate / review_required / withheld` row with zero Evidence is not creatable.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added; the canonical schema already carries the references this rule makes mandatory.
- transaction boundary: The predicate is evaluated inside the Issue-creation transaction against the Check Result's FROZEN Evidence Validation Decision statuses, not against a later re-read. A failing predicate creates no Issue and no partial write becomes visible.
- concurrency: The predicate is a pure function of already-immutable inputs -- the terminal Check Result and its frozen Decision statuses -- so concurrent derivation attempts evaluate it identically. A concurrent Evidence Validation Decision cannot retroactively make an already-created Issue unoriginated; it instead makes the current score projection unavailable and suppresses the affected Recommendation Artifacts, which is S-11's and S-13's path.
- idempotency: The rule is an invariant, not a command, so it has no key of its own: it holds identically on a first create and on an exact fingerprint replay that returns the existing Issue. A replay that would return an Issue whose Evidence has since become invalid still returns the stored Issue unchanged; the invalidity is handled by the score-unavailable path rather than by retroactively unmaking the Issue.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains a synchronous derivation step inside the Evaluation, not a job.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - a rejected Issue write is not retried; the Check Result that failed the predicate is terminal and the correct outcome is no Issue.
- terminal failure: `passed`, `not_applicable` and `error` create NO Issue -- `error` instead contributes to coverage failure or partial coverage. Invalid, missing, quarantined or cross-tenant Evidence creates no Issue. A Check Result that is not a member of the active Catalog creates no Issue. In each case the absence of an Issue is the correct terminal outcome and is recorded, not an error to recover from.
- reconciliation: Only effectively valid Evidence under its latest Validation Decision may support an Issue, an included Score Contribution, a Recommendation Artifact or a customer-visible citation. The SOLE exception is a zero-value, excluded `invalid_evidence` diagnostic Contribution inside an unavailable calculation; it explains the failure and is never score support or promotion input. That exception is S-13's and is named here so it is not mistaken for a second origination path.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-011 emits no event of its own; it gates `IssueCreated`, which S-12 emits, and it is the reason no `IssueCreated` follows a passed, not-applicable or error Result.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The Issue-creation and Issue-write path, before mutation. The rule is a data-integrity gate that runs in addition to, never instead of, the tenant-scoped service identity check.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-011 adds no permission. It binds every writer regardless of permission: no grant, including `issue.adjudicate` or `evidence.validation.manage`, authorizes an Issue with zero valid Evidence or a cross-Organization Evidence reference.
- tenant boundary: Referenced Organization, Project, Source and Evaluation records MUST belong to the same Organization. A cross-Organization reference, missing referenced bytes, digest mismatch or unknown schema version MUST reject the downstream write with a data-integrity error AND an audit event -- the audit obligation is part of the rule, so a silently rejected write breaches it.
- error contract: Cross-Organization reference, missing referenced bytes, digest mismatch or unknown schema version rejects the downstream write with `F1-DATA-409` and an audit event. Zero Evidence, invalid Evidence, quarantined Evidence or a missing required Evidence field rejects Issue creation, including for a withheld review candidate. A `passed`, `not_applicable` or `error` origin is not an error at all: it correctly produces no Issue.
- audit record: Every rejected downstream write records the Check Result ID, the attempted Issue type, the offending Evidence ID and reason, the Organizations involved, and the correlation ID. Every accepted Issue records `check_result_id`, `check_definition_id`, `check_definition_version` and its ordered `evidence_references` with content digests and effective Validation Decision IDs and statuses.
- observability: The absence of an `IssueCreated` after a passed, not-applicable or error Result is asserted rather than assumed; a rejected write emits its data-integrity audit event, so an integrity rejection is never silent.
- retention: The Issue's frozen Evidence references and Decision statuses are retained as `product_history` and never mutate, including after the referenced Evidence Payload is destroyed. The window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: every persisted Issue originates from exactly ONE failed catalog Check Result -- an Issue with a null or non-catalog `check_result_id` is not creatable. TYP-DATA: `passed` creates no Issue; `not_applicable` creates no Issue; `error` creates no Issue and instead contributes to coverage failure or partial coverage -- three separate fixtures, each asserting `IssueCreated` ABSENT. TYP-DATA: every persisted Issue references at least one effectively valid Evidence record AT CREATION, including a withheld `candidate / review_required / withheld` review candidate -- the withheld case is asserted separately because it is the one a permissive implementation exempts. TYP-SEC: Issue creation fails for zero Evidence, invalid Evidence, quarantined Evidence, a missing required Evidence field, and a cross-Organization Evidence reference -- five fixtures, each creating no Issue (this is AC-SM-003, owned by S-12 and satisfied through this rule). TYP-SEC: a cross-Organization reference, missing referenced bytes, digest mismatch and unknown schema version each reject the downstream write with a data-integrity error AND emit an audit event -- the audit event is asserted present, because a silent rejection satisfies the first clause and breaches the second. TYP-DATA: only effectively valid Evidence under its LATEST Validation Decision may support an Issue, an included Score Contribution, a Recommendation Artifact or a customer-visible citation. TYP-DATA: the sole exception is the zero-value excluded `invalid_evidence` diagnostic Contribution in an unavailable calculation; it is asserted never to act as score support or promotion input. TYP-SEC: no grant creates an exemption -- an actor holding `issue.adjudicate` or `evidence.validation.manage` still cannot create an Issue with zero valid Evidence or a cross-Organization reference. TYP-DATA: Evidence bypassing Check Result and Issue directly into score is a prohibited transformation and is asserted to have no code path. TYP-DATA: a later Evidence Validation Decision does not retroactively unmake an existing Issue; it makes the Current Score Projection unavailable with `invalid_evidence` and suppresses the affected published Recommendation Artifacts, leaving history unrewritten. TYP-DATA: the predicate reads the Check Result's FROZEN Decision statuses, not a live re-read -- asserted by changing a Decision between Check persistence and Issue derivation and observing the frozen value govern.
- rollout: S-09 owns PRULE-011 for S-09, S-11 and S-12. S-11 (CAP-013 Evidence capture) consumes the validity predicate and owns the Validation Decision transitions that feed it; S-12 (CAP-014 Issue creation) consumes the origination predicate and owns the Issue writes it gates. Neither re-contracts the rule. OD-010 is ratified, so nothing here is withheld.

### MTX-063 - AC-PRULE-012 (PRULE: PRULE-012)

- Slice: S-09,S-12
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#prule-012-impact-and-confidence-metadata
- interface type: Metadata-completeness rule for every failed catalog Check used for Issue creation or downstream decisioning. PRULE-012 governs CAP-010 and CAP-014 and applies to WF-007. Its decision dependencies OD-003 and OD-010 are both RATIFIED. S-09 owns this rule; S-12 consumes the metadata at Issue derivation and does not redefine it.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-012 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains what a Check Result carries; see MTX-061.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf007::PersistCheckResult` for every Result, and re-read unchanged by S-12's Issue derivation. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-061 output.
- command output: A Check Result carrying `impact_band`, `impact_rule_version`, `confidence_value`, `confidence_status`, `confidence_band` and `confidence_policy_version`, populated by the rules below.
- actor: Tenant-scoped evaluation service identity; the rule constrains the record, not a principal.
- organization scope: The Evaluation's Organization.
- aggregate: CheckResult; Issue is the downstream consumer, owned by CAP-014 in S-12.
- aggregate boundary: The metadata is frozen on the Check Result and COPIED, never re-derived, onto the Issue and then onto the Recommendation Artifact. That is what makes the chain auditable: `expected_impact_band`, `confidence_value` and `confidence_band` on an Artifact equal the origin Issue values and are not independently inferred.
- value objects: ImpactBand, ImpactRuleVersion, ConfidenceValue, ConfidenceStatus, ConfidenceBand, ConfidencePolicyVersion, EffortBand, EffortBasis, EffortPolicyVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - the impact mapping and band mapping are pure functions declared by the immutable Check Definition and `confidence-policy-v1`.
- repository: `CheckResultRepository`, `CheckDefinitionRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - the rule makes named `check_results` fields mandatory: `impact_band` required for `failed` and NULL for `passed`, `not_applicable` and `error`; `impact_rule_version` always; `confidence_value` a decimal `0.0000` through `1.0000`, nullable only when confidence is missing or invalid; `confidence_status` one of `valid`, `missing`, `invalid`; `confidence_band` one of `low`, `medium`, `high`; `confidence_policy_version` always. The numeric column MUST be base-10 decimal: binary floating-point output MUST NOT determine a persisted score.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added; the canonical schema already carries every named field with decimal precision.
- transaction boundary: The metadata is computed and validated inside the persist transaction, from the Definition's declared rules only. A post-hoc impact change is prohibited; changed mapping requires a new Check Definition and impact-rule version and a new Evaluation or explicit policy recalculation.
- concurrency: As MTX-061 - the materialized identity serializes the persist. The mapping is a pure function of the frozen input, so concurrent attempts compute identical metadata or fail as `check_nondeterministic_output`.
- idempotency: As MTX-061 - `impact_band`, `confidence_value`, `confidence_status` and `confidence_band` are part of the semantic output tuple covered by `deterministic_output_hash`, so replay reproducing different metadata is `check_nondeterministic_output` rather than an accepted correction.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains a persist, not a job.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry. The retried attempt it constrains reuses the identical input hash and therefore recomputes identical metadata; a two-attempt terminal error uses the fixed fallback below.
- terminal failure: A Definition with a missing or nonexhaustive impact mapping cannot enter an active Check Catalog. Discovery of catalog corruption or an unmapped failed outcome after activation fails the Evaluation as `check_catalog_integrity_failure`; it does NOT create an Issue and does NOT silently downgrade the outcome. An unlisted failed outcome under `effort-interim-v1` is `effort_policy_unavailable` and blocks Recommendation publication and Priority Decision creation WITHOUT changing the Issue or the score.
- reconciliation: The confidence fallback is the reconciliation path, not an error path: missing or invalid confidence maps to display band `low`, creates an Issue with adjudication status `review_required`, and records `confidence_status`; the raw invalid value MUST NOT be used downstream. That withheld candidate contributes zero to score and priority under the ratified OD-009 policy until it is adjudicated eligible.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-012 emits no event of its own; it constrains the impact and confidence values carried by `CheckResultCreated` and, downstream, by `IssueCreated`.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The persist command, before mutation. The rule's own authority clause is that manual and AI post-hoc impact changes are prohibited, so no actor has an entry point to alter this metadata after the fact.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-012 adds no permission, and expressly grants none: no actor, including a SecurityOperator holding `issue.adjudicate`, may change a persisted `impact_band` or `confidence_value`. Adjudication changes `adjudication_status` and eligibility, never the metadata.
- tenant boundary: The metadata derives only from the Definition's declared rules and the frozen same-Organization input tuple, so it carries no cross-tenant value. `impact_band` and `confidence_value` inherit the strongest classification of any Evidence used to derive them.
- error contract: `check_catalog_integrity_failure` for an unmapped failed outcome or a nonexhaustive impact mapping, failing the Evaluation. `effort_policy_unavailable` for an unlisted failed outcome under `effort-interim-v1`, blocking Recommendation publication and Priority Decision creation without changing the Issue or score. `output_schema_invalid` for an out-of-domain confidence value that is neither a valid decimal in `0.0000`-`1.0000` nor an explicit missing or invalid status.
- audit record: `impact_band` with `impact_rule_version` and the matched rule; `confidence_value`, `confidence_status`, `confidence_band` and `confidence_policy_version`; `effort_basis` exactly `effort-interim-v1:<check_definition_id>:<outcome_code>:<matched-rule>` with `effort_policy_version`. Every value is traceable to a declared rule rather than to a runtime judgement.
- observability: `CheckResultCreated` carries status, outcome and the impact and confidence metadata, so a band change is attributable to a Definition version rather than to an untracked recalculation.
- retention: The metadata is frozen on the immutable Check Result and copied onto the Issue at creation; neither mutates. The window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA (OD-003 ratified): the numeric confidence domain is `0.0000` through `1.0000`, rounded half up to four decimal places BEFORE band mapping. TYP-DATA: `confidence-policy-v1` band boundaries are exact -- `low` for `0.0000 <= value < 0.6000`, `medium` for `0.6000 <= value < 0.8500`, `high` for `0.8500 <= value <= 1.0000` -- asserted at `0`, `0.5999`, `0.6000`, `0.8499`, `0.8500`, `1.0000`, plus missing, invalid and out-of-domain. TYP-DATA: rounding half up is applied before mapping, so `0.59995` maps to `medium` and `0.599949` maps to `low`. TYP-DATA: a deterministic binary check uses `1.0000` unless its immutable Definition declares a different calibrated confidence rule; all seven catalog Definitions use `1.0000`, `confidence_status=valid`, band `high` for passed, failed and not-applicable. TYP-DATA: missing or invalid confidence maps to display band `low`, creates an Issue with adjudication status `review_required`, records `confidence_status`, and the raw invalid value is asserted ABSENT from every downstream computation. TYP-DATA: handled errors use the common null value, `confidence_status=missing`, band `low` fallback. TYP-DATA: `impact_band` is required for `failed` and NULL for `passed`, `not_applicable` and `error` -- four fixtures. TYP-DATA (FX-CHECK-COM-012): every impact and effort boundary maps exactly, for every catalog Check that can fail -- `impact-CHK-TI-001-v1` at 1/4/5/19/20 absent targets; `impact-CHK-CQ-001-v1` missing -> `medium`, multiple -> `low`; `impact-CHK-TR-001-v1` missing or invalid -> `medium`, mismatch -> `high`; `impact-CHK-SP-001-v1` rate `0.0000` -> `high`, `0.0000 < rate < 1.0000` -> `medium`; `impact-CHK-AIP-001-v1` the same; `impact-CHK-AS-001-v1` zero attributable -> `medium`; `impact-CHK-LP-001-v1` all absent -> `high`, partial or inconsistent -> `medium`. TYP-DATA: `effort-interim-v1` maps `CHK-TI-001` to low/medium/high by absent count, `CHK-CQ-001` to `low`, `CHK-TR-001`, `CHK-SP-001` and `CHK-LP-001` to `medium`, and `CHK-AIP-001` and `CHK-AS-001` to `high`; `effort_basis` is exactly `effort-interim-v1:<check_definition_id>:<outcome_code>:<matched-rule>`. TYP-DATA: an unlisted failed outcome is `effort_policy_unavailable` and blocks Recommendation publication and Priority Decision creation WITHOUT changing the Issue or the score -- asserted by observing the Issue and score unchanged. TYP-DATA: a Definition with a missing or nonexhaustive impact mapping cannot enter an active Catalog; an unmapped failed outcome discovered after activation is `check_catalog_integrity_failure`, creates no Issue and does NOT silently downgrade the outcome. TYP-SEC: manual and AI post-hoc impact changes are prohibited -- an attempted update to a persisted `impact_band` or `confidence_value` by any actor, including one holding `issue.adjudicate`, has no side effect; changed mapping requires a new Definition version, a new impact-rule version and a new Evaluation or explicit policy recalculation. TYP-DATA: all decimal calculations use base-10 decimal arithmetic; a binary floating-point path is asserted absent from any persisted score-determining value. TYP-DATA: the Issue and, later, the Recommendation Artifact COPY `impact_band`, `confidence_value` and `confidence_band` from the origin rather than re-deriving them -- asserted by mutating the Definition's rule after Issue creation and observing the Issue unchanged.
- rollout: As MTX-061. S-12 consumes this metadata at Issue derivation and S-14 copies it onto the Recommendation Artifact; neither re-derives it. OD-003 and OD-010 are both ratified, so nothing here is withheld.

### MTX-064 - AC-PRULE-013 (PRULE: PRULE-013)

- Slice: S-09
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#prule-013-chk-tr-001-result-context
- interface type: Persistence-completeness rule for the `CHK-TR-001` Check Result. PRULE-013 governs CAP-011 and applies to WF-007. Its decision dependency OD-010 is RATIFIED. This row adds no command and no interface; it constrains what MTX-011 must persist.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-013 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-011 output rather than an input; see MTX-011.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf007::PersistCheckResult` for every `CHK-TR-001` expected entry. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-011 input tuple, which it does not redefine.
- command output: The persisted `CHK-TR-001` Check Result carrying its exact subject, outcome code, normalized identity observation, Catalog, Definition and schema versions, deterministic input hash, and valid Source-root Evidence context.
- actor: Tenant-scoped evaluation service identity; the rule constrains the record, not a principal.
- organization scope: The Evaluation's Organization.
- aggregate: CheckResult.
- aggregate boundary: The rule binds the Check Result root only; it crosses no boundary. Its point is that decision-grade status requires the complete semantic context in the SAME record, so a Result that omits a field cannot be repaired by a join.
- value objects: CanonicalSubjectKey, OutcomeCode, NormalizedObservation, PublicIdentityProfileId, PublicIdentityProfileVersion, StructuredDataParserVersion, StructuredDataSchemaVersion, CheckCatalogVersion, CheckDefinitionVersion, DeterministicInputHash, EvidenceSetHash.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-013 names no domain service; completeness is validated in the persist command.
- repository: `CheckResultRepository`.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-013 introduces no persistent record; it makes named fields on the `check_results` row mandatory for `CHK-TR-001`: `canonical_subject_type=source` with `canonical_subject_key` the Source ID, `outcome_code`, the normalized identity observation, `check_catalog_version`, `check_definition_id`, `check_definition_version`, the structured-data parser and schema versions, `deterministic_input_hash`, and the ordered `evidence_references` with their effective Validation Decision ID and status pairs.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added; the canonical schema already carries every named field.
- transaction boundary: Completeness is validated before the persist commits, so an incomplete `CHK-TR-001` Result never becomes visible.
- concurrency: As MTX-061 - the materialized identity serializes the persist; a second concurrent persist for the same preimage returns the stored Result rather than writing a second, differently-populated one.
- idempotency: As MTX-061 - exact replay returns the stored Result with the identical persisted context. An altered context under the same result identity is `idempotency_conflict` and fails the Evaluation, so context cannot be edited by replay.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains a persist, not a job. `CHK-TR-001` execution runs in MTX-061's `CheckExecutionWorker`.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry; the single common-executor retry it constrains is MTX-061's and reuses the identical input hash, so a retried attempt persists the identical context.
- terminal failure: Any omission prevents decision-grade status: a `CHK-TR-001` Result missing its subject, outcome code, normalized observation, a version field, its input hash or its Source-root Evidence context is `output_schema_invalid` and no Issue derives from it. The rule fails the Result closed rather than persisting a partially-populated decision.
- reconciliation: The persisted context is the sole authority for `CHK-TR-001` history: `impact-CHK-TR-001-v1` mapping and `REC-CHK-TR-001-v1` rendering read `outcome_code`, `canonical_subject_key` and the normalized observation from this record and nothing else, so a later profile change cannot retroactively reinterpret it.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-013 emits no event of its own; it constrains the payload of the `CheckResultCreated` that MTX-011 emits.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The persist command, before mutation; the rule is a completeness gate, not a second authorization.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-013 adds no permission. Persistence runs under MTX-061's tenant-scoped evaluation service identity.
- tenant boundary: The Source-root Evidence context MUST be same-Organization and effectively valid, so a Result cannot carry another tenant's identity observation. Cross-Organization context fails the Evaluation through MTX-061's boundary.
- error contract: An omitted or blank required context field is `output_schema_invalid` with restricted diagnostics and no decision-grade status. Invalid Source-root Evidence is `input_evidence_invalid`; a missing root input is `input_evidence_missing`. Neither invents a substitute value.
- audit record: The persisted context IS the audit record for `CHK-TR-001`: subject, outcome code, normalized identity observation with ordered node positions, locators, schema-validity and field-match booleans, `public_identity_profile_id` and version, Catalog, Definition and schema versions, `deterministic_input_hash`, `evidence_set_hash`, and the Evidence references with their frozen Validation Decision IDs and statuses.
- observability: `CheckResultCreated` for `CHK-TR-001` carries Definition version, subject and outcome, so an incomplete Result is detectable at the event rather than at score time. Per CAP-011, parsing and validation success or failure metrics.
- retention: The frozen context is immutable and never mutates when a later Validation Decision changes the underlying Evidence; the window is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: every `CHK-TR-001` absence, exact-match, normalized-match, mismatch, malformed, error and timeout result contains the exact subject `canonical_subject_type=source` with `canonical_subject_key` equal to the opaque Source ID unchanged -- seven fixtures, each asserting the full context is present. TYP-DATA: each result persists `outcome_code` from the exhaustive set `organization_schema_missing`, `organization_identity_consistent`, `organization_schema_invalid`, `organization_identity_mismatch` plus the handled error reasons, and no other value. TYP-DATA: each result persists the normalized identity observation containing `source_id`, `canonical_root`, `public_identity_profile_id`, `public_identity_profile_version`, `node_count`, `valid_node_count`, `matching_node_count` and ordered node positions and locators with schema validity and field-match booleans. TYP-DATA: each result persists `check_catalog_version`, `check_definition_id`, `check_definition_version`, the structured-data parser and normalization-schema versions, and `deterministic_input_hash`. TYP-DATA: each passed or failed result persists valid Source-root Evidence context -- ordered `evidence_references` with content digests and the effective Validation Decision ID and status pairs as of Check creation. TYP-DATA: omission of ANY named field prevents decision-grade status and produces `output_schema_invalid` -- asserted once per required field rather than as one aggregate fixture, because an aggregate fixture passes while a single field is silently defaulted. TYP-DATA: a timeout result persists the subject, versions and input hash with null impact and `confidence_status=missing`, so even the terminal error is attributable. TYP-DATA: the persisted context does not mutate when a later Evidence Validation Decision changes the underlying Evidence; the frozen Decision IDs and statuses are retained. TYP-DATA: exact replay returns the identical persisted context and emits no second event; an altered context under the same result identity is `idempotency_conflict` and fails the Evaluation. TYP-OBS: `impact-CHK-TR-001-v1` and `REC-CHK-TR-001-v1` resolve entirely from this persisted context, asserted by rendering the template with the Evidence and profile removed from the datastore.
- rollout: As MTX-061. PRULE-013 lands with MTX-011 and gates nothing beyond it; S-13 and S-14 read the context this row makes mandatory.

### MTX-065 - AC-PRULE-014 (PRULE: PRULE-014)

- Slice: S-10
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/APPLICATION_LAYER.md#prule-014-airesponse-and-citation-integrity
- interface type: Integrity rule over AI-assisted output. PRULE-014 governs CAP-012 and applies to WF-009. Its decision dependency OD-007 is RATIFIED. This row adds no command; it constrains the MTX-012 records.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-014 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-012 schema.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf009::RequestAiResponse`, `ProposeCitations` and `ValidateAiResponse`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-012 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-012 output.
- actor: AI orchestration service identity and the tenant-scoped citation-validation service; the rule constrains the records, not a principal.
- organization scope: The origin Issue's Organization and Project, carried explicitly on both records.
- aggregate: AIResponse and Citation.
- aggregate boundary: The rule's one-parent clause is what fixes the boundary: exactly one AIResponse per Citation and exactly one Evidence per Citation. That, plus the absent Evaluation link, is why a Citation cannot be a join table and why evaluation traversal must go through the Artifact and the origin Issue.
- value objects: As MTX-012 - RequestFingerprintPreimage, CitationFingerprintPreimage, ClaimKey, ClaimSha256, EvidenceLocator, AiResponseStatus, CitationStatus.
- domain service: The tenant-scoped citation-validation service, as MTX-012. PRULE-014 names no other.
- repository: `AiResponseRepository`, `CitationRepository`.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-014 introduces no record. It constrains `citations`: `ai_response_id` NOT NULL with exactly one parent, `evidence_id` NOT NULL with exactly one target, and NO `evaluation_id` column; and `ai_responses`: the retained request-fingerprint preimage as uniqueness authority with the hash as an index only.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added. An `evaluation_id` column on `ai_responses` or `citations`, or a many-to-many join between Citation and AIResponse or Evidence, MUST NOT be migrated: OD-007 is ratified and a direct Evaluation write link is a prohibited transformation.
- transaction boundary: As MTX-012 - one fingerprinted attempt, one atomic generation transaction, one validation transaction that decides every proposed Citation.
- concurrency: One fingerprinted AIResponse attempt per preimage: concurrent exact requests resolve to that attempt and neither calls the provider twice nor validates twice.
- idempotency: Replay safety is the rule's own term. Exact preimage replay returns the original attempt; idempotency-key reuse with a different preimage is `idempotency_conflict`; a same-hash/different-preimage record never merges for either AIResponse or Citation and emits its restricted collision telemetry.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains records, not workers. The lifecycle timers are MTX-012's.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry; it forbids the hidden one. Every provider timeout and retry is a named AIResponse transition, and a retry is a new attempt linked by causation ID.
- terminal failure: No incomplete response supplies published content. Only a validated, unexpired, bound response with complete verified coverage may supply an AI-assisted Artifact; a rejected or expired response is terminal and publishes nothing.
- reconciliation: Exhaustive decisions and states is the rule's own clause: one validation transaction leaves no Citation proposed, and every AIResponse reaches exactly one of generated, validated, rejected or expired.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-014 emits no event of its own; it constrains the eleven AIResponse and Citation events MTX-012 emits.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The orchestration and validation commands, before mutation; the rule is an integrity gate that runs in addition to, never instead of, the service-identity check.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-014 adds no permission. It binds every writer: no grant authorizes a Citation with two parents, a direct Evaluation link, or an uncovered claim.
- tenant boundary: `tenant_mismatch` is the first Citation validation reason, so a cross-tenant linkage is refused before any other check can mask it. Both records carry Organization and Project explicitly rather than inferring them from the Artifact.
- error contract: As MTX-012 - the exhaustive AIResponse rejection reasons and the seven-reason Citation first-match order, plus `idempotency_conflict`. PRULE-014 adds no reason of its own; its contribution is that the sets are exhaustive and ordered.
- audit record: Both fingerprint preimages, the ordered claim manifest with keys and digests, every Citation decision with its deciding service identity and first-match reason, the coverage result, the binding, and every transition timestamp.
- observability: Every AIResponse and Citation transition, collision, replay, timeout, coverage result and binding event, per CAP-012.
- retention: Immutable lineage retained as `product_history`; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-AI: every AC-CAP-012 field, one-parent, fingerprint, replay, collision, claim-coverage, lineage, locator, boundary, decision, state and no-direct-Evaluation-link fixture passes -- PRULE-014's acceptance is exactly AC-CAP-012's, and MTX-012's `test_contracts` enumerate them. TYP-DATA: ONE fingerprinted, replay-safe AIResponse attempt per preimage -- asserted sequentially and concurrently. TYP-DATA: exactly ONE AIResponse and exactly ONE Evidence per Citation -- a Citation with zero, two or more of either parent is not creatable, asserted as four separate fixtures. TYP-SEC: no direct Evaluation write link on AIResponse or Citation -- the column is asserted ABSENT from the schema, and the read-model projection that denormalizes the traversal is asserted to have no write path. TYP-DATA: complete claim coverage -- every claim has at least one verified Citation, every Citation targets a manifest claim, and no Citation remains proposed or invalid; each clause fails independently. TYP-DATA: decisions and states are exhaustive -- every AIResponse reaches exactly one of generated, validated, rejected or expired, and one validation transaction leaves no Citation proposed. TYP-AI: no INCOMPLETE response supplies published content -- a response with one uncovered claim, one invalid Citation, or one proposed Citation each publishes nothing.
