TYP-DATA: only a VALIDATED, UNEXPIRED, BOUND response may supply an AI-assisted Artifact -- the expired case is asserted separately because expiry wins at exactly `publication_expires_at_utc`. TYP-OBS: no hidden provider retry -- every retry is a new linked attempt visible in the audit trace.
- rollout: As MTX-012. PRULE-014 lands with CAP-012 in S-10; S-14 consumes the validated bound response as a publication predicate and does not re-contract this rule.

### MTX-066 - AC-PRULE-015 (PRULE: PRULE-015)

- Slice: S-10,S-14
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-015-ai-safety-and-prohibited-advisory-domain
- interface type: Safety and advisory-scope rule over AI and deterministic Recommendation responses. PRULE-015 governs CAP-012 and CAP-016 and applies to WF-009; its Decision Dependency is None. This row owns its CAP-012 limb -- the response, the scanner and the rejection path. The CAP-016 limb, which is the deterministic template output and Artifact publication gate, is owned by S-14 and is referenced here, not re-contracted.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-015 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains generated and deterministic output rather than a request.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf009::ValidateAiResponse` before Citation validation, and within S-14's Artifact validation for the deterministic limb. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains output content.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains output content: a violating response publishes nothing.
- actor: The AI orchestration service's output scanner and the Artifact validator; the rule constrains content, not a principal. It applies to BOTH generation modes, so a deterministic template is not exempt.
- organization scope: The origin Issue's Organization and Project.
- aggregate: AIResponse for the CAP-012 limb; RecommendationArtifact for the CAP-016 limb owned by S-14.
- aggregate boundary: The rule crosses no boundary; it is a content predicate evaluated before publication on each root.
- value objects: AdvisoryScope fixed to `discoverability_only`, ScannerReasonCode, RejectionReason, GenerationMode.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-015 names no domain service. The prohibited-content scanner is named by the signed `ai-safety-interim-v1` artifact and runs inside the validation command; the deterministic limb's check is a pure predicate over the rendered template.
- repository: `AiResponseRepository`, `RecommendationArtifactRepository` read-only at this row's boundary, `AiPolicyArtifactRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable - PRULE-015 introduces no persistent concept. It constrains the content of records MTX-012 and S-14's MTX-016 and MTX-034 write, and fixes `advisory_scope=discoverability_only` on every Artifact.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object.
- transaction boundary: The scanner runs before Citation validation and inside the same validation transaction, so a violating response is never observable as validated. A rejected response and its Citation decisions are retained in that same transaction for audit.
- concurrency: The predicate is a pure function of the rendered content, so concurrent evaluations agree. There is no race in which a violating response wins publication: publication requires validation, and validation applies the scanner first.
- idempotency: A rejection under this rule is terminal for that attempt and is idempotent by the AIResponse's request fingerprint: replaying the identical request returns the same rejected attempt without rescanning or recalling the provider. Correction is a NEW linked attempt, not a re-decision.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains a synchronous validation step, not a job.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - a `prohibited_advisory_domain` failure is NONRETRYABLE. Only a new linked attempt after input or policy correction is permitted.
- terminal failure: A personalized legal or tax conclusion, a compliance determination, a filing position, or an instruction to take a legal or tax action fails nonretryably with `prohibited_advisory_domain` in BOTH generated and deterministic output and publishes nothing. `advisory_scope` other than `discoverability_only` blocks publication.
- reconciliation: The permitted boundary is precise and is the reconciliation rule: a Recommendation MAY state an observed discoverability fact and MAY direct the customer to consult a qualified professional; it MUST NOT supply the professional conclusion. Template rendering never introduces a legal or tax conclusion or a direct production mutation.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-015 emits no event of its own; it gates `AIResponseRejected` with its reason and, on the S-14 limb, blocks `RecommendationPublished`.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The validation command, before the response can become validated. The rule is a content gate with no actor-facing entry point: no principal can approve prohibited content.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-015 adds no permission and grants no override. No actor, including one holding `recommendation.publish`, may publish a response that fails the scanner or carries a prohibited advisory conclusion.
- tenant boundary: `secret_or_personal_data` is the second scanner reason, so leaked tenant secrets or personal data reject the response before advisory checks. Restricted diagnostics for a scanner match remain restricted and never enter customer output.
- error contract: Scanner first-match order, applied before Citation validation: `prompt_instruction_leakage`, `secret_or_personal_data`, `prohibited_advisory_domain`, `unsafe_external_action`, `output_schema_invalid`. Any match changes the AIResponse to rejected with `policy_denied` EXCEPT `prohibited_advisory_domain` and `output_schema_invalid`, which are already canonical reasons and are used directly. `prohibited_advisory_domain` is also the exact WF-009 failure reason for the deterministic limb, positioned fifth in that workflow's first-match order.
- audit record: The matched scanner reason, the rejection reason, the generation mode, the target Recommendation and version, the origin Issue and state version, and the correlation ID. Restricted diagnostics retain the matched content for security review and are never surfaced to the customer.
- observability: Every rejection under this rule is observable with its exact reason and generation mode, so a safety rejection is distinguishable from a provider failure and from a schema failure.
- retention: Rejected responses and their Citation decisions are retained as immutable audit lineage; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-AI: every policy and safety fixture publishes nothing and records the exact `prohibited_advisory_domain`, other nonretryable, or fallback outcome. TYP-AI: a personalized legal conclusion, a personalized tax conclusion, a compliance determination, a filing position and an instruction to take a legal or tax action each fail nonretryably with `prohibited_advisory_domain` -- five separate fixtures. TYP-AI: the same five fixtures fail identically in `deterministic_template` mode -- the rule applies to BOTH modes, and the deterministic case is asserted separately because a template is the path an implementation assumes is safe by construction. TYP-AI: the permitted boundary holds -- a Recommendation stating an observed discoverability fact publishes, and one directing the customer to consult a qualified professional publishes; only supplying the professional conclusion fails. TYP-SEC: the scanner first-match order is exactly `prompt_instruction_leakage`, `secret_or_personal_data`, `prohibited_advisory_domain`, `unsafe_external_action`, `output_schema_invalid`, asserted with a fixture where two reasons would both apply. TYP-SEC: the scanner runs BEFORE Citation validation -- asserted by a response that would fail both, which records the scanner reason. TYP-SEC: a scanner match changes the AIResponse to rejected with `policy_denied` except the already canonical prohibited-advisory and schema reasons, publishes nothing, and permits only a NEW linked attempt after input or policy correction -- a re-decision of the same attempt is asserted impossible. TYP-DATA: `advisory_scope` is fixed to `discoverability_only` on every Artifact; any other value blocks publication. TYP-DATA: template rendering introduces no legal or tax conclusion and no direct production mutation -- asserted across all seven `REC-...-v1` templates. TYP-SEC: no permission overrides the rule -- an actor holding `recommendation.publish` cannot publish a scanner-failing or prohibited-advisory response. TYP-OBS: restricted scanner diagnostics are asserted absent from customer output.
- rollout: S-10 owns the CAP-012 limb of PRULE-015; the CAP-016 limb lands with S-14 and references this section rather than restating it. The rule's Decision Dependency is None, so nothing here is withheld -- and because it binds deterministic output too, it is fully exercised at the baseline even though the `ai_assisted` path is gated closed.

### MTX-067 - AC-PRULE-016 (PRULE: PRULE-016)

- Slice: S-11,S-13,S-14
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-016-evidence-contract-retention-split-and-propagation
- interface type: Complete-contract and propagation rule for Evidence. PRULE-016 governs CAP-013, CAP-015 and CAP-016 and applies to WF-003, WF-006, WF-007, WF-008, WF-009 and WF-013. This row owns the rule for S-11, S-13 and S-14; S-13 consumes its score-unavailability limb and S-14 its Artifact-suppression limb, and neither re-contracts it. Its decision dependencies OD-011, OD-029 and OD-030 are all RESOLVED; nothing here is withheld.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-016 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-013 schema and the producer contracts owned by S-05 and S-08.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced at every Evidence creation performed by a producing workflow, within `Workflows::Wf007::SubmitEvidenceValidationDecision`, and as a precondition on every downstream Issue, Score Contribution, Recommendation Artifact and Citation write. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-013 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-013 output.
- actor: The producing workflow's service identity at creation; the integrity-validation service and, for quarantine only, a scoped SecurityOperator at transition. The rule constrains the record and the propagation, not a principal.
- organization scope: The Evidence's Organization; the same-Organization clause is part of the rule's own text.
- aggregate: Evidence and EvidenceValidationDecision as the constrained roots; CurrentScoreProjection, ScoreSnapshot, RecommendationArtifact and Citation as propagation targets.
- aggregate boundary: The rule's atomicity clause is what makes the propagation commit wide rather than eventual: expiry, hold or another effective transition away from valid MUST atomically make affected current score and recommendation projections unavailable or suppressed WITHOUT rewriting history. Both halves are load-bearing -- a narrow commit leaves a stale numeric score readable, and a history-rewriting commit destroys the immutable snapshot the product's explainability rests on.
- value objects: ValidationStatus, EvidenceValidationDecisionId, DataClassification, PayloadRetentionClass, ProductHistoryClass, ContentSha256, EvidenceProvenance, UnavailableReasonCode, PublicationStatus.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-016 names no domain service; the tenant-scoped integrity-validation service it relies on is named by CAP-013 and contracted in MTX-013.
- repository: `EvidenceRepository`, `EvidenceValidationDecisionRepository`, `CurrentScoreProjectionRepository`, `RecommendationArtifactRepository`, `CitationRepository`, `ScoreSnapshotRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-016 introduces no record. It makes the complete Evidence field, digest, provenance, validation, classification, immutability and same-Organization contract mandatory before downstream use, and it fixes the retention split at the column level: only `payload_retention_class` on the referenced Payload is `product_evidence_payload`; the envelope and lineage are `product_history` and retain after payload destruction.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added; this row requires no canonical namespace for an unassigned record, so the OD-032 limb withheld on MTX-013 does not reach it.
- transaction boundary: As MTX-013 - one transaction per Decision performing every applicable current effect before commit and enqueuing idempotent recalculation. The rule's contribution is that the transaction is mandatory and its scope is exhaustive, not that it introduces a new boundary.
- concurrency: The mandatory expected validation-state version prevents two concurrent transitions on one Evidence record. Because history is never rewritten, a concurrent historical read is unaffected by an in-flight transition: it reads the frozen state-at-snapshot values.
- idempotency: The Decision replay contract is the rule's idempotency guarantee: exact replay returns the Decision without repeating suppression, the event or recalculation. Recalculation is itself idempotent and replaces the complete reason set rather than applying a delta, so a redelivery converges to the same reason set instead of accumulating codes.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains a synchronous commit and the recalculation it enqueues; it defines no job of its own. The retention-expiry and destruction jobs are MTX-013's.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - PRULE-016 defines no retry. A rejected creation or Decision is not retried; the correct recovery is new Evidence or a new Decision against the latest version.
- terminal failure: Evidence failing the complete contract has NO downstream use: it supports no Issue, no included Score Contribution, no Recommendation Artifact and no customer-visible citation. `invalid` is terminal. The rule fails downstream writes closed rather than degrading them.
- reconciliation: History is never rewritten. Historical views reconstruct Issue and adjudication state only from each ScoreSnapshot's immutable Contributions, including captured Issue state version, lifecycle, publication and adjudication values, Case references, Evidence IDs and Validation Decision IDs and statuses. Current views use the Current Score Projection and return no numeric score while it is unavailable, even though the retained last snapshot remains accessible AS HISTORY. That separation is the reconciliation: the same Evidence transition makes the current view unavailable and leaves the historical view intact.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-016 emits no event of its own; it gates `EvidenceValidationChanged` and `EvidenceRetentionExpiring`, which MTX-013 emits, and it is why a suppression is committed before, not after, the event.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Each Evidence creation and each Decision command, before mutation. The rule is a completeness and propagation gate that runs in addition to, never instead of, the actor limits MTX-013 enforces.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-016 adds no permission. It binds every writer: no grant permits Evidence that fails the complete contract to support an Issue, a Contribution, an Artifact or a citation, and none permits a classification to be lowered.
- tenant boundary: The same-Organization contract is part of the rule's own text and must hold BEFORE downstream use, not at read time. A derived field inherits the strongest classification of any Evidence used to derive it, so a cross-classification leak cannot enter through a derivation.
- error contract: As MTX-013 - creation rejection for an invalid field, missing bytes, digest mismatch, unknown schema, tenant mismatch or an unknown `payload_retention_class`; downstream data-integrity rejection with an audit event; rejection without a Decision for a stale version, same-status request or unlisted transition or reason. On the propagation limb, `invalid_evidence` enters the Current Score Projection's de-duplicated fixed-precedence unavailable-reason set at its own position.
- audit record: As MTX-013, plus the propagation record: which projections were made unavailable, which Artifacts were suppressed by origin and which independently by rationale or Citation reference, the invalidating Decision IDs retained on the projection, and the enqueued recalculation ID.
- observability: The unavailable-reason set is customer-visible and ordered by fixed precedence, so an unavailable score names its cause rather than presenting as an outage. Every suppression and republication emits an audited state event.
- retention: The split is the rule's own clause: the envelope and lineage retain as `product_history` while only the referenced Payload uses `product_evidence_payload`. The window values are owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: every Evidence field, digest, immutability, tenant and classification fixture in AC-CAP-013 passes -- MTX-013's `test_contracts` enumerate them and this row asserts the same set as the rule's acceptance. TYP-DATA: the nonpersisted Evidence Source view is asserted never separately stored. TYP-DATA: the `product_history` envelope versus `payload_retention_class=product_evidence_payload` split is asserted at the field level, including AFTER payload destruction, when the envelope, digest, provenance, Validation Decisions and downstream lineage all remain readable. TYP-SEC: the operator and service transition matrix is exact, with a SecurityOperator confined to `valid -> quarantined` for `security_restriction`, `consent_review` and `retention_review` only. TYP-OBS: the 30-day warning fires exactly once per Payload, with the boundary asserted immediately before, exactly at and immediately after. TYP-DATA: the exact 24-month Payload expiry on the capture cursor, not accelerated to the accrued minimum. TYP-DATA: hold quarantine and release -- destruction is suspended and occurs at the first eligible instant after release. TYP-DATA: byte destruction leaves the `product_history` envelope and lineage frozen and readable. TYP-DATA: Issue, Check and Recommendation propagation -- an effective transition away from valid atomically makes affected current score and recommendation projections unavailable or suppressed BEFORE bytes disappear, while history remains frozen. TYP-DATA: history is not rewritten -- a historical view reconstructed from a ScoreSnapshot's immutable Contributions returns the same values before and after the transition. TYP-DATA: a current view returns no numeric score and the exact ordered reason codes while unavailable, even though the retained last snapshot remains accessible as history -- the two views are asserted to diverge, which is the point. TYP-DATA: recalculation replaces the COMPLETE reason set with all and only reasons applicable to the recalculated inputs; a redelivery converges rather than accumulating codes. TYP-SEC: Evidence Classification fixtures return exact visibility; declassification is prohibited and asserted ineffective for each downward step. TYP-DATA: exact replay of a Decision repeats no suppression, event or recalculation.
- rollout: S-11 owns PRULE-016 for S-11, S-13 and S-14. S-13 (CAP-015 scoring) consumes the score-unavailability limb and owns the ScoreSnapshot and Current Score Projection records it writes; S-14 (CAP-016 recommendations) consumes the Artifact-suppression limb and owns the publication lifecycle. OD-011, OD-029 and OD-030 are resolved, so nothing here is withheld, and this row needs no canonical namespace for an unassigned record so the OD-032 limb does not reach it.

### MTX-069 - AC-PRULE-018 (PRULE: PRULE-018)

- Slice: S-01,S-23
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: OD-032 -- OD-032: canonical namespace for an unassigned record withheld; behaviour permitted under the interim
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-018-account-lifecycle-authority
- interface type: Auditable-outcome rule applied to every Account, Bootstrap Grant, Invitation, Session and derived Membership lifecycle attempt, including denials and replays. PRULE-018 governs CAP-001 and CAP-025 and applies to WF-001 and WF-013. It is an emission and return obligation, not an interface. This row owns its WF-013 limb; the WF-001 limb is owned by S-01.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-018 is an outcome rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains what every lifecycle attempt EMITS or RETURNS, not what it accepts. It applies to the MTX-038 schemas unchanged.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema. The rule constrains the audited outcome accompanying every response, including a denial.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter; PRULE-018 is a rule.
- command: Enforced within every `Workflows::Wf013` command in MTX-038 and, for the Bootstrap Grant and sign-in limbs, within `Workflows::Wf001`, which S-01 owns. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - the rule constrains the outcome of the MTX-038 inputs, not their shape.
- command output: Exactly one auditable outcome per attempt, carrying actor or service, entity or view, from and to status, outcome, reason and UTC time.
- actor: Every actor and every named lifecycle service that attempts a lifecycle transition. The rule's sharpest clause is about actor identity: an outcome MUST NOT fabricate a human actor for service work, so a timed expiry records its lifecycle service identity and never attributes itself to the OrganizationAdmin who created the record.
- organization scope: The target record's Organization. A Bootstrap Grant attempt has no Organization; under ratified OD-013 Option 1 `BootstrapGrantIssued` and `BootstrapGrantExpired` alone substitute the immutable `bootstrap_principal_id` for `organization_id`, and that substitution is confined to those two event types and is never available once an Organization exists. That limb is S-01's and is referenced here, not restated.
- aggregate: Account, Bootstrap Grant, Invitation and Session as records; `OrganizationMembership` as a derived VIEW. The rule names the view expressly, which is why the outcome field is 'entity or view' rather than 'entity': a Membership outcome reports a derived status change with no record behind it.
- aggregate boundary: The rule attaches one outcome to each attempt against each of those roots or the derived view; it crosses no boundary and mutates nothing.
- value objects: ActorOrServiceIdentity, EntityOrViewReference, FromStatus, ToStatus, Outcome, Reason, TimestampUtc, MembershipDerivedStatus (`active`, `no_active_grant`, `suspended`, `revoked`).
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-018 names no domain service; the outcome is written by the command or lifecycle service that made the attempt.
- repository: `AccountRepository`, `InvitationRepository`, `SessionRepository`; the Bootstrap Grant repository is S-01's. The Membership view is resolved through the access-policy resolution path and has no repository of its own because it is not a record.
- persistence model: The rule introduces no persistent concept; it constrains the outcome each MTX-038 command already writes. Outcome records follow `security_audit`. WITHHELD under OD-032: no canonical DM-REQ-001 identifier namespace or DM-REQ-001 lifecycle owner is asserted for Session, which DM-REQ-001's core-entity catalogue omits. This is precisely where the withholding bites this rule and it is stated rather than glossed: DM-REQ-006 requires identifier namespaces to appear in logs, audit events and telemetry labels, and SM-REQ-004 requires every transition attempt to emit an audit event including `entity_id`, so a Session outcome uses Volume I's logical `session_id` field name and asserts no `ses_`-style canonical namespace. The outcome obligation itself is unaffected: the audit event is emitted, with the logical identifier, and only the namespace assignment is withheld.
- migration: Not applicable - PRULE-018 adds no schema object.
- transaction boundary: The outcome commits in the SAME transaction as the attempt it records, including a denial that changes no state. This is what makes the rule's 'including denial and replay' clause true under failure: a denial that rolled back its own audit record would satisfy the rule in the happy path and breach it exactly when it matters.
- concurrency: One attempt yields exactly one outcome under concurrency: two commands racing the same record produce one accepted outcome and one denied or stale outcome, never a shared or merged record. A derived Membership status change emits no outcome of its own when it results from an Assignment expiry, because the Assignment's outcome already records the transition and the view has no independent lifecycle -- `onboarding-interim-v1` states Membership has no independent expiry.
- idempotency: Exact replay emits NO duplicate transition, event or Session, and returns the stored result with its original outcome rather than writing a second one. The rule's own words are that every attempt emits or returns exactly one auditable outcome; a replay is not a second attempt.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains outcomes, not workers. The timed transitions it covers run as the ScheduledActions in MTX-038, and each records its lifecycle service identity as the actor.
- queue: Not applicable - PRULE-018 has no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry. A denial is a completed outcome, not a transient failure.
- terminal failure: An attempt that produces no outcome, produces more than one, omits any of the six required fields, or attributes service work to a human actor is a breach of the rule and is a test failure rather than a tolerated gap.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-018 emits no event of its own. It constrains the outcome carried by the MTX-038 and WF-001 event sets and by the audited command outcome that a rejected no-state command emits instead of an event.
- event payload: Not applicable - PRULE-018 defines no event of its own; it fixes the six fields the outcome must carry.
- event producer: Not applicable - PRULE-018 defines no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - PRULE-018 defines no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure; PRULE-018 is a rule.
- authorization entry point: Not applicable as a check - PRULE-018 authorizes nothing. Its relationship to authorization runs the other way: it obliges every DENIED attempt at the MTX-038 entry points to emit its auditable outcome, so an authorization denial is never silent.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-018 adds and requires no permission. The permissions of the attempts it records are MTX-038's, and the outcome records which named permission was used.
- tenant boundary: Each outcome carries its record's Organization, so one tenant's lifecycle outcomes are never readable as another's. The single exception is the pre-Organization Bootstrap Grant substitution under OD-013 Option 1, which is S-01's limb.
- error contract: A denial emits its outcome with the exact first-match reason from the governing contract and changes nothing. Existing-account sign-in is the one place the outward reason is deliberately normalized to `F1-AUTHN-401 / authentication_failed` while the exact restricted internal reason is retained in the audit outcome; that limb is S-01's and this row does not weaken it by logging the internal reason outward.
- audit record: Exactly one auditable outcome per attempt with all six fields: actor or service, entity or view, from and to status, outcome, reason and UTC time. No human actor is fabricated for service work.
- observability: Every lifecycle attempt is observable through its single outcome, including denials and stale attempts. The rule is what makes the WF-013 audit clause's 'every denied or stale attempt' obligation enforceable rather than aspirational.
- retention: Outcome records follow `security_audit` under `retention-interim-v1`: 7 elapsed years after terminal transition, irreversible destruction only with two-person security approval. PRULE-018 states no window of its own.
- test contracts: TYP-OBS: every Account suspend, reactivate, revoke and delete attempt, accepted AND denied, emits exactly one auditable outcome with all six fields. TYP-OBS: every Invitation create, approve, reject, revoke, accept, decline, reissue and timed-expiry attempt does the same. TYP-OBS: every Session creation, revocation and expiry attempt does the same. TYP-OBS: every Bootstrap Grant issue, consume and expiry attempt does the same, asserted at the S-01 boundary this row references. TYP-OBS: a derived Membership status resolution reports the view with its tuple, Account status and state version, Organization authorization epoch and ordered Assignment IDs and state versions, and emits no independent lifecycle outcome, because Membership has no independent expiry. TYP-SEC: no human actor is fabricated for service work -- a timed Invitation expiry, role-assignment expiry, support-session expiry, closure-request expiry and closure execution each record their own lifecycle service identity, asserted by proving the actor field is NOT the requesting administrator. TYP-DATA: exactly ONE outcome per attempt -- not zero, and not two under a concurrent race; two commands racing one record produce one accepted and one denied or stale outcome. TYP-DATA: replay emits no duplicate transition, event or Session and returns the stored result with its original outcome. TYP-DATA: the outcome commits in the same transaction as the attempt, proven by a rolled-back attempt leaving no outcome and a denied attempt leaving exactly one. TYP-SEC: a denial's outcome carries the exact first-match reason; the existing-account sign-in outward normalization to `authentication_failed` is preserved while the restricted internal reason stays internal. TYP-OBS: an attempt missing any of the six fields fails the fixture rather than being accepted as partially audited. WITHHELD under OD-032 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting a DM-REQ-001 identifier namespace or DM-REQ-001 lifecycle owner for the Session record, including any `ses_`-style prefix in a log, audit event or telemetry label. The outcome obligation is contracted in full using Volume I's logical `session_id` field name; only the namespace assignment is withheld.
- rollout: The WF-001 limb lands with S-01 and the WF-013 limb with S-23; the rule applies from the first lifecycle attempt in either. The OD-032 limb requires owner ratification and a controlled 011 DOMAIN_MODEL.md change and blocks no behaviour.

### MTX-070 - AC-PRULE-019 (PRULE: PRULE-019)

- Slice: S-02
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-019-organization-state-authorization-gate
- interface type: Authorization gate applied at every protected mutation, in every slice. PRULE-019 governs CAP-002 and applies to WF-001 and WF-013. It is an enforcement predicate, not an interface.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-019 is an authorization predicate, not an interface.
- command: Enforced by every command that performs a protected mutation, in every slice. The Organization lifecycle commands that move the state it gates (`SuspendOrganization`, `ReactivateOrganization`, `RequestOrganizationClosure`, `DecideOrganizationClosure`, `ExpireOrganizationClosure`, `ExecuteOrganizationClosure`) are owned by WF-013 in S-23. This row owns the gate, not the transitions.
- actor: Any actor attempting a protected mutation. The gate is actor-independent: it tests Organization state, not identity.
- organization scope: The Organization owning the target resource. WF-013 requires every command on an existing record to carry its expected Organization authorization epoch when effective access could change.
- aggregate: Organization (state and authorization epoch) as the gate subject; the target aggregate of each protected mutation as the gated object.
- aggregate boundary: The gate reads Organization state and epoch and does not mutate them, so it crosses no aggregate boundary. Reading a canonical lifecycle state as a guard is permitted; effecting a transition is not this row's concern.
- value objects: OrganizationState (`pending`, `active`, `suspended`, `closed`), AuthorizationEpoch, ResolvedPermissionSet.
- domain service: Not applicable - the gate is an application-layer authorization predicate evaluated before mutation.
- repository: `OrganizationRepository` for state and epoch; the resolved permission set is read through the access-policy resolution path.
- persistence model: Reads Organization state and authorization epoch. Writes nothing.
- migration: Not applicable - the gate introduces no schema object.
- transaction boundary: Evaluated inside the mutating command's transaction, before its state mutation, so an Organization suspended concurrently cannot be raced past the gate. The epoch check makes a stale authorization context fail rather than proceed.
- concurrency: Guarded by the expected Organization authorization epoch that WF-013 requires whenever effective access could change. A command carrying a stale epoch is rejected without side effects rather than executing against superseded permissions.
- idempotency: Not applicable - the gate is a read-only predicate and performs no mutation, so it has no replay semantics of its own. The commands it gates carry their own idempotency contracts.
- background job: Not applicable - the gate is synchronous within each command. It is not a controller-only check: WF-013 requires it wherever a protected mutation is reached, including through services and lifecycle jobs.
- queue: Not applicable - not asynchronous.
- retry policy: Not applicable - a denial is a terminal authorization outcome, never retried. The rule admits no transient failure mode.
- terminal failure: A protected mutation attempted against a suspended or closed Organization is denied with its enumerated reason and no side effect. Closed is terminal, so no later attempt can succeed.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - no state is written.
- domain events: Not applicable - the gate emits no event of its own. Denials are recorded as audited command outcomes.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Every protected mutation, before the mutation. Because suspended and closed MUST deny every protected mutation, the gate cannot live only in a controller: it is enforced in the application command, which is the single path shared by routes, services, event consumers and lifecycle jobs.
- permission checks: Active Organization permits protected behaviour only within the resolved permission set, so an active Organization is necessary and not sufficient. Suspended or closed denies every protected mutation regardless of the resolved permissions. The Organization lifecycle and protected Legal Hold/deletion execution are admitted in suspended or closed state exactly as their own contracts require, which is a precondition WF-013 states explicitly and this gate must not override.
- tenant boundary: The gate is per-Organization; one Organization's suspension never gates another's mutations.
- error contract: Denial carries the enumerated authorization reason with no side effect. A stale authorization epoch is a conflict outcome, not an authorization outcome, and is rejected without side effects.
- audit record: Every denial is recorded as an audited command outcome with its reason, the Organization state that caused it, and the correlation ID. Immutable audit logs per WF-013 Security Notes.
- observability: Denial reason and Organization state are observable per decision; the resolved permission set is not logged in raw form.
- retention: Not applicable - the gate defines no retention obligation; audit retention is owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: an active Organization permits a protected mutation only when the resolved permission set allows it - an active Organization with no allowed permission is denied. TYP-SEC: every protected mutation is denied against a suspended Organization, asserted across routes, services and lifecycle jobs rather than at one entry point. TYP-SEC: every protected mutation is denied against a closed Organization, and closed is terminal. TYP-SEC: the WF-013 exceptions still execute in suspended or closed state - Organization reactivation/closure lifecycle and protected Legal Hold/deletion execution. TYP-SEC: reactivation restores only unexpired explicit grants; an expired grant stays expired and is not revived. TYP-DATA: a command carrying a stale Organization authorization epoch is rejected without side effects.
- rollout: Applies from the moment the first Organization exists; no predecessor behaviour to migrate.

- route: Not applicable - PRULE-020 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-028 `method` field.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf003::CreateVerificationRequest`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-028 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-028 output.
- actor: As MTX-028 - the creating actor.
- organization scope: The Request Organization.
- aggregate: VerificationRequest.
- aggregate boundary: The rule constrains the Request root at creation only.
- value objects: VerificationMethod, restricted to `dns_txt` and `http_file`.
- domain service: Not applicable - a pure predicate in the creation command.
- repository: `VerificationRequestRepository`.
- persistence model: The `method` column admits exactly `dns_txt` and `http_file`; a CHECK constraint makes an unsupported method unrepresentable rather than merely rejected at the application edge.
- migration: Not applicable - the canonical schema owns the column and its constraint.

- idempotency: Not applicable - rejection creates no Request, so there is nothing to replay. A replayed valid creation is governed by MTX-028's idempotency key.
- background job: Not applicable - synchronous.
- queue: Not applicable - no asynchronous work.
- retry policy: Not applicable - `unsupported_method` is a terminal validation outcome and is never retried.
- terminal failure: `unsupported_method`, before challenge issuance, creating no Request.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - nothing is written.
- domain events: Not applicable - a rejected creation emits one audited command outcome, not an event.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The creation command; the method check precedes authorization-independent token issuance.
- permission checks: As MTX-028 - the rule adds no permission.
- tenant boundary: Not applicable - the method set is global and identical in every Organization; no tenant may widen it.

- audit record: One audited command outcome carrying `unsupported_method` and the correlation ID.
- observability: The rejected method value is recorded as an enum outcome; no challenge material exists to redact because none is generated.

TYP-SEC: rejection occurs before challenge issuance - no token is generated, no Request row is written and no ciphertext is stored for an unsupported method. TYP-SEC: no method bypasses exact validation - an accepted `http_file` still fails on redirect, non-200, oversize body and content mismatch, and an accepted `dns_txt` still fails on case, whitespace, prefix and suffix differences. TYP-DATA: the persistence layer cannot represent an unsupported method, asserted by attempting a direct write.
- rollout: OD-001 is ratified as Option 2, so the method set is the approved baseline rather than a provisional choice. Adding a third method would be a controlled Volume I change, not an implementation decision.

### MTX-072 - AC-PRULE-021 (PRULE: PRULE-021)

- Slice: S-06
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-021-source-scope-predicate
- interface type: Scope predicate evaluated wherever a discovered URL is admitted. PRULE-021 governs CAP-006 and applies to WF-004 and WF-005. This row owns the predicate; its WF-005 enforcement at crawl time is applied by S-07 against the policy defined here.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-021 is a predicate, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the predicate's input is a candidate canonical URL plus the active policy intersection.
- response schema: Not applicable - the predicate returns allowed or a denial reason.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf004` when a policy version is activated, and applied by the S-07 crawl scheduler against the pinned policy. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - a pure function, not a command.
- command output: Not applicable - a pure function, not a command.
- actor: Not applicable - the predicate is actor-independent. It tests a URL against a policy, not a principal against a permission.
- organization scope: The Source's Organization; a policy names one exact `canonical_host` within it.
- aggregate: SourceScopePolicy as the predicate subject; the candidate URL is not persisted by this row.
- aggregate boundary: The predicate reads active policy versions and mutates nothing.
- value objects: CanonicalUrl, CanonicalHost, NormalizedPath, NormalizedQuery, PathPrefix.
- domain service: Not applicable - a pure function over the URL and the active policy intersection.
- repository: `SourceScopePolicyRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable - the predicate persists nothing. An out-of-scope URL is rejected or quarantined with its reason by the caller; the durable evidence is the caller's decision record, not a scope-evaluation row.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object.
- transaction boundary: Evaluated inside the caller's transaction against the pinned policy version, so a policy change cannot be raced past an in-flight admission.
- concurrency: The predicate is pure and has no race of its own. Its inputs are versioned: the caller pins the active policy version, so a concurrent policy activation does not retroactively admit or reject an already-decided URL.
- idempotency: Naturally idempotent - a pure function of the URL and the policy version returns the same result for the same inputs. Re-evaluating a URL under the same policy version cannot change its admission.
- background job: Not applicable - the predicate is synchronous within its caller.
- queue: Not applicable - no asynchronous work.
- retry policy: Not applicable - a pure predicate has no transient failure to retry. A denial is a decision, not an error.
- terminal failure: An out-of-scope URL is rejected or quarantined with its reason and never enters valid Evidence or evaluation input. This is terminal for that URL under that policy version.
- reconciliation: Not applicable - nothing is written.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-021 emits no event of its own; the caller records the rejection or quarantine.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Not applicable - the predicate is not an authorization check. It is a scope check, evaluated after authorization, and both must pass.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-021 adds no permission. Widening the policy the predicate reads requires `policy.source_scope.manage` and the MTX-029 dual-control rule.
- tenant boundary: A policy names one exact verified `canonical_host`, so a URL on any other host fails the predicate. Subdomains, alternate apex and `www` hosts, ports and schemes are outside scope unless separately present in the verified policy, which is what prevents boundary leakage by URL rather than by permission.
- error contract: A URL is allowed only when it matches every active policy intersection, at least one include prefix, no exclude prefix, and the query rule. Exclusion wins over inclusion. Every out-of-bound discovered URL is rejected or quarantined with its reason and never enters valid Evidence or evaluation input. An out-of-scope redirect is not followed.
- audit record: The caller records the rejection or quarantine with its reason; the predicate itself writes nothing.
- observability: Rejection and quarantine reasons are observable per candidate URL through the caller's decision record.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-021 persists nothing of its own.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: normalization order - host lowercase IDNA ASCII without trailing dot; default port removed; path dot segments and unreserved percent encoding normalized; fragment and user information prohibited; query pairs retained and sorted by decoded key then value while preserving duplicates. TYP-DATA: path prefix matches exactly or at a `/` segment boundary - `/shop` matches `/shop` and `/shop/item` but NOT `/shopping`. TYP-DATA: exclusion wins over inclusion - a URL matching both an include and an exclude prefix is rejected. TYP-DATA: query handling - `retain_all` retains every pair; an explicit allowlist retains only listed keys. TYP-SEC: a subdomain is out of scope unless separately present in the verified policy. TYP-SEC: an alternate apex or `www` host is out of scope unless separately present. TYP-SEC: a non-HTTPS scheme and a nondefault port are each out of scope. TYP-SEC: an out-of-scope redirect is not followed and the target does not become Evidence. TYP-DATA: a URL must match EVERY active policy intersection - passing one policy and failing another is rejected. TYP-DATA: every out-of-bound discovered URL is rejected or quarantined with a reason and never enters valid Evidence or evaluation input. TYP-DATA: the predicate is pure - the same URL under the same policy version always yields the same decision.
- rollout: The predicate is defined here and applied by S-07 at crawl time against the pinned policy version. S-07 does not redefine it.

### MTX-073 - AC-PRULE-022 (PRULE: PRULE-022)

- Slice: S-07,S-21
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-022-recovery-authority-envelope
- interface type: Authority envelope rule for every recovery action. PRULE-022 governs CAP-008 and CAP-023 and applies to WF-005, WF-006 and WF-017. This row owns its WF-005 limb; the WF-006 dead-letter replay limb is owned by S-08 and the WF-017 incident limb by S-24.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-022 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-030 recovery schema.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `RecoverCrawl` and `ReplayIngestionJob`. This row adds no command.
- command input: The mandatory envelope: named permission, scoped support or incident authority, expected state version, reason, idempotency key, audit envelope.
- command output: The linked recovery outcome, or a denial with no side effect.
- actor: Organization Administrator or Marketing Operator; a SecurityOperator only through an active Support Session naming the target and action.
- organization scope: The target record's Organization.
- aggregate: Crawl or IngestionJob as the recovery target.
- aggregate boundary: Recovery creates a new linked attempt rather than mutating the terminal record, so the old root is never reopened.
- value objects: RecoveryReason, RecoveryOfId, ReplayGeneration, SupportSessionId, ExpectedStateVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - enforced in the recovery commands.
- repository: `CrawlRepository`, `IngestionJobRepository`, `SupportSessionRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable - PRULE-022 introduces no persistent concept; it constrains the recovery records MTX-030 writes.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object.
- transaction boundary: The complete envelope is validated before any mutation, so an unauthorized, stale or altered replay commits nothing.
- concurrency: The expected state version is mandatory: a stale replay is rejected without side effect, so two operators cannot both recover the same terminal record.
- idempotency: The idempotency key is part of the mandatory envelope; a repeated recovery command returns its stored result rather than creating a second linked attempt.
- background job: Not applicable - recovery is a synchronous command; the attempt it creates is queued work.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - a denied recovery is terminal for that command and is never auto-retried.
- terminal failure: An unauthorized, stale or altered replay MUST have no side effect. This is the rule's whole point and is asserted rather than described.
- reconciliation: The audit envelope is what makes recovery reconcilable after the fact: every action carries its permission, authority, version, reason, key and envelope.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-022 emits no event of its own; it gates `IngestionReplayRequested` and the recovery-complete signal.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The recovery command, before mutation. A SecurityOperator's authority comes from the Support Session, not from the role.
- permission checks: The named permission per action - `crawl.recover`, `ingestion.recover` - plus, for a SecurityOperator, an active Support Session naming the exact target and action. Scope is per action rather than a general recovery grant.
- tenant boundary: The Support Session names the target, so a session for one Organization can never authorize recovery in another.
- error contract: Unauthorized, stale or altered replay: denied with no side effect. Each denial is enumerated and audited.
- audit record: The full envelope is the audit record: permission, authority, expected version, reason, idempotency key and correlation.
- observability: Recovery attempts and their outcomes, with the authority that permitted them.
- retention: Recovery attempts retained per CAP-008; window owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: recovery without the named permission has no side effect. TYP-SEC: a SecurityOperator without an active Support Session has no side effect; with a session naming a DIFFERENT target or action, also no side effect. TYP-DATA: a stale expected state version has no side effect. TYP-DATA: an altered replay under the same idempotency key has no side effect. TYP-DATA: a repeated recovery command returns its stored result and creates no second linked attempt. TYP-SEC: every recovery carries its full envelope in the audit record; a recovery with a missing envelope element is rejected. TYP-SEC: a Support Session for another Organization authorizes nothing.
- rollout: The WF-006 and WF-017 limbs land with S-08 and S-24.

### MTX-085 - AC-PRULE-034 (PRULE: PRULE-034)

- Slice: S-01,S-19,S-23
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: UPSTREAM-V1-CREDENTIAL-ROTATION-TOKEN-009 (OD-023) -- OD-023: credential rotation begin/complete withheld; other Credential lifecycle permitted
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-034-notification-delivery-authority
- interface type: Delivery authority, execution and replay rule enforced across the WF-014 dispatch path. PRULE-034 governs CAP-001, CAP-021 and CAP-025 and applies to WF-013 and WF-014. This row owns its CAP-021/WF-014 limb; the WF-013 Invitation and Role limb is owned by S-01 and S-23 and invokes this contract's delivery path rather than reimplementing it. Its owner is Chief Security. It has no decision dependency of its own beyond those the register cites.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-034 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains the MTX-039 schema rather than defining one.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `ConsumeNotificationEvent`, `ExecuteDeliveryAttempt`, `ReconcileDelivery`, `ConsumeProviderEvent` and `RequestNotificationReplay`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-039 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-039 output.
- actor: As MTX-039 per command. The rule is actor-scoped in one specific respect it states itself: administrative replay of an uncertain Delivery requires explicit duplicate-risk acknowledgment from the replaying SecurityOperator, so the acknowledgment is an actor obligation rather than a system default.
- organization scope: The event Organization; selectors resolve Accounts within that Organization and resource scope only.
- aggregate: Notification and Delivery, with Integration and Credential read and validated at the checkpoint.
- aggregate boundary: The rule's ordering clause is what fixes the boundary: 'claim one durable attempt before Integration/Credential validation'. The claim therefore commits on the Delivery root strictly before any Integration or Credential read, which is why a prepared-stage failure can be recorded against a real attempt rather than vanishing.
- value objects: DispatchCheckpoint, RequestDigest, ApplicationDeduplicationKeyDigest, ProviderCorrelationTokenDigest, ReplayMode, ReplayGeneration, DeliveryState, ReasonCode.
- domain service: Not applicable - enforced in the application commands and the adapter.
- repository: `DeliveryRepository`, `DeliveryAttemptRepository`, `DeliveryReconciliationRepository`, `NotificationEscalationRepository`, `NotificationReplayAcknowledgementRepository`; `IntegrationRepository` and `CredentialRepository` read-and-validate only.
- persistence model: Not applicable - PRULE-034 introduces no persistent record of its own; it constrains the records MTX-039 writes. Its one persistence-visible demand is that the attempt claim be durable before validation, which the `delivery_attempts` `prepared` checkpoint carries.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added by this row. WITHHELD under OD-023: no rotation-token column, rotation DDL function or grant may be migrated.
- transaction boundary: The rule's own ordering: the durable attempt claim commits, THEN Integration and Credential validation runs inside that claimed attempt, THEN `submission_started` commits before any network write. The rule forbids collapsing these into one commit, because a claim that is not durable before validation cannot prove which attempt a prepared-stage failure belongs to.
- concurrency: One claimed attempt receives exactly one first submission and no attempt is resubmitted at or after `submission_started`. This is the rule's exact assertion in AC-PRULE-034 and is what makes at-least-once application processing safe without provider idempotency.
- idempotency: Application-level only, and the rule says so in both directions. It REQUIRES: at-least-once application attempt processing; one durable claim before validation; local deduplication such that duplicate consumption, redelivery, replay and concurrency reuse the attempt identity; and duplicate-tolerant retained action references so a duplicate email duplicates no authority or product action. It PROHIBITS: claiming provider exactly-once delivery, and treating permitted provider-visible duplicates as a defect. Mailgun supplies no provider-side idempotency and this contract asserts none.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains the commands and workers, not a job of its own.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - PRULE-034 defines no retry schedule of its own; it constrains which outcomes may retry. Its constraint is exact and exhaustive: retry only exhausted retryable initialization, proven no-network-submission, or definitive provider nonacceptance; disable implicit client and transport retry; suppress automatic resend while acceptance is unknown. The 1-, 5- and 30-minute schedule and the four-attempt bound belong to `notification-policy-v1` and are contracted in MTX-039.
- terminal failure: The rule requires nonretryable prepared-stage failures to terminalize rather than linger, and forbids a false terminal-failure claim. Absence of provider evidence is therefore never terminal: `acceptance_unknown` is the honest outcome and MUST NOT be converted to success or terminal failure.
- reconciliation: Read-only exact-token reconciliation is mandatory under the rule and never sends. The uncertain aggregate, the escalation and the user-visible status must all be exposed rather than hidden, which is the rule's transparency limb.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-034 emits no event of its own; it constrains the seventeen WF-014 events and gates `NotificationReplayRequested` behind its acknowledgment requirement.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own. The rule does bound every payload: no sensitive payload or credential leakage.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Each command before mutation, and again before EVERY attempt - the rule's words are 'reauthorize/redact every attempt', so the per-attempt entry point is mandatory rather than a defensive extra.
- permission checks: The route's Required Permission per baseline route, re-evaluated at every attempt: `invitation.create`, `source.scope.propose`, `source.verify`, `history.read`, `score.summary.read`, `issue.read`, `issue.adjudicate`, `entitlement.notice.read`, `export.retrieve`, `security.notice.read`, plus the invitation binding for the direct Invitation route. `policy.notification.manage` for policy changes within each role's exact scope; `notification.replay` in an active support session for replay; `notification.inbox.read` for own-inbox reads. The rule adds no permission of its own. WITHHELD under OD-023: no `credential.rotation_token.issue` permission exists and every mint request is denied as an undefined permission under the Permission Baseline; `credential.rotate` is a canonical baseline token that remains unreachable while the rotation token contract is withheld.
- tenant boundary: The rule binds delivery to the platform-managed Integration and Credential of the event Organization: 'use only a valid platform-managed Integration/Credential'. There is no customer connector catalogue, no arbitrary endpoint entry and no customer credential-entry journey, so no tenant can point delivery at a provider or credential of its own choosing.
- error contract: As MTX-039. The rule's own error surface is exactly three envelopes fixed by 017 ERROR_MODEL.md: `provider_acceptance_unknown` as `F1-DEPENDENCY-503`, severity `error`, `retryable=false` for automatic dispatch, recovery `await_provider_reconciliation_or_submit_acknowledged_replay`, never converted to success, terminal failure, timeout retry or rate-limit retry; `duplicate_delivery_risk_unacknowledged` as `F1-DOMAIN-409`, severity `warning`, `retryable=false`, recovery `acknowledge_duplicate_delivery_risk_and_submit_new_replay_command`, changing no replay generation; and `notification_replay_ineligible` on the standard `F1-DOMAIN-409` envelope. `provider_retry_exhausted` applies only after four definitively nonaccepted attempts. A provider-visible duplicate after acknowledged replay or provider behavior is not an error when the local attempt claim and the underlying product action remain unique.
- audit record: As MTX-039. PRULE-034's fixture list names the record classes it requires: Invitation target, Integration/Credential, routing/address, local deduplication, prepared-stage outcome, transport retry disablement, response-loss/uncertainty, reconciliation, retry timing, provider-event precedence, aggregate, user status, duplicate-tolerant action, replay-mode/scope/acknowledgment, audit, and escalation.
- observability: As MTX-039 - uncertainty must be exposed rather than smoothed over. The rule requires the uncertain aggregate, the escalation and the user-visible status to be exposed, so an unknown Delivery is visible to the operator and to the recipient's own status view, never silently retried and never silently failed.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-034 defines no retention obligation of its own; the records it constrains fall in the `product_history` class owned by 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: baseline Notifications route through in-app AND Mailgun email Deliveries - no third channel and no delayed channel exists. TYP-DATA: the direct intended-recipient Invitation exception applies exactly once and every other route uses Account permission selectors. TYP-SEC: only a valid platform-managed Integration and Credential is used; no customer connector catalogue, arbitrary endpoint entry or customer credential-entry journey exists. TYP-SEC: every attempt is reauthorized and redacted - authorization present at consumption and absent at attempt time yields `suppressed` and no provider call. TYP-DATA: email execution uses at-least-once APPLICATION attempt processing, asserted distinct from external delivery counts. TYP-DATA: one durable attempt is claimed BEFORE Integration and Credential validation - no Credential read occurs without a durable `prepared` row. TYP-DATA: nonretryable prepared-stage failures terminalize; they neither retry nor linger. TYP-DATA: retry admits exactly three classes and no fourth - exhausted retryable initialization, proven no-network-submission, and definitive provider nonacceptance. TYP-INT: implicit client and transport retry is disabled, asserted at the transport layer. TYP-DATA: `acceptance_unknown` applies whenever provider acceptance cannot be disproved, and automatic resend is suppressed while unknown. TYP-DATA: reconciliation is read-only and exact-token and never submits a message. TYP-OBS: the uncertain aggregate, the escalation and the user-visible status are all exposed. TYP-DATA: action references are duplicate-tolerant - the same logical action reference used twice produces one product transition. TYP-SEC: administrative replay of an UNCERTAIN Delivery requires explicit duplicate-risk acknowledgment; without it `F1-DOMAIN-409 / duplicate_delivery_risk_unacknowledged` changes nothing and changes no replay generation. TYP-SEC: `delivery_replay` targets ONLY the same logical recipient and channel - a fixture asserting any other prior or current recipient, or the in-app channel, fails. TYP-DATA: only `empty_recipient_replay` may resolve a complete current selector union, and only for a Notification failed with `no_authorized_recipient`. TYP-SEC: NO EXACTLY-ONCE CLAIM is made anywhere - no code path, event, status, audit field, schema constraint or user-visible string asserts provider exactly-once delivery. TYP-DATA: permitted provider-visible duplicates are NOT treated as a defect; the fixture asserts the platform reports no failure. TYP-INT: one prepared claim receives exactly one first submission and no attempt is resubmitted after `submission_started`, asserted under concurrency and under job redelivery. TYP-SEC: no false failure and no false success - absence of provider evidence yields neither. TYP-DATA: a permitted duplicate external email causes no duplicate authority and no duplicate product action. TYP-SEC: `SEC-REQ-013` least privilege holds across the route permission set - holding one route's Required Permission delivers no other route's Notification. TYP-OBS: `OBS-REQ-019` telemetry is present for every delivery outcome class and carries no secret material. TYP-DATA: `ERR-REQ-004` and `ERR-REQ-018` envelopes are exact for `provider_acceptance_unknown`, `duplicate_delivery_risk_unacknowledged` and `notification_replay_ineligible`. TYP-DATA: `SB-REQ-003` and `SB-REQ-009` boundary assertions - every Mailgun call is outbound from a claimed attempt and the webhook ingress carries no product authorization. The complete AC-PRULE-034 fixture set is the AC-CAP-021 and AC-WF-014 set asserted in MTX-039; AC-PRULE-034 passes exactly when those pass. WITHHELD under OD-023 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting credential rotation initiation or completion, a rotation token issuer, permission, entropy, wire format, TTL, equality boundary, one-use consumption point or binding tuple, a `credential.rotation_token.issue` permission, a reachable `BeginCredentialRotation` or any `CompleteCredentialRotation` entry, a path setting pending material or entering `rotating`, a `CredentialRotationStarted` or `CredentialRotated` emission or route, a `credential_rotation_retry` scheduled action, or a rotation DDL function or grant. The settled delivery behaviour above is contracted in full and is NOT blocked; only the rotation limb is isolated under `UPSTREAM-V1-CREDENTIAL-ROTATION-TOKEN-009`. NOT CONTRACTED HERE - the WF-013 limb: PRULE-034 spans WF-013 and CAP-001/CAP-025, whose Invitation lifecycle and Role-expiry notification obligations are owned by S-01 and S-23. Note for those owners: OD-026's Ratified Behavior and WORKFLOW_SPECIFICATIONS.md:349 both assert a mandatory `RoleExpiryBlocked` route at severity `critical` requiring `security.notice.read`, but the `notification-policy-v1` baseline route table carries no such row, and no actor may add a mandatory route. That row is not invented here.
- rollout: As MTX-039. The WF-013 Invitation and Role-expiry limbs land with S-01 and S-23 and consume this contract's delivery path. The OD-023 rotation limb requires ratification and a controlled Volume I change before it can be completed, and production storage of a real Mailgun provider secret is blocked until then.

This row owns its WF-018 limb - immutable query scope, the per-Organization Support Session set, Investigation Audit Evidence Item digest and custody, gap and completeness, the access trail and `security_audit` retention. - request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains the MTX-043 schema rather than defining one.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `OpenInvestigation`, `CollectInvestigationInput`, `PublishInvestigationReport` and `CloseInvestigation`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-043 input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-043 output.
- actor: A SecurityOperator holding `security.investigate`, the collector service identity, and a different holder of `security.investigation.approve` for closure; the rule binds each of them equally and grants none of them an exception.
- organization scope: The Investigation's one owning Organization, per OD-013 Option 1.
- aggregate: Investigation, with its required inputs, Investigation Audit Evidence Items, custody records, access logs, gaps and report versions.
- aggregate boundary: The rule's per-Organization Support Session clause is what forces the collection boundary: authority is resolved per affected Organization rather than once for the Investigation, so the aggregate can never hold one authority that spans its scope. Its 'missing data MUST never be synthesized' clause binds the gap record to the Investigation root rather than to a report, which is why a gap survives every later report version.
- value objects: QuerySha256, InputPlanSha256, ContentSha256, CustodySha256, GapReasonCode, Completeness, ClassificationCeiling, ReportVersion, ReportDigest, SupportSessionId, RetentionClass fixed to `security_audit`.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-037 names no domain service; it is enforced inside the WF-018 commands and the collector. Completeness derivation is a pure function over the frozen required-input set.
- repository: `InvestigationRepository`, `InvestigationRequiredInputRepository`, `InvestigationAuditEvidenceItemRepository`, `InvestigationAccessLogRepository`, `InvestigationGapRepository`, `InvestigationReportRepository`; `SupportSessionRepository` read-only.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-037 introduces no persistent entity; it constrains the records MTX-043 writes. Its one persistence-shaped obligation is the retention class: every Investigation record, Investigation Audit Evidence Item, custody transfer, access-log entry, gap and report version is a `security_audit` record, and none is an Evidence record.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added by this row.
- transaction boundary: The query scope, interval, classification ceiling and required-input plan are frozen in the open transaction and are immutable thereafter, so nothing later in the Investigation can widen what was authorized. Each collected item's custody record and each gap commits in the page's final restricted transaction alongside its terminal state.
- concurrency: Immutability is the concurrency control here: the frozen query scope, input plan, Support Session binding and cursor cannot be rewritten, and a committed item or gap never reopens, so two concurrent pages cannot produce two different accounts of one required input. A session expiring concurrently with a query denies that query and yields a gap rather than a partial read.
- idempotency: The rule's digest and custody obligations make replay safe by content rather than by delivery: an Investigation Audit Evidence Item is keyed by its Investigation, Organization, required input, query hash and page sequence and carries its content digest, so a redelivered page recommits the same identity and the same digest or is rejected as an integrity failure. A gap is unique on its required input and report version, so a repeated gap creates no second record.
- background job: Not applicable - the rule constrains what the collector may write, not a job of its own; the `investigation_collect` job is contracted in MTX-043.
- queue: Not applicable - PRULE-037 defines no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry. Its consequence for retry is negative and exact: because missing data must never be synthesized and every failure yields a gap, there is no retry path that can convert a gap into a record without a new authorized collection appended in a new report version.
- terminal failure: A digest or custody failure, an out-of-session read attempt, a missing required report field or an attempt to synthesize a missing record changes no Investigation state and records the exact denial or integrity outcome. The completeness value is the terminal statement of what was actually collected and is never rounded up.
- reconciliation: The access trail and the custody chain are what make an Investigation reconcilable after the fact: every item carries its immutable input reference, collection query hash, collector and time, content digest, classification and custody transfers, and every access to it - including a denied one - is logged with actor or service, Support Session ID, action, time and outcome. Completeness derives exactly from the terminal state of the frozen plan, so the report's claim is checkable against the records rather than asserted.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-037 emits no event of its own; it constrains the content of `InvestigationAuditEvidenceCollected`, `InvestigationGapRecorded` and `SecurityInvestigationReported`, which MTX-043 produces.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: Every query, not once per Investigation. The rule's per-Organization Support Session clause is checked at each page against the frozen session and its current expiry or revocation state.
- permission checks: PRULE-037 adds no permission. It requires that the existing ones be resolved per Organization and per query: `security.investigate` within the frozen scope, the affected Organization's frozen Support Session for every cross-Organization query, and a different holder of `security.investigation.approve` for closure. Its point is that no standing role and no one session covers the whole investigation.
- tenant boundary: One Organization per collection transaction with its exact Support Session. The rule forbids the out-of-session read outright: a session that expires or is revoked denies its queries and produces gaps, and another Organization's session never substitutes.
- error contract: Every failed, denied, expired or integrity-invalid required input is retained as its own explicit gap with its reason code rather than collapsed into a single investigation failure. A digest or custody mismatch is `F1-DATA-409`; an out-of-session or over-ceiling query is `F1-AUTH-403`; a report missing a required field is `F1-VALIDATION-400`. Each changes no Investigation state.
- audit record: The rule IS an audit contract: exact Evidence and access history is retained for every Investigation Audit Evidence Item - immutable input reference, collection query hash, collected-by and time, content digest, classification, custody transfers and access log - together with every gap, the completeness derivation, every report version and digest, and the distinct closure approval. All of it is `security_audit`, and Audit Evidence is not an Evidence record.
- observability: The gap records and the completeness value are the observable statement of what an investigation did and did not see; the access log is the observable statement of who touched each item under which Support Session, including denials.
- retention: The `security_audit` contract that PRULE-037 mandates: 7 years, on a cursor of 7 elapsed years after the record's terminal transition, with irreversible destruction only under two-person security approval, per 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md. Ratified OD-011 fixes that window as the approved baseline; no customer-configurable retention exists and none may be inferred from a fixed policy. A legal hold suspends destruction and the record is destroyed at the first eligible instant after release. Routine retention-expiry destruction AT the 7-year maximum is OD-031's withheld limb and belongs to S-23 on MTX-025, MTX-038 and MTX-093 - not to this row. Under `retention-destruction-trigger-interim-v1` the settled behaviour this row asserts is that when a `security_audit` record reaches its 7-year maximum with no accepted Account deletion or Organization closure request, the lifecycle service performs NO destruction, creates no LifecycleDeletionJob and produces no Deletion Evidence, emits no domain event because Volume I defines none that could represent the condition, retains the record in full subject to legal hold, and records the condition as Audit Evidence and operational telemetry raising exactly one critical compliance escalation.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: the per-Organization Support Session set is resolved per QUERY, not per Investigation - a session valid at open and expired at collection denies that query and creates its gap, asserted at the exact expiry instant and one either side. TYP-SEC: no out-of-session read occurs on any path - a denied, expired, revoked or mismatched session yields a gap and the fixture asserts zero records collected under it. TYP-SEC: another Organization's session never substitutes for an expired one. TYP-DATA: the immutable query scope - the frozen query plan, interval, classification ceiling and required-input set cannot be widened, replaced or reordered after open; an attempt changes nothing. TYP-DATA: missing data is NEVER synthesized - a missing, expired, denied or integrity-invalid input yields exactly one explicit gap with its reason code and no fabricated record in the timeline. TYP-DATA: every failed input is retained individually and is separately addressable rather than collapsed into one failure. TYP-DATA: digest and custody - each Investigation Audit Evidence Item carries its immutable input reference, collection query hash, collected-by and time, content digest, classification and custody transfers; a digest mismatch is an integrity outcome with no state change; a missing custody record blocks the item. TYP-SEC: the access trail records every access to every item with actor or service, Support Session ID, action, time and outcome, INCLUDING denied accesses. TYP-DATA: completeness is derived exactly from the terminal state of the frozen plan and is never rounded up - a single failed required input in an otherwise successful set yields `partial`, not `complete`. TYP-SEC: exact Evidence and access history is retained across the seven-year `security_audit` expiry and hold fixture - a record at its 7-year maximum with no accepted deletion or closure request is asserted RETAINED IN FULL with no destruction, no LifecycleDeletionJob, no Deletion Evidence and no domain event, and with exactly one critical compliance escalation recorded; a legal hold suspends destruction and the record is destroyed at the first eligible instant after release. TYP-SEC: an Investigation Audit Evidence Item is `security_audit` and is asserted NOT an Evidence record - it appears in no Evidence projection and follows no `product_history` or `product_evidence_payload` schedule. TYP-DATA: no customer-configurable retention exists - a fixture attempting to configure an Investigation retention window changes nothing, and configurability is asserted not inferred from the fixed policy. TYP-OBS: the WF-017 diagnostic-only severity and restoration limb of this rule is exercised by S-24's MTX-042 and is asserted here only as not redefined.
- rollout: As MTX-043. The WF-017 limb lands with S-24. The OD-031 retention-expiry destruction limb lands with S-23 and is neither implemented nor asserted here.

### MTX-089 - AC-PRULE-038 (PRULE: PRULE-038)

- Slice: S-21
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-038-investigation-evidence
