- interface type: Administrative-remediation authority rule. PRULE-038 governs CAP-023 and applies to WF-017 and WF-018. This row owns its WF-018 limb, and that limb is unusual: WF-018 declares NO playbook and no mutating step, so the rule's playbook, checkpoint, rollback and restoration clauses are entirely WF-017's and are owned by S-24 on MTX-042. What remains here is exact - the report executes nothing, and the one high-risk artifact WF-018 does define is the investigation Export approval. Its decision dependency OD-012 is ratified.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-038 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains the MTX-043 and WF-016 schemas rather than defining one.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter.
- command: Enforced within `PublishInvestigationReport` and `CloseInvestigation`, which are asserted to perform no remediation, and at the boundary into the WF-016 Export command. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-043 input and the WF-016 approval envelope.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-043 output. A report's `proposed_actions` are inert text and are never an executable instruction.
- actor: A SecurityOperator holding `security.investigate` produces the report and requests the Export; a DIFFERENT active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve` approves each Export generation attempt and, separately, the closure. The requester can never be the approver on either path.
- organization scope: The Investigation's one owning Organization. An approved cross-Organization Investigation creates one separately approved Organization-scoped Export per Organization.
- aggregate: Investigation for the report limb; Export at the boundary only, as a separate root owned by S-20.
- aggregate boundary: The rule draws its boundary at the report: WF-018's Recovery Path states that remediation executes through WF-017 or another named workflow, NOT directly from the report, so the Investigation aggregate has no edge into any mutating command. The Export is a separate root reached only through WF-016 with `export.create`, its own entitlement reservation, its own single-use approval and its own manifest, redaction and lifecycle contract.
- value objects: ExportApprovalId, CanonicalRequestHash, ApprovedAtUtc, ExpiresAtUtc fixed to approval plus exactly 1 hour, UsedByExportId nullable, ApprovalStateVersion, LogicalFormat, ObjectAllowlist, FieldAllowlist, ProposedAction as inert text.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-038 names no domain service; it is enforced in the WF-018 commands and at the WF-016 approval consumption.
- repository: `InvestigationReportRepository`; `ExportApprovalRepository` and `ExportRepository` at the boundary only, owned by S-20.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-038 introduces no persistent entity on the WF-018 limb. The `export_approvals` record and its distinct-actor and one-use constraints are WF-016's and are owned by S-20; the `incident_step_attempts` and `high_risk_approvals` records are WF-017's and are owned by S-24. This row constrains what the report may and may not cause.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added by this row.
- transaction boundary: Export creation and its immediate `Pending -> Generating` checkpoint must BOTH commit before approval expiry; at the exact expiry instant expiry wins. Starting generation atomically sets `used_by_export_id` in that same commit, so the approval is consumed exactly when the work starts. Report publication and closure each commit in their own transaction and reach no other aggregate.
- concurrency: The one-use column is the control, not an application guard: `used_by_export_id` is set atomically at generation start, so two concurrent generation attempts cannot both consume one approval. At the exact expiry instant the expiry check wins over a generation attempt landing on the boundary. Approval expiry AFTER generation starts does not cancel that pinned attempt, but current permission revocation still stops it at the next protected checkpoint - so the approval bounds the START of work while the permission bounds its continuation.
- idempotency: Each create or linked-retry generation attempt requires its OWN single-use approval; the old one is spent and a retry without a new approval is rejected before reservation or generation. Exact replay returns the original generation result and never creates another package or consumption. Reuse under altered content or a different request hash is rejected. On the WF-018 side, a replayed publish returns the stored report version and a replayed close returns the stored closure; neither executes anything.
- background job: Not applicable - PRULE-038 defines no job. Export generation is WF-016 work owned by S-20; the WF-017 restoration timer is S-24's.
- queue: Not applicable - PRULE-038 defines no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule constrains retries rather than defining one. Its constraint is exact: a retry is never free, because each linked-retry generation attempt requires a NEW single-use approval from a distinct SecurityOperator, and a denied or expired approval is never auto-retried.
- terminal failure: Otherwise it performs NO side effect and emits a denial audit event - that is the rule's own terminal clause and it is asserted rather than described. A remediation attempted from the report, an Export without its approval, with a reused, altered, self-approved or expired approval, or a retry without a new approval each perform no side effect and emit the denial audit event. An unscoped export is denied and audited.
- reconciliation: Every high-risk attempt is reconcilable to exactly one approval and one approver: the approval carries the canonical request hash that binds it to the exact request, and `used_by_export_id` records which attempt spent it. A denial is reconcilable through its audit event even though it produces no artifact.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-038 emits no event of its own. It gates the WF-016 Export events, owned by S-20, and constrains `SecurityInvestigationReported` and `SecurityInvestigationClosed` to carry no executed action.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure.
- authorization entry point: The Export command before reservation or generation, where the approval is validated and consumed; and `PublishInvestigationReport` and `CloseInvestigation`, where the absence of any remediation edge is enforced structurally rather than checked. Retrieval and revocation do not consume or require a new generation approval; they reauthorize their named permission, current investigation scope and current policy independently.
- permission checks: PRULE-038 adds no permission and its central claim is a negative one: the approval NEVER substitutes for the underlying workflow permission. An investigation Export requires `export.create` AND the distinct single-use approval from a different active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve`; `security.investigate` grants neither. The Permission Baseline gives a SecurityOperator `export.retry` and `export.revoke` at 'authorized investigation scope only', which is the target object's permission and not an investigation authority. On the WF-017 limb, `incident.respond` and a High-Risk Approval likewise never substitute for a target-workflow permission; that limb is S-24's.
- tenant boundary: Multi-Organization export is prohibited outright: baseline creates one Organization-scoped Export per request with recipient equal to the requesting Account, and WF-018 must submit a separate approved Export per Organization. A combined multi-Organization package is always rejected - the rejection is unconditional and is not a policy the tenant can relax.
- error contract: `F1-AUTH-403` for an Export without its approval, with a reused, altered, self-approved or expired approval, for a retry without a new approval, for an unscoped export, and for a combined multi-Organization package - each performing no side effect and emitting the denial audit event. A remediation attempted directly from the report is refused with no side effect. `export_scope_no_longer_authorized` when a current-policy contraction makes the package ineligible; the package is never partially stripped. `policy_unavailable` on a missing or invalid current Export Policy.
- audit record: Every denial emits its audit event - the rule requires it explicitly, so a refused remediation or export is as auditable as a permitted one. Every permitted high-risk attempt records its `approval_id`, Organization, requester, approver, logical format, exact object and field allowlists, request hash, `approved_at_utc`, `expires_at_utc`, `used_by_export_id` and state version. WF-018's Audit and Observability requires the Export IDs and the linked remediation to be persisted on the Investigation, so the report's inertness is provable from the Investigation's own record.
- observability: The denial audit event is the observability signal for every refused high-risk attempt, and the linked-remediation and Export IDs on the Investigation are the signal for every permitted one. There is no unobserved administrative action on this limb, because there is no administrative action on this limb at all.
- retention: Every approval, denial and Export ID linked to an Investigation is a `security_audit` record retained 7 years per 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md and ratified OD-011; the window is owned by that document and this row states none. The Export package's own manifest, retrieval expiry and lifecycle are WF-016's and are owned by S-20.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: no remediation from the report - `PublishInvestigationReport` and `CloseInvestigation` perform NO mutation on any target; a `proposed_action` is inert text and every attempt to execute one directly from the report is refused with no side effect and a denial audit event. TYP-SEC: remediation reaches its effect only by entering WF-017 or another named workflow with THAT workflow's permission, scope, approval, state-version and idempotency contract, and only its immutable result may be referenced back. TYP-SEC: every SecurityOperator investigation Export is high-risk regardless of content - the fixture asserts a non-restricted-field investigation Export still requires the approval. TYP-SEC: the exact approval contract - `approval_id`, Organization, requester, approver, logical format, exact object and field allowlists, request hash, `approved_at_utc`, `expires_at_utc` exactly 1 hour later, nullable `used_by_export_id` and state version; the approver is a DIFFERENT active SecurityOperator holding `security.investigation.approve`. TYP-SEC: request-hash binding - an approval whose request hash does not match the submitted request authorizes nothing; altered content after approval is rejected before reservation or generation. TYP-DATA: the one-hour expiry - creation AND the immediate `Pending -> Generating` checkpoint must both commit before expiry, and at the EXACT expiry instant expiry wins, asserted at the boundary and one either side. TYP-DATA: single use - starting generation atomically sets `used_by_export_id`; two concurrent attempts cannot both consume one approval; reuse is rejected. TYP-DATA: retry - each create or linked-retry generation attempt requires its OWN new approval; a retry without a new approval is rejected before reservation or generation; exact replay returns the original generation result and creates no second package or consumption. TYP-SEC: self-approval is rejected - the requester approving their own investigation Export performs no side effect. TYP-DATA: approval expiry after generation starts does not cancel that pinned attempt, but a current permission revocation stops it at the next protected checkpoint. TYP-SEC: the approval NEVER substitutes for the underlying workflow permission - the fixture asserts `export.create` is checked independently and that `security.investigate` grants neither `export.create` nor the approval. TYP-DATA: one separately approved Organization-scoped Export per Organization for a cross-Organization Investigation; a combined multi-Organization package is ALWAYS rejected, asserted as unconditional and not policy-relaxable. TYP-SEC: an unscoped export is denied and audited. TYP-SEC: every denial on this limb emits its denial audit event and performs no side effect - asserted for a missing, reused, altered, self-approved and expired approval, for a retry without a new approval, and for a direct remediation attempt. TYP-DATA: the Investigation record retains the linked remediation and Export IDs, so the report's inertness is provable from the Investigation's own audit record. TYP-SEC: the `incident-diagnostic-interim-v1` limb - the playbook permits only diagnostic capture, named-remediation result linkage and two restoration checks, and every direct-mutation attempt is denied - is exercised by S-24's MTX-042 and is asserted here only as not redefined, because WF-018 declares no playbook, no mutating step and no High-Risk Remediation Approval consumption.
- rollout: As MTX-043. The WF-017 playbook, checkpoint, rollback, restoration and high-risk step-approval limb lands with S-24 on MTX-042; the Export lifecycle and its approval record land with S-20. This row is complete for the WF-018 limb and withholds nothing: OD-012's architecture is ratified, and its withheld customer-notification limb touches no path this rule reaches.

### MTX-090 - AC-PRULE-039 (PRULE: PRULE-039)

- Slice: S-22
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-039-entitlement-enforcement
- interface type: Enforcement rule constraining where and how a high-cost check happens. PRULE-039 governs CAP-024 and applies to WF-015 only. Its decision dependencies OD-005, OD-006 and OD-008 are all ratified with `Blocking Impact: None`, so nothing in this row is withheld. It is a rule, not an interface.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-039 is a rule, not an interface. It constrains the checkpoint inside the operation being gated; see MTX-040 for the one registered route, which belongs to the policy limb PRULE-039 does not govern.
- request schema: Not applicable - PRULE-039 constrains the MTX-040 enforcement envelope rather than defining one of its own.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema. PRULE-040 owns the Decision's field contract; PRULE-039 owns when and how it is produced.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter; the rule's entire point is that no client reaches this check.
- command: Enforced within `DecideEntitlement`, `ReserveEntitlement`, `CommitEntitlement` and `ReleaseEntitlement`. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-040 input. The rule does add one requirement to it: the policy and counter state resolved must be CURRENT at the checkpoint, so a snapshot pinned at queue time is not an admissible input.
- command output: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-040 output.
- actor: Not applicable - the rule constrains the checkpoint's placement and atomicity rather than a principal. The actor is fixed by WF-015 as the server-side entitlement service identity; see MTX-040.
- organization scope: The Organization named in the envelope; the counter window and reservation resolve within it.
- aggregate: BillingEntity as the canonical write entry, with the counter window and reservation as the constrained children.
- aggregate boundary: The rule's atomicity clause is what fixes the boundary: the check and the reserve are ONE operation against the counter window, so no interleaving can observe a checked-but-unreserved state. The gated operation's own aggregate stays outside.
- value objects: EntitlementDecisionId, ReservationId, RequestedUnits, HardLimit, CounterWindow, LeaseGeneration, RetryOfDecisionId.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-039 names no domain service; it is enforced inside the application commands under the counter-window lock.
- repository: `BillingEntityRepository` as the canonical write path over the counter window and reservation; `EntitlementPolicyRepository` read-only for the current version.
- persistence model: Not applicable as a new concept - PRULE-039 introduces no persistent record; it constrains the counter-window, Decision and reservation rows MTX-040 writes. The rule is nevertheless enforced BY persistence: `entitlement_reservations` allows one reservation per Decision and `entitlement_counter_windows` is unique per `(organization_id,counter_group,window_start,window_end)`, so the atomic reserve has exactly one row to serialize against.
- migration: Not applicable - no schema object is added; the constraints the rule relies on are already declared in schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md.
- transaction boundary: The rule states its own boundary: the check executes server-side with CURRENT policy and counter state and performs an atomic idempotent reserve at the protected execution checkpoint BEFORE side effects. Queue creation opens no transaction and creates neither Decision nor reservation. Commit and release each occur exactly once, in their own transaction against the same counter window.
- concurrency: Atomicity is the rule's mechanism and the counter window row is where it lives: check-then-reserve is one serialized operation, so two concurrent requests at the boundary cannot both observe headroom and both reserve. This is what makes 'never reserve or commit above hard limits' true under concurrency rather than true on average. Reservation lifecycle races are decided by the fixed winners at MTX-040 - expiry beats execution-start at the prestart instant, and the lease-expiry handler beats a heartbeat and a side effect at the heartbeat-plus-15-minute and maximum-execution instants.
- idempotency: The rule names three separate obligations and this row keeps them separate. The RESERVE is idempotent: the same key at the same checkpoint reserves once and replay returns the original Decision plus current reservation state. TERMINAL-KEY REPLAY never reopens a reservation: once the Decision is a Block or the reservation is `released` or `expired`, that key is spent and returns the stored outcome, never a fresh reservation. A NEW ATTEMPT uses a linked new key with `retry_of_decision_id` pointing at the preceding Decision, so the retry is auditable as a successor rather than indistinguishable from a first attempt. COMMIT/RELEASE occurs exactly once, guarded by the reservation's own terminal state rather than by delivery deduplication, so a redelivered `entitlement_reconcile` job cannot commit twice.
- background job: Not applicable as a job of its own - the rule constrains commands. The commit/release exactly-once obligation is discharged at the checkpoint or by the `entitlement_reconcile` lease handler at MTX-040, whichever wins under the fixed deadline rule.
- queue: Not applicable - PRULE-039 introduces no queue. Its queue-time clause is a prohibition, not a queue: queue creation MUST create neither Decision nor reservation.
- retry policy: Not applicable - PRULE-039 defines no retry and expressly forbids the shape of one that would consume twice. A new attempt is a new linked key, which the rule distinguishes from a retry precisely so that replay and retry cannot both consume usage.
- terminal failure: A high-cost operation whose check fails blocks BEFORE the first protected side effect with its exact reason; no provider call, no workflow side effect and no partial artifact precedes the Block. A reservation that reaches `released` or `expired` is terminal and its key is spent.
- reconciliation: The commit-or-release decision reconciles against persisted output evidence rather than timing: the lease handler commits exactly once only if the durable commit point committed strictly before the winning instant and otherwise releases exactly once, with `entitlement_commit_intents` recording the durable output type, ID and hash under `state CHECK ('pending','committed','released')` and unique reservation/output.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-039 emits no event of its own; it gates `EntitlementChecked`, `EntitlementReserved`, `EntitlementExecutionStarted`, `EntitlementCommitted`, `EntitlementReleased` and `EntitlementReservationExpired`.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure; PRULE-039's subject has no client-facing representation by design.
- authorization entry point: The checkpoint itself, server-side, before the first protected side effect. PRULE-039's SEC-REQ-005 linkage is why the placement is a security property and not a performance one: a check placed after a side effect, or performed anywhere but the server, is a bypass regardless of its arithmetic.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-039 adds no permission and grants none. The gated operation carries its own permission (`crawl.trigger` for `crawl.start`, `export.create` for `export.generate`, the named read permission for each low-cost read); entitlement is a capacity gate applied after authorization and never in place of it.
- tenant boundary: The counter window is unique per `(organization_id,counter_group,window_start,window_end)`, so the atomic reserve is per Organization by construction and one tenant's concurrent load cannot exhaust another's headroom.
- error contract: Block before side effect with the exact reason, selecting `F1-CAPACITY-429` for the entitlement bound with recovery `wait_for_next_window_or_change_approved_entitlement` and the Decision's own `wait_for_window`. Execution against an expired linked reservation is `F1-DOMAIN-409 / reservation_expired` with no new Decision and no side effect. A stale or missing high-cost policy or counter fails CLOSED - PRULE-039 admits no fallback for high-cost, and the low-cost snapshot path is PRULE-040's.
- audit record: Command and correlation IDs, the Decision and reservation identities, the counter values the reserve was taken against, the retry lineage linking a new-key attempt to its predecessor, and the commit or release with the durable output reference that justified it.
- observability: `EntitlementReserved` marks the atomic reserve, `EntitlementExecutionStarted` the transition into the lease, and `EntitlementCommitted`, `EntitlementReleased` or `EntitlementReservationExpired` the exactly-once terminal. A reserve with no matching terminal is the defect the 15-second `entitlement_invariant_sweep_due` exists to surface.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-039 defines no retention obligation; the records it constrains are retained under CAP-024.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: the check executes SERVER-SIDE - a client-asserted allowance, header or body field changes no Decision and grants no side effect. TYP-DATA: the check uses CURRENT policy and counter state at the checkpoint - a policy activated between enqueue and execution governs the execution, proving the queued action re-resolves rather than replaying a pinned snapshot. TYP-DATA: queue creation creates NEITHER a Decision NOR a reservation, asserted by counting rows in both tables after enqueue. TYP-DATA: the reserve is atomic and at the protected execution checkpoint BEFORE the first side effect - a fixture that fails the check performs no provider call and leaves no partial artifact. TYP-DATA: the reserve is idempotent - the same key at the same checkpoint reserves exactly once. TYP-DATA: N concurrent pre-execution reserves against one counter window admit only those that fit and never exceed the hard limit; the boundary case where the last admitted request lands exactly ON the hard limit is admitted. TYP-DATA: terminal-key replay never reopens - after a Block, and after `released`, and after `expired`, the same key returns the stored outcome and creates no reservation in all three cases. TYP-DATA: a new attempt uses a NEW key and sets `retry_of_decision_id` to the preceding Decision; a new attempt with a reused terminal key is refused. TYP-DATA: replay and retry cannot both consume - a replay followed by a linked retry consumes exactly one unit, not two. TYP-DATA: commit occurs exactly once - a redelivered `entitlement_reconcile` job against a `committed` reservation commits nothing further. TYP-DATA: release occurs exactly once - the same for a `released` reservation. TYP-DATA: the commit-or-release fork is decided by the STRICTLY-BEFORE test against the durable commit point, not by which handler ran first; a durable output committed exactly AT the winning instant releases. TYP-DATA: nested execution does not double reserve - a `reassessment.start` run never takes a `crawl.start` reservation for its internal WF-005 stage. TYP-SEC: an outage of the policy or counter store fails a high-cost operation CLOSED, with no fallback snapshot admitted and no side effect performed. TYP-OBS: every reserve has exactly one terminal event, and a reserve without one is detected by the 15-second invariant sweep rather than left stranded. TYP-SEC: PRULE-039 grants no authority - holding no operation permission but satisfying the entitlement check still denies the operation.
- rollout: As MTX-040. The `crawl.start` limb of this rule is already consumed by S-07's MTX-058; the reassessment, AI-generation and export limbs land with their own slices and add no new entitlement mechanism.

### MTX-091 - AC-PRULE-040 (PRULE: PRULE-040)

- Slice: S-22
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-040-usage-accounting
- interface type: Record-content and usage-accounting rule. PRULE-040 governs CAP-024 and applies to WF-015 only. Its decision dependencies OD-005, OD-006, OD-008 and OD-019 are all ratified with `Blocking Impact: None`, so the metered-read unit and the low-cost record contract are implemented here in full and no limb is withheld. It is a rule, not an interface.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-040 is a rule, not an interface. The low-cost read GET paths it accounts for remain blocked mappings in API_CONTRACTS.md pending the Volume II transport baseline, which is a transport-sequencing deferral and not an owner-decision block; see the `low_cost_metering_authority` note.
- request schema: Not applicable - PRULE-040 constrains what the Decision and the usage record CONTAIN rather than what the request carries.
- response schema: The immutable Decision's complete field contract, which is this rule's subject: decision ID, Organization, human Account ID or service-identity ID, operation, usage unit, policy and plan versions, counter-window start and end, soft and hard limits, committed units before and after, active reserved units before and after, requested units, reservation ID, nullable `cached_snapshot_id` and `cached_snapshot_age`, idempotency key, nullable `retry_of_decision_id`, decision, reason code, recovery action and timestamps.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter; see route.
- command: Enforced within `DecideEntitlement` and `RecordLowCostUsage`, and in the `Workflows::Wf015::LowCostRead` coordinator's durable-response commit. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - constrains the MTX-040 input. For a low-cost read the input identity is server-minted under OD-019 rather than supplied, so there is no client field for this rule to constrain.
- command output: Not applicable - see response_schema, which is this rule's actual output contract.
- actor: Not applicable - the rule constrains record content, not principals. The actor identity it REQUIRES on every record is the human Account ID or the service-identity ID, exactly one of the two, which the schema enforces as an actor/service XOR.
- organization scope: Every Decision and usage record carries its Organization; the counter window is resolved within it.
- aggregate: BillingEntity as the canonical write entry; the Decision, LowCostFallbackSnapshot and LowCostUsageRecord are the immutable appends this rule governs.
- aggregate boundary: The Decision is immutable and the reservation is not, which is why they are separate rows: the Decision records the counters AS DECIDED and never mutates to reflect a later commit, release or expiry. Reading a reservation outcome off a Decision would be reading a field that is correct only by accident.
- value objects: EntitlementDecisionId, ReasonCode, RecoveryAction, UsageUnit, CounterWindow, SoftLimit, HardLimit, RequestedUnits, CachedSnapshotId, CachedSnapshotAge, RetryOfDecisionId, PolicyVersion, PlanVersion.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-040 names no domain service. The nullability derivation and the recovery mapping are pure total functions of the reason code.
- repository: The immutable append paths for `entitlement_decisions`, `low_cost_usage_records` and `low_cost_fallback_snapshots`, under the `BillingEntityRepository` root; `EntitlementCounterWindow` read for the before values and advanced for the after values.
- persistence model: `entitlement_decisions` is `T-IMM` with `LINEAGE`, actor/service XOR, cached snapshot ID and age nullable, retry-of ID nullable. `low_cost_usage_records` is `T-IMM` with a UNIQUE Decision, which is the physical expression of the rule's replay-safety requirement rather than an incidental index. `low_cost_fallback_snapshots` is `T-IMM` with a unique whole-tuple hash, which is the physical expression of 'never combines values captured at different instants'.
- migration: Not applicable - schemas/POSTGRESQL_SCHEMA.md already declares the unique Decision on `low_cost_usage_records` and the unique tuple hash on `low_cost_fallback_snapshots`. No schema object is added.
- transaction boundary: The Decision commits with the counter-window read that produced its before values, so its before/after pair is never a reconstruction. The LowCostUsageRecord commits at the SAME durable checkpoint that makes the authorized response returnable, together with `EntitlementCommitted`, the query event intent, authorization/audit evidence and Session activity - a response that is returnable without its record, or a record without a returnable response, is a violation of this rule in either direction.
- concurrency: A low-cost Decision does not serialize a reservation because it takes none: its immutable decision-time before and after counters are EQUAL, and all usage accounting happens later, at the durable response checkpoint, through the separate record. Concurrent identical reads resolve to the same server-minted Decision ID under OD-019, so the unique Decision on `low_cost_usage_records` admits exactly one of them and the loser replays the stored record.
- idempotency: Decision ID is the LowCostUsageRecord's uniqueness key, and under OD-019 that ID is minted deterministically from Organization, human Account or service identity, declared operation, resolved target identity and state version, and counter window. That composition is what makes the record replay-safe with no client key: a repeated tuple within the window replays the stored record with no second increment and no second event, while a different target or a different state version is a genuinely different read and gets its own record. Exact command replay returns the stored response and record.
- background job: Not applicable - PRULE-040 constrains record content and defines no asynchronous work. The delayed authoritative reconciliation it tolerates is a property of counter resolution, not a job of this rule's.
- queue: Not applicable - no queue of its own.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry. A transport retry of a low-cost read is not a new usage: it resolves to the same server-minted Decision ID and replays the stored record.
- terminal failure: A denied read creates NO record. A request that never reaches the durable authorized-response checkpoint creates no record, including a conditional response that returns no representation. If response materialization or the durable commit fails, no response is returnable and no record commits. A Block commits its Decision but no usage record and returns no DTO.
- reconciliation: Counter resolution includes each LowCostUsageRecord exactly once BY RECORD ID, including while authoritative reconciliation is delayed - the delay changes the reconciliation state, never the count. The cached counter view additionally includes every unreconciled record for the same Organization and window committed strictly after snapshot capture, once by record ID, which is what stops a fallback Decision from under-counting reads that landed after the snapshot was taken.
- domain events: Not applicable as an event of its own - PRULE-040 constrains the fields carried by the MTX-040 events and requires `EntitlementCommitted` with a NULL reservation ID for each allowed low-cost read.
- event payload: Not applicable - no event of its own; the payload obligations are at MTX-040.
- event producer: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure. The actor-visible subset of these fields is the separately defined notice field set behind `entitlement.notice.read`, which this rule does not broaden.
- authorization entry point: Not applicable - PRULE-040 is a record-content rule and performs no authorization. The read it accounts for is authorized separately by its own named permission through the sole authorization facade, before the coordinator obtains the Decision.
- permission checks: Not applicable - PRULE-040 adds no permission. The five low-cost operation strings are metering classes, not permissions: `report.view`, `history.view` and `score.read` do not exist in the Permission Baseline at all, and the coincidence of `issue.read` and `recommendation.read` with real permission tokens grants nothing.
- tenant boundary: Every Decision, snapshot and usage record carries its Organization, and the cached counter view is scoped to the same Organization and window as the snapshot, so a fallback can never pull another tenant's unreconciled records into a count.
- error contract: The rule's own subject: reason code is EXACTLY one of `within_limit`, `soft_limit_reached`, `hard_limit_exceeded`, `organization_inactive`, `actor_inactive`, `service_unauthorized`, `entitlement_inactive`, `policy_unavailable`, `counter_unavailable`, `cached_policy_snapshot_used`, `cached_counter_snapshot_used`, `operation_unknown`, `reservation_conflict`; recovery action is exactly one of `none`, `wait_for_window`, `upgrade_plan`, `reactivate_organization`, `reactivate_actor`, `restore_policy`, `restore_counter`, `submit_new_attempt`, `contact_support`, and the mapping between them is fixed rather than chosen. An expired low-cost fallback returns a Block with `policy_unavailable` or `counter_unavailable` plus outward reason `entitlement_unavailable`.
- audit record: The Decision IS the audit record for the check, which is why immutability and completeness are the same requirement here: every field, the counters before and after, the recovery action, the cached-snapshot ID and age, the idempotency key and the retry lineage are persisted on the row and are never recomputed. Each LowCostUsageRecord and its reconciliation state are persisted alongside.
- observability: OBS-REQ-004 is discharged by field completeness rather than by a metric: a cached-fallback Decision is identifiable by its nonnull `cached_snapshot_id` and `cached_snapshot_age`, a retry by its nonnull `retry_of_decision_id`, and a low-cost commit by `EntitlementCommitted` with a null reservation ID. Degraded operation is therefore visible in the record itself.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-040 defines no retention obligation. The 24-hour fallback bound is an eligibility test on `cached_snapshot_age`, not a retention window; window ownership is 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md's.
- test contracts: TYP-DATA: a Decision is immutable and is exactly one of `allow`, `allow_with_warning`, `block` - no fourth value and no later mutation, including after its reservation commits or expires. TYP-DATA: the always-nonnull set holds for EVERY reason code - Organization/actor identity, operation, requested units, idempotency and retry lineage, decision, reason, recovery and timestamps. TYP-DATA: actor/service XOR - exactly one of human Account ID or service-identity ID is present on every Decision. TYP-DATA: the reason-specific null rules, each asserted separately. Organization/actor/service failure: ALL plan, policy, rule, counter, reservation and cache fields null. Inactive entitlement: assigned plan version retained when present; policy, rule, counter, reservation and cache null. Unknown operation: plan version retained; usage unit, policy, rule, counter, reservation and cache null. Unavailable policy: plan version retained; policy version, rule, counter, reservation and cache null UNLESS the bounded cached fallback is used, in which case the complete cached versions and values, snapshot ID and age are stored. Unavailable counter: plan, policy and rule fields stored; all FOUR before/after counter fields and the reservation and cache fields null unless the same complete cached fallback is used. TYP-DATA: every OTHER Block has nonnull before counters with AFTER counters EQUAL to before, and null reservation and cache fields - proving a Block never moves a counter. TYP-DATA: a high-cost Allow or AllowWithWarning has after active-reserved units equal to before PLUS requested units, a nonnull reservation, and UNCHANGED committed units - proving reserve is not commit. TYP-DATA: a low-cost Decision has NO reservation and equal decision-time before and after counters - proving the Decision does not account and the record does. TYP-DATA: the thirteen reason codes are exhaustive and the nine recovery actions are exhaustive; no fixture produces a value outside either set. TYP-DATA: the fixed recovery mapping for all thirteen reasons. TYP-DATA: exactly one LowCostUsageRecord per allowed low-cost response, containing Decision ID, Organization, operation, usage unit, counter window, requested units, idempotency key and commit time. TYP-DATA: Decision ID is the record's uniqueness key - a second insert for the same Decision ID is refused by the unique constraint, not by application logic. TYP-DATA: an allowed low-cost read emits `EntitlementCommitted` with a NULL reservation ID. TYP-DATA: exact replay returns the stored response and record with NO second increment and NO second event. TYP-DATA: a DENIED read creates no record. TYP-DATA: a request that never reaches the durable authorized-response checkpoint creates no record, including a conditional response returning no representation. TYP-DATA: a Block commits its Decision, no record and no DTO. TYP-DATA: failure of response materialization or of the durable commit leaves no returnable response and no record. TYP-OBS: the record commits at the SAME checkpoint as the response - neither can exist without the other, asserted in both directions. TYP-DATA: whole-tuple cache identity - the snapshot is captured only when policy and authoritative counter are simultaneously valid; a Decision never mixes a current component with a cached one; the one snapshot ID and age govern every cached field. TYP-DATA: `cached_snapshot_age = decision_time - snapshot_captured_at`, exactly 24 hours allowed, greater expired. TYP-DATA: both dependencies unavailable resolves to `cached_policy_snapshot_used`, proving policy wins. TYP-DATA: the cached counter view includes each unreconciled record for the same Organization and window committed strictly after capture, once by record ID - a read landing after capture is counted, and counted once. TYP-DATA: counter resolution includes each record exactly once even while authoritative reconciliation is delayed. TYP-DATA: a stale counter path never silently omits a record. TYP-INT: the missing-counter fixture - `counter_unavailable` for a high-cost operation Blocks with all four counter fields null and no reservation. TYP-OBS: every warning and every denial is reconstructible from its own persisted Decision fields alone, with no recomputation against live policy. TYP-SEC: the actor-visible surface exposes only the separately defined notice fields under `entitlement.notice.read`; a Decision's internal fields are asserted absent from that projection.
- rollout: As MTX-040. The low-cost accounting limb is fully contracted here under OD-019; its GET transport activates with the API_CONTRACTS.md Volume II baseline. MTX-097 (PRULE-046) carries the generic envelope obligation across all slices and is not duplicated by this row.

### MTX-092 - AC-PRULE-041 (PRULE: PRULE-041)

- Slice: S-23
- Status: Complete
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-041-suspension-propagation-and-convergence
- interface type: Revocation-propagation and convergence rule. PRULE-041 governs CAP-025 and applies to WF-013 and WF-015, sourced from SEC-REQ-023 and SEC-REQ-024, whose obligation is that deprovisioned identities lose active access IMMEDIATELY. It is a timing and enforcement guarantee, not an interface. This row owns its WF-013 limb; the WF-015 entitlement limb is owned by S-22.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-041 is a propagation rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains what happens AFTER an accepted suspension, not what a command accepts. It applies to the MTX-038 suspension schemas unchanged.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema. The rule requires that suspension does not return success until authoritative Session revocation has committed.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter; PRULE-041 is a rule.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf013::SuspendAccount`, `RevokeAccount`, `SuspendOrganization` and `ExecuteOrganizationClosure`, and at every protected-request checkpoint and durable checkpoint of a running privileged action. This row adds no command.
- command input: Not applicable - the rule constrains the effect of the MTX-038 suspension inputs, not their shape.
- command output: Success returns only after authoritative Session revocation has committed; the audited outcome records the affected sessions and work and the cache convergence time.
- actor: Not applicable - the rule is actor-independent. It constrains the propagation of an accepted revocation, and the propagation is identical whichever authorized actor or lifecycle service caused it.
- organization scope: The suspended Account's or Organization's Organization. Organization suspension revokes every active HUMAN Session in that Organization; Account suspension revokes that Account's Sessions.
- aggregate: Account and Organization as the suspended roots; Session as the revoked root; the authorization context and its caches as derived, non-authoritative state.
- aggregate boundary: Suspension and Session revocation commit together, which is the boundary that makes the rule true: `onboarding-interim-v1` states suspension and revocation commit Session revocation before success returns, so no window exists in which the Account is suspended and its Sessions are still authoritative.
- value objects: AccountState, OrganizationState, SessionState, AuthorizationContextVersion, AuthorizationEpoch, ConvergenceDeadline (60 seconds), RevokeReason.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-041 names no domain service. The next-request denial is produced by the effective-authorization algorithm at the protected-request checkpoint, and the checkpoint stop is produced by the running operation's own recheck.
- repository: `AccountRepository`, `OrganizationRepository`, `SessionRepository` for the authoritative revocation; the authorization cache is implementation-owned technical infrastructure and is not a product record.
- persistence model: The rule introduces no persistent concept. It constrains two things the MTX-038 records already carry: the Session status written at suspension and the authorization context version every protected request atomically validates. The authorization cache is implementation-owned and MUST NOT be promoted to a product entity: Volume I names no cache entity and DM-REQ-001 defines none.
- migration: Not applicable - PRULE-041 adds no schema object.
- transaction boundary: The atomic suspension transaction revokes Sessions before success returns. Cache convergence is explicitly OUTSIDE that transaction and is bounded rather than atomic, which is the rule's actual shape and is why the 60 seconds is not a correctness hole; see concurrency.
- concurrency: The load-bearing precision, stated because it is routinely misread: the 60-second bound applies ONLY to cache convergence, never to authority. `onboarding-interim-v1` states that every protected request atomically validates the current Session version and resolves current Account, Organization, Role Assignments, Access Policies, support scope and classification, and that CACHE LAG CANNOT AUTHORIZE A REQUEST AFTER AN AUTHORITATIVE RECHECK DENIES IT. The authoritative record is therefore correct at the instant suspension commits and the next protected request is denied immediately -- not within 60 seconds. The 60 seconds bounds only how long a stale cached context may persist before it too reflects the change. A design in which a cached context could authorize a request for up to 60 seconds after suspension satisfies neither PRULE-041 nor SEC-REQ-024 and is a defect, not an accepted latency.
- idempotency: Replay emits no duplicate transition: an already-suspended Account replayed under the same idempotency key returns the stored result, revokes no second time, and creates no second convergence obligation. AC-PRULE-041 requires exactly this.
- background job: Authorization-epoch effects propagate through the `control` queue per BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md, whose maximum queue latency before alert on `control` is 30 seconds -- inside the 60-second product bound, so the alert fires before the product obligation is breached rather than after. The revocation itself is synchronous and is not a job.
- queue: `control`, whose identity and ownership are BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md's; this row does not rename it.
- retry policy: Not applicable - the rule defines no retry. A propagation that has not converged is not retried into correctness; the authoritative recheck is already denying, and a missed convergence deadline is an observability and alerting condition rather than a retryable failure.
- terminal failure: A protected request served successfully after suspension committed, a running privileged action that performs a protected side effect after revocation, or a cache still authorizing beyond 60 seconds each breach the rule. Suspension and closure are terminal with respect to this rule: no propagation is ever undone by anything except an accepted reactivation.
- reconciliation: The audited outcome records the affected sessions and work AND the cache convergence time as a measured value, so a convergence breach is detectable after the fact rather than merely asserted in a fixture. A running privileged operation rechecks at each durable checkpoint and stops before its next protected side effect.
- domain events: Not applicable - PRULE-041 emits no event of its own. It constrains the effect of `AccountSuspended`, `AccountRevoked`, `OrganizationSuspended` and `OrganizationClosed`, which MTX-038 emits.
- event payload: Not applicable - PRULE-041 defines no event of its own.
- event producer: Not applicable - PRULE-041 defines no event of its own.
- event consumers: Not applicable - PRULE-041 defines no event of its own. Cache convergence consumes the authorization-epoch effect as technical propagation, not as a domain event.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure; PRULE-041 is a rule.
- authorization entry point: Every protected request, and every durable checkpoint of a running privileged action. The rule cannot be satisfied at a controller alone: the next protected request must be denied wherever it arrives, and a running privileged action must stop before its next protected side effect, so enforcement sits in the application command and the checkpoint recheck, which are the paths shared by routes, services, event consumers and lifecycle jobs.
- permission checks: Not applicable as an addition - PRULE-041 adds no permission. It constrains the effect of suspension on the resolved permission set: the Organization-state gate that denies a suspended Organization's protected mutations is PRULE-019, owned by S-02's MTX-070 and consumed here without redefinition. The permissions that CAUSE suspension are `account.suspend`, `account.revoke`, `organization.suspend` and `organization.close`, all MTX-038's.
- tenant boundary: Propagation is per Organization: one Organization's suspension never revokes another's Sessions or converges another's caches. One human identity holding separate tenant-scoped Accounts keeps the other Accounts active, because the `(organization_id, identity_issuer_key, identity_subject)` uniqueness key confers no cross-Organization authority.
- error contract: The next protected request after suspension is denied with its enumerated reason and no side effect: `F1-AUTH-403 / account_suspended` for a valid subject bound to a suspended Account, and `F1-AUTH-403 / organization_inactive` for an inactive Organization. A running privileged action stops before its next protected side effect rather than failing mid-effect.
- audit record: The affected sessions and work, the cache convergence time, the Account or Organization from and to state, the authorization epoch, the reason, and every denied attempt that the propagation produced.
- observability: Cache convergence time is recorded per suspension and is a measured value, not an assumption. Access-revocation telemetry is required by CAP-025's Observability Requirements. The `control` queue's 30-second latency alert is the leading indicator for a convergence breach.
- retention: Not applicable - PRULE-041 defines no retention obligation. The audit outcomes it requires follow `security_audit` under `retention-interim-v1`, whose window is 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md's.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: Account suspension revokes authoritative Sessions BEFORE success returns, proven by asserting the Session is already `revoked` at the instant the suspension response is observable. TYP-SEC: Organization suspension revokes every active human Session in that Organization and prevents new scheduled work. TYP-SEC: the NEXT protected request after suspension is denied -- asserted immediately, with no 60-second grace, returning `account_suspended` or `organization_inactive`. TYP-SEC: cache lag cannot authorize a request after an authoritative recheck denies it -- a deliberately stale cached authorization context is denied at the authoritative checkpoint, proving the 60 seconds bounds convergence and never authority. TYP-OBS: the 60-second convergence boundary asserted immediately before, exactly at, and immediately after the instant, with every cache reflecting the change at or before it. TYP-SEC: a running privileged action stops before its next protected side effect after revocation, asserted at a durable checkpoint by proving the side effect did not occur rather than that an error was raised. TYP-SEC: the denial holds at every entry point -- route, service, event consumer and lifecycle job -- rather than at one. TYP-DATA: replay emits no duplicate transition; an already-suspended Account replayed under the same idempotency key returns the stored result and revokes nothing a second time. TYP-DATA: propagation is per Organization -- suspending one Organization leaves another Organization's Sessions and caches untouched, including for the same human identity holding a separate tenant-scoped Account. TYP-OBS: cache convergence time is recorded per suspension as a measured value and appears in the audited outcome. TYP-SEC: reactivation is the only thing that undoes the propagation, and it restores only still-active unexpired explicit grants.
- rollout: The WF-013 limb lands with S-23 and the WF-015 entitlement limb with S-22. The rule applies from the first suspension. No predecessor behaviour exists and no limb is withheld: none of PRULE-041's decisions is pending, and OD-016, its only cited decision, is ratified.

### MTX-093 - AC-PRULE-042 (PRULE: PRULE-042)

- Slice: S-23
- Status: Complete; limb withheld
- Withheld limb: OD-031 -- OD-031: routine retention-expiry destruction withheld under retention-destruction-trigger-interim-v1
- contract owner: specification/volume-ii/SECURITY_PERFORMANCE.md#prule-042-retention-and-deletion
- interface type: Authority and deletion-mechanism rule for Account and Organization revocation, closure, retention and deletion. PRULE-042 governs CAP-025 and applies to WF-013, sourced from DLC-REQ-010 through DLC-REQ-016 and DLC-REQ-029. Its decision dependency OD-011 is RESOLVED. It is a compound obligation, not an interface.
- route: Not applicable - PRULE-042 is a rule, not an interface.
- request schema: Not applicable - the rule constrains the authority and mechanism of the MTX-038 revocation, closure and deletion commands rather than their shape.
- response schema: Not applicable - see request_schema.
- controller: Not applicable - no HTTP adapter; PRULE-042 is a rule.
- command: Enforced within `Workflows::Wf013::RevokeAccount`, `DeleteAccount`, `RequestOrganizationClosure`, `DecideOrganizationClosure`, `ExecuteOrganizationClosure`, `RequestLegalHold`, `DecideLegalHold`, `RequestLegalHoldRelease`, `DecideLegalHoldRelease` and `RetryDeletion`, and within `Workflows::DataLifecycle::StartLifecycleDeletion`, `ExecuteLifecycleDeletionCheckpoint`, `FailLifecycleDeletion` and `VerifyBackupTombstone`. This row adds no command.
- command input: The rule's own mandatory elements: the WF-013 named permission, the expected state version, the authorization epoch for the last-admin predicate, the distinct approver for closure dual control and for a Legal Hold, and the authorized new replay generation for a failed job.
- command output: The accepted transition, or -- for completion alone -- the completed job together with its immutable Deletion Evidence in the same transaction. There is no completion output without evidence.
- actor: Organization Administrator holding the named permission for its own Organization; a distinct Security Operator supplying closure approval and Legal Hold dual control through a Support Session; and the tenant lifecycle and deletion lifecycle services, which execute only accepted records. The rule's point about actors is that no single actor completes a closure or a hold: dual control is structural.
- organization scope: The target Account's or Organization's Organization, with WF-013's admission of protected Legal Hold and deletion execution in suspended or closed state.
- aggregate: Account, Organization, BillingEntity, ClosureRequest, LegalHold and LifecycleDeletionJob, each a separate root, plus the immutable Deletion Evidence record.
- aggregate boundary: Two boundaries are fixed by the rule rather than chosen. Completion binds the job root and its Deletion Evidence into one transaction, so no job is completable without evidence. Closure binds the Organization, BillingEntity, ClosureRequest and the created LifecycleDeletionJob into one commit, per MTX-038.
- value objects: DeletionMode (`logical_deletion`, `physical_deletion`, `archival`, `revocation`, `irreversible_destruction`), ResourceClassManifest, RetentionPolicySnapshotId, HoldSnapshotId, AttemptGeneration, ReplayGeneration, PrimaryDueAt (30 elapsed days), BackupPurgeDueAt (35 elapsed days after primary completion), TombstoneId, DeletionEvidenceDigest, BlockedReason `deletion_blocked_legal_hold`.
- domain service: Not applicable - PRULE-042 names no domain service. The hold scope-intersection predicate, the last-admin predicate and the separation predicate are pure functions applied inside the commands and at each destructive checkpoint.
- repository: `AccountRepository`, `OrganizationRepository`, `BillingEntityRepository`, `ClosureRequestRepository`, `LegalHoldRepository`, `LifecycleDeletionJobRepository`, and the Deletion Evidence record written only by the completing transaction.
- persistence model: The job record carries job and schema version; Organization and nullable Account or resource; requested deletion mode; requester, approver and service identities; request reason; active retention-policy and hold-snapshot IDs and hashes; the exact resource and class manifest; status (`queued`, `running`, `blocked`, `failed`, `completed`); attempt and replay generation; per-store, index, cache, key and backup outcomes; blocked or failure reason; primary due time; backup purge due time; state version; idempotency key; timestamps; and correlation ID. A missing mode is invalid. LifecycleDeletionJob, LegalHold and Deletion Evidence are `security_audit`; held data retains its ORIGINAL single class rather than acquiring the hold's. WITHHELD under OD-031 and MUST NOT be built: any retention-cursor-driven partition drop. The physical schema presumes one at its partition-drop clause; OD-031's Risks clause records that no higher layer authorizes it and that it must be corrected.
- migration: Not applicable at this row - no product schema object is added. Two DDL prohibitions are load-bearing: under OD-033 no schema, migration, function or grant may admit a `queued -> completed` edge; under OD-031 no retention-expiry destruction function, grant, scheduled sweep or partition-drop migration exists.
- transaction boundary: Immediate revocation and logical deletion commit BEFORE success returns. Physical and irreversible stages execute asynchronously, and holds are re-evaluated at EVERY destructive checkpoint rather than once at admission. Private object deletion is a separate checkpoint: the worker verifies the entry, deletes the versioned object and key material, proves absence or a version tombstone, then calls the database function to append the outcome -- a timeout or uncertain object result NEVER marks deletion complete. The final transaction reconciles every manifest outcome, writes Deletion Evidence and only then completes the job.
- concurrency: The authorization epoch serializes the last-admin predicate so competing revocations cannot both pass, per MTX-038. Closure dual control serializes on the Closure Request state version: pending to approved, rejected or expired happens once, and expiry wins at the 24-hour equality. The manifest builder emits entries in a fixed dependency order -- ephemeral secret and key material, staged and derived payload objects, join and projection rows, attempt and checkpoint rows, mutable child lifecycle rows, aggregate rows, then optional tenant root -- and the deletion function validates child-before-parent dependency completion, so concurrent checkpoints cannot destroy a parent before its children. A row count other than the expected zero or one result raises and records no success.
- idempotency: Every accepted delete or closure request creates OR REPLAYS exactly one LifecycleDeletionJob; exact replay duplicates no deletion. The deletion function is keyed on `(job_id, entry_id, expected_entry_digest, attempt_generation)` and validates prior outcome ABSENCE for that generation, so a redelivered checkpoint appends no second outcome. A failed job returns to queued only through an authorized new replay generation; completed never reopens.
- background job: `lifecycle_deletion_start` within 60 seconds of acceptance, `lifecycle_deletion_attempt_due` for the initial attempt and its single dependency retry, `lifecycle_deletion_deadline` for primary and backup deadline escalation, and `backup_tombstone_verify`, all on the `lifecycle` queue; `deletion_tombstone_invariant_sweep_due` on `maintenance` every five minutes. WITHHELD under OD-031: no retention-expiry sweep, cursor job or scheduled destruction action exists, and none may be added.
- queue: `lifecycle` for deletion manifests, key and object destruction and backup tombstones; `maintenance` for the invariant sweep. Queue identity and ownership are BACKGROUND_PROCESSING.md's.
- retry policy: Exactly as `retention-interim-v1` fixes it and no wider: each primary, index, cache and key attempt has a 15-minute timeout and exactly ONE retry after 5 minutes, only for `deletion_dependency_unavailable`. Schema, scope, digest, policy and authorization failures are nonretryable. No hidden retry exists.
- terminal failure: Failure or a missed deadline emits `LifecycleDeletionFailed` and a critical escalation WITHOUT claiming completion. A blocked stage is a nonfailure hold state, not a failure. Completed is terminal and never reopens. An unauthorized, stale or self-approved hold or closure request changes nothing.
- reconciliation: Blocked to queued after hold release and failed to queued by an authorized new replay generation are the ONLY recovery transitions. At release the lifecycle service queues each affected blocked job ONCE against current policy; a changed policy may lengthen but never silently shorten an already accrued mandatory minimum. Every restore applies tombstones before any product read. Restore verification runs at least once every 90 elapsed days into an isolated tenant with digest and lineage verification and destruction of the drill copy within 24 hours.
- domain events: Not applicable as an addition - PRULE-042 emits no event of its own. It constrains `AccountRevoked`, `AccountDeletionRequested`, `AccountDeletionBlocked`, `AccountDeletionCompleted`, `OrganizationDeletionRequested`, `OrganizationDeletionBlocked`, `LifecycleDeletionFailed`, `OrganizationDeletionCompleted`, `LegalHoldRequested`, `LegalHoldActivated`, `LegalHoldRejected`, `LegalHoldReleased`, `BillingStateChanged` and `OrganizationClosed`, all emitted by MTX-038. WITHHELD under OD-031: no event represents routine retention expiry and none may be minted.
- event payload: Not applicable - PRULE-042 defines no event of its own. It constrains two payload facts: `LegalHoldActivated` and `LegalHoldReleased` identify exact scope and never include held payload, and a completion event carries its Deletion Evidence reference rather than any deleted payload.
- event producer: Not applicable - PRULE-042 defines no event of its own; the producers are MTX-038's commands and the deletion lifecycle service.
- event consumers: Not applicable - PRULE-042 defines no event of its own.
- serializer: Not applicable - no transport exposure; PRULE-042 is a rule.
- authorization entry point: Each command before mutation, and again at EVERY destructive checkpoint, where the current active LegalHold intersection is re-evaluated. Authority established at acceptance is not trusted at destruction time: a hold placed after acceptance blocks the irreversible stage.
- permission checks: The WF-013 named permissions and no general deletion grant: `account.revoke` and `account.delete` for an OrganizationAdmin subject to the last-admin rule, or for a SecurityOperator for security action with one active Support Session naming the Account and exact action; `organization.close` for an OrganizationAdmin to request and a protected SecurityOperator grant to approve or reject; `legal_hold.manage` as a protected explicit grant plus Support Session and distinct approval, requiring two different active SecurityOperators with the release approver differing from the release requester; `deletion.retry` for an OrganizationAdmin's own Organization's failed job or a SecurityOperator under a Support Session. The Permission Baseline contains `deletion.retry` as its only deletion-related permission and an undefined permission is denied by default, which is precisely why no actor or service can execute retention-expiry destruction today and why OD-031's limb is unreachable rather than merely unimplemented.
- tenant boundary: The job names exactly one Organization, and the deletion function validates the Organization, target, retention class, due instant and entry digest before every entry. `f1_lifecycle` cannot issue arbitrary `DELETE`, holds no `BYPASSRLS`, and reaches only `lifecycle_apply_manifest_entry`, whose target-type branches use fixed qualified SQL with same-Organization predicates and accept no table, column or SQL text. A hold in one Organization can never block another's deletion.
- error contract: `deletion_blocked_legal_hold` for the irreversible-destruction stage ONLY, while access remains revoked. `F1-DOMAIN-409 / billing_entity_state_invalid` for a missing or nonactive current BillingEntity at closure. `last_organization_admin` for a revocation or deletion that would strand the tenant. `stale_state_version` and `stale_authorization_epoch` for stale commands. An unauthorized, altered, stale, self-approved or cross-scope hold request changes nothing. A missing deletion mode is invalid.
- audit record: The full WF-013 audit set in MTX-038, plus the deletion specifics: job, manifest, policy and hold snapshot; every store, index, cache, key and backup outcome and time; pre-destruction digests; tombstone ID; verifier service; completion time; and the SHA-256 content digest of the Deletion Evidence, which contains no deleted payload. No command silently hard-deletes Audit Evidence. Restore-drill records use `security_audit` retention.
- observability: Deletion state, deadline, retry, block, failure and completion-evidence telemetry, plus the five-minutely deletion, hold, deadline and tombstone invariant sweep. WITHHELD under OD-031 and required as telemetry instead of destruction: a `product_history`, `identity_commercial` or `security_audit` record reaching its 7-year maximum with no accepted deletion or closure request is recorded as Audit Evidence and operational telemetry under the existing `security_audit` and `operational_telemetry` classes -- which need no event profile -- and raises exactly ONE critical compliance escalation through the existing operational escalation path.
- retention: `retention-interim-v1` is the FIXED Volume I retention baseline approved by resolved OD-011; despite its name it is not interim, not provisional and not customer-configurable, and OD-011 records that configurability remains disabled and is never inferred from a fixed policy. Protected classes `product_evidence_payload`, `product_history`, `identity_commercial` and `security_audit` are in daily encrypted backup coverage; `ephemeral_secret`, `temporary_processing` and `delivery_package` are excluded; `operational_telemetry` has NO assigned backup disposition and no implementation may infer one -- it MUST NOT be added to or excluded from backup coverage until the OD-011 approval package records that decision separately. Account deletion and Organization closure always include immediate revocation and logical deletion plus eventual irreversible destruction of eligible classes; a hold may block only the latter. WITHHELD under OD-031: the routine retention-expiry destruction trigger at the 7-year maxima of `product_history`, `identity_commercial` and `security_audit` where no accepted request exists.
- test contracts: TYP-SEC: every revoke, close, delete, hold and retry path requires its WF-013 named permission; no general deletion grant exists, and an undefined permission is denied by default. TYP-SEC: closure dual control -- the requester and approver are distinct and separation is PROVEN at execution, not merely at approval; a self-approved closure commits nothing. TYP-DATA: the authorization-epoch last-admin race -- competing revocations from the same epoch cannot both pass. TYP-SEC: Legal Hold requires two different active SecurityOperators, `legal_hold.manage`, a Support Session for customer-Organization scope, and a release approver differing from the release requester; altered, stale, self-approved and cross-scope requests change nothing. TYP-DATA: hold SCOPE is an exact Organization plus resource, class and time intersection evaluated before EVERY irreversible-destruction checkpoint, not once at admission; a hold placed after acceptance blocks the irreversible stage. TYP-DATA: a hold blocks irreversible destruction ONLY -- it does not prevent access revocation, Organization closure, logical deletion or package retrieval expiry, and held data retains its original single class. TYP-DATA: immediate access revocation and logical deletion commit BEFORE success returns. TYP-DATA: the job starts within 60 seconds of acceptance. TYP-DATA: the 30-elapsed-day primary deadline and the 35-elapsed-day post-primary backup deadline, each asserted immediately before, exactly at, and immediately after the instant; at due-time equality the deadline and escalation checkpoint WINS. TYP-DATA: backup tombstones are written BEFORE primary completion and every restore applies them before any product read. TYP-OBS: a missed deadline emits `LifecycleDeletionFailed` and a critical escalation and never fabricates completion evidence. TYP-DATA: exactly one 5-minute retry, only for `deletion_dependency_unavailable`; schema, scope, digest, policy and authorization failures are nonretryable and are asserted NOT retried. TYP-DATA: completion requires immutable Deletion Evidence in the SAME transaction as the completion event, listing job, manifest, policy and hold snapshot, every store, index, cache, key and backup outcome and time, pre-destruction digests, tombstone ID, verifier service, completion time and SHA-256 content digest, and containing no deleted payload. TYP-DATA: OD-033 -- no `queued -> completed` transition exists; every job enters `running` first; an EMPTY manifest or an all-already-destroyed manifest still enters `running` and completes from `running` recording that manifest; a `queued to completed` attempt is rejected and logged as an invalid transition under SM-REQ-007 and no schema, migration, function or grant admits it. TYP-DATA: `blocked -> queued` after exactly one hold release and `failed -> queued` by an authorized new replay generation are the only recovery transitions; completed never reopens; exact replay duplicates no deletion. TYP-SEC: `f1_lifecycle` cannot issue arbitrary `DELETE`, holds no `BYPASSRLS`, and reaches only `lifecycle_apply_manifest_entry`, which validates job, generation, entry, Organization, target, class, due instant, digest, hold intersection, prior-outcome absence and child-before-parent completion, and raises on any row count other than zero or one. TYP-DATA: a timeout or uncertain private-object result never marks deletion complete. TYP-DATA: immutable product history, Audit Evidence, legal-hold, deletion outcome and evidence and retained opaque references are marked RETAIN and are never passed to a delete branch; no command silently hard-deletes Audit Evidence. TYP-DATA: backup coverage is exactly the four protected classes; `operational_telemetry` is asserted NEITHER added to nor excluded from backup coverage, because no implementation may infer its disposition before the OD-011 package records it. TYP-DATA: restore verification at least every 90 elapsed days restores into an isolated tenant, verifies digest and lineage, applies tombstones before read, and destroys the drill copy within 24 hours. WITHHELD under OD-031 and asserted ABSENT: any test asserting routine retention-expiry destruction, a retention-expiry LifecycleDeletionJob, retention-expiry Deletion Evidence, a retention-expiry domain event, a retention-expiry service permission, a retention-cursor sweep, or a retention-cursor-driven partition drop for `product_history`, `identity_commercial` or `security_audit`. TYP-OBS: the POSITIVE interim assertion that replaces them -- such a record at its 7-year maximum with no accepted request performs NO destruction, creates NO job, produces NO Deletion Evidence and emits NO domain event; it is retained in full subject to legal hold, recorded as Audit Evidence and operational telemetry, and raises exactly one critical compliance escalation. TYP-DATA: the ratified neighbour is NOT withheld -- the `product_evidence_payload` 24-month path under resolved OD-029 and OD-030 is asserted to work, and its distinct `security_audit` deletion audit record is asserted to be used INSTEAD of a LifecycleDeletionJob, proving the two mechanisms stay separate.
- rollout: S-23 depends on S-02. The OD-031 limb requires owner ratification, a PM-REQ-009 controlled change to 016 STATE_MODEL.md and 015 DATA_LIFECYCLE.md, and a correction to the physical schema's presumed retention-cursor partition drop. OD-031's own Latest Responsible Decision Point places it before the OD-011 policy package can name its primary-destruction trigger and before any Volume II or schema artifact implements retention-cursor-driven destruction; this contract implements none.

## Change Control

Any change to this matrix MUST:

1. cite the governing Volume I source for every affected row;
2. preserve row identifiers;
3. keep acceptance-criterion coverage total;
4. pass `scripts/validate_volume_ii.py` and its negative controls.
